# Local PS2Recomp patches

Correctness fixes to upstream [ran-j/PS2Recomp](https://github.com/ran-j/PS2Recomp) that we carry
locally until they are upstreamed. `scripts/01_setup.sh` applies every `*.patch` here after
cloning a per-game toolchain into `tools/<game>/PS2Recomp/`.

## ★ ONE PATCH PER FILE — and why

Patches are partitioned **by source file**: each file is touched by exactly one patch. This is a
hard guarantee of disjointness, and it exists because the previous topical split was **not
reproducible** — overlapping full-file diffs of the same file made a fresh clone silently come up
with a PARTIAL patch stack. The cost of file-level patches is losing per-topic revert granularity,
which is largely moot: **every behavioural fix carries an env kill switch** (`PS2X_DMA_TTEQW`,
`PS2X_DMA_REFVIF`, `PS2X_VIF_DIRECTSPAN`, …), so A/B testing happens at runtime, not by
unapplying patches.

**Baseline commit: `14b1e5c` ("Start the main thread with COP0 Status.IE set (#214)").**

### 2026-08-27 upstream migration (ee14958 → 14b1e5c) — what was discarded and why

Upstream landed four large refactors (VU1 #191, GS #204, EE scheduler #184, EE timers #203) that
**absorbed most of our VU/GS correctness stack**. The following patches were verified absorbed (or
their machinery deleted) and **removed** — their full root-cause write-ups live in this file's git
history (`git log -p -- patches/README.md`, state at engine commit `72c4219`):

| Removed patch | Why |
|---|---|
| `02-recomp-vu-translation-helpers` | VOPMULA/VOPMSUB now emitted as true cross-product halves (yzx/zxy shuffles) |
| `03-recomp-instructions-header` | `VU0_CR_I = 21` now upstream |
| `06-kernel-mpeg-stub` | MPEG stub fully redesigned around the EE scheduler (PTS-timed GetPicture, CD-stream integration); our old callback-driving machinery no longer exists. Empirically re-check the FMV; re-derive a fix on the new design if it starves |
| `07-kernel-interrupt-syscall` | vblank worker deleted by #184; pad delivery re-homed → `07-kernel-ee-scheduler.patch` |
| `08-gs-gpu-header`, `09-gs-gpu` | files deleted by GS refactor. PRMODECONT-correct PRIM handling absorbed (`m_prim = m_prmodecont ? m_primRegister : m_prmodeRegister`); presentation moved off the contended state mutex (old present-fairness presumed obsolete — verify empirically); psm-table overrun (SIGSEGV) structurally fixed (64-slot table); census/displog/giflog/quadlog/desync-agg instruments dropped (re-instrument on demand) |
| `10-gs-rasterizer` | file deleted; undefined-psm sampling re-fixed in the new backend → `10-gs-cpu-backend.patch` |
| `14-runtime-core` | wired `GsPendingImageFn` via `m_gs.pendingImageQwc()` — that accessor was OUR addition to the deleted GS; the new frontend has no equivalent yet. **DIRECTCARRY is therefore inert** (patches 11/15 null-guard the unset fn; DIRECTSPAN remains active). If the FMV image-carry defect reproduces, port a `pendingImageQwc()` accessor into `gs_frontend` and re-add the wiring **cont.253:** **`PS2X_GIF_CENSUS`** (default OFF, read-only; `PS2X_GIF_CENSUS_EVERY`, default 10 s) -- GIF traffic census on the runtime's OWN resumable decode (never a parallel re-walk; a tag's payload spans submission boundaries). Tags by FLG with NLOOP and implied payload bytes, plus every GS register write with a per-register last-value table to flag value-unchanged writes. File-static in gs_frontend.cpp on purpose -- gs_frontend.h is included by the generated code. Results (build 606, ablated, per frame): PACKED 6.03 MB (vertex, irreducible), **IMAGE 3.37 MB = 35.9% of GIF bytes** (texture upload -- the decoded-texture cache's target), REGLIST ~0. Duplicate submission FALSIFIED (3.31% of packets, 0.05% of bytes). ★ 72.1% of register writes carry an unchanged value, but **55% of those are PRIM, whose handler ends `m_vtxCount = 0; m_vtxIndex = 0;` -- it RESETS primitive vertex assembly, so skipping it would merge primitives and corrupt geometry.** The safely-skippable remainder is 33% of writes but ~0.3% of bytes, all plain field stores: no win. Lesson: 'value unchanged' != 'no side effect' -- read the write handler, not the value history. ⚠ The census roughly DOUBLES GIF-path cost, so a census run is not a timing run. |
| `16..19-vu1-*` | the VU1 refactor is PCSX2-faithful and superseded the whole group: operand read-side clamp (`normalizeOperand` ≡ vuDouble), result clamp+flags from exact `long double` arithmetic (≡ `PS2X_VU_CLAMP`+`PS2X_VU_MACFLAGS`, plus cycle-accurate flag latency we never had), corrected flag-op decode table incl. FMOR/FCGET (≡ `PS2X_VU_FLAGFIX`), integer-domain CLIP **with** the denormal-w special case (≡ `PS2X_VU_CLIPINT` + the follow-up we had deferred), saturating FTOI, XGKICK walk bounded + reserved-format abort (≡ the 0x4000 cap). KICKCENSUS/FLAGCENSUS/MEMCENSUS instruments dropped with the code they measured |

Still-needed patches were re-verified against `14b1e5c` (symptom present upstream, fix absent)
and re-anchored where the refactors moved code. The level-era VU bistability investigation
(cycles 90–94) must be **re-based empirically**: the VU1 interpreter it measured no longer exists.

## Patch inventory

| Patch | File | Contains |
|---|---|---|
| `01-recomp-fpu-translator.patch` | `ps2xRecomp/src/lib/fpu_translator.cpp` | SQRT.S/RSQRT.S read **ft**, not fs (still fs upstream at 14b1e5c) **cont.230:** DIV.S / RSQRT.S now emit `FPU_DIV_S_EE(ctx, fs, ft)` / `FPU_RSQRT_S_EE(ctx, fs, ft)` (the inline `copysignf(INFINITY, ...)` guard produced an INFINITY the EE never does -- it returns +-Fmax), and MAX.S / MIN.S emit `FPU_MAX_S` / `FPU_MIN_S` (PCSX2 fp_max/fp_min bit-pattern order) instead of `std::max/min`. Semantics live in `ps2_runtime_macros.h` under `PS2X_FPU_EE`; a regen is required after this change. |
| `04-runtime-cmakelists.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/CMakeLists.txt` | unity-build excludes game overrides (via `game_overrides.manifest`); builds `ps2_dbcman_hle.cpp`. **cont.166:** desktop-only `gs_gpu_device.cpp` + EGL link + `PS2X_HAS_GS_GPU_DEVICE` define (0 on Vita/Android -- CPU decode only) |
| `05-kernel-gs-stub.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/Stubs/GS.cpp` | `sceGsExecLoadImage`/`ExecStoreImage` vram_addr is in 64-word BLOCK units (×8 bug still at both sites upstream) |
| `06-kernel-mpeg-datacb.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/Stubs/MPEG.cpp` | **`PS2X_MPEG_DATACB`** (default ON): drive libmpeg's non-stream type-1 "bitstream empty" data-request callback (`sceMpegAddCallback` registrations the refactored HLE stored but NEVER invoked — the 2026-08-27 triage wrongly presumed old patch 06's drive absorbed; the decoder starved forever). Port of the old drive onto the new scheduler design: `sceMpegGetPicture` queues ONE type-1 invocation (`GuestInvocationKind::RpcCallback`, same mechanism as the stream callbacks) before its external wait; the handler's `sceMpegAddBs` feeding wakes the waiter itself; `onComplete` re-arms while fed (v0≠0) and no frame decoded (≤512 rounds/picture), and on v0==0 marks exhaustion + wakes the waiter so GetPicture returns (the game's handler ends the movie through its own job engine). Real-libmpeg semantics: the decode driver pulls its elementary stream through this callback (LOTR ROTK handler 0x13B3A0) **★★★ cont.230 (LOTR FMV block corruption): `sceMpegAddBs` consumes `((size + 19) >> 4) << 4` bytes, as the real libmpeg does** (the game's own copy, SLES_520.17 @0x117188: `addiu v1,a2,19 … sra a2,v1,4 … sll a2,a2,4` around the internal bitstream add) — a 16-byte-aligned request reads one extra quadword past `ptr+size`. LOTR's demuxer relies on it (`AddBs(payload, 4016)` per 4032-byte `2GPM` sector payload); the literal copy dropped 16 real bytes per sector and libavcodec reported ~11,000 damaged slices per movie, while the file's payloads decode with zero errors standalone. Also **`PS2X_MPEG_DUMP=<path>`** (default OFF): appends every fed byte + logs the AddBs calls (`requested`/`fed`) for diffing against the file. |
| `07-kernel-ee-scheduler.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/EeScheduler.cpp` | (a) per-vblank `ps2_dbcman_hle::deliverPadData()` in the `VBlankStart` event (frequency is load-bearing; re-homed from the deleted Interrupt.cpp vblank worker); (b) **`PS2X_HOSTPUMP_RTC`** (default ON): `ps2xBeginHostPump()/ps2xEndHostPump()` scope — while a game-side dispatch pump is open, `checkpointDue()` reports false so pumped guest chains run to completion instead of one basic block per dispatch (the latched `m_checkpointPending` is only cleared by the scheduler run loop, so manual pumps crawled and aborted chains mid-record — the cont.150 boot-parse wedge). PCSX2 parity: `R5900.cpp _cpuEventTest_Shared` delivers interrupts BETWEEN blocks via `cpuException`, never unwinding a half-run chain; (c) **`PS2X_VBLANK_WALLCLOCK`** (default ON): deadline pass floors `m_eeCycle` up to any deadline whose host time arrived — coarse per-backward-edge cycle crediting under-runs the emulated clock and collapsed vblank to ~1.5 ticks/s. PCSX2 parity: `Counters.cpp VSyncStart` paces vsync per cycle-credited frame + `Throttle()` to real time; (d) **`PS2X_INVOKE_COALESCE`** (default ON): `queueInvocation` drops an event-edge guest invocation (GsCallback/Interrupt/Alarm) when an identical one (same kind + entry pc) is already pending or stacked un-started — a >1s guest dispatch (e.g. a host-pump chain) backlogs 60+ vblank deadlines that then fire in one pass and stack 64 nested invocations, exhausting the 1MB per-depth async-stack arena ("EE invocation stack space exhausted"). PS2/PCSX2 parity: INTC latches ONE status bit per cause (`hwIntcIrq` ORs I_STAT) — N masked vblanks deliver one interrupt, and sceGsSyncVCallback misses vsyncs the EE slept through; (e) diagnostics: `PS2X_INVOKE_LOG` (default OFF) invocation-lifecycle trace + an error-path dump of the stacked invocations on the exhaustion throw **cont.232:** the vblank period follows the video mode as PCSX2's `Counters.cpp GetVerticalFrequency()` (PAL 50.00/49.76, NTSC 59.94/59.82, 480p 59.94, VESA/HDTV 60): from `SetGsCrt` (`ps2xSetGsVideoMode`, low byte of each arg like PCSX2's `SYSCALL SetGsCrt`), else SMODE1 CMOD (2 NTSC / 3 PAL, `refreshVblankFromSmode1` every VBlankStart), else the console region default (`ps2xSetDefaultVideoRegion`, from the ELF name; LOTR never programs the CRTC mode). `PS2X_VBLANK_HZ=<f>` / `PS2X_REGION=PAL|NTSC` override. It was a fixed 16667 us (60 Hz) for every game: a PAL title ran its vsync-counted clock ~1.2x fast. ⚠ Open: an intermittent EE-scheduler race (a resumed context with pc=ra=s1=0x3, sp=0x10fcd0; cont.231 §8 saw it once under gdb) hit 1 of 3 level runs at 50 Hz (`abl_vb419.txt`). **cont.232 (d) COP0 Count:** `ctx->cop0_count` was never advanced (frozen at 0 -- any guest `mfc0 Count` timing/seed read a constant); the run loop now refreshes it every 8th dispatch from the host steady clock x the EE clock (294.912 MHz). PCSX2 `COP0.cpp` MFC0 case 9: `Count += cpuRegs.cycle - lastCOP0Cycle` on every read, i.e. Count IS the EE cycle counter; our EE has no cycle counter, so wall time x clock is the equivalent. **cont.232 (e) invocation backlog cap:** `PS2X_INVOKE_COALESCE` is now the maximum number of IDENTICAL pending/un-started event-edge invocations (default **8**; `1` = the previous coalesce-at-one; `0` = never). This EE cannot take the vblank interrupt mid-function, so the N vblanks of an N-vblank frame come due in one processDueDeadlines pass; coalescing them at one delivered ONE sceGsSyncVCallback per frame however long it took (LOTR level: 5 vblanks counted as 4, 16% of the game's vsync ticks lost, its clock at 0.84x wall). Delivering the backlog reproduces the hardware count (PCSX2 `Counters.cpp` VSyncStart -> `hwIntcIrq(INTC_VBLANK_S)`, taken at the next instruction by `cpuTestINTCInts`); the cap keeps the 1 MB invocation-arena guard for a genuinely stalled EE (INTC latches one bit per cause). **cont.251:** **`PS2X_SCHED_PACE`** (default OFF, read-only; `1` = a periodic `[sched:pace]` report every `PS2X_SCHED_PACE_EVERY` seconds (default 10), `2` = also a `[sched:wait]` line per wait >=0.5 ms): instruments `waitForEvent()` -- which deadline source won (VBlankStart / VBlankEnd / alarm / EE timer), requested vs actually-slept host time, signaled-vs-timed-out, a slept-duration histogram, the catch-up cycles credited, and a wait-reason census of every guest thread on long waits (why nothing was runnable). Answers the cont.250 open question 'is part of the 16 fps ceiling frame PACING rather than compute?' -- measured: 100% of waits are the guest vsync-locked in WaitVSync waiting on the real-time-anchored vblank ladder, 0 from the EE timer, so the idle is the vsync quantization remainder (~14 ms/frame, constant), not reclaimable overhead. PCSX2 parity: `Counters.cpp` VSync + `Throttle()` likewise pace to the console refresh rather than running ahead. **cont.251 (fix):** `PS2X_INVOKE_COALESCE` default **8 -> 32**. The cap is a CLOCK CAP: any frame longer than it silently loses the guest's vblank ticks, because this EE delivers N backlogged vblank deadlines in one pass instead of preempting N times as hardware does. Measured with `[hero:dt]` against the real-time 50 Hz ladder (Helm's Deep, 300 s): at cap=8 reported dt tracks reality to 10 vblanks then SATURATES at 10.1 however long the frame really was (11..21 all report ~10.1), putting the game's simulation clock at 0.844x real over the run and 0.59x through the heavy cutscene phase (frames 10-16 vblanks); at cap=32 dt tracks 1:1 and the clock is 1.000x with zero saturated frames. Same defect cont.232 found at cap=1 (clock 0.84x) -- raising 1 -> 8 fixed gameplay frames (5-9 vblanks) but not heavy scenes, so the whole-run figure returned to 0.844x. `=0` still aborts with 'EE invocation stack space exhausted' during the level load, so the cap stays; 32 covers a 640 ms frame and is half the depth-64 arena limit. Validated on BOTH cards (fresh -> Helm's Deep, save -> Pat01). **cont.252:** **`PS2X_EE_PROF`** (default OFF, read-only; `PS2X_EE_PROF_EVERY` sets the report interval, default 10 s) -- direct ns accumulators + call/byte counts around `processPendingTransfers` (DMA drain), `processVIF1Data` and `submitGifPacket`, printed as `[ee:prof]`. Nesting is gif < vif1 < dma so inclusive AND derived exclusive are printed; the instrument's own clock cost is reported and measured at <=0.2% of wall. Built because VIF1/DMA had only ever been PROFILE-SHARE estimates. Results (build 605, per guest frame, raster ablated): VIF1 **26.2 ms** inclusive / 19.0 ms exclusive (51% of EE compute -- not the estimated ~9 ms), GIF submit **7.2 ms** (not ~3 ms), and the DMA chain-walk **0.01 ms**, which REFUTES the '~6 ms DMA' budget line outright. In production `submitGifPacket` costs 39.1 us/call vs 2.1 us ablated at IDENTICAL bytes/call, localizing the raster backpressure precisely: **62% of the EE thread is blocked inside submitGifPacket**. |
| `08-gs-frontend.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_frontend.cpp` | (a) GIF-tag **FLG=3 consumes NLOOP qwords** (ps2tek "Disable" == IMAGE; PCSX2 GIFPath shares one case for `GIF_FLG_IMAGE`/`IMAGE2`) — upstream consumed zero payload and re-read the data as tags. (b) **Per-path resumable GIF parse** (`PS2X_GIF_RESUME`, default ON; PCSX2 `Gif_Path` parity): a tag's payload legally spans DIRECT/DMA submission boundaries, so the active tag (format, loops left, reg cursor, image remainder) persists PER PATH and resumes on that path's next packet — the stateless parser dropped split-tag tails and misparsed the next packet from byte 0 (the movie's `[16 B bare IMAGE tag][data DIRECTs]` shape, the level's spanning draw lists). `ps2xGsSetGifPath()` (extern-C, no header change) selects the path; the native packed fast path defers while PATH3 owes data. Diagnostics: `PS2X_GIF_WALKDUMP`, `PS2X_GIF_SEEDLOG`, `[gif:susp]`. **cont.165:** the privileged-write cases 0x59/0x5b forward DISPFB1/2 changes (with the PRE-write pair) to `ps2xGsNotifyDisplayFlip` — the display-flip presentation snapshot (see patch 10) **cont.230:** `GS::endPacket()` forwards the arbiter's end-of-drain to `GSRasterBackend::EndPacket()` (the DrawRun flush point). **cont.230:** `ps2xGsPathPrims[4]` -- primitives drawn per GIF path (`vertexKick`), printed as `p1= p2= p3=` on the throughput line (`10`). |
| `09-gs-cpu-backend-header.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | `SampleTexture(..., uint32_t mip = 0)` signature for per-triangle mipmapping (safe header: not included by generated code). **cont.158c perf:** `ReadVramFunc`/`WriteVramFunc` are raw function pointers, not `std::function` (every table entry is a plain GSMem free function; the type-erasure trampoline was measured in the per-pixel path), + the `ResolvedDraw` per-batch cache struct (`m_draw`). **cont.165:** `OnDisplayFlip(preFb1, preFb2)` + `ps2xGsNotifyDisplayFlip()` declaration (display-flip presentation snapshot), `m_presentCopy{HasRegs,Dispfb1,Dispfb2}` (the copy pairs with its own display registers), `m_clutShadow` map (CLUT shadow — see patch 10). **cont.166:** `CopyFrameToHostRgba` split into a GPU/CPU dispatcher + `CopyFrameToHostRgbaCpu`; `m_gpuDecode`/`m_gpuSourceId` (GPU present decode, set only on Present()'s snapshot backend); `m_presentCopySeq` capture sequence (unique upload-cache key — the ms tick collides). **cont.168 (phase 1b):** `mirrorPatchRect`/`mirrorFlushPokes`/`mirrorFlushPokeSlot`/`markDrawPages` declarations, the `MirrorPatchSource` attribution enum, the 8-slot `PokeBox` coalescing state and the 512-byte `m_drawPages` mask. `mirrorPatchRect` is declared unconditionally (one class definition per TU) and only DEFINED under `PS2X_HAS_GS_GPU_DEVICE`. **cont.169:** `RasterVerifyCtx` + `rasterVerifyBegin`/`rasterVerifyEnd` (the phase-2a per-primitive shadow verify)  **cont.225:** `ResolvedDraw::fastCt32Z24` -- true when the draw targets the pair this game overwhelmingly uses (CT32 frame + Z24 depth), which selects the devirtualised path in `WritePixel`. **cont.227 (decoded CLUT):** `ResolvedDraw` gains a 16-entry `clut[]` plus `clutValid`/`clutMask` and the reuse state `clutKey`/`clutGen`/`clutPage`/`clutStomped` -- the palette the sampler now indexes instead of re-reading VRAM per texel, and the keys that decide when it must be re-decoded. Sized by a census, not a guess: 83.0% of ALL texel fetches in the capture are PSMT4, every one takes a CLUT lookup, and the CLUT descriptor changes only 201 times in 20,000 primitives. **cont.228 (devirtualised texture path):** `ResolvedDraw` gains the hoisted SampleTexture prologue (`texMip`/`texTbp`/`texTbw`/`texW`/`texH`/`texWf`/`texHf`/`texFstScale`/`texWrapU`/`texWrapV`/`texMinU..texMaxV`) plus `texFastP4`, and declares `resolveTexMip` and `sampleFastP4Diag`. The mip is constant for a primitive, so all of it is per-draw state that used to be recomputed per texel. **cont.230:** `WorkItem::Kind::DrawRun` + `run` vector, `EndPacket()` override, `flushPendingDraws()`, `m_pendingRun`, `m_runPool`, `m_queuePrims`, `queuePrimsOf()`, `recycleRunVectorLocked()`. **cont.231:** `PixelPlan` / `TriRowSetup` (nested, defined in the .cpp), `makePixelPlan`, `writePixelDispatch`, and the member templates `writePixelT` / `sampleTextureT` / `drawTriangleRowsFast` + `drawTriangleFastDispatch` (all defined in the .cpp; the header stays safe -- not included by generated code). **cont.231:** `WorkItem::Kind::FlipSnapshot` + `flipFb1/flipFb2` (the pre-flip display registers the copy pairs with). **cont.232:** `m_presentCopySeq` is atomic; `PresentSourceFresh(uint64_t &seq)` + the free `ps2xGsPresentSourceFresh()` (the presenter's cadence source). **cont.233:** `GpuBatchAccum::lastStateIdx` (state-dedup fast path + the state-run census). |
| `10-gs-cpu-backend.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | undefined texture PSM reads/writes as CT32, not Null — PCSX2 `GSLocalMemory.cpp` fills undefined `m_psm[64]` slots with PSMCT32; LOTR movie sprites carry literal TEX0 psm=0x20 (invisible FMV with a Null fallback). Per-triangle **mipmapping** (LOD from texel-area/pixel-area ratio, MIPTBP1/2 level bases, MXL clamp). Diagnostics: `PS2X_GS_CENSUS2` (draw/xfer censuses, `[gs2:samp]` 1-in-4096 draw sampler, `[gs2:target]`/`[gs2:present]` state traces, `[gs2:degen]` all-zero-XYZ counter), `PS2X_GS_VRAMDUMP` (one-shot 4MB dump), `PS2X_GS_PRESENT_SAVE` (rotating saves of the exact presented pixels), `PS2X_GS_ERA_FILE` (sentinel-file era gate that resets censuses + times the dump/saves to the post-FMV level era). **cont.158c perf (GS de-virtualization step 1):** `DrawPrimitive` resolves the frame/z/tex psm-table function pointers + `framePageBaseToBlock` bases ONCE per batch into `m_draw` (batch.state is immutable per batch — cannot go stale); `WritePixel`/`SampleTexture` call through the cached raw pointers instead of per-pixel table lookups; `if (!m_vram) return` at DrawPrimitive entry (behavior-identical: no VRAM = reads 0/writes no-op). Pure hoisting, pixel semantics untouched. **cont.161/162: `PS2X_GS_THREAD` (default ON since the cont.162 soak; `=0` reverts)** — off-thread rasterization: Submit/BeginTransfer/UploadImage enqueue self-contained copies to a worker thread (strict FIFO preserves draw↔upload ordering; bounded items + upload bytes with producer backpressure); every read-side entry (ReadVram/WriteVram/Snapshot/transfer-state/local-to-host/Clear) drains first; `Flush()`/`Sync()` drain, and the frontend already sequences Flush+Sync(Finish) BEFORE latching CSR FINISH, so guest-visible completion timing stays faithful (the backend interface was designed for async: "GPU backends may wait on fences here"). OFF = byte-equivalent legacy paths. **cont.165 (the menu-text arc — both default ON):** (a) **`PS2X_GS_FLIPSNAP`** display-flip presentation snapshot — the menu redraws its DISPLAYED buffer every frame (clear→scene composite→cover→text), so the finished frame exists only briefly and the per-Sync capture/live present sampled mid-frame states (black or the uncovered hall, never the text); now each DISPFB1/2 flip drains the worker (bounded: one frame's tail, a few/s — NOT the starving per-present drain) and captures VRAM **paired with the PRE-flip display registers**; `Present()` renders a fresh copy with the paired registers; the per-Sync capture stays as fallback when no flip for >400ms (`m_presentCopyHasRegs=false` → live regs). (b) **`PS2X_GS_CLUTSHADOW`** — the level-preload scene renders into a 512×512 CT32 target at fbp page 384 = block **12288** with FBMSK=ff000000 (RGB written, alpha kept), exactly over the font CLUTs the game's own VRAM allocator placed there (an allocator-state divergence vs the real boot); the text DMA stalls on FLUSHA behind the very VU1 batch that stomps, so any restore-at-submit loses. Every 8×2 CT32 CLUT upload payload is shadowed per dbp; when a CSM1 CT32 entry samples RGB-zeroed with the uploaded alpha intact, `LookupCLUT` serves the uploaded entry — what real HW would sample had the buffers not collided. Also **CSM1 ignores TEXCLUT** (cou/cov/cbw apply to CSM2 only; PCSX2 `GSLocalMemory.cpp` parity). Menu-text diagnostics `PS2X_GS_FONTTRACE` (fontdraw/fontsamp/fontup/fontxfer/coverdraw/pixwatch/fontwrites/flipsnap traces). **cont.166 (GPU-backend arc phase 0):** (a) **`PS2X_GS_GPU_PRESENT`** (default OFF) — the presented display rects decode on the GPU (`gs_gpu_device`, patches 22/23) instead of the per-pixel CPU walk; CPU stays the automatic fallback (init failure/non-CT psm). (b) **`PS2X_GS_GPU_VERIFY`** (default OFF) — shadow mode: BOTH decodes run per rect, byte-compared (`[gsgpu:verify]`), CPU authoritative; the promotion gate for defaulting (a) ON. Upload cache keyed by `m_presentCopySeq` (run172: two same-ms Sync captures shared a tick key → the GPU decoded a stale upload → FMV-era adjacent-frame LSB mismatches; level era was 0-mismatch even then). (c) **`PS2X_GS_STATECENSUS`** (default OFF) — draw-state combo census (`[gs2:sc]`, packed-key histogram per DrawPrimitive, 10s cumulative dumps, bit 63 = era gate): the coverage list for the future GPU raster ubershader. run171: 7.2M draws = **49 combos** (CT32 frames only, Z24 only, no DATE/PABE/DTHE/CSM2/region-clamp). **cont.167 (phase 1):** (d) **`PS2X_GS_GPU_XFER`** (default OFF) — `UploadImageUnlocked` posts every CT32 host→local chunk (99.9997% of the game's 1.76M transfers) to the device's persistent VRAM-mirror SSBO, pre-loop cursor baked into the chunk; non-CT32 + badDims descriptors counted/skipped (`[gsgpu:xfer] skipped` — run174: exactly 1, the T4HL 256×232 font atlas). (e) **`PS2X_GS_GPU_XFERVERIFY=<N>`** (default OFF, implies (d)) — every Nth chunk re-read post-upload from CPU VRAM and compared on-GPU (`[gsgpu:xverify]`). run174 (N=4): **438k chunks / 113M words, 0 mismatches**, present-verify clean alongside. **cont.168 (phase 1b, MIRROR COMPLETENESS):** every non-raster VRAM writer now feeds the mirror via `mirrorPatchRect()` — it resolves a written rect to whole 8KB GS PAGES (`PageId()` is monotonic in x/y; +2-page tail for the intra-page block spill a non-page-aligned `bp` causes via `Address()`'s `(bp%32 + BlockId)*PixelsPerBlock`, ≤63/32 of a page) and splits the 4MB address wrap into two spans. Two routes: the **C32-layout psm family** (CT32/CT24/Z32/Z24/T8H/T4HL/T4HH all share `PageTableC32` and a 32-bit pixel stride, so pixel (x,y) is the SAME word and only the touched bit-field differs) reads the finished word back **as CT32** and rides the phase-1 swizzle kernel — which is how the **T4HL font atlas** (the census's one unmirrorable transfer) now mirrors with no new kernel; everything else takes the page copy. Hooked: non-CT32/badDims uploads (through a destructor guard — the write loop returns early on a short payload tail), `PerformLocalToLocalTransfer`, both `ClearFramebuffer` psm paths, `WriteVram`. **Poke coalescing:** LOTR's `LOTR_MOVBLIT` movie HLE blits each FMV frame as ~229k single-pixel `WriteVram` calls (23M/100s, run176a) — mirroring each cost 6.5 GB/s of page copies, so pokes accumulate into **8 dirty boxes**, one per live (psm,bp,bw) key (the blit walks 4 column strips, `bp = 0x3003 + (x/128)*0x400`, so ONE box flushed 1792x/frame), flushed on eviction / size cap / before any other mirror op (device FIFO order must equal VRAM write order) / at every drained boundary: **1,818,624 -> 3,528** flushes for the same data. (f) **`PS2X_GS_GPU_MIRRORVERIFY=<ms>`** (default OFF, implies the mirror) — at a drained display flip, compare the ENTIRE mirror against CPU VRAM on the GPU, minus the pages `DrawPrimitive` recorded as written this window (scissor-clipped primitive bbox, frame + unmasked Z), since un-mirrored draws are expected until phase 2. Attribution counters `[gsgpu:mpatch]`. **run180a: 77/77 verifies, `UNEXPLAINED{pages=0 words=0}` every time** — the only mirror divergence left is the rasterizer itself. Measured incidentally: this game issues **zero** local->local transfers and **zero** `ClearFramebuffer` calls in boot->FMV->level. **cont.169 (phase 2a):** (g) **`PS2X_GS_GPU_RASTERVERIFY=<N>`** (default OFF) — every Nth supported primitive is rasterized on the GPU and byte-compared against the CPU oracle, PER PRIMITIVE: `rasterVerifyBegin` seeds the mirror's frame/Z pages with the pre-draw bytes and posts the raster, `rasterVerifyEnd` posts the post-draw bytes to compare ([gsgpu:raster], tagged with the census key so a divergence names the draw-state combo). Sampling one primitive at a time is the point: it proves the PIXEL PIPELINE with no tiling or submission-order machinery in play. Gated to triangles; DATE/PABE/CSM2 excluded (the census says this game uses none). (h) **`PS2X_GS_GPU_RASTERTEXSEED`** (default ON) — also seed the texture+CLUT pages, since a draw that samples a RENDER TARGET would otherwise read texels only the rasterizer produces and diverge for a reason unrelated to the pipeline; with it OFF the verify instead MEASURES render-to-texture (result: 1 range in 8,551 — this game barely uses it). DrawTriangle's per-triangle LOD block was extracted to `gs2TriangleMip` so the oracle and the GPU prim builder resolve the same level and cannot drift; `s_gsMipmap` moved up to the env-flag block for that (a runtime static — position carries no semantics). **cont.170:** sprites join the gate (`rasterVerifyBeginSprite`); shape-independent state moved to `gs2FillCommonPrimState` and the seed-then-raster sequence to `rasterSeedAndPost`. Two HARNESS bugs fixed, both of which looked like sampler/raster bugs: (a) **`gs2PageSpill()`** — the +2-page intra-page-block-spill allowance is now conditional on `bp % 32 != 0` (with a page-aligned bp `Address()` cannot spill at all), because a fixed +2 pushed a 512x512 texture based at the last page group past the top of 4MB, so the range was REJECTED and the texture seed silently never happened (272,046 divergent words from one full-screen DECAL sprite sampling the page-384 render target); (b) the Z range is now SEEDED whenever the depth test READS it (ZTST 2/3), not only when the draw writes it — a ZMSK=1 draw still tests against Z, and a stale mirror Z made the GPU keep/discard different pixels. Also **`[gsgpu:rvcover]`**, per-reason gate coverage, because "0 mismatches" means nothing without knowing how much was actually verified: run188a sampled 99,755 prims, **accepted 99.8%**, and every rejection is a degenerate triangle the oracle draws nothing for either. **cont.171 (phase 2b step 2):** the BATCH accumulator — a batch is consecutive primitives sharing one render target with no non-draw VRAM mutation between them (both halves load-bearing: tiles are only VRAM-disjoint within a target, and a batch rasterizes at flush time so a mid-batch upload would be visible to every primitive in it), flushed EAGERLY before each mutation posts its own mirror op. Binning is done HERE, on the host: cheap at these sizes and trivially in submission order, which a GPU atomic append would not be. **`PS2X_GS_GPU_BATCHVERIFY=<N>`** (default OFF, takes precedence over the per-prim gate) seeds the mirror from CPU VRAM at batch start and compares the WHOLE 4MB after the flush with nothing excused; **`PS2X_GS_GPU_BATCHMAX`** caps a batch (default 4096). On failure it runs an A/B REPLAY — the same primitives, one at a time, through the proven single-primitive path — which is what proved the 2 residual failures are PER-PRIMITIVE and not an ordering bug. Plus a read-after-write hazard flush (a primitive sampling a page the batch already wrote is ordered within a tile but not across tiles), `[gs2:batch]`/`PS2X_GS_BATCHCENSUS` scouting, and `g_vramMutationSeq`. run200a/201a: 9,764 batches, 488 verified, **486 byte-exact over the whole 4MB**. **cont.172 (residual isolation, all default-OFF):** `gpuBisectFailingBatch` binary-searches a failing batch — for prefix k, re-run the oracle over prims [0..k) into a scratch VRAM from the retained pre-batch bytes while GPU-replaying the same prefix, then compare 4MB (~12 iterations). Needs **`m_rawDraw`** (DrawPrimitive draws PIXELS ONLY, so the replay cannot recurse into the census/accumulator/gate), `MirrorReadback`, and a `quiet` MirrorVerifyFull. Also prints a CPU-vs-GPU coverage mask, a per-pixel diff, a host-side FMA A/B, and a determinism probe. Isolated prim 602/2922 (2 of 40 bbox pixels; z cpu=0x19975 = the flat vertex Z vs gpu=0x19833, a value the prim cannot compute). REFUTED: FMA contraction, alpha-test edge, coverage, non-determinism. **cont.173:** the quiet verify also returns WHERE it diverged (`outFirstWord`/`outStaleWords`) so the bisect stops ASSUMING the divergence is inside the culprit's rect, plus a three-way before/cpu-after/gpu-after dump. That established the failure MODE: before-states identical on both sides, CPU writes colour+Z, **GPU writes neither — it REJECTS the pixel**, so the residual is a failing coverage/alpha/Z test rather than shading drift. ⚠ The bisect assumes failure is monotonic in k; trust its verdict only when the culprit's rect explains ALL the stale words. See the game repo's progress.md cont.172/173. **cont.174 (the perf answer):** **`PS2X_GS_GPU_BATCHPERF`** (default OFF) runs the batch accumulator + GPU tile rasterizer FOR REAL with no seeding and no compares (the no-verify mode an authority flip would need), timing both sides: **GPU 14.4 us/prim vs CPU 28.9 us/prim, ~2.1-2.6x** (lower bound — the GPU figure includes a `glFinish` per batch and excludes host binning). ⚠ A `GL_TIME_ELAPSED` query was tried first and reported 59,000x, which is not believable; wall clock around an explicit `glFinish` replaced it. **`PS2X_GS_NORASTER`** (default OFF) ABLATES the CPU rasterizer entirely — the ceiling on any raster optimization — and **`PS2X_GS_THRUPUT`** reports prims/s plus frames presented. **Result: raster ON 41.04/38.24 fps vs ABLATED 40.02/39.00 fps — overlapping distributions, NO effect.** The rasterizer is not the bottleneck, so the authority flip would buy nothing today; the VU1 JIT is the real frontier. (prims/s is confounded — ablation made it go DOWN, since it tracks where in the timeline a run reached, not speed) **cont.207/209/210 BANDED RASTERIZATION (`PS2X_GS_RASTER_THREADS`, cont.210 default = `hardware_concurrency()` capped at 8; `=1` restores the single-threaded path):** the cont.206 profile found ONE rasterizer thread at 99% with the EE blocked on it 71% of the time, on a 4-core box using ~1.4 cores. Rows are partitioned across threads so no two ever write the same pixel (no VRAM lock) and each pixel still sees its primitives in submission order. cont.209 made the fan-out **batch-level** -- one handoff per RUN of consecutive Draw items, every thread walking the whole run -- so it is amortised over hundreds of primitives instead of the ~5% that were big enough for per-primitive fan-out; `m_draw` became `static thread_local` for that. ★ Runs are SPLIT whenever the frame or Z target changes: scanline banding is sound only while a row maps to a unique VRAM address, and a full-screen sprite whose `zbp=256` was the previous primitive's `fbp=256` aliased two bands together (bisected on the bench to primitive 14879). **cont.210 STRIPES the rows** -- `((y >> shift) % N) == k`, `PS2X_GS_BAND_STRIPE` = log2(rows per unit, default 2 = 4 rows; negative restores cont.209's contiguous strips) -- mirroring PCSX2 `GS/Renderers/SW/GSRasterizer.cpp`, whose ctor builds `m_scanline[i] = (i % threads) == id` and tests `m_scanline[top >> m_thread_height]` with `compute_best_thread_height()` = 4. Contiguous strips left threads idle because the geometry concentrates in a few rows; striping took the cont.208 bench from 87.1 ms (1 thread) / 40.5 (8, contiguous) to **26.6 ms (8, striped) = 3.3x**, and 2 striped threads (49.6) beat 4 contiguous (69.6). The unit-size sweep at 8 threads: 1 row 38.2 / 2 rows 26.2 / 4 rows 26.3 / 8 rows 29.9 / 16 rows 30.3 / 32 rows 39.7 / 64 rows 41.0 -- 1 row splits a 32-bit GS column (8x2 pixels in one 64-byte line) across threads, and large units stop interleaving. **Every configuration returns the identical `vramHash=a421dc9534b6048a`, so this is PIXEL-EXACT, not approximate**; real game, matched on rasterizer work: 188 kprim/s vs 77. `RasterRunFanOut` carries the same never-fan-out-for-diagnostics guard `RasterFanOut` has (`m_rawDraw`/FONTTRACE/CENSUS2 use non-atomic per-pixel statics). **The bench itself lives here too:** `PS2X_GS_RASTERCAP=<afterPrims>`/`_N`/`_FILE` dumps a real primitive stream plus the VRAM snapshot taken before it, and `PS2X_GS_RASTERBENCH=<file>` (+ `_REPS`/`_LIMIT`/`_DUMP`/`_VRAMOUT`) replays it at VRAM attach, printing ms, ns/prim and the VRAM hash, then exits -- repeatable to ±0.2% single-threaded against ±40% for anything measured in a live run.  **★★★ cont.225: `PS2X_GS_FASTPATH` (default ON, `=0` restores the function-pointer path) -- the DEVIRTUALISED rasterizer path for CT32 frame + Z24 depth.** cont.224 measured the swizzle/access block at ~24% of raster self time and then proved the cost is INDIRECT CALLS plus a 128 KB page table that misses L1, not the arithmetic: splitting the accessors behind MORE function pointers turned N indirect calls into N+1 and ran 1-4% slower. This removes calls instead -- `WritePixel` calls `PixelStorageTraits<C32>`/`<Z32>` directly so `Address`/`ReadAt`/`WriteAt` inline, and each pixel's address is computed ONCE per buffer rather than once per access (the `TODO: only one address lookup for rmw` the source had carried). Every other format keeps the original path. **Measured on the deterministic bench with ALTERNATING runs (the machine drifts, and a non-alternating pair produced a 42.25 ms outlier): 1 thread 85.83/85.49/87.34/86.51 vs 88.81/88.38/89.35/88.91 = ~3.0%, 4/4 pairs, ranges non-overlapping; 8 threads 26.18/26.78/27.55 vs 28.48/28.04/28.87 = ~6.1%, 3/3 pairs, ranges non-overlapping.** Pixel-exact: `vramHash=a421dc9534b6048a` in every configuration; level-era screenshot shows correct full-HUD gameplay. **★★ cont.227 (decoded CLUT, `PS2X_GS_FASTCLUT`, default ON, `=0` reverts):** the `// TODO: clut cache` this file has carried from upstream. `resolveDraw` decodes the 16 T4-family palette entries once and `SampleTexture` indexes that array, replacing -- for 83.0% of all texel fetches -- `resolveClutIndex` + a swizzled `ReadCT32` through the 128 KB page table + a `std::map` CLUT-shadow probe + `applyTexa` with one L1-resident load. **PCSX2 parity: `GS/GSClut.cpp GSClut::Read32` expands the palette into `m_buff32` on a dirty TEX0/TEXA and the texture unit samples THAT, never VRAM** -- so a per-draw palette is *more* faithful than the per-texel read it replaces, not less. ★ **A decode-per-draw was a 12% REGRESSION at 8 threads** (a 7.7% win at 1): under the band pool every thread resolves EVERY primitive, so the decode is paid N times while its saving is only that thread's share of the texels. The decode is therefore REUSED across draws, invalidated exactly as PCSX2 does (`GSClut::InvalidateRange`): a process-wide VRAM generation bumped by every non-rasterizer writer (`ResetUnlocked`/`WriteVramUnlocked`/`UploadImageUnlocked`/`PerformLocalToLocalTransfer`/`Initialize`), plus `clutStomped`, a per-draw page test for the rasterizer's own writes -- run for EVERY draw, since an *untextured* one can also write over the page a cached palette came from. Marking takes effect for the NEXT draw, which is what hardware does (the texture unit holds its latched CLUT for the whole primitive) and what PCSX2's `is_draw = true` means. **Verified three ways: (a) the bench VRAM hash `a421dc9534b6048a` in EVERY configuration (1T/8T, ON/OFF); (b) new `PS2X_GS_CLUTVERIFY=1` (default OFF), a differential oracle on the REAL sampling path -- 664,187,296 checks / 0 mismatches in a full game run to f=2280, with both invalidation paths actually firing (genMiss=3,209, stompsArmed=3,627); (c) MUTATION-PROVEN via `PS2X_GS_CLUTMUTATE=<n>` -- 1 = never re-decode, 2 = index rotated one entry, both die with distinct signatures (decodes=1 vs 69) AND a diverged hash.** Per the cont.216 lesson this verifier deliberately runs on the production path, not a copy. Reuse is 277x: 173,840 decodes across 48,122,988 resolves. **Measured with ALTERNATING runs: 1 thread 78.92/83.55/77.39/77.62 vs 88.71/91.21/89.84/90.21 = 13.1% on medians, 4/4 pairs, ranges NON-OVERLAPPING; 8 threads median 23.60 vs 27.72 = ~17.5%, 6/8 pairs (the two ON losses are whole-process scheduling outliers of the kind cont.223 documented).** Level-era screenshot correct. Also adds `PS2X_GS_TEXCENSUS=1` (default OFF), the per-TEXEL census that sized this -- cont.129's PSMT4-vs-CT32 figure counted DRAWS, which cannot justify a per-pixel change. **★★ cont.228 (devirtualised texture path, `PS2X_GS_FASTTEX`, default ON, `=0` reverts):** two changes, both the cont.225 move of REMOVING work from the per-texel path. (a) The `SampleTexture` PROLOGUE -- the MIPTBP1/2 decode, texW/texH and the six CLAMP fields -- is resolved once per primitive in the new `resolveTexMip` instead of on every texel fetch; the mip is constant for a draw (`triMip` is computed once per triangle, sprites always pass 0), and keying on it means no call site changes. The FST divisor becomes a multiply by the exact reciprocal of `16 << mip` -- a power of two, so bit-identical. (b) A PSMT4 texel is read by calling `PixelStorageTraits<P4>` directly (patch 26 exposes `PageTableP4`) so the whole swizzled lookup INLINES, with its palette hit served by the cont.227 decoded CLUT -- an indirect call, a `ReadP4` frame and the per-texel psm switch all removed for **83.0% of all texel fetches**. ★ **The kill switch is complete and shares one implementation:** with the flag off `resolveTexMip` simply runs on EVERY call, reproducing the old behaviour without a second copy of the prologue that could drift (very slightly pessimistic -- the off path stores into `m_draw` where the original used locals -- so a measured gain is an upper bound by those stores). ★★ **The diagnostics are OUT-OF-LINE, and that was measured, not assumed:** inlining the verify block into the sampling lambda cost ~5% (73.3 -> 78.0 ms at 1T) because it runs four times per bilinear pixel and the extra live state stopped it inlining. `sampleFastP4Diag` is `noinline` behind a single `s_gsClutDiag` branch. It also CLOSES a coverage hole the fast path opened: bypassing `LookupCLUT` silently removed 83% of texels from `PS2X_GS_CLUTVERIFY`, so the oracle now runs there too, comparing against the original `texRead` -> `LookupCLUT` composition. New **`PS2X_GS_CLUTMUTATE=3`** corrupts the INLINED READ's coordinate, proving the oracle covers the address computation and not just the palette hit -- all three mutations now die with distinct signatures (1,069,969 / 1,083,564 / 850,188 mismatches) and distinct diverged hashes. **Verified: `vramHash=a421dc9534b6048a` in every configuration; 947,452,817 CLUTVERIFY checks / 0 mismatches in a full game run with both invalidation paths firing; correct full-HUD level-era screenshot.** **Measured (alternating runs, best-of-15 reps): 1T 73.99/74.32/74.18 vs 79.53/80.45/79.31 = 6.7%, 3/3 non-overlapping; 8T 23.04/23.11/22.74/23.65 vs 26.16/25.85/25.73/26.38 = 11.3%, 4/4 non-overlapping.** **★★ cont.229 (analytic row span, `PS2X_GS_FASTTRI`, default ON, `=0` restores the full-bbox walk):** `DrawTriangle` walked the whole bounding box of every triangle and ran the three-edge test per pixel. New `PS2X_GS_TRICENSUS` measured the waste before anything was written: **85.45% of all edge tests could never pass** -- 42.79% of scanlines cover NOTHING, and lead+trail waste is another 48.6%. The textbook fix (incremental DDA edge functions) is NOT bit-exact, and the pixel-exact VRAM hash is the correctness gate here, so the per-pixel predicate is left **exactly** as it was and only pixels **proven** unable to pass it are skipped: each edge function is affine in x, so each admits a half-line; the intersection is solved in double precision and widened. ★★ **The slack is DERIVED, not guessed** -- `E = A*(px-fx2) + B*(py-fy2)` is a catastrophic cancellation for a thin triangle, so its absolute error is set by the magnitude of the two terms, not by the small result; the bound is `(|A|*umax + |B|*|vy|) * |k| * 32 * 2^-24`. The three divisions do not depend on y and are hoisted per triangle (19,712 triangles vs 131,631 rows). ⚠ **The first cut used a fixed 1e-3 slack plus a 2-pixel margin. It verified clean -- but so did a deliberately SHRUNK span, which is the tell that a bound is doing no work; that margin was also more waste than the narrowing removed.** With the derived bound, `PS2X_GS_SPANMUTATE=1/2` now escapes 7,802 / 133,373 pixels. **Verified: `PS2X_GS_SPANVERIFY=1` walks the FULL bbox and flags any covered pixel outside the span -- 0 escapes on the capture, and 0 escapes across 177,248,519 rows / 7,838,146,638 skipped pixels in a full game run to f=2280.** `vramHash=a421dc9534b6048a` everywhere; correct fully-rendered level-era frame. Edge tests **2,194,463 -> 516,240 (4.25x fewer)**, covered ratio 14.55% -> 61.84%, mean walked row 16.7 -> 3.92 px. **Measured (alternating, best-of-15): 1T 72.86/71.77 vs 74.90/74.67 = ~3.5%, 5/5 pairs across two builds, ranges NON-OVERLAPPING. 8T ~2.4% on medians, 5/7 pairs, ranges OVERLAP -- NOT demonstrated there.** Kept default ON because it is strictly less work, pixel-exact and kill-switchable (the cont.220 precedent), but the honest reading is that the 8T pool's ~2.95x scaling hides per-thread compute savings. ★ **The wider finding: the edge tests were NOT what made `DrawTriangle` 20.7% of raster self time** -- removing 76% of them bought only ~3.5%, so the cost is the per-COVERED-pixel arithmetic (colour/texcoord/fog interpolation), which is where the next attempt should go. ⚠ The OFF path still runs the per-triangle span setup (~20 double ops x 19,712), so the measured gain is overstated by roughly 0.2%. **cont.230 (artifact hunt, all default OFF):** `PS2X_GS_RASTERCAP_TRIGGER=<path>` arms the raster capture when that FILE appears (the user fires it by hand at the moment the artifact is on screen; the count becomes a floor) · `PS2X_GS_RASTERBENCH_PPM=<prefix>` decodes fbp 0/128/384 of a replay to PPM (the artifact frozen in a file, which is what made the region-wise prefix bisect possible) · `PS2X_GS_GEOCENSUS=1` (non-finite / max-coordinate / over-scissor / thin / vertex-luminance census, per bench and per game) · `PS2X_GS_BIGPRIM=<pct>` (full draw state + decoded palette of any primitive covering >= pct of the scissor, deduped by draw state, counts kept) · `PS2X_GS_DROPBIG=<pct>` (ABLATION: skip untextured near-black big prims). Two instrument fixes: the big-prim tally raced across the 8 band threads (`s_gsBigPrim` joins the fan-out guard), and `g_gs2EraOpen` started closed so every era-gated dump silently required `CENSUS2`. Also `[gs2:presentsrc]` (always on, one line per 256 presents: how often the presenter falls back to live VRAM). The capture's `GSPrimitiveBatch` is 312 bytes (3x40 `GSVertex` + count + 184 `GSDrawState`) -- parseable offline. `PS2X_GS_PRESENT_SAVE_EVERY` (default 32) / `PS2X_GS_PRESENT_SAVE_SLOTS` (default 16): the present-save rotation's interval and depth, so a series can cover a whole level-era walk. **cont.230 (2026-09-02, build 376/377):** (a) **`PS2X_GS_DRAWRUN`** (default ON, `=0` = one queue item per primitive): draws accumulate in `m_pendingRun` and go to the worker as ONE `DrawRun` item per GIF packet (`EndPacket()` from the arbiter's drain-done hook), before any transfer/upload/drain, or at 1024; backpressure now counts PRIMITIVES (`m_queuePrims`, same 65536 bound) and a 16-vector pool keeps the flush allocation-free. ★ The first build counted a coalesced item's primitives AFTER recycling its vector (0 instead of N) and the EE thread parked forever at the movie's end -- count first. Measured -9.5% wall at matched guest work (e28 75.0 s vs e29 82.9 s). (b) `flips=` on the `[gsgpu:thruput]` line = guest DISPFB flips (one per guest frame; the honest frame rate -- `presents` is the host presenter's cadence). (c) raster-pool default = physical cores - 1 when the automatic CPU plan is active (`ThreadNaming.h`); the worker and band threads pin themselves for the GS role. **cont.230 (later):** the throughput line carries `p1= p2= p3=` (primitives per GIF path, from `08`). **cont.231:** the `PS2X_GS_NORASTER` ablation now also short-circuits `RasterRunFanOut` -- it was only checked inside `DrawPrimitive`, which the band threads never enter, so with the pool on by default (cont.210) an "ABLATED" run still drew two of every three bands and read as "the raster is not the wall". Verified on the level: raster free = 11.3-15.4 fps vs 5.0-5.9 on (build 389, `PS2X_GS_RASTER_THREADS=1` path). **cont.231 (`PS2X_GS_FASTLERP`, default ON):** the bilinear filter runs as ONE SSE 4-channel lerp (`bilinear4`) with an exact integer round -- bit-identical to the four scalar `lerpChannel` calls (per-lane SSE is IEEE-identical, no FMA in this build; `lround` == `floor(v + 0.5)` in DOUBLE for v in [0,255]) -- and the four taps wrap their two u / two v once. Bench (120 k level prims): 3 threads 166 -> 153 ms, 1 thread 431 -> 392 ms, VRAM hash unchanged (`aa9cd72b600021ce`). Not a hardware-model change: the GS's own filter is fixed-point (PCSX2 `GSVertexSW`/`GSDrawScanline` lerps in 16-bit), which this runtime never modelled; the change preserves the existing float filter exactly. **cont.231 (`PS2X_GS_FASTPIX`, default ON):** the per-state specialised triangle pixel loop -- `drawTriangleRowsFast<tme,fst,linear,iip,fge>` (20 instantiations) with the cont.229 span solve verbatim; `SampleTexture` and `WritePixel` are now thin dispatchers over the ONE shared bodies `sampleTextureT<fst,linear>` / `writePixelT<fast,fge>` (the generic loop, sprites and lines use the same code), the pixel-write constants are a per-primitive `PixelPlan` (was re-derived per pixel), and the sampler/writer/helpers are `always_inline` (GCC -O2 had left every one of them as a per-pixel call). Bench (120 k level prims, hash unchanged `aa9cd72b600021ce`): 3 threads 153 -> 133 ms, 1 thread 392 -> 322 ms; the inlining + plan is most of it (`FASTPIX=0` = 134 / 337). Not a hardware-model change. **cont.231 (build 394):** the fast loop interpolates colour ([r,g,b,a]) and attributes ([s,t,q,fog] / [u,v,fog,0]) as SSE lanes with the generic loop's exact per-lane expression ((v0*w0)+(v1*w1))+(v2*w2) and the same casts (cvtt + packus == static_cast<int> + clampU8); `PS2X_GS_FASTCOMBINE` (default ON) does MODULATE in 16-bit lanes ((t*v)>>7 <= 508 exact, packus == clampU8). Bench: 3 threads 133 -> 128.5 ms, 1 thread 322 -> 307, hash unchanged; the combine alone measures zero. **cont.231 (`PS2X_GS_FLIPASYNC`, default ON):** the display-flip snapshot is a `FlipSnapshot` queue item the raster WORKER executes at the same FIFO point the drained flip captured (after every draw submitted before the flip, before any after it -- the cont.165 text-in-frame property holds, present series checked), so the EE thread no longer parks in `drainQueue()` at every flip (49% of the level frame in the cont.231 profile). `=0` restores the drained flip; the whole-mirror verify keeps it. Live A/B (5 alternating runs, equal level-era clocks): NEUTRAL within +-5% -- the raster is still the wall, so overlapping the EE only hides its work under the raster; kept for the day the raster is cheaper than the EE. Not a hardware-model change. **cont.231 (build 396):** `PS2X_GS_BAND_W0`/`PS2X_GS_BAND_W` stripe weights (default 1/1 = round-robin; every other split measured slower on the bench -- the coordinator's prologue is not the imbalance); `PS2X_GS_FASTUPLOAD` (default ON; CT32/Z32/T4 uploads call the psm write function directly, no per-pixel atomic generation bump / psm switch / divisions -- same function, same (x,y,value) per pixel); `PS2X_GS_PRESENT_CACHE` (default ON; Present() returns the last decoded frame while the snapshot sequence and every register the decode reads -- display regs, vsync field parity, context frames, preferred source -- are unchanged; the presenter re-presented the same frame ~6x at 45 Hz). Live A/B (two pairs, equal clocks): +4% heavy windows, +7-20% lighter ones. Bench unchanged. **cont.231 (build 397):** CT32/Z32 upload ROW GROUPS inside the FASTUPLOAD section -- eight 8-aligned pixels of one row share the page and block, at ColumnTable32[y%8][0..7] from the block base (offsets derived from the traits on first use), so one Address() per group replaces eight; the level streams ~4.9 MB of CT32 per frame (24 uploads of 64-wide columns, census cont.231 s10). `PS2X_GS_UPLOADVERIFY=1` re-walks every fast chunk through the generic read: 1.79 M chunks / 1.06 G pixels through the level, 0 mismatches. Live: +4% heavy windows, +10-15% lighter vs FASTUPLOAD=0 at equal clocks. **cont.231 (builds 398-400):** `[gsgpu:handoff]` / `[gsgpu:items]` on the throughput line (runs, prims/run, the coordinator's tail wait, the bands' idle, upload pixels/time, present-cache hits; per-item dequeue / coordinator-raster / tail / other times, coalesced batches); the per-primitive steady_clock reads are bench/ablation-only (PS2X_GS_THRUPUT only needs the count; a countdown replaces the 64-bit modulo); the present cache keys on the vsync field parity only in field mode, two slots; `PS2X_GS_FASTCOORD` (default ON): inside a run the coordinator calls RasterBand() directly instead of the DrawPrimitive preamble (capture hook + throughput count kept) -- bench 130.7 -> 126.9 ms at 3 threads, hash unchanged; live +4% heavy windows. Finding: the bands idle ~20 ms per 121 ms frame EACH between runs -- every run is a full barrier although the rows are disjoint (per-band run queues are the next design). **cont.231 (builds 401-402):** gap timers on the items line (coalesce copy, presenter-priority yields, empty-queue wait, teardown, queue items) with the sub-run accounting fixed; `[gsgpu:updep]` (page-overlap counters between uploads and the draws around them -- the first version's page ranges are too coarse to conclude); `PS2X_GS_RUNMAX` (default 4096) + `PS2X_GS_RUNFLUSH_PACKET` (default OFF): the EE no longer flushes a DrawRun per GIF packet (~3600 items/frame merged by copying every batch, 3.4-6.6 ms/frame); runs arrive whole and are swapped in. Live with the presenter active: NEUTRAL (8.3/7.7 vs 8.2/8.0 fps); kept as the default (strictly less work). **cont.232 (build 404):** `PS2X_GS_FIELD_BOB` (default OFF): FIELD-mode (INT=1, FFMD=0) presents the FULL frame instead of line-doubling one field per vsync parity -- PCSX2's default for a non-FFMD game (`GSRenderer::Merge` leaves mode=-1, `GSDevice::Interlace` default branch presents `m_merge` as-is; `GSPCRTCRegs` halves `framebufferRect` only when FFMD && INT); one decode per snapshot, no per-line bob. `kPresentCopyFreshMs` names the 500 ms rule shared by Present() and PresentSourceFresh(). **cont.232 updep v2:** `PS2X_GS_UPDEP=1` (default OFF) replaces the always-on coarse `[gsgpu:updep]` ranges with PAGE-EXACT 512-bit masks (`Gs2PageMask`, page geometry per psm): per upload chunk/transfer WAR against the last 1/2/4 runs' texture pages, per run RAW against the last four chunks -- `[gsgpu:updep2]`; the gate for the per-band run-queue design. **cont.232 GPU bench:** `PS2X_GS_RASTERBENCH_GPU=1` replays the capture through the GPU tile rasterizer asynchronously (mirror seeded per rep, batch accumulator as the flip would run it, one readback + hash; `submit` = host side alone) -- bit-exact (`aa9cd72b600021ce`, 15/15 reps, 0 differing pixels) but 281-327 ms/frame vs the CPU's 127 ms at 3 threads, host conversion alone 78-116 ms. `gs2RvRejectedTotal()`. ⚠ the conversion reads the RESOLVED target (`m_draw`): call `resolveDraw()` before `gpuBatchAppend()` outside DrawPrimitive. **cont.233 hardware-raster spike:** the state dedup compares against the LAST state first and hashes WORDS on a miss (`gs2StateBytesHash` byte loop was 48% of the submit thread in the gdb profile `hw428sub2`): host submit 77 → 34 ms per frame; host binning is skipped when `GsGpuPresentDevice::HardwareRasterRequested()`; the GPU bench prints per-rep `gpu` ms from `PerfStats` deltas (needs `PS2X_GS_GPU_BATCHPERF=1`) and its BEST figures exclude rep 0 (the shader compile); `PS2X_GS_HWRASTER_CENSUS=1` (default OFF) tallies consecutive same-state runs and 8x8-tile conflict layers per batch (`[gsgpu:hwcensus]`: 21,870 state runs and 30,244 layers per level frame -- both cheap ordering schemes are dead). `resolveDraw()` per primitive is unchanged. **cont.234 (sizing the raster; `PS2X_GS_RASTERABL=<bits>`, default 0, ABLATION ONLY -- the output is garbage and the bench hash will not match):** bit 1 makes the whole texel-fetch path (addressing, wrap, VRAM gather, CLUT) return a constant, bit 2 skips the frame/Z read-modify-write. Both checks sit on the single funnel every path shares (`sampleTextureT`, `writePixelT`), so neither can silently stop ablating the way `PS2X_GS_NORASTER` did when the band pool moved the live path (cont.231 s2). Measured on `cap_e59_mvu.bin`, best-of-15, alternating with the baseline: texel fetch 32% of the raster, frame/Z RMW 35%, both together 62%, leaving 38% of loop skeleton -- three near-equal thirds. Two arithmetic experiments came out of it, both NEGATIVE and both recorded in the code: an exact single-precision replacement for `bilinear4`'s double round trip (verified over all 1,132,396,545 floats in [0,255], hash-clean) measured DEAD NEUTRAL, and a trivial-linear-address ablation of the swizzled frame/Z addressing bought only 9.4% at one thread and 2.6% at three. The CPU rasterizer is bound by its memory accesses, not by its arithmetic. No PCSX2 cross-check applies: at the default 0 this is byte-identical to the previous behaviour. **cont.267 `PS2X_GS_SIMD4` (default OFF) -- TIER 2 four-wide, BIT-EXACT AND REFUTED:** `drawTriangleRowsSimd4<>` walks a row four pixels per iteration (barycentrics, edge mask, z in double, every interpolant in SoA lanes), dispatched on the two keys the cont.262 selector seam already specialises (top-3 draw states, 89.7% of draws), with the scalar sampler/pixel write in a tail loop over the covered lanes. `PS2X_GS_SIMD4_ALIGN` (default 1) picks 4-aligned groups vs starting at spanX0. It holds vramHash=aa9cd72b600021ce over 20 reps in BOTH alignments, so the widening is correct -- and it is SLOWER: 61.602 G (aligned) / 61.272 G (unaligned) instructions vs the scalar 60.756 G. `PS2X_GS_TRICENSUS` says why: meanWalkedW=4.31 px (7.47 over non-empty rows), 42.25% of rows empty, and only 60.12% of walked pixels covered -- under two four-lane groups per row, with the scalar loop abandoning an uncovered pixel after ~15 instructions where a group computes all four lanes. PCSX2 uses the SAME shape (`GSDrawScanline.cpp`: `skip = left & (vlen-1)`, a precomputed head/tail `test` mask, `fzbc[left >> 2]` for one frame/Z address per 4-aligned group) and wins with it only because its JITted scanline never leaves the vector registers -- it pays no extraction tail, which is where our +0.85% lives (head-lane waste is only 0.54 of the 1.39 points). Kept default OFF as the measurement that retires the idea. Also in this change: `GSCpuBackend::solveRowSpan()` lifts the cont.229 row-span solve out of `drawTriangleRowsFast` so the scalar and four-wide loops share one body instead of a third copy (hash-verified unchanged). **cont.268 `PS2X_GS_TEXWRAP` + `PS2X_GS_TEXTAP` (both default ON, `=0` each restore the old path) -- hoist the sampler's loop-invariant decisions out of the per-TEXEL path:** `wrapTextureCoordinate` is a `switch (mode & 3)` on `m_draw.texWrapU/V` called FOUR TIMES PER PIXEL under bilinear (it inlines as a compare chain -- 25 `$0x3` sites in the hot symbol, no jump table), and every tap additionally runs `if (s_gsTexCensus)` / `if (s_gsTexDiag)` / `if (s_gsClutDiag)` on globals fixed at startup. `PS2X_GS_TEXCENSUS` says this game uses only two wrap pairs -- REPEAT/REPEAT 96.15%, CLAMP/CLAMP 3.85% -- and T4 is 82.56% of texels, so `resolveTexMip` now resolves `texWrapClass` / `texWrapMaskU/V` / `texTapFast` ONCE PER DRAW and the sampler branches once per call instead of per texel; each arm is the corresponding `wrapTextureCoordinate` case (and the `fastP4` tap) copied verbatim, so it is bit-exact by construction. The cont.262 PIPESPEC move one level down. Measured on the level capture, 1 thread, 20 reps, `perf stat -e instructions`: **60.756 G -> 58.606 G = -3.54%** (WRAP alone -0.90%, TAP alone -2.74%, and hoisting both to per-draw a further -0.86%), `vramHash=aa9cd72b600021ce` in all four flag combinations and at both 1 and 3 threads. On the LIVE level path, prim-matched to 25.5 M prims with a balanced A/B/B/A (the box's wall clock drifts): ON 206.8 / 201.1 s vs OFF 234.0 / 218.2 s = **-9.8%**, non-overlapping populations; run invariants clean (`missing-target: 0`, `UNACCOUNTED=0`). The live gain exceeds the single-thread instruction reduction, consistent with cont.265's finding that at IPC 3.13 on a 4-physical-core host the raster threads contend for EXECUTION PORTS -- removing instructions relieves that superlinearly. No PCSX2 cross-check applies to the arms themselves (they are our own existing case bodies unchanged); the structure mirrors PCSX2 `GSScanlineSelector`, which likewise decides wrap mode at setup time. **cont.270 `PS2X_GS_QUADWRITE` (default ON, `=0` restores the scalar write) + `PS2X_GS_SIMD4` promoted to default ON -- the pixel write FOUR AT A TIME with colour resident in 16-bit SIMD lanes.** This is the STRUCTURE of PCSX2 `GS/Renderers/SW/GSDrawScanline.cpp` (rb/ga 16-bit lanes carried through fog, blend and write; colour never unpacked to scalar bytes) with OUR arithmetic preserved bit-for-bit -- a verbatim port was rejected because PCSX2 keeps colour PRE-SCALED (`gaf.srl16<7>()`) and blends with `modulate16<1>` = `sll16<2>`+`mul16hrs` = (rb*As+32)>>6, a different scale AND rounded where ours truncates, which would change every blended pixel and cost the bench-hash oracle. Exactness devices: the blend's ((s-d)*a)>>7 needs 17 signed bits, reconstructed exactly as `srli(mullo,7) | slli(mulhi,9)`; fog's (f*c)>>8 products are <=65025 so a single unsigned `mullo` is exact; ZTST=GEQUAL's UNSIGNED compare is done by biasing both sides with 0x80000000 (SSE4.1 has no unsigned 32-bit compare); clampU8 is min/max against 0/255. Addressing follows PCSX2's `fzbc[left >> 2]`: one `Address()` per buffer per 4-aligned group plus the page-table row, since a 4-aligned group never crosses a page in x. Inactive lanes are written back with the value just read, which is observationally identical because band threads own whole rows (cont.210) and keeps the store branch-free. ★★★ THE RESULT REVERSES cont.267: the same four-wide loop that measured +2.3% with a scalar tail measures **56.573 G instructions vs 58.583 G scalar = -3.43%** once the write is wide too -- a 5.7-point swing, and **bit-exact (`aa9cd72b600021ce`) at 1, 3 and 8 threads**. Partial widening cannot pay; the glue at the seam costs more than the width saves. The dispatcher now takes the four-wide loop ONLY where the four-wide write can follow (no DATE, no armed diagnostic), falling back to the scalar loop rather than the hybrid. `PS2X_GS_QUADOFF` is a per-stage bisect mask (1 fog / 2 alpha test / 4 blend / 8 fbmsk+preserve-alpha). Live A/B/B/A was directionally positive but INCONCLUSIVE at n=2/arm (ON 153.8/171.0 s vs OFF 173.1/178.9 s, overlapping); invariants clean (missing-target 0, UNACCOUNTED=0). **cont.271 `PS2X_GS_QUADTEX` (default ON, `=0` restores the per-pixel sampler) -- the bilinear sampler's SETUP four pixels at a time** (perspective divide, texture scale, -0.5, floor, the fractional weights, the wrap), which cont.267's attribution put at 24.7% of the hot symbol and which is pure lane arithmetic on values the SIMD4 loop already holds in SoA. ⚠ Deliberately narrow: the 16 texel gathers stay scalar (irreducible) and `bilinear4` stays PER PIXEL because it is already lane-parallel across RGBA -- widening it would move the same 16 floats through the same op count. Serves only the devirtualised PSMT4 tap (82.56% of texels) with REPEAT/REPEAT or CLAMP/CLAMP (100% of this game's wrap pairs); anything else keeps `sampleTextureT`. Bit-exactness comes from writing each lane as the scalar expression with the same operands in the same order: `divps` is correctly rounded per lane, `texUf = (s*invQ)*texWf` keeps the scalar's left association, and `fx` is `su - _mm_cvtepi32_ps(u0)` (converted back from the INT, not from the floor result, so it matches even where the two could differ). Measured, 1 thread, 20 reps: **56.637 G -> 53.175 G = -6.11%**, the largest single step of the arc; against the fully scalar path 58.605 -> 53.175 G = **-9.27%**. `aa9cd72b600021ce` at 1, 3 and 8 threads. Live A/B/B/A prim-matched to 25.5 M prims: ON 150.7 / 157.9 s vs OFF 178.6 / 173.9 s = **-12.4%, NON-OVERLAPPING** (worst ON below best OFF); invariants clean. Cumulative for the raster arc (cont.268 + 270 + 271): **60.756 G -> 53.175 G = -12.5% instructions, all bit-exact.** |
| `11-memory-header.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_memory.h` | `GsPendingImageFn` hook (cycle 83 DIRECT image carry; currently UNWIRED — see the 14 row above) |
| `12-memory-dma.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_memory.cpp` | scratchpad SPR channels (ch8/ch9 — load-bearing for all long text); chain-walk cap 4096 → `1<<20` (`PS2X_DMA_MAXCHAINTAGS`); **`PS2X_DMA_TTEQW`** 16-byte parity-preserving TTE embeds; **`PS2X_DMA_REFVIF`** ref-tag (id 3/4) TTE embeds; DIRECTSPAN reset calls. Diagnostics (CHAINLOG/CHAINBIG/CHAINSRC/TAGVIF) dropped in the 2026-08-27 migration. **cont.165:** the three GS privileged-register write paths forward DISPFB1/2 changes (with the PRE-write pair) to `ps2xGsNotifyDisplayFlip` — the recompiled code's `sd`/`sw` stores to 0x12000070/90 land here, not in the frontend case (this was the path the flips actually take) |
| `13-pad.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_pad.cpp` | headless CROSS driver + `PS2X_PAD2_LOG` (both default-OFF, test infra) **cont.230: `PS2X_PAD_LY_AT=<call>` / `PS2X_PAD_LY_LEN` (100) / `PS2X_PAD_LY` (0x00 = stick fully up) / `PS2X_PAD_LX` (0x80) / `PS2X_PAD_UP=1`** -- headless LEFT-STICK driver, the same shape as the CROSS driver (forced stick bytes for LEN readState calls from call #AT, optional d-pad UP), so a run can walk the player without window focus. Default OFF. **cont.232:** `PS2X_PAD_SCRIPT=<file>` (headless input SCRIPT: `<fromCall> <lx> <ly> <rx> <ry> <pressHex>` per line; a step OWNS the buttons, releasing the CROSS driver's hold). **cont.232 keyboard split:** the ARROW keys are the d-pad ONLY and WASD is the left stick ONLY (before, either set cleared the d-pad bit AND deflected the stick, so a screen accepting both moved twice per press -- the user's 'up/down presses twice'). `PS2X_PAD_KEYSTICK=0` restores digital-only keys (arrows + WASD both on the d-pad, no stick). PCSX2 keeps DPad*/LeftStick* as distinct bindings (`pad/PadDualshock2.cpp`). **cont.237:** `PS2X_PAD_RECORD=<file>` -- the INVERSE of `PS2X_PAD_SCRIPT`: records the effective pad state (after all drivers, before publication) one line per CHANGE, in exactly the format the script replays, flushed per line so a killed run still leaves a usable trace. Hand-written pad scripts do not work for anything needing real play (cont.232 abandoned "scripting past the orcs"; cont.237 got a ladder climb only after several calibration failures, and when the ladder becomes available varies run to run) -- recording lets the user play a sequence ONCE and replay it headlessly forever. No hardware behaviour: diagnostic harness only, default OFF. |
| `15-vif1-interpreter.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_vif1_interpreter.cpp` | **`PS2X_VIF_DIRECTCARRY`** (inert until GsPendingImageFn is re-wired) + **`PS2X_VIF_DIRECTSPAN`** (cross-transfer DIRECT span state; PCSX2 `vif1.tag.size` persists); the DIRECT GIF walkers treat **FLG=3 as IMAGE** (companion to patch 08); with `PS2X_GIF_RESUME` the VIF-side image-continuation carry stands down (its synthesized IMAGE tags would corrupt a resuming stream); **★★ `DIRECT imm=0` consumes ZERO qwords** (`PS2X_VIF_IMM0DIRECT=65536` restores the nominal reading; `PS2X_VIF_IMM0SPAN=1` additionally re-arms its cross-buffer span) — the game ends EVERY frame chain with a terminal `[NOP][DIRECT imm=0]` TTE embed followed by nothing, and the fictional 56,370-qw span ate the first ~900 KB of every subsequent frame chain (the cont.156 phantom-DIRECTHL corruption cascade; the original imm=0-span evidence, run_c86A, was the half-phase misparse that PS2X_DMA_TTEQW fixed); sized (imm≠0) DIRECTs still span — the FMV's 32767-qw uploads legitimately split. **Offline proof (vifbuf_bad.bin): with imm=0→0 the captured 3.7 MB level chain parses PERFECTLY at both layers** (VIF walk consumes the buffer exactly, 0 unknown opcodes; the concatenated 3.16 MB GIF stream = 184 tags, 0 implausible, 0 overrun) — with the 65536 reading it swallowed ~1 MB per occurrence (embeds decode as GIF NOPs so it half-worked, until UNPACK float payloads were read as tags → the phantom-PACKED garbage register writes). Result: post-movie garbage FRAME writes 1024+→0, garbage targets 1442→0, presents restored to full cadence, the level renders fully textured gameplay. Diagnostics: `PS2X_VIF_CENSUS`, `PS2X_VIF_SEQLOG` (per-delivery + big-chain end forensics), `PS2X_VIF_DIRECTBIG` (+ command ring), `PS2X_VIF_BUFDUMP` **cont.257:** **`PS2X_VIF_PROF`** (default OFF, read-only; `PS2X_VIF_PROF_EVERY`, default 10 s) -- the INTRA-VIF1 breakdown, and it CORRECTS cont.252's headline. cont.252's `[ee:prof]` `vif1` accumulator wraps the whole of `processVIF1Data`, but MSCAL/MSCALF/MSCNT run the VU1 microprogram SYNCHRONOUSLY inside it (`ps2_runtime.cpp` `setVu1MscalCallback` -> `m_vu1.execute`) and DIRECT submits GIF from inside it, so the real nesting is **gif < vu1 < vif1 < dma** -- "VIF1 = 26.2 ms/frame, 51% of EE compute" was an aggregate of three subsystems. Per-class scopes (unpack / unpack write loop / direct / mpg / mscal) with counts; `other` derived. **Measured (build 612, ablated renderer, 4936 frames / 291 s): VIF1 inclusive 21.21 ms/frame = VU1 13.43 (63.3%) + unpack 6.42 (30.3%, write loop 5.94) + direct 0.84 + other 0.51. VIF1's OWN work is 7.78 ms/frame, not 19.0.** Cross-validated twice: `[vif:prof] total` 104.68 s vs `[ee:prof] vif1` 104.41 s, and `[vif:prof] mscal` 66.28 s vs `[vu1:microvu] ns` 66.81 s. ★ **`PS2X_VIF_SLOW2=1`** (default OFF) = the BIT-EXACT 2x-slow sizing knob for the unpack write loop (as `PS2X_VU0_SLOW2` sized VU0): repeats the idempotent write loop; excluded for VIF mode 2, which accumulates into `vif1_regs.row` (this game issues mode 2 zero times over 59.4M unpacks). Verified faithful -- per-call unpack 542 -> 1057 ns (1.95x), fps 16.96 -> 14.01. ⚠ **`PS2X_VIF_ABL=1` (skip the unpack write loop) is NOT a valid ceiling ablation**: with no unpack writes VU1 runs on stale data memory, loops on garbage counters and burns its whole 65536-cycle budget -- mscal 4.97 -> 103.32 us/call (20x), 787 frames instead of 5041. It changes the workload instead of removing work (the cont.231 ablation-path-drift trap); kept only to reproduce that result. ★ Also measured, `PS2X_VIF_CENSUS` over 59.4M unpacks: vl.vn V4-8 29.0% / V4-32 26.7% / V3-32 14.2% / V2-16 14.1% / V4-16 13.7%; mask 28.5%, mode 0 86.3% / mode 1 13.7% / mode 2+3 ZERO, cl<wl (fill) NEVER, cl==wl 86.3%. |
| `16-vu1-core.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/vu/ps2_vu1_core.cpp` | Three XGKICK fixes (cont.155, all default ON with kill switches): **`PS2X_VU1_KICKSTALL`** — a second XGKICK while PATH1 still streams STALLS the VU until it completes (hardware behavior, PCSX2 models it); upstream reset `m_xgkick` unconditionally, silently destroying every in-flight kick's packet — with PATH1 at ~8 B/cycle and batch kicks a few dozen cycles apart this lost nearly every packet except each program's last (level-era kick-drops 1024+→24, scene coverage transformed). **`PS2X_VU1_KICKFLG3`** — GIF-tag FLG=3 is IMAGE ("Disable", ps2tek; PCSX2 `GIF_FLG_IMAGE2` falls through), not reserved; upstream aborted kick+program on every FLG=3 packet. **`PS2X_VU1_KICKDROP_SOFT`** — an oversized/garbage tag walk drops THE KICK (bounded-walk host safety) but no longer kills the whole microprogram via the reserved-instruction abort (hardware never stops the VU over GIF data content). Plus rate-limited `[VU1 kick-drop]` byte dumps + one-shot per-pc `[VU1 kick-dump]` deep dumps (VI regs + microcode window). **`PS2X_VU1_COMMITSKIP`** (default ON, `=0` reverts; cont.158a perf): pipeline-commit tracking — a file-static owner-guarded watermark (earliest readyCycle among valid entries) + per-array valid counts let `commitReadyPipelines()` return O(1) when nothing is due, skip empty arrays when scanning, and `pipelinesPending()` answer O(1); profiling measured the unconditional ~50-slot 7-array scan per VU cycle at 28% of total runtime (hot from the pair loop, which scans TWICE per cycle — top-of-loop + `advanceOneCycle`). Pure memoization: commit timing/order/values untouched (no hardware semantics changed, nothing to PCSX2-mirror); staleness is one-directional (can only add scans, never skip a due commit). **cont.158d:** `resetScheduler()` no longer clears the ready/latest-write bookkeeping (m_cycle + write sequence are monotonic across programs ⇒ stale entries inert; `reset()` — the only place m_cycle goes backward — clears it itself) and skips the pipeline-array clears when the tracking proves them empty; companion to patch 18's no-reset-per-VCALLMS (PCSX2 `VU0micro.cpp vu0ExecMicro`: no wholesale reset on VCALLMS). **cont.159:** FMAC exact-result fast path — decode + q/i normalization hoisted to once per instruction (was per LANE ×4, with the whole arithmetic re-run in x87 `long double` per lane); per-lane exact arithmetic now computes in **double** by default (`PS2X_VU1_EXACTLD=1` restores long double). Fidelity: the exact value only drives clamp/flag decisions, float products are EXACT in double, and PCSX2's reference (`VUops.cpp vuDouble` — returns *float*) computes VU arithmetic in plain FLOAT, so double stays strictly more precise than the proven reference. **cont.160:** per-unit tracking slots (`s_pipeSlots[2]`, pointer-keyed `pipeFor(this)`) — the single shared slot ping-ponged between the VU0/VU1 instances, forcing a full status-quo scan per switch and starving VU0's resetScheduler skip; eviction (>2 instances, never today) degrades to full scans, safe by construction. **cont.175 (ABLATION INSTRUMENT, default OFF): `PS2X_VU1_NOSCHED=1`** — makes the cycle-accurate pipeline/hazard model FREE: `calculatePairReadyCycle` returns `m_cycle` (nothing ever stalls), `markPairWrites` is a no-op, and every queued write (vf/vi/acc/store + the FMAC flag entry) applies to architectural state immediately, so the pipeline arrays stay empty and `commitReadyPipelines()` answers O(1) via the cont.158b watermark. **Deliberately unfaithful — never enable for a correctness run**; it exists to measure the CEILING on any VU1 work that resolves hazards statically (a JIT), per the ablate-before-optimizing rule. Measured cont.175: frame-matched level-era guest frames f1740→f1980 take 161.1/95.7 s baseline vs 70.1/75.3 s ablated (non-overlapping ⇒ real) = **≈1.8× (bounds 1.27–2.30×)**, with MOVIE-END unchanged (216.7/216.4 vs 215.5/215.5 s) as the control, 0 kick-drops/degen/reserved in both, and presented frames still visually correct. **cont.175 stage 1a — `PS2X_VU1_FASTSCAN` (default ON, `=0` reverts to the legacy scans in the same binary for A/B):** `calculatePairReadyCycle` and `markPairWrites` now walk only the SET bits of the lane/VI masks (`nextSetBit`/`__builtin_ctz`) instead of scanning all 15 VI regs ×2 usages + 4 lanes per vf read per call — and `run()`'s "which VI does this pair write" scan is a single `ctz`. Pure loop transformation, **bit-exact by construction**: the same slots are visited and every consumer is `std::max()` (commutative/associative) or a write to an independent per-lane/per-register slot, so the changed visit order is immaterial — nothing to PCSX2-mirror (no modelled hardware semantics touched). **`PS2X_VU1_PERF` (default OFF)** reports interpreter throughput as **ns per issued instruction pair** (one clock read per `run()` call): the low-variance metric this arc needs — 0.2% run-to-run vs 68% for wall-clock frame timing. Measured: legacy **280.7/281.2 ns/pair → stage 1a 208.1/208.3 → NOSCHED ceiling 139.4** ⇒ **1.35×, capturing ~51% of the ablation ceiling**, with 0 kick-drops/degen/reserved, identical pairs/call (same microcode workload) and a correct HUD gameplay frame. **cont.175 stage 1b:** the `PipeTrack` per-array VALID COUNTS became per-array **valid-slot BITMASKS** (every prior use compared them to 0, which a mask answers identically), so `commitReadyPipelines` retires **O(valid) instead of O(capacity)** (`retirePipe`) and the queue* helpers pick a free slot with one `ctz` instead of a linear probe (`freeSlot`, which re-checks `.valid` and falls back to the probe, so a stale mask can cost a scan but can never overwrite a live entry). Commit order is ascending slot index in BOTH paths and cross-array order is unchanged. **`PS2X_VU1_PIPEVERIFY` (default OFF)** cross-checks each mask against a full scan and reports+repairs any slot the mask MISSED (the only unsafe direction): **0 MASK MISS over a full run to MOVIE-END**. Worth only **~1.5% (208.2 → 204.6 ns/pair)** despite collapsing the `commitReadyPipelines` profile leaf 18% → 3% — the work moved to the lambda `operator()` (8%), a reminder that sampling-leaf attribution shifts between symbols and **ns/pair is the ground truth**. Cumulative stage 1a+1b: **280.7 → 204.6 ns/pair = 1.37×, ~54% of the NOSCHED ceiling**. **cont.176 ablation instruments (both default OFF, measurement only):** **`PS2X_VU1_NOEXACT=1`** skips the per-lane exact double recompute (measured worth only **7.5%** despite the FMAC leaves summing to ~15% of the profile) and **`PS2X_VU1_NOFLAGS=1`** skips ALL FMAC flag work — exact recompute + clamp + MAC/status derivation + the flag-pipeline entry — which is worth **27% (204.2 → 149.4 ns/pair)** and sizes the "lazy flags" optimization (compute flags only for microprograms that actually read them). A bit-exact FASTFMAC shortcut was built, verified and **REVERTED**: it is sound only for SINGLE-rounding ops (the verify mode produced the counterexample `op=0x29` MADD, float `7f7ffffe` vs an exact sum that overflows to `7f7fffff`+flag, because `acc+vs*vt` rounds twice), and MADD/MSUB dominate this workload, so it measured **zero** benefit. **cont.177 — `PS2X_VU1_LAZYFLAGS` (default 1 = ON, `=0` reverts): LAZY FMAC FLAGS, 210.7 → 175.5 ns/pair = 1.20×.** MAC/status flags are derived for every FMAC but are only observable through ten lower opcodes, so `rebuildDecodedCodeCache` (which already walks the whole buffer and re-runs on every code-generation change) now also records "does this program contain a MAC/status reader" — `0x14/0x16/0x17` FSEQ/FSAND/FSOR read status, `0x18/0x1A/0x1B` FMEQ/FMAND/FMOR read mac; the CLIP readers are deliberately excluded because clip comes from `queueClip` and is untouched (including them would disqualify most T&L programs, CLIP+FCAND being the standard cull test). When the scan is clean the fast path skips `calculateFmacProductSticky` + `updateFmacFlags`. **★ It does NOT skip `normalizeFmacResult`** — that call *clamps the lane values in place* (`fmacClampExact`/`normalizeResult` take the float by reference) and the PS2 has no NaN/Inf, so `PS2X_VU1_NOFLAGS` (which does skip it) is **value-unfaithful**, not merely flag-unfaithful: its 27% is an upper bound, and the ~17% that is genuinely flag-only is what this takes. VU1 only — VU0's flags ARE EE-visible (`copyVu0StateToContext` copies `state.mac/clip/status` into the R5900 context on every VCALLMS). PCSX2 parity: microVU does the same analysis and more aggressively — `microVU_Analyze.inl` `mVUanalyzeMflag`/`mVUanalyzeSflag`/`mVUanalyzeCflag` set `mVUlow.readFlags`, and `flagSet()` walks backward so only the last few FMACs before a reader set `mFLAG.doFlag`/`sFLAG.doNonSticky`; our whole-program gate is strictly more conservative than its per-block one. **Cross-program safety (the non-obvious part):** mac/status persist ACROSS programs, so a reader in a LATER program could observe flags an earlier fast-path program skipped — and reader programs are real here (122 of 1166 scanned). An always-on guard at the reader-issue site latches the optimization off for the session and reports once if a reader ever issues while flags are stale; `s_macDirty` clears on the next real flag write (mac is overwritten wholesale) but `s_stickyDirty` only on FSSET, because a later commit *preserves* status bits 4..11.
