Verified over full runs: **0 scan MISS, `preWrite=0`** (no reader ever issues before its own program wrote flags), **`stsRd=0`** (no status reader executes at all — all 1.0–1.5M reads are MAC reads, so the sticky question is moot), guard never tripped (`off=0 dirtyReads=0`), 0 kick-drops/degen/reserved, MOVIE-END reached, correct level-era frames. `PS2X_VU1_LAZYVERIFY=1` provides those tallies; `PS2X_VU1_LAZYFLAGS=2` forces the fast path but is **useless as a ceiling instrument** — stale flags change what the guest does, so it measured 208.6, slower than the honest mode. ⚠ A VRAM byte-compare at a fixed draw index is NOT a valid equality test here: the same-config control also differs (the runner is not deterministic at a fixed draw index)**cont.178 — `PS2X_VU1_FASTDISPATCH` (default ON, `=0` reverts): per-pair dispatch overhead, 177.9 → 167.6 ns/pair = 1.061×.** A 100-sample profile of the lazy-flags build showed the EE thread's cost is byte-moving and dispatch rather than any single computation (`memset` 6% + `memcpy` 5% + `memmove` 2% + `getDecodedInstructionPairForPc` 4%). Two pure same-result-less-work fixes: (a) the decode cache is resolved ONCE per `run()` instead of per pair — the loop re-ran a five-field freshness check and returned the ~80-byte `DecodedInstructionPair` BY VALUE every iteration, and now holds a `const &` into `m_decodedCodeCache`; hoisting the check is safe because the guest cannot upload microcode while its own VU program is running (the same argument that lets lazy flags arm once per run), and it additionally makes the FIRST run after an upload eligible for lazy flags (armed runs 268,945 → 283,043). (b) `run()`'s six 16-byte vf/acc scratch arrays are no longer zero-initialised — 96 bytes of dead memset per pair; each is `memcpy`-filled before its only read under the identical guard (`hasUpperWrite`/`hasDistinctLowerWrite`/`accWrite != 0`), verified line by line. Matched-pairs A/B in ONE binary: 177.89 → 167.63 @300M, 179.08 → 169.22 @200M, 175.74 → 164.77 incremental; identical health counters, clean lazy tallies, fully correct level-era frame. Cumulative VU1 interpreter: **280.7 → 167.6 ns/pair = 1.67×**. Nothing modelled changed, so nothing to PCSX2-mirror**cont.179 — `PS2X_VU1_JITCENSUS` also emits a SCHEDULER census** (`[vu1:schedcensus]`: readyCycle calls per pair, stalled-pair fraction, stall cycles). Over 440M pairs: **1.003 readyCalls/pair, only 0.3% of pairs stall**, mean stall 2.42 cycles — VU1 has no VF interlocks, so the game's microcode is compiler-scheduled to avoid hazards. ⇒ the `NOSCHED` 33% is **bookkeeping** (markPairWrites + queueVfWrite/queueAccWrite + commitReadyPipelines), NOT stalls; the write pipeline remains load-bearing for write VISIBILITY (a read inside a write's latency window legitimately sees the old value); and the block JIT's answer is **compile-time register renaming** rather than a faster runtime pipeline. Also killed, before a line was written, a provably-sound hoist of the invariant register reduction out of the stall loop (the ready tables are written only by `markPairWrites`/`reset()`, never by `advanceTo`) — worth 0.003 calls/pair. Design: `docs/vu1-jit.md` §5. **cont.194 — the tier census gained a NON-cumulative tier 6, `EMITTED+br`** (the lower ops actually emitted today + branch terminators = tier 3 + branch). Tiers 0-5 are cumulative, so tier 5's 61.1% silently assumed ILW/ISW — and **ILW is the first lower op whose VI write is not latency-1** (`PipelineLsu, latency = 4`), so it cannot use the immediate-write equivalence every other emitted VI write relies on. Measured over 800M pairs: tier 6 = **59.8% compilable, mean run 2.71, 50.3% of pairs in runs >= 2** vs tier 5's 61.1%/2.82/51.9% => ILW/ISW is worth only 1.3 points and a latency-4 VI write pipeline is NOT worth building before block assembly. Classification-only change (no modelled hardware semantics), so nothing to PCSX2-mirror. Detail: `docs/vu1-jit.md` 5i. **cont.195** — the block driver in `run()`: entry guard (precise pending-write intersection test rather than globally-empty pipelines), ready-table replay, cycle accounting, the branch-delay tail backup, block shadow-verify and the `[vu1:block]` census (including the per-condition guard breakdown that identified pending VF writes as 99.7% of rejections). All behind `PS2X_VU1_BLOCK`, default OFF. Nothing modelled changed — the block reproduces the interpreter's own semantics — so nothing to PCSX2-mirror. Detail: `docs/vu1-jit.md` 5j. **cont.196** — a pending VF write no longer rejects a block (it was 99.7% of all rejections; now 0). Handled by the same stall argument that makes immediate writes safe: APPLY IT EARLY (a pair reading that slot before `readyCycle` stalls until it commits, so the old value is unobservable), RE-DERIVE THE SCHEDULE (early application only costs cycle fidelity; one monotonic forward pass shifts the static schedule using the per-pair read sets), and BUMP `m_vfLatestWrite` afterwards so a still-pending write cannot commit later and clobber the block's newer value. Only 1.4% of entries needed a schedule shift => almost all rejected intersections were WRITES. Guard rejections 14,355,964 -> 407,520; every remaining per-entry loop is driven off the cont.175 `PipeTrack` valid-slot bitmasks, and the mask computation is skipped for the ~88% of pairs with no compiled block. The verifier now also checks **CYCLE FIDELITY** (register comparison cannot catch a scheduling error). `PS2X_VU1_BLOCKSTRICT=1` restores the cont.195 guard for same-binary A/B. Measured ~2% (1.005-1.051x) at 12.8% coverage. ⚠ **Absolute ns/pair is NOT comparable across measurement sessions** — machine drift moved the same config from ~128 to ~145-152 over this arc and made two readings look like regressions when the matched baselines showed blocks ahead. Detail: `docs/vu1-jit.md` 5k. **cont.197** — `PS2X_VU1_BLOCKPROF=1` rdtsc-times each SEGMENT of the block fast path (timing, not ablation: ablating a block corrupts VU1 output, which feeds the geometry the guest then processes, so the workload would diverge). ★★ It settled the question: **the emitted code is NOT the bottleneck** — `fn` is ~16-19 cycles/PAIR (~5 ns/pair, better than cont.185's 7.72) while the driver around it costs ~208 cycles/entry (sched 78, retire 65, apply 27, lookup 38), i.e. **3.5:1 against the compiled code** over a mean block of 3.16 pairs. Fixed the largest piece: every fast-path arming condition is loop-INVARIANT (and so is the store's generation check — the guest cannot upload microcode while its own program runs), so they are hoisted out of the pair loop and `byPair` is re-encoded with **0 = known-uncompilable**, making the per-pair test one array load + compare against zero with no call: `lookupMiss` 248,134,039 -> **237,365**, segment 300 -> 38 cycles. ★ The pair index is BOUND-CHECKED, never masked — masking would ALIAS a different PC's block and silently execute the wrong code. ⚠ End-to-end barely moved (~1.03x either way) because **the 300-cycle figure was largely the profiler's own cost** — that segment ran on all 263M pairs and each miss paid two `rdtsc`; an rdtsc probe on a path executing hundreds of millions of times measures itself. Detail: `docs/vu1-jit.md` 5l **cont.212:** the block driver's `retire` segment -- cont.211's largest, and measured to be essentially all READY-TABLE replay (16.49 records/entry over a mean 4.04 pairs; cont.204's per-FMAC MAC-flag replay is 0.0% of entries and 0.6% of retire) -- now consumes ONE masked record per write-group instead of one per lane, with a straight-line four-store path for a full dest. Same binary, back to back: ready records/entry 16.74 -> 6.01, retire 173 -> 95, total 406 -> 345 cycles/entry, coverage unchanged; normalised to the untouched `fn` segment that is retire -49% / total -21%. **`PS2X_VU1_READYVERIFY=1`** (default OFF) is the differential that covers it -- `PS2X_VU1_BLOCKVERIFY` structurally CANNOT, because the whole retire path is inside `if (!s_vu1BlockVerify)` -- replaying the list both ways from one snapshot and comparing `m_vfReady`/`m_viReady`/`m_accReady`/`m_vfLatestWrite`/the write sequence, with the reference deriving lanes by explicit bit test where production walks the mask with ctz: 73,360,457 checks, 0 mismatches, and mutation-proven (reversing the quad supersede lane order is caught). `[vu1:blockprof2]` splits retire by population. **cont.213 (measurement):** `PS2X_VU1_BLOCKPROF` gains LEVELS -- `=2` brackets the WHOLE block entry with ONE rdtsc pair, which is how the probe's own cost was measured (level-1 whole 305 cycles/entry vs level-2 whole 269 => the four internal reads are ~36 cycles, ~12%). `=2` also prints probe-free counters (`[vu1:blockprof0]`): `blockNoPending` 94.5% and supersede 5.5% (both candidate levers killed outright), plus the block PAIR histogram that found **51% of all entries are 2-pair blocks**. `[vu1:blockstop]` histograms `Block::stopReason` per ENTRY with the 2-pair population broken out. **★★ cont.214 (VU0):** `perfTrack` was `s_vu1Perf && m_unit == Unit::VU1`, so VU0 had NEVER been measured -- new `[vu0:perf]` (same units, directly comparable) puts it at 90M pairs / 213.4 ns/pair against VU1's 61.7 = **41.9% of VU time**, from 2 programs at 17 pairs per VCALLMS. `s_lazyScan` is now PER-UNIT (it was a single shared slot, which is the only reason VU0 could never arm blocks; the scan itself always ran for both), with the matching `getVU0CodeGeneration()`. **`PS2X_VU0_BLOCK=1`** (default OFF, implies `PS2X_VU1_BLOCK`) lets VU0 use the block JIT via cont.204's flag-emitting mode -- valid because VU0's programs read MAC but not STATUS, and in this game the EE never reads VU0 flags at all (`ctx->vu0_status` / `vu0_mac_flags` / `vu0_clip_flags`: zero occurrences in every generated file). Verified at 12,663,965,677 shadow comparisons, 0 mismatches, 62.3% coverage -- but **default OFF because partial coverage does not pay** (neutral-to-worse at matched cumulative pairs; VU1 needed ~85%). **`PS2X_VU0_REJECTLOG=1`** dumps all 39 VU0 PCs that cannot form a block. **cont.215:** **`PS2X_VU_LASTBLK=1`** (default OFF -- it writes on every block entry) rings the last four blocks per unit and prints them at a reserved-instruction report, plus a microcode + generation dump. It is what showed that `PS2X_VU0_BLOCK=1` drives VU0 into 311,058 `[VU0 reserved upper]` events (0 with it off) while `BLOCKVERIFY` reports zero -- ★ because the whole real-execution path is inside `if (!s_vu1BlockVerify)`, so a shadow verifier proves COMPUTATION, not EXECUTION. VU0 blocks remain default OFF pending that fix; cont.214's 'partial coverage does not pay' A/B is RETRACTED (it compared a healthy run to a corrupted one).  **★★ cont.216 — THE VU0 DRIVER BUG (cont.215's diagnosis was WRONG).** The `[VU0 reserved upper] instruction=0xffffffff` storm under `PS2X_VU0_BLOCK=1` was never a decode/PC/stale-block problem: `0xffffffff` is a hand-picked SENTINEL that `updateFmacFlags` passes to `reportReservedInstruction` when `freeSlot(m_flagPipeline, ...)` returns -1. The block driver replays a whole block's clip + MAC flag entries in ONE pass with NO retirement between them, so the 8-slot array overflowed and the guest program was HALTED mid-flight. VU1 never hit it (normally lazy-armed, so `emitsFlags` is ~0% of its entries); VU0 is NEVER lazy-armed, so flag-emitting mode is its ONLY block mode and every block replays. Fix: `retireDueFlagEntries()` runs before each replayed entry, retiring exactly what the interpreter would have retired at that same cycle -- strictly MORE faithful than deferring the batch (which also retired in SLOT order where the interpreter retires in issue order). Plus `reportPipelineFull(which)`: all nine pipeline-full sites (`flag(mac|fsset|clip|fcset)`, `efu`, `store`, `vf`, `vi`, `acc`) now name themselves instead of masquerading as a garbage decode through a `0xFFFFFFF*` sentinel -- that disguise is what cost cont.215 a whole cycle. New `PS2X_VU_DECPROBE=1` (default OFF) prints the pair the loop actually decoded beside a fresh read of the same address. **Result: VU0 blocks 62.3% -> 85.7% coverage, meanPairs 6.43, 0 reserved / 0 pipeline-FULL events over 38M VU0 pairs, BLOCKVERIFY and READYVERIFY both clean, and a same-binary A/B at 19 matched cumulative-pair points is 1.89-2.55x on VU0 (e.g. 38M pairs: 123.4 vs 314.7 ns/pair).**  **★★ cont.217 — `PS2X_VU1_FLAGVERIFY=1` (default OFF): the differential oracle the flag replay never had.** `BLOCKVERIFY` cannot cover it — the whole driver path from early-apply through retire is inside `if (!s_vu1BlockVerify)` — and cont.212's `READYVERIFY` covers the ready-table replay, a different list; that blind spot is what hid the cont.216 overflow. From ONE snapshot it runs production and an INDEPENDENT restatement, then compares. Production walks clip and MAC events in two passes and picks slots by mask+ctz; the reference merges both streams into one ascending-cycle sequence (the order the INTERPRETER issues them in) and uses linear scans for retire and slot choice — so it tests the interpreter's ordering contract, not a copy of the driver. **★ It compares at a CHECKPOINT** (`entry + dynCycles`, the commit the next loop iteration performs), because production legitimately DEFERS the commits the interpreter would make mid-block and nothing reads mac/status/clip in between; comparing earlier reports that transient as a fault (measured: only 0.04% of entries defer). Live entries are compared as a multiset sorted by issue cycle, since a valid-slot mask that is a superset of reality makes ctz pick a different but equally free slot. **★★ MUTATION-PROVEN via `PS2X_VU1_FLAGMUTATE=<n>` (default 0, diagnostics only): 1 = never retire during the replay (the cont.216 bug), 2 = drop the last MAC event, 3 = reverse MAC order, 4 = issue one cycle late. The FIRST cut caught only 1 and 3 — 2 and 4 SURVIVED because the reference built its event list from the RECORDERS and so inherited production's own count and cycles** (the same gap cont.194 found in the JIT self-tests: calling the producer directly proves nothing about which field feeds which argument). Fixed to derive the count from `blk->clipCount`/`blk->flagCount` and every cycle from `entry + dynIssue[pair]`, taking only the VALUES from the recorders; **all four then die with distinct signatures** (`count=1`, `cycle=1`, `mac=1`). Clean run: **16,900,001 checks / 80,435,178 flag events / 0 mismatches / 0 overflow**, VU0 ns/pair unchanged (162.84 vs 163.33 at matched 2M pairs).  **★★★ cont.218 — THE BLOCK JIT IS NOW DEFAULT ON.** `PS2X_VU1_BLOCK` and `PS2X_VU0_BLOCK` flip from default OFF to default ON, `=0` being the kill switch for each (`return !(e && e[0] == '0')`, the same idiom as `PS2X_VU1_FASTSCAN`). ⚠ `PS2X_VU0_BLOCK` only has effect while `PS2X_VU1_BLOCK` is also on -- `blockArmed` tests `s_vu1Block` first, so the old comment claiming VU0_BLOCK \"implies\" VU1_BLOCK was wrong and is corrected (setting VU0_BLOCK=1 alone silently did nothing, which cost a run in cont.216). New `[vu:block] VU1=on VU0=on` startup banner so every run log records which mode produced it. **Justification, all measured:** VU1 93.7% of pairs compiled / 12.4x frame-matched; VU0 85.7% / 1.89-2.55x at 19 matched cumulative-pair points on 36.9-55.3% of VU time; and every layer has an oracle shown capable of FAILING -- BLOCKVERIFY (computation, 12.7G comparisons), READYVERIFY (ready-table replay, mutation-proven) and FLAGVERIFY (flag replay, mutation-proven against four injected defects), all 0 mismatches. Verified after the flip: no env vars -> VU1=on VU0=on, 85.8% VU0 coverage, 167.22 ns/pair; `PS2X_VU0_BLOCK=0` -> 330.40 ns/pair (1.98x, same VU1 config); `PS2X_VU1_BLOCK=0` -> both off, 0 blocks entered. Shipped-default run: 12,000,001 FLAGVERIFY checks / 50,977,740 events / 0 mismatches, 0 READYVERIFY mismatches, 0 reserved/pipeline-FULL, correct full-HUD level-era gameplay.  **cont.219 (two instruments, both default OFF; no optimization shipped):** `PS2X_VU1_LINKCENSUS=1` (execution-weighted block-graph census: chained fraction, 1- vs 2-slot successor prediction, exits to the interpreter, and the terminator branch-kind histogram) and a **`dispatch` sub-segment** in `blockprof` that splits the `lookup+guard` segment into the array lookup and the pending-write guard. Together they **measured BLOCK LINKING OUT**: the reclaimable half is only ~17 true cycles (the reported 25.2-26.0 contains one ~9-cycle rdtsc read) of a ~258-cycle entry, on the 78.2% of entries that are chained = **5.2% of driver cost, ~0.5% of wall** -- because a linked successor still needs its OWN guard (blocks apply writes immediately and only record ready-table entries, so the incoming pending set is unchanged) and the dispatch is already ONE L1 load from an 8 KB int32 array. The census also killed **trace extension across unconditional branches** (exact, no speculation, removes a whole entry): **B+BAL are just 1.0% of terminators**, against IBNE 50.0% / IBEQ 10.0%. ★ Kept because they are reusable: a **2-slot successor cache predicts 100%** of chained entries, so any future superblock design has a free, perfect successor oracle. ★★ What they DID find: **`retire` is the dominant segment -- 115 cycles/entry VU1-only (38%), 145 blended with VU0 (43%), 7-9x the entire linking opportunity.**  **cont.220: `PS2X_VU1_FASTFLAGRETIRE` (default ON, `=0` reverts)** -- track the earliest `readyCycle` among live flag entries and skip `retireDueFlagEntries`' walk when nothing can be due (the `pipe.nextReady` short-circuit `commitReadyPipelines` already uses). Correct by construction: it only skips a walk that would retire nothing; `flagMinReady` resets at every block entry because the pipeline is touched between blocks, and a value too LARGE would skip a due retire = the cont.216 overflow. Verified FLAGVERIFY 0 mismatches ON **and** OFF, 0 pipeline-FULL, `FLAGMUTATE=1` still dies. ⚠ **Measured at the noise floor, NOT a win, and it refutes cont.219's lead:** new probe-free counters (`flagRetire calls=/entry skipped=% retired=/entry`, BLOCKPROF-gated) show `retireDueFlagEntries` runs **0.58x per ENTRY, not the ~6x cont.219 assumed** -- VU1 is normally lazy-armed so `emitsFlags` is ~0% of its entries and the MAC replay loop never runs; only VU0 blocks replay every entry and VU0 is 2.4% of entries. Work removed ~0.15 walks/entry. ⇒ **`retire` (163-184 cy/entry) is the READY-TABLE replay, not the flag replay** -- ~6 records/entry into m_vfReady/m_viReady/m_accReady plus m_vfLatestWrite bumps, which cont.212 already halved (16.49 -> 6.01). Kept because it is 10 lines, strictly less work, and helps the VU0 path.  **★★★ cont.221: `PS2X_VU1_FASTKICK` (default ON, `=0` restores the byte loop) -- XGKICK's per-qword copy was 16 hardware DIVIDES per 16 bytes.** It ran a byte at a time with `% m_activeVuDataSize` on EVERY byte; the compiler cannot prove that is a power of two, so each 16-byte qword cost 16 `div`s (~20-40 cycles each). VU data memory IS a power of two (VU1 16 KB, VU0 4 KB -- the block entry guard already relies on it), so the qword is one masked `memcpy`, split in two only on wrap. **Bit-identical by construction**: for `base < size`, `i < 16`, `(base + i) % size` is `base + i` or `base + i - size`, exactly the two-part copy. **PCSX2 parity:** `Gif_Path::CopyGSPacketData` / `_vuXGKICK` (`Gif_Unit.h`, `VUops.cpp`) also mask into VU memory and bulk-copy -- behaviour unchanged, only cost. **Sized first** (`[vu1:xgkick]`): 25.7M qwords = 411 MB streamed over 220M pairs, **`wraps=0`** so every modulo was waste. **Measured 1.08-1.16x on VU1 time** (same binary, matched cumulative pairs, 8 level-era points all one direction; ON reached 220M pairs where OFF reached 200M in the same 340 s). **`PS2X_VU1_KICKVERIFY=1` (default OFF) covers the path the game never takes:** the workload has `wraps=0`, so the wrap split never runs in game -- (a) a one-shot EXHAUSTIVE self-test over every base offset hits all 15 wrap alignments (4,096 cases, 0 mismatches, BIT-IDENTICAL) and (b) a live per-qword differential vs the original byte loop (135,531,383 qwords, 0 mismatches). ★ The self-test is MUTATION-PROVEN via a standalone host harness (~2 s, no game build): wrong mask, swapped wrap split, flipped byte -- all caught.  **★★★ cont.222: `PS2X_VU1_FASTKICKRESET` (default ON, `=0` restores `m_xgkick = {}`) -- `resetScheduler()` was doing a 64 KB memset on every VCALLMS.** `XgkickPipeline` carries a `std::array<uint8_t, 0x10000> packet`, so the innocuous-looking `m_xgkick = {}` value-initialised 64 KB; for VU0 that is 5.3M times per run = ~347 GB of memset, caught by the cont.222 profile as **8.3% of EE-thread SELF time**, all ten samples on the stack `executeVU0Microprogram -> execute -> resetScheduler -> memset`. ★ cont.158d did exactly this surgery to the rest of the function; this one survived because the size is hidden behind a member's type. Now clears only the eight control fields -- safe because packet bytes are WRITTEN BEFORE READ (the qword lands at `packet[copiedBytes]` before the GIFtag is parsed from that same offset, and `finishXgkick` submits only `totalBytes = currentTagEnd <= copiedBytes`). **`PS2X_VU1_KICKVERIFY=1` now checks that exact invariant at every submit: 581,306 submits, 0 unwritten.** Measured twice: the profile shows `__memset_avx2_erms` GONE (10/120 samples -> absent from the top 20) with EE blocked time 28.3% -> 16.7% and EE busy 75% -> 87%; the same-binary A/B at matched cumulative VU0 pairs is 1.12x / 1.05x / 1.02x / 1.03x, all four favouring ON. **★★★ cont.230 -- THE BLOCK-ENTRY GUARD'S PROMISE WAS NEVER KEPT IN PRODUCTION.** cont.201's guard admits a block whose first CLIP-flag / Q reader issues AFTER a pending CLIP write / pending FDIV would have committed (`blockApplyClip` / `blockApplyQ`), but only the BLOCKVERIFY branch ever applied those writes (to `blockVerifyRef`); production ran the block against the STALE `m_state.clip` / `m_state.q`. Invisible to `PS2X_VU1_BLOCKVERIFY` by construction (its copy got the writes) and absent under `PS2X_VU1_BLOCK=0` (the interpreter commits them on time). On screen: the level-era dark wedges/bands -- one character's near-plane clipper output (triangle fans, q = 1/near) computed with stale clip flags, covering the whole frame at near Z and Z-rejecting everything drawn after it -- plus a JIT-only spread of z/q across ordinary vertices (stale Q in the perspective divide). Fix: apply the latest-ISSUED pending clip entry (queueClip chains the full 24-bit register; slot order is not issue order) and the pending Q to `m_state` before `blk->fn`, and use the same latest-issued rule in the verify copy. Reference: PCSX2 `VUops.cpp` `_vuCLIP` (working `VU->clipflag <<= 6 | bits` at issue) + `_vuFMACflush` (`VI[REG_CLIP_FLAG] = fmac[i].clipflag` at the 4-cycle flush) -- the same working-copy-then-publish model our interpreter implements; the fix only makes blocks see the published value when hardware would. Also **`PS2X_VU1_KICKSCAN=1`** (default OFF; `PS2X_VU1_KICKSCAN_DIR`, `PS2X_VU1_KICKSCAN_MINCYCLE` = 450000000 level-era gate): at `finishXgkick` parses the kicked GIF packet (PACKED with Q on the ST qword, REGLIST, A+D; IMAGE skipped) and reports any vertex with q > 0.1 more than 768 px outside the GS window with the running program's startPC/top/itop; the first hit dumps `kickscan_{code,data,packet}.bin`. **cont.230 audit:** the guard-admitted early applies (pending VF writes, the latest-issued pending CLIP entry, the pending Q) now live in ONE lambda `applyGuardAdmitted(dst)` run by both the production block (`m_state`) and the shadow-verify copy (`blockVerifyRef`) -- the two preambles can no longer drift. Blocks can read only VF/VI/ACC/memory/Q/clip (no MAC/status readers, MFP, R ops, DIV or XGKICK are compiled), and the guard rejects pending VI/ACC/store, so those three applies are the complete set. Census in the `[vu1:block]` line (`PS2X_VU1_PERF=1`): `early-apply(vf= clip= q=)` -- one level-era run: 47.2M entries, clip 6.75M, q 1.70M, vf 1.41M. **cont.230 (2026-09-02):** `PS2X_VU1_KICKEAGER` (default OFF -- measured neutral; see the commit) and `[vu1:blockstop5]`: the reason-5 ("branchrej") stop histogrammed by the lower word's opcode (`Block::stopLower`), which showed 83% of those stops are DIV (Q pipeline) and 6% ERLENG, not branches. **cont.230 (2026-09-02, builds 379-389):** (a) ★★★ **XGKICK double parse FIXED** (`288a749`): since the per-path GIF-resume line was added unbraced under `else if (m_activeGs)`, the direct `processGIFPacket` ran after the arbiter submit and every kicked packet was drawn TWICE (found by the microVU oracle; prims/frame 146k → 71k, the old path 2.9 → 4.1-4.6 fps). (b) **`PS2X_VU1_MICROVU`** (default ON from 389; `=0` = this interpreter + block JIT) routes `execute()`/`resume()` to the PCSX2 microVU port (`src/lib/vu/microvu/`, seam `ps2x_microvu.cpp`). (c) **`PS2X_VU1_PROGVERIFY`**: the differential oracle (re-run every program on this interpreter from the same input on a copy of VU memory with kicks recorded; compare registers, flags (sticky apart), Q/P/I/R, pc, memory, kick list); `[interp:kick]` log under `PS2X_MICROVU_KICKLOG`. |
