| `24-vu1-jit.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/vu/ps2_vu1_jit.h` | **cont.182-183 (new file): the VU1 JIT foundation** — an RWX code buffer, a minimal x86-64/SSE4.1 emitter, an instruction compiler and a cache; included only by `ps2_vu1_core.cpp`/`ps2_vu1_upper.cpp` so there is no new translation unit and no CMakeLists change. Constrained to **xmm0-7 and rdi/rsi/rdx/rcx so no REX prefix is ever emitted**, `pand`/`pandn`/`por` for select instead of `blendvps` (which pins the mask to xmm0), fully branchless. ⚠ The first version segfaulted because **RIP-relative displacements are signed 32-bit while `mmap` places the buffer far more than ±2GB from the data segment** — the constant pool now lives INSIDE the mapping; `PS2X_VU1_JITDUMP=1` dumps emitted bytes. **`PS2X_VU1_JIT` (default OFF)** replaces `execUpper`'s body for the covered FMAC ops (ADDbc/SUBbc/MADDbc/MSUBbc `0x00-0x0F`, MULbc `0x18-0x1B`, ADD/MADD/MUL/SUB/MSUB `0x28/29/2A/2C/2D`, plus every `A` form writing acc), reproducing `execUpper` + `applyFmacDest(Acc)` exactly so the pipeline/shadow-dance/cycle accounting are untouched; armed only while lazy flags is active, since it derives no MAC/status flags. **Verified at three levels, all zero-mismatch: emitted operand clamp 10,240 cases; emitted full FMAC pair 38,400 cases (5 ops x 5 broadcasts x all 16 dest masks x 24 random states); and `PS2X_VU1_JITVERIFY=1` live shadow-verify against the interpreter during real play — 138,889,257 executions, 0 mismatches, 40.8% of all pairs.** ★ Measured verdict: that 40.8% coverage bought only **1.032x**, because a pair costs ~420 host cycles and `execUpper` is only ~15-20% of it — the rest is the interpreter LOOP. A JIT pays only when a compiled BLOCK subsumes the loop (one call per ~6-pair block, operands in registers, pipeline replaced by compile-time register renaming). Default OFF: +3% does not justify running hand-encoded machine code against guest state by default. Design for the next stage: `docs/vu1-jit.md` §5b. **cont.194 — the lower slot is FINISHED: IADDIU/ISUBIU (4.6%) + the four clip readers FCEQ/FCAND/FCOR/FCGET (10.3%)**, on top of SQ 15.1% + LQ 5.3% + NOP 46.4%. New GPR encodings (`mov [mem],r32`, `sub/and/or/cmp eax,imm32`, `movsx r32,r16`, `setcc r8`, `movzx r32,r8`) — still no REX. **An immediate VI write is equivalent to the queued one for all six**: `decodeLowerUsage` gives them `PipelineIalu, latency = 1`, and `advanceOneCycle()` at the BOTTOM of each pair iteration (`ps2_vu1_core.cpp:3149`) commits before pair i+1 begins, with nothing in between reading VI. **But the branch-read backup is NOT equivalent and is now a reported per-instruction fact** — new `LowerPlan::delaysNextBranchRead`, **true for IADDIU/ISUBIU, false for the clip readers**; the emitters deliberately do not write the backup (it lives in the interpreter object, not `VU1State`), so honouring it is the block compiler's contract. New `classifyLowerPlan`/`emitLowerPlan` are the lower-slot twin of `classifyUpperPlan`/`compileUpper` and the contract block assembly consumes. **Verified: 70,161 cases 0 mismatches** through the real classify->emit path against a reference copied from `ps2_vu1_lower.cpp`; **objdump cross-check** of the hand-rolled encodings; live in-game on the guest thread with all five self-tests PASS and JIT shadow-verify at **540,981,434 executions, 0 mismatches**. **MUTATION-TESTED (10 injected defects): 9 died at once, and the survivor — SQ's base swapped `VIT`->`VIS` — exposed a real gap**, that `selfTestLQ` calls the emitters DIRECTLY and so never tested which instruction FIELD feeds which argument; a decode-contract block now asserts every plan field against `ps2_vu1_detail.h`'s accessors and all ten die. Still NOT wired into execution (no lower-slot path until block assembly). Detail: `docs/vu1-jit.md` 5i. **cont.195 — BLOCK ASSEMBLY** (`PS2X_VU1_BLOCK`, default OFF; `PS2X_VU1_BLOCKVERIFY=1` shadow-verifies): `PairInfo`/`Block`/`planBlock`/`emitPairInto`/`findBlock`/`buildBlock` emit a run of consecutive pairs as ONE native function. **Writes are applied IMMEDIATELY with no pipeline** — sound because a reader always stalls until its operand is ready (`calculatePairReadyCycle` maxes over `m_vfReady` for every read lane and `run()` advances a cycle at a time committing), so a stale read cannot happen; the pipeline is reproduced only as TIMING (cycle count + ready tables) by the C++ driver. Intra-pair ordering avoids the `upperVfShadowReg` dance by exploiting opcode orthogonality (SQ/IADDIU/clip before the upper, LQ after, LQ skipped when suppressed). Stalls are ABSORBED statically (each pair carries its issue cycle; the block consumes `lastIssue+1` cycles) rather than cutting the block. The cont.194 branch-delay contract is honoured by capturing the tail VI value before the call and replaying `recordViWriteForBranch` after. Blocks get their OWN code buffer (`blockBuffer()`), reset on every microcode-generation invalidate — sharing the instruction cache's buffer exhausted its 8 MB after ~7,400 blocks and silently rejected everything after. **Verified: 24,654,237,535 shadow comparisons, 0 mismatches.** ⚠ Measured **6.4% coverage, ~1% ns/pair — inside the baseline's own 124.8-128.2 swing, i.e. not yet a win**, exactly as "partial coverage cannot pay" predicts. Guard census names the one lever: **99.7% of the 14.36M rejections are a pending VF write intersecting the block's touched slots**. Detail: `docs/vu1-jit.md` 5j. **cont.196** — `PairSched` per-pair reads + static issue cycle, `Block::vfWritten`, and `ReadyWrite` keyed by PAIR INDEX so the driver can RE-DERIVE the schedule at entry. Coverage 6.4% -> **12.8%**, **34.3 G shadow comparisons, 0 mismatches**. Detail: `docs/vu1-jit.md` 5k. **cont.198-205 — THE BLOCK JIT LANDS (91.2% coverage).** Branches execute INSIDE blocks (branch pair + delay slot, block writes m_state.pc; targets are compile-time constants except JR/JALR and the selected target rides in **ECX, which is RESERVED** for that across the delay slot). Lower-special ops (IADD/ISUB/IADDI/IAND/IOR, MOVE/MR32, MTIR/MFIR), the memory ops (ILW/ISW/ILWR/ISWR, LQI/SQI/LQD/SQD), pending clip/Q applied early when provably due before the block's first reader (guard blocks 41.3M -> 826K), **flag-emitting mode** (blocks now run in programs with a MAC reader: each FMAC stashes its pre-clamp result and the driver replays updateFmacFlags at that FMAC's own issue cycle -- bit-exact by construction; requires no STATUS reader since the product sticky needs operands that are gone by replay time), and CLIP (stashes its two operand quads, driver replays queueClip). **All shadow-verified with 0 mismatches** across values, cycle fidelity, PC, mac/status/clip/q and the queued flag/clip entries. **Frame-matched: 12.4x (18.2 fps vs 1.46).** ★★★ **VU1 IS NO LONGER THE WALL** — it is now 11.6% of wall time (34.59 s of 297.3 s) vs ~97% of the EE thread before; raster ablation on matched guest-frame intervals is 3.50x in heavy scenes, so the GS CPU rasterizer is the bottleneck and the GPU arc should be UNPAUSED. Detail: `docs/vu1-jit.md` 5m **cont.212:** `ReadyWrite` carries a LANE MASK (bit b = lane 3-b) instead of a single lane, so `recordPairWrites` emits one record per vf/acc write-group rather than one per lane -- the ready-table replay was 16.49 records per entry and is now 6.01. `PS2X_VU1_QUADREADY=0` emits the old per-lane records (A/B baseline + kill switch). **cont.213:** `Block::stopReason` records why `planBlock` stopped growing a block (11 exit sites), so short blocks can be diagnosed rather than guessed at: ALL entries branch=69.8% branchrej=18.9% cap8=9.0%, and of the 2-pair half, 67.7% are a branch plus its delay slot. ⚠ `kMaxBlockPairs` stays **8**: raising it to 16 was tried and left 2-pair entries at 53.3% (from 51.0%) while per-entry cost got slightly WORSE (216 -> 222 cycles, the cont.197 cold-cache-line effect), proving block length is bounded by the microcode's branch structure, not by our window. **cont.214:** `blockStore()` / `blockBuffer()` / `blockBufferReset()` are **per-unit** (index 1 = VU1, so existing call sites are unchanged) and `BlockStore` carries its own `unit` so `invalidate()` resets the right code buffer -- VU0 and VU1 interleave constantly and the store is PC-keyed, so one shared store would invalidate itself on every switch. Plus the VU0 rejection log (`PS2X_VU0_REJECTLOG`), which found that **19 of the 39 VU0 rejections are `iBit` pairs**: `planBlock` rejects those in the same test as the E/D/T halt bits, but `iBit` only means the lower word is a float immediate for the I register, not a halt condition. **★ cont.215 (`iBit`):** pairs with the upper's bit 31 set now compile. The lower word there is a 32-bit float IMMEDIATE for the I register, not an instruction, and `planBlock` was rejecting it in the same test as the E/D/T HALT bits. `LowerPlan::setsI` carries the value folded through `vuNormOperandBits` at PLAN time (a constant, so no run-time clamp, and it reuses the interpreter's exact function instead of restating it); the emitter stores it with one `mov [rdi+kOffI], imm32` **after the upper**, since an upper reading I in the same pair must still see the OLD value. Delay slots included. Verified 12,687,487,535 comparisons / 0 mismatches; VU1 coverage 92.1% -> 93.7%, VU0 62.3% -> 89.9%. ⚠ The first cut emitted it in the pre-upper group, where `lo.op == Nop` meant it was never emitted at all -- caught immediately by shadow-verify at 1,720,627 mismatches. **cont.230:** `BlockStore` gains the early-apply census counters `earlyVf/earlyClip/earlyQ` (see patch 16). **cont.230:** `Block::stopLower` records the lower word at a reason-5 stop (planBlock) for the `[vu1:blockstop5]` histogram. |
| `20-dbcman-hle-header.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_dbcman_hle.h` | DBCMAN.IRX HLE declarations (new file) |
| `21-dbcman-hle.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_dbcman_hle.cpp` | DBCMAN.IRX HLE implementation (new file) |
| `22-gs-gpu-device-header.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h` | `GsGpuPresentDevice` declaration (new file; cont.166 GPU-backend arc phase 0). **cont.167:** `GsGpuUploadChunk` + `MirrorUpload`/`MirrorVerify` (phase-1 VRAM mirror API). **cont.168 (phase 1b):** `MirrorPatchRaw` (raw authoritative-byte range copy into the mirror) + `MirrorVerifyFull(vram, size, drawPageMask)` (whole-4MB shadow verify, draw-explained pages subtracted). **cont.169 (phase 2a):** `GsGpuPrim` — one primitive fully resolved for the rasterizer as a flat block of 4-byte scalars (unambiguous std430), with the host pre-computing everything that would otherwise need a GPU division or transcendental (winding, 1/|denom|, the resolved mip level's tbp/tbw/size/region bounds, the FST divisor) and carrying vertex Z as `packDouble2x32` halves — plus `RasterPrim`, `MirrorVerifyRange` and `RasterStats` **cont.233:** `HardwareRasterRequested()`; the batch comment records that bins stay empty for the hardware path. |
| `23-gs-gpu-device.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | GPU presentation-decode device (new file): EGL surfaceless GL 4.3 core context on a **dedicated device thread** (the present thread holds raylib's GLX context; one current GL context per thread regardless of binding API — eglMakeCurrent there = EGL_BAD_ACCESS 0x3002, run171) + a compute kernel replicating `CopyFrameToHostRgbaCpu` bit-exactly (page tables built with the same `PixelStorageTraits<>` templates + hardware block/column constants as `ps2_gs_memory.cpp`; PCSX2 `GSLocalMemory.cpp` holds the equivalent swizzle tables; CT32/CT24/CT16/CT16S). VRAM uploads cached per capture-seq sourceId; every GL entry point loaded via `eglGetProcAddress` (links only EGL). Validated: run173 shadow-verify — see patch 10 row. **cont.167 (phase 1, VRAM residency):** the single-slot job became a **bounded FIFO** (decode jobs synchronous, mirror jobs fire-and-forget; 64MB/8192-job producer backpressure) + a persistent 4MB **mirror SSBO** with a CT32 upload swizzle kernel (replicates `UploadImageUnlocked`'s cursor + `WriteCT32`'s page walk; per-pixel word ownership, no atomics) and an on-GPU verify kernel (atomic mismatch tally + first-divergence capture, 16-byte readback). run174: 438k sampled chunks / 113M words bit-exact. **cont.168 (phase 1b, mirror completeness):** two more job kinds on the same FIFO — **PatchRaw** (`glBufferSubData` of authoritative CPU VRAM bytes straight into the mirror: no kernel, so it cannot diverge, and it is the universal route for write patterns with no kernel) and **VerifyFull** (SYNCHRONOUS, so it runs behind every queued mirror job): a 1M-word compare kernel over the whole mirror vs a 4MB upload of CPU VRAM, with an atomic mismatch tally, first-divergence capture and a **per-8KB-page histogram**; the report splits stale pages into draw-explained (via the caller's draw-page mask) vs **UNEXPLAINED** ranges — the latter is the actual completeness gate. run180a: 77/77 verifies, UNEXPLAINED 0 pages / 0 words, 0 GL errors. **cont.169 (phase 2a, THE PIXEL PIPELINE):** `kRasterShader` — a compute rasterizer replicating the CPU oracle line by line (DrawTriangle's barycentric setup/coverage/interpolation; SampleTexture's wrap modes, bilinear, TEXA and CLUT incl. the CSM1 bit-3/4 swap and the cont.165 CLUT-shadow substitution; combineTexture's four TFX modes; classifyAlphaTest + AFAIL; the Z test; the ALPHA blend equation; WritePixel's FBMSK/FBA/preserve-alpha write-out). Three more page tables uploaded — **Z32 (its own block order), P8 and P4 (their own page geometry AND column order)** — so texture/Z addressing shares the CPU's `PixelStorageTraits<>` math. One primitive per dispatch, one invocation per bbox pixel: race-free by construction (a pixel owns its frame word and its Z word). **★ `precise` is load-bearing:** GLSL may contract `a*b+c` into an FMA and the oracle is built `-msse4.1` (no FMA at all); a sub-ULP difference lands on the other side of `floor(x+0.5)`, which is exactly the 48-range / 634-word ±1-LSB divergence run181b measured — qualifying the weights, interpolations, texture coords and bilinear lerp took it to 3. The per-pixel `1/q` is done in fp64 and rounded once (double rounding is provably innocuous for division: 53 >= 2*24+2). New job kinds **RasterPrim** and **VerifyRange** (the full-compare kernel gained a `uBase` offset so it serves both). **run183_deep: 104,228 primitives, 199,673 range compares, 0 divergent words, 0 GL errors**. **cont.171 (phase 2b step 2, THE TILE RASTERIZER):** the shader is now ONE shared pixel pipeline (`kRasterCommon` + `shadePrimPixel`) with TWO entry points built from it — `kRasterMainPrim` (one primitive over its bbox) and `kRasterMainTile` (a batch: one workgroup per non-empty 16x16 tile, one thread per pixel, each thread walking its tile's primitive list in SUBMISSION ORDER). **No barriers**: pixels are independent of one another and only the per-pixel primitive order matters. A duplicated pipeline would drift, so there is exactly one. `GsGpuPrim` split into a dedup'd `GsGpuState` table + per-primitive `GsGpuGeom`, which makes the single-primitive path literally a batch of one. `MirrorVerifyFull` now returns its result (it fell off the end without returning when first changed, aborting the run with `std::system_error` — caught and fixed). **cont.170 (phase 2b step 1):** the SPRITE path — DrawSprite is not a degenerate triangle, so the shader spells out its differences: flat colour/Z/fog from v1 (IIP ignored), Z a plain truncation of v1.z with no +0.5, an axis-aligned rect, and a screen-space UV lerp including the FST fixed-point ROUND TRIP (`int(texUf*16+0.5)`, then SampleTexture divides by 16 again) and the non-FST divide-then-multiply by the texture size — neither is identity in float, so both are replicated. `writePixel()` extracted so both primitive paths share one write-out; `exactDiv()` (fp64, rounded once) wherever the oracle divides. The mismatch report now dumps the whole prim, which is what identified both cont.170 walls immediately **cont.233 hardware-raster spike (`PS2X_GS_HWRASTER=1`, default OFF; the CPU rasterizer and the tile kernel are untouched):** a vertex+fragment program on a no-attachment 2048x2048 framebuffer draws one hardware triangle per primitive (`glDrawArrays`, the vertex stage reads the same GsGeom SSBO by `gl_VertexID/3`; a sprite is one enclosing right triangle), `GL_INTEL_conservative_rasterization` (`PS2X_GS_HWRASTER_CONS`, default 1) plus an optional centroid push (`PS2X_GS_HWRASTER_DILATE`, default 1 px; 0 was exact on the level capture) guarantees a fragment for every pixel the CPU's epsilon test covers, and the fragment stage re-runs the CPU's exact coverage test and discards BEFORE the interlock. The shared pipeline is split into `shadePrimColor` (coverage, interpolation, texture, combine, fog, Z -- needs no ordering: the accumulator flushes read-after-write hazards) and `writePixel` (the frame/Z RMW), and only the latter sits inside `beginInvocationInterlockARB()`/`end...` with `layout(pixel_interlock_ordered)` (`GL_ARB_fragment_shader_interlock`; the mirror is `coherent` in this program only via `MIRROR_QUAL`). Bit-exact on the level capture (`aa9cd72b600021ce`, every rep, builds 426-431). Measured (GPU ms per level frame, 5 reps, alternating with controls): **170 ms faithful vs the tile kernel's 210 and the CPU's 127 at 3 threads** -- so 1.2x the old GPU design, 0.75x the CPU, and 4x short of the 40 ms go/no-go. Decomposition: the ordered interlock cost 700+ ms with the whole pipeline inside it and ~40 ms with only the RMW (the section length is the cost; a discarded fragment is free); unlocked the same shader is 101-130 ms (33,937 instructions, SIMD8, 619 sends -- every geometry/state field is an SSBO gather, the texture sample is ~43 ms, the raster+coverage floor ~35 ms). Ablations: `PS2X_GS_HWRASTER_LOCK` (2 ordered / 1 unordered / 0 none), `PS2X_GS_HWRASTER_COHERENT`, `PS2X_GS_HWRASTER_ABL` bits (1 coverage-only + count, 2 float Z, 4 untextured, 8 no frame/Z reads, 16 count covered: 2.0M covered fragments per frame, `[gsgpu:hwcount]`). The device bins a batch itself if the hardware path is unavailable (`binBatch`). PCSX2 parity: its OpenGL renderer also reaches for `pixel_interlock_ordered` only as the accurate-blending fallback (`GSDeviceOGL.cpp` / `tfx.glsl`) -- the hardware interlock is the known-slow path there too. |
| `25-vu1-header.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_vu1.h` | **cont.216: `kMaxFlagEntries` 8 -> 16.** 8 was sized for the INTERPRETER, which calls `commitReadyPipelines()` at the top of EVERY pair and so never holds more than ~`kFmacLatency` flag entries. The block driver replays a whole block's clip + MAC entries in ONE pass, so its live set is bounded by the BLOCK LENGTH instead -- the array overflowed, `freeSlot()` returned -1, and the queue helpers HALTED the guest program. PCSX2 parity: its MAC flag history is a rolling 4-entry ring indexed by cycle (`VU->macflag[VU->cycle & 3]`, `VUops.cpp`), so overflow is not an error condition there either and a fixed array that can fail is our own invention. 16 covers `kMaxBlockPairs`(8) of block entries plus a full latency window of incoming ones; the valid-slot masks are `uint32_t`, so up to 32 costs nothing. Also declares `reportPipelineFull()` (see patch 16) |
| `26-gs-memory-header.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/ps2_gs_memory.h` | **cont.225 (new file patch): the split + the exposed hot page tables.** `PixelStorageTraits<>::Read`/`Write` now split at exactly the address boundary into `Address` + new `ReadAt`/`WriteAt`, so a read-modify-write can compute `Address()` once and the two forms cannot drift. `PageTableC32` / `PageTableZ32` are declared **extern** so the rasterizer can call the templates DIRECTLY and have them inline -- which is the whole point: cont.224 proved the ~24% swizzle block is indirect-call plus 128 KB-table cache cost, NOT address arithmetic, and routing the split through extra function pointers ran 1-4% SLOWER. **cont.228:** `PageTableP4` is now **extern** too, for the same reason -- 83.0% of texel fetches are PSMT4 and each reached VRAM through the `m_draw.texRead` pointer into `ReadP4`; exposing the table lets `SampleTexture` call the traits directly so the lookup inlines. |
| `27-gs-memory.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/ps2_gs_memory.cpp` | **cont.225 (new file patch):** drop `static` from `PageTableC32` / `PageTableZ32` so they match the extern declarations in patch 26. Nothing else changes -- every existing `ReadCT32`/`WriteZ24`/... still routes through the same traits. **cont.228:** drop `static` from `PageTableP4` as well, matching the new extern declaration. |
| `28-runtime-macros.patch` | `ps2xRuntime/include/ps2_runtime_macros.h` | **★★★ cont.230: `FPU_CVT_W_S` TRUNCATES (with saturation), as the EE does.** Upstream had `(int32_t)nearbyintf(a)` -- round-to-nearest -- for CVT.W.S, the instruction the SDK compiler emits for a C `(int)` cast. PCSX2 `FPU.cpp` `CVT_W`: `(s32)_FsValf_` when `(bits & 0x7F800000) <= 0x4E800000`, else `0x7FFFFFFF` / `0x80000000` by sign; `iFPU.cpp` `recCVT_W`: `cvttss2si`. `ps2FpuCvtWS()` is that exact form. Found via LOTR's collision grid: `0x212920` floors a cell coordinate as `cvt.w.s` (minus one only for negatives), so every fraction above a half selected the next cell and the sweep query `0x213100` tested the wrong cells -- Gandalf walked into rubble and fell through walkways while the terrain (a different path) held. 199 generated units use the macro; expect the whole recomp to recompile when it changes. **cont.230 EE FPU SEMANTICS (`PS2X_FPU_EE`, default 1; `=0` = the plain-IEEE macros for A/B):** the R5900 FPU has no NaN/Inf/denormals -- operands READ as +-Fmax (all-ones exponent) / +-0 (zero exponent), results clamp on overflow and flush on underflow, DIV/RSQRT by a zero-or-denormal divisor return +-Fmax (sign = xor) with the D (or I for 0/0) + sticky flags, SQRT(negative) = sqrt(|x|), compares are ordered on the clamped operands, MIN/MAX order by the signed bit pattern. Mirrored from PCSX2 `FPU.cpp` (fpuDouble, checkOverflow/Underflow, checkDivideByZero, fp_min/fp_max, ADD_S..RSQRT_S, C_cond_S). Helpers `ps2FpuIn/Out/Div/Rsqrt/Sqrt/Max/Min`; 22-case value test in the LLMPS2Recomp session notes. Census of the LOTR recomp: ADD/SUB/MUL 5.5k sites, compares 3.1k, the old `copysignf(INFINITY` divide guard 736, SQRT 251. |
| `29-thread-naming` (new, cont.230) | `ps2xRuntime/include/ThreadNaming.h` | **CPU pinning** (2026-09-02, build 377/378). `PinCurrentThreadForRole(EE|GS)`: an explicit `PS2X_EE_CPUS` / `PS2X_GS_CPUS` list ("0", "1,2,3", "1-3,5-7") always wins; else, unless `PS2X_CPU_PIN=0`, the **automatic plan** from sysfs topology (`AutoCpuPlan()`): the EE thread on one logical CPU of the lowest physical core (its SMT sibling left to the OS), the raster pool on every logical CPU of the other cores; needs >= 3 physical cores. WHY: the EE thread (VU1 inside it) is the long pole and the OS put a ~45%-busy raster thread on its SMT sibling. Measured with ALTERNATING on/off pairs (build 378, e35-e38): VU1 50-54 ns/pair pinned vs 55-56 unpinned (~7%), 2-5% less wall for the same guest frames. (The first, non-alternating comparison read +38% -- host clock drift, see progress.md §15.7; the laptop runs this at a ~2 GHz median under powersave/balance_power.) Not an emulation-semantics change (no PCSX2 counterpart; PCSX2 leaves affinity to the OS). Linux only, no-op elsewhere. |
| `30-gs-backend-header` (new, cont.230) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_backend.h` | `virtual void EndPacket() {}` -- end of a GIF packet / arbiter drain, the DrawRun flush point (default no-op for other backends). |
| `31-gs-frontend-header` (new, cont.230) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_frontend.h` | `GS::endPacket()` declaration (see `08`). |
| `32-gif-arbiter-header` (new, cont.230) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/ps2_gif_arbiter.h` | `DrainDoneFn` + `setDrainDoneFn()` (see `14`). |
| `33-microvu` (new, cont.230) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/vu/microvu/**` | **The PCSX2 microVU recompiler, ported** (2026-09-02; `README.md` there for provenance). `pcsx2/` = PCSX2 sources verbatim (GPL-3.0+; microVU, VU.h/VUops.h/VUmicro.h, the x86-64 emitter, common headers), one modified copy (`microVU_Macro.inl` stub: COP2 macro mode needs the EE recompiler); `shim/` = the PCSX2 headers we lack (`Config.h` with the EmuConfig fields + `CHECK_*` macros, `R5900.h`/`iR5900.h`/`iCore.h`, `Vif.h`, `Gif.h`/`Gif_Unit.h`, `MTVU.h`, `GS/MultiISA.h`, `common/Console.h`/`Perf.h`/…); `ps2x_microvu.{h,cpp}` = the seam (PCSX2 globals, a 64 MB RWX code cache, XGKICK → `GetGSPacketSize` tag walk + `submitGifPacket(Path1)`, program invalidation from `getVU1CodeGeneration()`, `run()` converting `VU1State` ↔ `VURegs`, `PS2X_MICROVU_CLAMP` default `extrasign` = PCSX2 Extra + Preserve Sign, `PS2X_MICROVU_KICKLOG`). Own CMake target `ps2x_microvu` linked into `ps2_runtime`. Verified by `PS2X_VU1_PROGVERIFY` over 1.53 M programs (registers/Q/P/pc/memory/kicks identical but 5 memory runs and the sticky-flag approximation). VU1: 4.4-5.2 ns per cycle vs 33 ns per pair on the interpreter. Design: `docs/vu1-program-compiler.md`; survey: `docs/microvu-survey.md`. |
| `34-host-audio-optin` (new, cont.245) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | `PS2X_AUDIO` (default **0** = no host audio device; `=1` opens one). The game is silent (no SPU2), and raylib's `InitAudioDevice` runs on the main thread right after `InitWindow`; with a degraded desktop audio server (2026-09-05: SOF DSP failed after a resume, PipeWire sink in error, `pactl` segfaulting) miniaudio's PulseAudio connect wait hung ~30 s and GNOME flagged the window "not responding". Host UI only, no PS2 hardware modelled (no PCSX2 counterpart). Re-enable by default once SPU2 output exists. |
| `35-ipu` (new, cont.245) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ipu/**`, `ps2_memory.{h,cpp}`, `ps2_runtime.cpp`, `CMakeLists.txt` | **The PCSX2 IPU, ported** (2026-09-05; `src/lib/ipu/README.md` for provenance). `pcsx2/IPU/` = PCSX2 sources verbatim (GPL-3.0+/2.0+): the register block, the FIFOs and bit reader, DMA channels 3/4 (source chains), the MPEG-2 IDEC/BDEC/VDEC/FDEC/SETIQ/SETVQ/CSC/PACK decoder, BT.601 CSC, dither. `shim/` + `ps2x_ipu.cpp` = the EE-side state and a synchronous event pump (PCSX2's scheduled IPU/DMA events run to quiescence inside the guest store). Wired at 0x10002000 (regs), 0x10007000/0x10007010 (FIFOs), 0x1000B000/0x1000B400 (DMA kicks; the channels report their real STR). `PS2X_IPU=0` restores the old register stub; `PS2X_IPU_LOG=1` traces. Why: LOTR's frontend backgrounds are `ipum` MPEG-2 intra pictures decoded strip by strip with FDEC/IDEC (`sub_00143B20`); with the stub the raw bitstreams were drawn as pixels. The seam mirrors `Dmac.cpp DmaExec`'s normal-mode rule (a CHCR kick with QWC 0 transfers 0x10000 qwords; the driver's first fromIPU arm is 1024 macroblocks = 65536 qwords = a 16-bit QWC of 0). `PS2X_IPU_DUMP=<dir>` writes the first four finished fromIPU buffers as PPMs (macroblocks read row-major and column-major) -- the instrument that separated a decoder fault from a layout one. |
| `36-ipu-init-tables` (new, cont.246) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/Stubs/IPU.cpp` | The `sceIpuInit` stub read the IQ/VQ tables it feeds SETIQ/SETVQ from two hard-coded GUEST addresses (0x1721e0 / 0x172230 -- one game's copy of libipu's data) and called a hard-coded guest helper (0x126428). On SLES_520.17 those addresses hold CODE, so the IPU's intra quantiser matrix was instruction bytes (0, 177, 123, 224, ...) and every IDEC picture came out as striped, over-saturated ringing (2026-09-05, the title/menu backgrounds). Now the stub carries libipu's own tables as constants (read from SLES_520.17's sceIpuInit at 0x1189B0: the MPEG-2 default intra matrix in zigzag order, the all-16 non-intra row sent four times, the 16-entry RGB16 CLUT) and does the helper's register write itself (D4_CHCR = DIR). ELF-independent. PCSX2 reference: the tables are consumed by `IPU_MultiISA.cpp ipuSETIQ` / `get_intra_block` (`quant_matrix[i]`, i = scan position). |
| `37-ipu-libmpeg-seam` (new, cont.247) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ipu/ps2x_ipu.{h,cpp}`, `ps2_memory.cpp` | **The seam gaps the game's own libmpeg needs** (2026-09-05; the FMV now decodes natively -- the sceMpeg*/sceIpuStopDMA/RestartDMA stubs left `recomp/config.toml`). (1) 64-bit IPU register accesses: libmpeg polls IPU_CMD and IPU_TOP with `ld` (bit 63 = BUSY, bits 0-31 = the VDEC/FDEC result); `read64` composed the stale m_ioRegisters slots -- now PCSX2 `HwRead.cpp _hwRead64` page 0x02 -> `ipuRead64` / `HwWrite.cpp` -> `ipuWrite64`. (2) A CHCR write with STR CLEAR on channel 3/4 never reached the seam (it only landed in m_ioRegisters); `sceIpuStopDMA` uses it to park the bitstream chain while the CSC borrows D4 -- now PCSX2 `Dmac.cpp DmaExec`: a running channel drops STR only (MADR/QWC/TADR keep the progress, the pending DMA event is cancelled), a stopped one takes the value; and a kick while the channel runs is ignored as DmaExec ignores it (loud `[ipu] WARN` for the first 20). (3) `dmaGetAddr` honours the SPR bit (bit 31) of a DMA address as PCSX2's `Dmac.cpp dmaGetAddr` does (`DMA_TAG(addr).SPR -> Scratch[addr & 0x3ff0]`): libmpeg's `receiveDataFromIPU` lands the BDEC blocks in scratchpad (D3_MADR = spr | 0x80000000); the seam mapped it into RAM. (4) The DMA channel registers of 3/4 read back the seam's live state (MADR/QWC/TADR, not just STR): `sceIpuRestartDMA` rewinds MADR/QWC by the FIFO's IFC+FP qwords from those reads. Instruments: `PS2X_IPU_STATS=<n>` (a command histogram + kicks/stops/ignored kicks/pump depth line every n commands), `PS2X_IPU_DUMP_SKIP=<n>` / `PS2X_IPU_DUMP_CSC=1` (skip n qualifying fromIPU dumps / dump only CSC output). |
| `38-recomp-mmio-hint-only` (new, cont.247) | `ps2xRecomp/src/lib/instruction_translator.cpp`, `function_emitter.cpp`, `ps2xAnalyzer/src/elf_analyzer.cpp` | **The config's `[mmio]` table no longer supplies an address** (2026-09-05). ps2_analyzer pairs a load/store with the nearest LUI of its base register and ignores any ORI/ADDIU on that register in between, so the SCE-library idiom `lui v1,0x1000; <insn>; ori v1,v1,0x2000; sw v0,0(v1)` was recorded as 0x10000000; the recompiler then HARD-CODED that value (`runtime->Store32(rdram, ctx, 0x10000000u)`). On SLES_520.17, 96 of the 208 table entries were the LUI-only 0x10000000 (IPU_CTRL x18, I_STAT x9, D3/D4 channel registers, D_ENABLER, toSPR CHCR ...): libmpeg's IPU_CTRL polls read the EE timer, its FDEC commands went to T0_COUNT and the native FMV decoder looped forever in `_nextStartCode` with no IPU traffic (found with gdb: `_flushBuf` entered repeatedly, the seam never written). Fix: `effectiveMemoryHintFor` keeps `isMmio` (the runtime-dispatched `Load/Store` path) but never takes the table's address -- the literal comes from the emitter's own constant tracker (LUI/ORI/ADDIU/ANDI/XORI, cleared at every label) or the access uses the guest's computed address; the emitted comment now reads `MMIO (analyzer hint 0x...)`. The analyzer also accumulates ORI/ADDIU on the base register and aborts on any other write to it (`writesGpr`), so future tables are right; the table is a performance hint either way. Upstream `14b1e5c` has the same bug (both files unchanged there). Not a hardware-model change: no PCSX2 counterpart. |
| `39-recomp-branch-sign-64` (new, cont.247) | `ps2xRecomp/src/lib/control_flow_emitter.cpp` | **BLEZ/BGTZ/BLTZ/BGEZ (and the likely/link variants) test the full 64-bit register** (2026-09-05). The emitter compared `GPR_S32` for the four sign-test branch families; the R5900 compares the whole 64-bit GPR (PCSX2 `R5900OpcodeImpl.cpp` `RepBranchi32`: `cpuRegs.GPR.r[_Rs_].SD[0] op 0`). The two agree for sign-extended 32-bit values, which is why the game ran this far, but not for genuine 64-bit values: libmpeg polls IPU_CMD with `ld` + `bgez` (bit 63 = BUSY, bits 0-31 = the FDEC/VDEC data) and the third FDEC of HELMFMV1 returned 0x00000000B3200200 (the sequence-header bytes, low-word bit 31 set) -- read as "busy" forever, so the decoder never got past the first 3 bytes of the stream (found with PS2X_IPU_LOG under gdb: `read64 CMD -> 0x00000000b3200200` repeated with BUSY clear). BEQ/BNE, SLT/SLTU/SLTI/SLTIU and MOVZ/MOVN were already 64-bit. Codegen change: every function with such a branch regenerates. |
| `40-spr-dma-modes` (new, cont.247) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_memory.cpp` | **Scratchpad DMA (ch8 fromSPR / ch9 toSPR): CHAIN and INTERLEAVE modes, per PCSX2 `SPR.cpp`** (2026-09-05). Only normal mode existed (a memcpy of QWC qwords); a chain kick (QWC 0, TADR -> tags) copied nothing and reported completion. libmpeg's motion compensation fetches the REFERENCE macroblocks with a toSPR source chain (`dmaRefImage` 0x116538: CHCR 0x105, a REF/REFE tag list written with `sd`, SADR = the scratchpad slot), so every P/B picture was predicted from stale scratchpad data: the I-frames decoded right, the rest were the CSC of garbage (the green-with-dots FMV of build 559). Now: toSPR chain = `_dmaSPR1` + `hwDmacSrcChain` (REFE/CNT/NEXT/REF/REFS/CALL/RET/END, ASR stack, TTE tag transfer, TIE+IRQ end); fromSPR chain = `_dmaSPR0`'s destination chain (tags read from the scratchpad at SADR, CNTS/CNT/END); interleave = `_SPR0interleave`/`_SPR1interleave` with D_SQWC TQWC/SQWC; SADR wraps in the 16KB (`memcpy_to_spr`/`memcpy_from_spr`); a chain kick with QWC > 0 resumes the block first; MADR/TADR/SADR/ASR read back the transfer's end, D_STAT bit 8/9 + the DMAC cause are raised. `PS2X_SPR_LOG=1` traces kicks and tags. Not modelled: VU-memory targets, MFIFO, stall control (STS/STADR). |
| `41-mc-getinfo-changed-card` (new, cont.248) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/Stubs/MemoryCard.cpp` | **`sceMcGetInfo` reports the card as CHANGED on the first call** (2026-09-05). libmc contract (ps2sdk `ee/rpc/memorycard/include/libmc.h`, `mcGetInfo`: the result read back through `mcSync` is `0 = same card as last getInfo call`, `-1 = formatted card inserted since last getInfo call`, `-2 = unformatted card inserted since last getInfo call`, `< -2` = error); on hardware that first detection comes from mcman's `McDetectCard` at IOP boot. The stub answered 0 on every call, so a game never saw the card arrive. Per port: the first GetInfo after the card is detected returns -1 (formatted) / -2 (unformatted), later calls 0 with the format state in `*format` (`McPortState::infoSeen`). PCSX2 has no HLE of libmc (the real MCSERV IRX runs on its emulated IOP), so the reference is the SDK contract. LOTR: the card-manager probe `0x14CDE0` ignores the result code (it classifies by `*type`/`*format`), so this alone did not move the fresh-card boot -- that was the game-side `LOTR_PADCAPS` misread (game repo cont.248) -- but the stub now honours the contract (verified live with a gdb watch on the stubs: first GetInfo -1, then 0). |
| `42-irq-handlers-on-arena` (new, cont.248) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/EeScheduler.cpp` | **INTC/DMAC handlers and alarms run on the per-thread invocation arena** (2026-09-06). `dispatchIrq` / the alarm event built their invocation with `sp = handler.sp` / `alarm.sp` = the REGISTERING thread's live $sp at the AddIntcHandler/AddDmacHandler/SetAlarm syscall (LOTR: the VIF1 DMAC handler 0x13ed70 at 0x10ff40, libmpeg's fromIPU handler 0x116858 at 0x10fc50). A handler's frames then grow downward from an init-time position INSIDE the interrupted thread's live stack whenever that thread is deeper at the checkpoint (crash contexts at sp 0x10fca0..0x10feb0), overwriting an ancestor's saved ra/s-registers. Now sp=0 -> `invocationStackTop()` at the push, exactly as the vsync callback already did (upstream #184 `setGsVSyncCallback` discards the registered sp). The hardware kernel runs INTC/DMAC handlers in its own interrupt context, never inside a thread's frames (PCSX2 executes that kernel from the BIOS). Removes one writer of the "EE scheduler missing-target" race (cont.232..248); a second, on-thread corruption inside the frontend draw walk remains (row 43). |
| `43-stack-guard` (new, cont.248, diagnostic) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/EeScheduler.cpp` | **`PS2X_STACKGUARD=1`** (default OFF): after every dispatch snapshot [sp, sp+0x400) of the context that ran, keyed by (thread, invocation depth); when that key is dispatched again after other keys ran, compare and print the changed words plus the intervening dispatches (thread/depth/kind/pc) -- names any writer that touches a switched-out thread's stack. On the "EE scheduler" missing-target path it also dumps the guest stack words around sp and the last 64 dispatches. Findings (2026-09-06): the only switched-out change is libmpeg's fromIPU handler updating a job record the waiting thread keeps on its stack (legitimate); the race's corruption happens INSIDE a main-thread dispatch under the draw root 0x247100 (last dispatch 0x2471c8 -> 0x239fc0 chain) with varying jump targets (0x3, 0xd1ba00, 0x28ec80). Next: rebuild with `PS2X_STRICT_RETURN_DIAGNOSTICS` so the returning function is named at its `jr ra`. |
| `44-write-watch` (new, cont.249, diagnostic) | `ps2xRuntime/include/ps2_runtime.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | **`PS2X_WRITE_WATCH=<guest addr>`** (default unset = off), `_LEN=<bytes>` (default 4), `_PC=<guest pc>`: a SOFTWARE watchpoint on a guest range. `ps2TraceGuestWrite` (already called by every generated `WRITE8..WRITE128` and runtime `Store*`, previously an empty stub) records the storing instruction's guest pc, `sp`, `ra` and value. Two views: a 256-entry ring of recent hits, and a **per-word last-writer table** (one slot per watched guest word, overwritten in place) dumped for the crashing frame by `ps2WriteWatchDumpRange(from, to)` from the EE scheduler's missing-target path. The table is what the ring cannot be: a 10-minute LOTR run takes 378 M writes through a 0x400 window, so the ring rolls over long before any crash and can never answer 'who last wrote THIS word'. Deterministic and timing-neutral (one predictable branch per store when unset), unlike a gdb hardware watchpoint -- which matters because the failure it was built for only appears after minutes of play. This is what identified row 45. |
| `45-dispatch-unwind-propagate` (new, cont.249) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/EeScheduler.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ee_scheduler.h` | **`PS2X_DISPATCH_UNWIND_FIX`** (default **ON**; `=0` restores the old behaviour for A/B) -- ★★★ **the root cause of the long-standing 'EE scheduler missing-target' failure** (pc=0x3 / 0xd1ba00 / 0x28ec80; cont.232..248 called it a race, which it is not: hits are byte-identical in register state). `dispatchGuestBranch` ended with `if (ctx->pc == entryPc) { ctx->pc = fallthroughPc; }`, which exists for game **override hooks** -- they never touch `ctx->pc`, so the caller must continue at its fallthrough. But a **checkpoint** abandons a guest call chain mid-flight and unwinds to the run loop with `ctx->pc` left on the in-flight target; when that target EQUALS this call's own target the two are indistinguishable, which happens exactly under recursion. LOTR's scene-graph walk is mutually recursive (`0x15bba0 --jal--> 0x15a320 --jalr(23-entry type table at gp-0x70C0)--> 0x159a30 --jal--> 0x15a320`), so `0x15a320` is in flight at two depths: the inner call's unwind was consumed by the OUTER call's `entryPc` test, and `FUN_0015bba0` resumed INLINE while `$sp` still belonged to `FUN_00159a30`'s frame. Its epilogue then read `ld ra,0x20(sp)` out of the inner frame and jumped to a saved **s1 register value** (`0x10fc90 = 0xd1de70`, written by `0x159a40` = `sq s1,32(sp)` -- proven by the row 44 last-writer table). Fix: `EeScheduler` gains a **checkpoint epoch** bumped whenever `checkpointDue()` reports a checkpoint; `dispatchGuestBranch` samples it across the nested call and, if it changed, propagates the unwind (`return false`) so the run loop re-dispatches `ctx->pc` like every other abandoned chain -- the guest's return chain is intact on its own stack. Epoch-gated rather than unconditional **because override hooks legitimately leave `ctx->pc == entryPc`** and would otherwise be dispatched twice. PCSX2 parity: `R5900.cpp _cpuEventTest_Shared` delivers interrupts BETWEEN recompiled blocks and never abandons a half-run chain (the same reference cited by row 07's `PS2X_HOSTPUMP_RTC`); our scheduler does unwind, so the unwind must at least be FAITHFUL -- the abandoned call has to be resumed, not silently skipped. Always-on `[dispatch:swallowed-unwind]` counter names every occurrence. Validated 2026-09-06 by A/B on one binary: `tmp/lw1.txt` (fix off) logged the event at `source=0x15bbd8 target=0x15a320` and died on the very next line; `tmp/lw2.txt` (fix on, identical env and pad script) logged the same event plus a second one at `source=0x15b890 target=0x159860`, both `[propagated]`, with **zero** missing-target reports and 1.8x as many hero updates in the same 600 s. |
| `46-vu0-slow2` (new, cont.250, measurement) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | **`PS2X_VU0_SLOW2`** (default **OFF**) -- a BIT-EXACT 2x slow knob that SIZES VU0 on the EE critical path. `executeVU0Microprogram` runs the production pass first and commits it, then re-runs the same program from the same pre-call input state on a SCRATCH COPY of VU0 data memory and discards the result: VU0's work doubles, every architectural effect is unchanged. Safe because XGKICK is gated to `Unit::VU1` (`ps2_vu1_core.cpp`), so VU0 cannot queue GS packets, and nothing outside this function reads `m_vu0.state()` (the next call rebuilds it via `copyVu0ContextToState`'s memset). **Why a slow knob and not an ablation:** the guest CONSUMES VU0's output (VCALLMS computes matrices/collision it reads back), so falsifying the result changes control flow instead of removing work -- the first attempt (`PS2X_VU0_ABLATE`, seeding idle-success) produced runs that were not work-matched and disagreed with themselves by 2.1x, and was withdrawn in this same commit. **Result:** doubling VU0 costs 17.46 ms/frame (62.44 -> 79.90 ms, raster ablated so the EE is the critical path), so VU0 ~= 17.5 ms/frame = 28% of the EE half -- corroborating the sampling profile's 25.3% inclusive, and REFUTING cont.234's '24% of wall worth ~1%' (which was measured while the EE sat blocked behind the rasterizer). The 4 KB scratch memcpy is charged to the slow side, so 17.5 ms is an upper bound. PCSX2 parity: `vu0ExecMicro` (VU0micro.cpp) is unchanged on the production path. |
| `47-vu0-microvu` (new, cont.250) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/vu/microvu/ps2x_microvu.{h,cpp}`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/vu/ps2_vu1_core.cpp` | **`PS2X_VU0_MICROVU`** (default **OFF** until a differential oracle clears it) -- run VU0 microprograms on the ported microVU instead of our block JIT. **Wiring, not a port:** PCSX2's microVU is natively dual-unit and the cont.230 port kept that structure (`VURegs vuRegs[2]`, `recMicroVU0`/`CpuMicroVU0` beside the VU1 pair, `CpuVU0 = &CpuMicroVU0` already present, VU0 clamp config already in `init()`, `getVU0CodeGeneration()` already in PS2Memory); only the VU0 register-block pointers, `CpuMicroVU0.Reserve()` and a unit parameter were missing. `init()` split into `ensureShared()` + per-unit reserve, `initVU0()` added; `toMicroVU`/`fromMicroVU`/`runUnit` take a unit index; `run()` kept verbatim as a `runUnit(1, ...)` wrapper so the VU1 hot path is untouched. Per-unit constants taken from the ported source, not derived: busy `isVU1 ? 0x100 : 0x001`, D/T stop `0x200/0x400` vs `0x002/0x004` (`microVU_Branch.inl`), FBRST DE/TE `0x400/0x800` vs `0x004/0x008` (`microVU_Compile.inl`), micro size `0x4000` vs `0x1000` (`VUmicro.h` VU0_PROGSIZE) and hence TPC/pc masks `0x7FF/0x3FF8` vs `0x1FF/0x0FF8`. VPU_STAT and FBRST live in `vuRegs[0]` for both units -- only the bit differs. **FBRST is now a masked read-modify-write**: both units share the register and the old code assigned the whole word, a latent clobber the single-unit version could not hit. VU0 gets its own ns accumulator so it cannot pollute the `[vu1:microvu]` figures. **Result** (A/B/A/B, raster ablated so the EE is the critical path): fps 15.79 -> 18.44 (+16.8%), VU0 16.98 -> 2.19 ms/frame = **7.75x**, per call 1575 -> ~207 ns. ⚠ The gain is BANKED: with raster on the EE is 50% blocked in `enqueueWork`, so it does not show in a normal run until the rasterizer is fixed. ⚠ Correctness INDICATED not PROVEN (missTgt=0, both configs deterministic, trajectory intact, but hero-per-fps differs ~3.5%) -- VU0's output is consumed by the guest, so the flag stays OFF pending a PROGVERIFY-analogue VU0 oracle. |
| `48-vu0-progverify` (new, cont.250) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/vu/ps2_vu1_core.cpp` | **`PS2X_VU0_PROGVERIFY`** (default OFF) -- the VU0 differential oracle, and the clearance that let row 47's `PS2X_VU0_MICROVU` go **DEFAULT ON**. After every microVU VU0 program it re-runs the same program on the block JIT from the same input state on a COPY of VU0 data memory, comparing registers, flags, Q/P/I/R, pc and VU memory; no kick comparison (XGKICK is gated to Unit::VU1) and sticky status bits tallied apart, as on VU1. Verification only -- it runs both engines on every program, so never a timing run. **Result over 46,313,472 programs: `vf=0 vi=0 qp=0 pc=0 mem=0`** -- every value the guest can observe is bit-exact; `pc=0` independently verifies row 47's hand-derived TPC/start_pc masks (0x1FF/0x0FF8) and `mem=0` the data path. The only divergent class, `flags` (0.51%, plus sticky 5.2%), is microVU's documented lazy-flag approximation and is **provably unobservable in this game**: the generated code contains ZERO `cfc2` (no instruction can read a COP2 control register; one `ctc2` write exists), never references `ctx->vu0_{mac,status,clip}_flags`, and the VU0 programs report `readsStatus=0`. `cycleDiff` is 100%, the same accepted situation VU1 has shipped with since build 389 -- a stronger clearance than the VU1 port got, since VU1's flags ARE reachable and VU0's are dead state. Validated on BOTH memory-card states (fresh card -> Helm's Deep, 6213 hero updates; save present -> Pat01), `UNACCOUNTED=0` and `missing-target=0` on both. |
| `49-vram-pagegen` (new, cont.250, foundation) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | `g_gs2PageGen[512]` -- a PER-PAGE VRAM generation (one counter per 8 KB GS page), the invalidation foundation a persistent texture cache needs. `g_gs2VramGen` (row cont.227b) is process-wide, so any upload invalidates every cached entry at once: free for a 16-entry palette, ruinous for textures, and precisely the shape that made the cont.250 §15 linear-decode spike slower than no cache. Bumped at the six existing non-rasterizer sites (uploads, transfers, pokes, reset), which fire rarely and are cheap. **Behaviour-neutral: nothing consumes it yet.** ⚠ The rasterizer's own half was implemented as a per-DRAW bump of the pages a draw covers and **REVERTED after measuring 127 -> 725 ms on the CPU bench (5.7x, production path)**: a full-screen draw spans ~120 pages per range, two ranges, times 120k primitives = ~29 M contended atomic RMWs per rep. `clutStomped` survives at per-draw granularity because it is one page COMPARISON, not 240 atomic increments. The rasterizer's half must therefore accumulate a page range in thread-local state and publish it ONCE PER BATCH. Until that exists **`g_gs2PageGen` is NOT sufficient for render-to-texture invalidation and no cache may rely on it alone** -- a comment at the reverted site says so. PCSX2 parity: the transfer/draw invalidation split mirrors `GSClut::InvalidateRange`. |

| `54-ee-guestprof` (new, cont.258, measurement) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/EeScheduler.cpp` | **`PS2X_EE_GUESTPROF`** (default OFF, read-only; `PS2X_EE_GUESTPROF_EVERY`, default 10 s) -- direct ns around the recompiled-function call in the EE dispatch loop, depth-guarded so a re-entrant dispatch pump cannot double-count (`nested=0` observed over 1.84 M calls). **Guest code was the last EE item never measured directly**: cont.250 priced it off PROFILE LEAF SHARE ("~16% inclusive but essentially zero leaf"), the method that mis-priced VU0 by 28x and whose EE-thread sampling cont.252 found biased. Nesting verified before deriving: every DMA drain path (`ps2_memory.cpp:1706` CHCR write, `ps2_runtime.cpp:2468` `kickGifDmaChainFromMMIO`, the `Kernel/Stubs` HLE) is reached FROM guest code and VU0 runs on COP2 inside it, so **guest-exclusive = guestIncl - `[ee:prof]`vif1 - `[vu0:microvu]`ns**. ★★★ **Measured (build 613, renderer ablated, 5373 frames / 290.2 s = 54.01 ms/frame): guestIncl 200.62 s (69.1% of wall); guest EXCLUSIVE 88.50 s = 16.47 ms/frame = 42.3% of EE compute -- the LARGEST single EE item, bigger than VU1 (12.54 ms).** The budget closes to 100%: guest-excl 16.47 + VU1 12.54 + VIF1-own 6.12 + VU0 2.21 + other/sched 1.61 + vsync idle 15.06 = 54.01 ms/frame. Overhead negligible (clock <=0.08 s) and a no-instrument control matched within 0.9% (5373 vs 5423 frames). ⚠ Refutes the BROAD reading of cont.250 ("the transliterated guest code is NOT a performance factor"); it does NOT settle cont.250's NARROW claim that the per-instruction `ctx->pc` writes and dispatch routing specifically are cheap -- this counter cannot separate transliteration overhead from genuine guest work. |
| `55-gpr-count` (new, cont.259, measurement) | `ps2xRuntime/include/ps2_runtime_macros.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/EeScheduler.cpp` | **`PS2X_GPR_COUNT`** -- a dynamic count of scalar GPR writes, incremented in `Ps2SetGprLow64` (every `SET_GPR_*` funnels there) and printed as `gprWr=` on `[ee:guest]`. ★ **COMPILE-TIME on purpose, against the usual env-var rule**: this is the hottest path in the build, so a runtime `if` would tax the DEFAULT build for every user *and* perturb the very cost being measured. Build one throwaway binary with `-DPS2X_GPR_COUNT` (or a temporary `#define` in the header), read the number, throw it away -- **never define it for a timing or play build**. Measured (build 614, 290.2 s, 5291 frames): **8,455,625,368 writes = 29.1 M/s = 1.598 M per frame**; the counter itself cost ~1.5% of frames, so the count is not self-distorting. ★★★ **What it calibrated (cont.259):** guest-exclusive is 17.37 ms/frame for 2.2-4.0 M guest instructions = **18-33 host cycles per guest instruction**, while a microbenchmark of the ACTUAL emitted macro bodies (verbatim from this header) costs **0.616 ns = 2.6 cycles** cache-warm. **So the emitted per-instruction shape is only ~10-15% of guest-code time; the rest is the execution environment** (i-cache pressure across 5608 generated functions in a 913 MB binary, d-cache misses over 32 MB of rdram) plus HLE-stub work reached from guest code (`memcpy`/`memmove` was cont.250's largest leaf at 10%). ✓ This **confirms cont.250's narrow claim** that the per-instruction `ctx->pc` writes and dispatch routing are not a measurable cost, and it prices the one real emission tax: the **`__m128i` GPR representation** (every scalar write = 16-byte load + `_mm_move_sd` blend + 16-byte store, value round-tripping GPR->XMM) at **2.46x throughput-bound / 11.0x latency-bound vs a scalar representation = 0.70-2.24 ms/frame**, i.e. 1.8-5.7% of EE compute -- real but modest, and it needs a codegen change plus a FULL regen. ⚠ Also verified cheap and NOT worth chasing: `ps2TraceGuestWrite` (one predictable branch on a hot global), `isSpecialAddress` (inline range test), `dispatchGuestBranch` (`lookupFunction` is a direct table index, not a hash). |
| `56-stub-prof` (new, cont.260, measurement) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/Stubs/LibC.cpp` | **`PS2X_STUB_PROF`** (default OFF, read-only; `PS2X_STUB_PROF_EVERY`, default 10 s) -- a scope on all 24 LibC stubs (`memcpy`/`memmove`/`memset`/`memclr`/`memcmp`/`str*`/alloc/`printf` family), reported as `[stub:prof]`. An env gate is fine here, unlike the compile-time `PS2X_GPR_COUNT`: these run orders of magnitude less often than a GPR write. **Result: REFUTED as a cost. TOTAL = 0.13 s of 290.4 s = 0.025 ms/frame = 0.1% of guest-exclusive** over 4.22 M calls (strlen 0.09 s/3.86 Mc, strcmp/memcpy/memmove 0.01 s each). ⚠ **This corrects the reading of cont.250's profile**: its "memcpy+memmove 10.0% leaf" is **host `std::memcpy`** -- called from VIF1 unpack (a memcpy per vector), GIF submit and texture upload -- **not** `ps2_stubs::memcpy`. A leaf's NAME is not an attribution to a caller. |
| `57-runner-size-opt` (new, cont.260, experiment, DEFAULT OFF) | `ps2xRuntime/CMakeLists.txt` | **`PS2X_RUNNER_SIZE_OPT`** (CMake option, default **OFF**) -- compile `ps2EntryRunner` with `-Os`. Built to test whether the guest half is INSTRUCTION-FETCH bound: the runner's `.text` is 37.9 MB of which **33.92 MB is generated guest code = 99.7 host bytes per guest instruction, a 24.9x expansion over 4-byte MIPS and ~27x L2**, while cont.259 measured 23.9 host cycles per guest instruction against 2.6 for the same emitted macro bodies cache-warm. **Result: REFUTED. `-Os` more than HALVED the generated code (33.92 -> 15.81 MB, 99.7 -> 46.5 bytes/instr, 24.9x -> 11.6x) and the frame rate did not move (20.79 -> 20.89 fps, +0.5% = noise); guest-exclusive even rose slightly (16.71 -> 17.41 ms/frame).** If instruction fetch were ~21 of the 24 cycles, halving the footprint could not have been a wash. ⚠ The test is asymmetric by design (`-Os` also degrades the code it shrinks, so a null result is ambiguous in principle) -- but the magnitude settles it. **Left at default OFF with the result recorded so the experiment is not re-run**; build 618 restored `-O2` (`.text` back to 40,283,125 bytes, byte-identical to 615). This also removes PGO / hot-cold splitting as a lever. |
| `58-gs-pipespec` (new, cont.262) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | **`PS2X_GS_PIPESPEC`** (default **ON**, `=0` restores the all-runtime pixel write) -- **the selector seam**, this backend's answer to PCSX2's `GSScanlineSelector`. cont.261 diffed our rasterizer against PCSX2's `GS/Renderers/SW/GSDrawScanline.cpp`: PCSX2 JITs one scanline per draw state, keying a `u64` over ~30 dimensions so the emitted code branches on NONE of them, while we hoisted the state DECODE into `PixelPlan` but still branched on its VALUES per pixel. `PS2X_GS_STATECENSUS` then showed this game uses **only 46 distinct draw states, top 3 = 89.7% of 48.8 M draws**, so a JIT is unnecessary: `PipeClass` + `pipeClassFor()` select an EXPLICIT LIST of specialised classes, and `kPipeStdAlpha` (ABE with ABCD=0101 = Cd + (Cs-Cd)*As>>7, no PABE, ZTST=GEQUAL) covers those top three. It replaces **nine `pickRGB()` selector chains and a `switch (ztestMethod)` per pixel** with straight-line code. Wired only at geometry keys 22/23 (textured, non-FST, bilinear, gouraud, fog off/on) -- ⚠ an explicit (key, class) LIST, never a cross product of template dimensions: the row loop already has 5 bool dims (32 instantiations) and this binary is 900 MB. ★ **Verified BIT-EXACT**: every bench run, both configs, 16/16 reps at reference hash `aa9cd72b600021ce`. **Measured (off-line raster bench, best-of-15): 1 thread 274.82 -> 241.15 ms (-12.2%); 3 threads 124.89 -> 119.30 (-4.5%); 8 threads A/B/A/B 179.78/177.66 -> 166.17/172.38 (-7.6%/-3.0%).** ⚠ The first 8-thread pair read -21% *slower* and did NOT replicate -- between-process thread placement is not captured by within-run reps, so **alternate A/B/A/B at high thread counts**. Live production A/B was +1.9%, inside the +-2.5% single-run noise floor. ★★ **Strategic read: the win shrinks as threads rise (12% -> 3-7%) because the rasterizer is MEMORY-bound in production** (cont.234/235) -- removing per-pixel branches is real but bounded, so specialising the remaining top-8 states should be expected to yield diminishing returns. **A JIT would not have helped either**, which the census saved us from discovering the expensive way. A future game whose census shows a long tail plugs into `pipeClassFor()` at this same seam. |
| `59-audio-streaming` (new, cont.282) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_audio.h`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_audio_vag.h` (new), `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_audio.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_audio_vag.cpp` | **A continuous PCM stream sink on `PS2AudioBackend`** -- `streamOpen` / `streamFreeAdpcmBytes` / `streamPushAdpcm` / `streamQueuedFrames` / `streamUnderrunFrames` / `streamStop` / `streamStopAll`. The existing `play()` is a ONE-SHOT: it wants the whole sample decoded up front, so a caller streaming long music had to cap it at whatever it was willing to buffer (LOTR capped its level music at 1 MB = 41 s of a 239 s track, game repo cont.281). The sink instead takes raw PS-ADPCM in pieces and drains decoded PCM on the audio thread. **Implementation:** up to 4 slots, each a raylib `AudioStream(rate, 16, 1)` driven by `SetAudioStreamCallback`, fed from a 262144-frame (5.9 s @ 44.1 kHz) SPSC ring with monotonic frame counters (index = counter & mask; one release-store per pump publishes every block). raylib's `AudioCallback` carries **no user pointer**, so each slot needs its own trampoline -- hence the file-static slot array (there is exactly one `PS2AudioBackend`, a member of `PS2Runtime`). An underrun zero-fills and is COUNTED rather than papered over by repeating the tail. Teardown is safe by construction: the mixer calls our callback while holding `AUDIO.System.lock` (`raudio.c OnSendAudioDataToDevice`) and `UnloadAudioBuffer` takes the same lock, plus the slot's `active` flag is released first. **`ps2_vag`** gains `AdpcmState` + `decodeBlock()` (one 16-byte block -> 28 samples) and the whole-buffer `decode()` is refactored onto it, so the two paths cannot drift; the existing decode is byte-for-byte unchanged. ★ **PCSX2 parity:** the ADPCM filter history MUST survive a chunk boundary -- `pcsx2/SPU2/Mixer.cpp` passes `V_Voice::Prev1/Prev2` by reference into `XA_decode_block` and propagates them on a cache hit too; a decoder that restarts them per chunk clicks at every seam. The block header's flag byte is the same `XAFLAG_LOOP_END/LOOP/LOOP_START` set PCSX2 acts on in `V_Core::PlainDMAWrite`'s loop handling -- a straight PCM decode does not need it, and LOTR's own stream carries `XAFLAG_LOOP` on all 376,832 blocks with no LOOP_START/END anywhere, so there is nothing to loop to (verified on disk before writing this). Also here: `audioReady()`, a const getter for the flag that already existed -- it means "a host audio device is open" (PS2X_AUDIO=1) and the streaming path refuses to allocate without one. ⚠ Streaming is opt-in per caller; nothing in the runtime opens a stream on its own, so a build with no game-side streamer is byte-for-byte unchanged. |
| `60-audio-pan` (new, cont.284) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_audio.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_audio.cpp` | **`play()` gains a `pan` parameter** (default 0.5 = centred) applied via raylib `SetSoundPan`, plus `audioReady()`'s companion plumbing through `playDecodedSample`. Needed because the game's audio module mixes every emitter by POSITION (`Emi_UpdInstance` @0x01fe8 forms `L = (0x10000 - p)/2`, `R = (0x10000 + p)/2` from `p = -x/dist`) and the backend had volume but no pan at all. ★ raylib's parameter IS the left gain (`raudio.c MixAudioFrames`: `left = buffer->pan; right = 1 - left`), so handing it AUDIOPF's own L is faithful; ⚠ raylib then applies a SINE pan law where AUDIOPF's gains are linear -- a mild difference, recorded rather than hidden. Validated live in the game repo (build `audposl-662`): near sounds pan 0.226/0.906 at vol 0.84-0.91, distant ones centre and fade; `panned=78/200`, `missing-target 0`, `UNACCOUNTED=0`. Pan is opt-in per call, so every existing caller is unchanged. |
| `61-audio-stop` (new, cont.285) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_audio.cpp` | **`play()`'s companion `stop()` actually stops a sound.** It was an empty stub (`(void)voiceId;`), so a game issuing an explicit key-off (AUDIOPF `OnKeyOff` @0x064c0 -> `Trk_RndrStopTone`) had nowhere to send it and every tone ran to the end of its sample regardless of the score. Now stops and unloads the sound under that key; `play()` admits one sound per key so the mapping is one-to-one. ⚠ A HARD stop -- hardware releases through the tone's ADSR envelope, which the backend cannot express (raylib `Sound` has no per-voice envelope); that needs a voice mixer, see the game repo's docs/audio_sequencer.md §17. |
| `65-audio-stream-slots` (new, cont.297) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_audio.cpp` | **`kMaxStreams` 4 -> 12.** One Helm's Deep run requests **eight** distinct streams (`0x0201/0x0202/0x0207/0x020d/0x020e/0x020f/0x0210/0x0211`): four opened and **four were REFUSED** (`[aud:stm] id=0x... backend refused the stream (rate=44097)`). Voice-over is streamed, so those refusals are silent VO -- the user's real-hardware report was "I could not hear voices" -- and a refused music stream leaves the level partly silent too. Idle slots are free: the 512 KB ring is allocated in `streamOpen` and released in `streamStop`, so a larger static slot array costs nothing until a slot is used; 12 gives headroom over the 8 observed. **PCSX2 parity:** hardware is not the constraint -- SPU2 has 48 voices (`V_Core::Voices[24]` x 2 cores), so a handful of simultaneous streamed sources is ordinary for this game rather than an abuse of the model. raylib's `AudioCallback` carries no user pointer, so each slot needs its own thunk: `streamCb0..11` are written out and `g_streamCallbacks` sized to match. ⚠ Does NOT address the separate underruns (826 and 1440 frames on two streams) -- re-measure those once the refusals are gone. |
| `66-gs-keycensus-seamkey20` (new, cont.317) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **`PS2X_GS_KEYCENSUS=1`** (default OFF, read-only): rasterizer TIME per (geometry key, pipe class, texture format) plus a pixel-plan signature table for the generic-pipe draws, printed with every `[gsgpu:thruput]` line. The cont.261 census counted DRAWS; time is what the frame pays. On the live Helm's Deep fight: key 20 (textured, bilinear, FLAT, no fog) = 71% of raster ticks, on the SCALAR path. **`PS2X_GS_SEAMKEY20`** (default ON): key 20 joins the cont.262 selector seam (Simd4 + Fast kPipeStdAlpha instantiations; the four-wide loop already carried the flat-colour branch). Bit-exact: `aa9cd72b600021ce` on the e59 capture, `f754386ce41560b1` on the new Helm's Deep fight capture (`tmp/cap_hel_fight.bin`, 120k batches) at 1 and 3 threads. Helm's capture 1428 -> 1147 ms at 1 thread (-19.7%), 512 -> 418 ms at 3 (-18.4%). |
| `67-gs-fastp8` (new, cont.317) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/ps2_gs_memory.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/ps2_gs_memory.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **`PS2X_GS_FASTP8`** (default ON): the PSMT8 twin of the cont.227/228 PSMT4 fast path -- the per-draw decoded palette grows to 256 entries (same key / VRAM-generation / one-page stomp reuse rule: a 256-entry CT32 CLUT is 16x16 pixels, CT16 at most 32x16, both inside the page at CBP), `PageTableP8` exported, `texFastP8` + the devirtualised `PixelStorageTraits<P8>` tap in the scalar sampler and the four-wide prologue, `sampleFastP8Diag` under the same `PS2X_GS_CLUTVERIFY` oracle. cont.227 saw T8 at 0.01% of texels on the e59 capture; the cont.317 time census put T8 draws at 50% of Helm's Deep raster ticks (terrain). Bit-exact on both captures; live oracle 3.23 G checks / 0 mismatches. Helm's capture 1147 -> 981 ms at 1 thread (-14.7%), 418 -> 360 at 3 (-14.0%); e59 274 -> 259 (-5.4%). Cumulative with row 66: -31% / -30%. PCSX2 reference: `GSClut::Read32` expands the whole palette once per TEX0 change and the texture unit samples that buffer, never VRAM per texel -- the same model for the 8-bit case. |
| `68-gs-pipe-addalpha` (new, cont.317) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **`kPipeAddAlpha`** -- the SECOND selector-seam pipe class: abe, ABCD=0201 (Cs,0,As,Cd) => Cd + (Cs*As>>7), no PABE, ztst=GEQUAL (the Helm's Deep terrain's plan; `[gs:keysig]` put it at 28% of raster ticks after rows 66/67, all key 20 on the generic scalar loop). `pipeClassFor` recognises it; `writePixelT` resolves the blend at compile time (the generic arm's pickRGB selectors folded); `writePixelQuadStdAlpha` becomes `writePixelQuad<kFge, kPipe>` with the additive blend as mullo + logical >>7 (Cs*As <= 65025 fits an unsigned 16-bit lane, so it is the exact scalar `int*int>>7`); key 20 gets Simd4 + Fast instantiations for it behind **`PS2X_GS_SEAMADD`** (default ON). The key census carries the class in 2 bits (512 slots). Bit-exact: `f754386ce41560b1` (Helm's capture) and `aa9cd72b600021ce` (e59) on/off, 1 and 3 threads. Helm's capture 981 -> 904 ms at 1 thread (-7.9%), 360 -> 334 at 3 (-7.2%); live census: that slice 31.2 -> 12.6 ticks per bbox pixel. Cumulative rows 66-68: 1428 -> 904 ms (-36.7%) / 512 -> 334 (-34.8%). |
| `69-raster-pool-all-cpus` (new, cont.317) | `ps2xRuntime/include/ThreadNaming.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **The auto CPU plan's raster pool takes every logical CPU but the EE's own, and the raster default is that count** (7 of 8 on the i7-1165G7; was 3 = physical cores - 1 on the three non-EE cores). cont.230 chose 3 when the EE was the long pole (96% busy) so that no worker sat on its sibling. After rows 66-68 the raster is the pole: perf + schedstat on the Helm's Deep fight had three workers at 78-92% and the EE at 30-62%. On the fight capture: 7 threads on the wide pool 265-277 ms vs 3 on the old pool 335-341 (-20%, three alternating pairs); a second series 292-315 vs 338-341 (-8..-13%; the box's clock drifts run to run, only alternating pairs are trusted). 6 on the old 3-core pool was -11%/+0% across two series and 4 was bimodal (a shared affinity set lets the OS stack two workers on one core). Live at 7: the workers drop to 33-54% each and the EE thread rises to 69% -- the raster wall is gone and the EE side is next. `PS2X_GS_RASTER_THREADS` / `PS2X_GS_CPUS` still override. Hash `f754386ce41560b1` unchanged (banding is pixel-exact at any count). |

| `71-ee-timers-batched` (new, cont.317) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_memory.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_memory.cpp` | **`advanceEeTimers` batches.** The scheduler charges 8 cycles per guest call and 32 per loop back-edge through `checkpointDue` -> `accountCycles` -> `advanceEeTimers`, and the per-timer walk (4 timers, rational tick accumulator) ran on every one: 3.3-4.2% of the EE thread's samples live. Cycles now accrue in `m_eeTimerPendingCycles` and the walk runs once per `kEeTimerFlushCycles` (512 EE cycles = 1.7 us). The accumulator is exact under batching (counts, remainders and interrupt decisions identical; only their latency is bounded by the slack); every guest read/write of a timer register and the scheduler's deadline query (`cyclesUntilNextEeTimerInterrupt`, now non-const) flush first, so observed counts are exact; interrupts raised by an out-of-band flush are latched and returned by the next `advanceEeTimers`. PCSX2 Counters.cpp is the same lazy model (counters advance at event boundaries, `rcntRcount` derives the exact count on read). After: `advanceEeTimers` left the profile's top list; dispatch+checkpoint 12.4% -> 10.7%. Hash unchanged; 200 s live run clean. |
| `72-sif-sendcmd-abi` (new, cont.317) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/Syscalls/RPC.cpp` | **`sceSifSendCmd` reads its 5th/6th arguments from `$t0`/`$t1`, and copies nothing.** The EE ABI passes the first eight arguments in a0-a3, t0-t3; the stub read `dest_extra`/`size_extra` from 0x10(sp)/0x14(sp) (an o32 layout the EE does not use) and got stack leftovers -- `dst=0xffffffff size>1 MB` on every call (`PS2X_RPC_COPYLOG`) -- so each of the game's per-frame SIF flushes (0x154920: a3 = EE buffer, t0 = IOP destination, t1 = 64-byte-rounded length) ran a 1 MB byte copy that wrote a shifted copy of RAM 0x4001.. over 0x0..0xFFFFE: 2.5-5% of the EE thread. `dest_extra` is an IOP address; the runtime has no IOP RAM and the IOP-side HLE reads EE memory directly, so the extra-data DMA has no target here and is not copied. (PCSX2 runs the real IOP kernel; no equivalent stub.) After: `rpcCopyToRdram` is gone from the profile (rpc 3.8% -> 0.0%); hash unchanged; 150 s audio-on fight: 8218 sequence starts, 48 streams, no errors. | `73-vif-fastunpack` (new, cont.317) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_vif1_interpreter.cpp` | **`PS2X_VIF_FASTUNPACK`** (default ON): the VIF1 UNPACK write loop as one SSE vector per write. perf had `processVIF1Data` at 14.7% of the EE thread (its largest symbol) with the samples in the generic per-field loop. A vector-weighted census (`PS2X_VIF_CENSUS` now prints `[vif:shape]`: 658 M vectors / 232 s) put 72% of vectors in three shapes -- V4-8 zext (colours), V3-32 with W = COL[0] (positions), V2-16 sext with Z = ROW[2], W = COL[0] (texcoords) -- all cl=wl=1 mode 0, plus 17% V4-16 sext mode 1 (ROW add); fill cycles and modes 2/3 = 0. The fast path precomputes per-cycle lane selects (data/ROW/COL/keep) from MASK, decodes each vector with one pmovzx/pmovsx or a straight load (exact-size, compile-time-sized source copies, never a wide load past the DMA buffer), mirrors every generic rule (missing lanes = old VU line, S formats broadcast, mode-1 ROW add before the selects, spec 3 keeps old) and falls back for mode 2/3, V4-5, fill and vl=3. **`PS2X_VIF_FASTVERIFY=1`** (default OFF) is the oracle: both paths on every unpack, VU images compared -- 13 M unpacks, **0 mismatches** (the first cut's S-format broadcast bug was caught this way at 1% of unpacks). PCSX2 reference: x86/Vif_UnpackSSE.cpp (the same per-shape structure; exact integer moves/adds so the arithmetic is identical). After: `processVIF1Data` 14.7% -> 7.7% of EE samples. ★ Bench TIME drifted 340 -> 450 ms on the same raster code today; `perf stat -e instructions` on the e59 bench is 37.455 G on 733 and 37.459 G on 734 -- the instruction count is the codegen oracle, the wall time is the box. |

(1) `checkpointDue` is inline: it accounts the cycles, then returns false when nothing is pending, no stop is requested, the deadline is not reached and the slice has not ended -- every branch of the old function returned false under those four too -- and otherwise calls `checkpointDueSlow` (the old body minus the accounting: host-pump gate, epoch bump, deadline latch, priority/slice logic, same order). (3) `dispatchGuestBranch` walks the function table ONCE (`hasFunction` + `lookupFunction` each recomputed the slot); the dispatch-history push stays, after the found check. After: dispatch+checkpoint 14.8% -> 6.4% of EE samples; e59 bench 37.480 G instructions (raster untouched, hash unchanged); 600 s soak: 0 missing-target / stackguard, 3 `[dispatch:swallowed-unwind] [propagated]` = the cont.249 case at its baseline rate (1 per 233 s on build 726); 150 s audio-on fight clean. |
