| `79-gs-band-dynamic-groups` (new, cont.318) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `ps2xRuntime/include/ThreadNaming.h` | **The raster pool's per-run barrier, measured and then balanced dynamically.** (1) `[gsgpu:bandbal]` (always on): per participant (b0 = the coordinator) busy ns in the run raster walk, ns finished-but-waiting at the barrier, how often it finished LAST, CPU migrations between runs, last CPU. Build 743 on the Helm's Deep fight: every participant idle ~21 s of 150 (14%, ~0.6 ms of a ~3.2 ms run), EVENLY -- each "last" ~1/7 of runs, busy within 3%, ~11% of runs migrate. (2) Three knobs that did NOT move it, kept for A/B, all default OFF: `PS2X_GS_PIN1TO1` (each raster thread on its own logical CPU: migrations 0, idle unchanged), `PS2X_GS_BAND_ADAPT=<alpha%>` (adaptive stripe weights by measured rate, smooth weighted round-robin owner table: worse, it chases noise), `PS2X_GS_BAND_SPIN=<us>` (spin before the condvar on both sides, PCSX2 `Threading::WorkSema::WaitForWorkWithSpin`/`WaitForEmptyWithSpin`, `SPIN_TIME_NS` = 50 us in `common/HostSys.cpp`: 50 and 300 us, idle unchanged). Stripe size 1/2/3 unchanged too: the spread is random per-run execution jitter, which no static partition absorbs. (3) `PS2X_GS_BAND_SKIP` (default ON): a participant skips a primitive outright -- no `resolveDraw`, edge setup or CLUT decode -- when its conservative row extent (floor(min y)-ofy-1 .. ceil(max y)-ofy+1, scissor-clipped) has no row it owns; before, all seven threads ran every primitive's prologue to draw on one or two. PCSX2 pushes a draw only to the workers whose row blocks its bbox meets (`GSRasterizerList::Queue`, `m_scanline[top..bottom]`). Helm's capture 7 threads 273 -> 250 ms (-8%, two alternating pairs). (4) **`PS2X_GS_BAND_DYN=<rows>` (default 16; `=0` = the static striped ownership): dynamic row-group claiming inside a run.** The coordinator publishes per-primitive row extents and cuts the run's drawn extent into aligned groups of N rows; every participant claims the next group from an atomic counter and rasterizes each primitive whose extent meets it, clipped by the rasterizers' own `[bandY0, bandY1]` (the cont.209 contiguous path). Each row drawn by one thread in submission order -> byte-identical VRAM: `f754386ce41560b1` / `aa9cd72b600021ce` at 1/7 threads in every configuration. Helm's capture 7 threads: static 252-268 -> 8 rows 234 / **16 rows 235** / 32 rows 241 / 64 rows 281-299 (7 groups, too coarse); e59 capture 93 -> 74 ms. Live 150 s fights: barrier idle per participant 24 s static -> 12 s at 16 rows -> 5.6 s at 8 rows, but 8 rows costs +5% busy in setup replication (tie on busy+idle; 16 burns less CPU on the EE's shared core); per primitive the raster side is -10%. Coordinator qwait (waiting for EE items) rose 10% -> 15%: the EE is co-critical again. Points still drawn by the coordinator's pre-pass exactly as the fast coordinator path always did. Gates (build 746, defaults): 600 s soak clean (7017 presents, 0 `not found`, 1 swallowed-unwind = the cont.249 baseline case; barrier idle 8.5% per participant over the whole run vs 14% static); 150 s audio-on fight clean (5 streams, `[aud:seq]` starts); raster hashes exact at 1 and 7 threads. |
| `80-gs-pipeline-cap-knob` (new, cont.318) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_gs_pipeline.cpp` | **`PS2X_PIPE_CAP_MB` (default 16 = the former constant) + the cap's empty-queue deadlock fixed.** Added to test why stage 2 (row 78) is still -20% presents after row 79 balanced the raster: `[gsgpu:items]` shows the raster +25..55% slower PER PRIMITIVE whenever the consumer thread is on, not explained by a 6-thread pool nor by a 6-CPU pool set that frees a CPU for the consumer. Hypothesis: up to 16 MB of copied pipeline data (> the 12 MB L3) between producer and consumer. First build hung at `=2` (boot) and `=4` (level start): the byte count is released at dequeue, so a single command larger than the cap could never satisfy `pending + bytes <= cap`; an EMPTY queue now always admits the push. Result on the fixed build: 2 / 4 / 8 MB = 1836 / 2000 / 1819 presents vs ~2250 synchronous; single commands reach ~8.8 MB, so the cap cannot hold less than one DMA pass in flight. NOT confirmed; mode 2 stays opt-in (open question in `docs/vu1-thread-design.md`). |
| `81-gs-pipeline-hle-ordering` (new, cont.318) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_gs_pipeline.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_gs_pipeline.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_pipe_capture.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_pipe_capture.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/Stubs/Helpers/Support.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/Stubs/GS.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **Stage 2's real defect: the kernel HLE wrote the GS drawing context BEHIND the pipeline thread.** `sceGsSwapDBuff` / `sceGsSwapDBuffDc` / `sceGsPutDrawEnv` apply the double-buffer's draw environment (`applyGsRegPairs`: FRAME, ZBUF, XYOFFSET, SCISSOR, TEST, ...), the HLE framebuffer clear (`clearFramebufferContext`) and the clear packet (TEST/PRIM/RGBAQ/XYZ2) with direct `gs().writeRegister` calls from the game thread. In modes 0/1 that thread also drains the packet stream, so the writes are ordered; in mode 2 the consumer lags up to a frame and the NEXT buffer's FRAME landed while the previous frame was still being drawn. How it showed: `[gs:keycensus]` (now with a `typ` column: tri / spr / lin -- sprites and lines were uncounted) said mode 2 spent HALF the ticks inside the rasterizers per frame at matched game-frame windows yet the coordinator was busy 40% longer; `perf` on the raster threads then said why: `SampleTexture` 14.7% (2.1% in mode 1), `writePixelDispatch` 14% (7.7%), the scalar `DrawTriangle` body 10.2% (1.3%), `GSMem::WriteCT24`/`ReadZ24` only in mode 2, the four-wide loops 34% (60%) -- ~60% of the raster on the scalar fallback because the draws' frame target/format was not the CT32+Z24 the fast path needs (and were drawing into the wrong buffer). Fix: two ring commands, `enqueueGsReg(GS*, addr, value)` and `enqueueGsClear(GS*, ctx, rgba)` (+ capture events 7/8 so the replay oracle carries them), and `gsWriteRegisterOrdered` / `gsClearFramebufferOrdered` in Support.h that ride the ring when `ps2gs::threaded() && producerSide()` and apply directly otherwise -- modes 0/1 byte-identical. Result at matched windows: mode 2's triangle ticks and raster ms per frame now equal mode 1's for equal primitives, and its frame rate leads in most windows (f1200-1320: 17.4 vs 14.6 fps at 21k prims; f960-1080: 15.6 vs 12.4 at 29k). Gates (build 750): pipeline hash `061e22452b040a80` / vu1DataHash `7e2bec49a68a907d` in both modes; four alternating 150 s fights threaded 2272 / 2265 presents vs synchronous 2184 / 2287 (parity, from -20%; +6% prims drawn in the same wall time); threaded 600 s soak clean (6852 presents, 0 `not found`, 1 swallowed-unwind baseline); threaded audio fight clean (7 streams, 8031 sequence starts). Default stays `PS2X_GS_PIPELINE=1`: at parity the raster is the pole in both modes; mode 2 is now positioned to pull ahead once the raster gets cheaper (its EE thread sits at ~35%, its coordinator never starves). |
| `82-gs-quad-shade` (new, cont.319) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **The four-wide raster loop's last scalar seam widened: `PS2X_GS_QUADSHADE` (default ON, `=0` restores the per-lane tail).** A precise-instruction profile (`perf record -e instructions:pp`) of the Helm's Deep fight capture at one thread put ~16% of the hottest `drawTriangleRowsSimd4` instantiation in the per-covered-lane tail between `sampleQuadBilinearP4` and `writePixelQuad`: `combineTexture` x4, `pack32` x4, the fog clamp, the z round, the ctz loop -- and four 4-byte stores into `cq`/`fq`/`zq` (plus `coverLanes`, `dstPix`, `storedZ` inside the write) re-read as 16-byte loads: `ld_blocks.store_forward` = 191 M per bench run. Now (1) the sampler assembles its four texels in a register (pshufb lane insert) and stores once; (2) the MODULATE combine runs on all four pixels in 16-bit lanes -- the SAME expression as combineTexture's fast path (t*v <= 65025 exact in u16, >>7, packus = clampU8; TCC clear takes the vertex alpha via blendv), the gouraud colour straight from the lerp results (cvttps -> packus_epi32 -> min_epu16(255) == packChan's two packus), fog = cvttps + min/max == clampU8((int)f), z = cvttpd(z+0.5) with a scalar fallback whenever a lane converts to the 0x80000000 indefinite; uncovered lanes carry garbage the write already masks; (3) the write gathers frame/Z straight into lanes (pinsrd) and expands the cover mask in registers. Only TFX=MODULATE; other modes keep the scalar tail. **Bit-exact**: `f754386ce41560b1` (Helm's) and `aa9cd72b600021ce` (e59) at 1 and 7 threads, every rep. Helm's bench at 1 thread: 84.73 -> 71.98 G instructions (**-15.1%**), 28.55 -> 24.96 G cycles (-12.6%), 906 -> 793 ms, store-forward blocks 191 M -> 4.9 M; at 7 threads 234.5 -> 201.4 ms (-14.1%). Structure: PCSX2 `GSDrawScanline` keeps colour in `rb`/`ga` 16-bit lanes from sample to write; our arithmetic kept bit-for-bit (its `modulate16` scale/round differ, see the cont.270 QUADWRITE row's note). |
| `83-gs-pipeline-default-2` (new, cont.319) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_gs_pipeline.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_gs_pipeline.h` | **`PS2X_GS_PIPELINE` default 1 → 2: the VU1/GIF pipeline thread (stage 2) is the shipped path.** cont.318 parked it at parity with the synchronous ring because the raster was the pole in both modes; row 82 took the raster down 15% and the flip condition was met on build 751: four alternating 150 s recorded Helm's Deep fights, every window from f≥600 binned by prims/frame, mode 2 ahead in every populated bin (20k prims/frame: 16.71 vs 13.74 fps, n=6 each; 24k: 15.07 vs 13.93; 12k: 16.85 vs 14.51; 28k: parity), 0 not-found / 0 swallowed-unwind. Hygiene closed first: `applyGsDispEnv` needs no ring (it fires no flip -- the flip is detected on the guest's own DISPFB write, same thread, and rides the ring in mode 2 -- and never touches the draw context; its only other reader is the presenter's request, asynchronous in every mode); a 600 s WITH-SAVE soak in mode 2 (menu chain → Pat01, 7634 presents, 0 not-found, 0 swallowed-unwind).
