| `84-gs-sprite4-and-zte` (new, cont.319b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | **(1) `PS2X_GS_SPRITE4` (default ON; a class bitmask 1 Darken / 2 StdAlpha / 4 Opaque, `=0` = the per-pixel sprite loop): sprites four pixels at a time.** The key census (now covering sprite plans: `[gs:keysig]` typ column) charged sprites 34% of all raster ticks on the recorded Helm's Deep fight, every one on the generic per-pixel path. A sprite is an axis-aligned rectangle (t/v per row, s/u linear in x, colour/z/fog the second vertex's), so `drawSpriteRowsSimd4` is the triangle loop without the edge test, writing through `writePixelQuad<kFge,kPipe,kZ>` with a new `kZAlways` shape (ZTST=ALWAYS: no compare; Z read only when ZMSK is clear) and two new pipe classes: `kPipeDarken` (ABCD=2101 = Cd + ((0-Cd)*As>>7), the StdAlpha 17-bit product with Cs=0) and `kPipeOpaque` (abe off). Three instantiations, the measured plans: FST-nearest Darken (the full-screen darken passes, CT24 DECAL), bilinear StdAlpha (T4 particles, through the four-wide sampler prologue with q=1.0f), untextured Opaque (clears, fills). Also: no Z store when ZMSK is set (the old write-back was a no-op; with kZAlways it would have stored zeros -- build 754's hash break). Bit-exact: `f754386ce41560b1` / `aa9cd72b600021ce` with `PS2X_GS_ZTE=0`; Helm's bench −8.9% instructions per rep, e59 −9.5% ms. **(2) `PS2X_GS_ZTE` (default ON): TEST.ZTE=0 = depth test off and Z not written**, PCSX2's rule (`GSRendererSW::GetScanlineGlobalData`: `zm = ZBUF.ZMSK || TEST.ZTE == 0 ? 0xffffffff : 0`, `ztest = TEST.ZTE && TEST.ZTST > ZTST_ALWAYS`; only ZTE=1 && ZTST=NEVER skips a draw). We applied ZTST regardless of ZTE, so this game's two full-screen ZTE=0/ZTST=NEVER sprites per frame (abe ABCD=0122 FIX=128, COLCLAMP off then on; `PS2X_GS_STATECENSUS` keys `a00000a400114046` / `800000a400114046`) were rejected at the z test and never drew. New reference hashes with the rule on: Helm's `3467e2e7823fcb8e`, e59 `647a498ab2b94862` (+4.8% instructions = the two full-screen blends). Visual verdict against real hardware pending (the pair is an identity where Cs >= Cd, black where Cs < Cd). |
| `85-gs-texcensus-per-upload-reuse` (new, cont.320) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **`PS2X_GS_TEXCENSUS` extended: texture reuse PER UPLOAD GENERATION (read-only, default OFF, no production path touched -- hash `3467e2e7823fcb8e` unchanged with the flag unset).** cont.250's `reuse=3.03x` decoded each distinct texture ONCE for the whole run, but 77-94% of uploads re-upload the same TEX0 every frame and a decoded-texture cache re-decodes after every upload that touches the texture's memory. New: a census-owned per-PAGE generation (512) and per-256-byte-BLOCK generation (16384; block = `PixelStorageTraits<>::Address() / PixelsPerBlock()`, format-independent, so a CT32 upload into a T4-read texture resolves exactly; only the C32/Z32/P4/P8 tables are exported, the 16-bit family falls back to whole pages and is counted), bumped by each upload chunk's exact footprint (`gs2RectPages` / `gs2RectBlocks`, the updep geometry), by a local-to-local copy's destination, by a guest poke and by Reset -- gated to the game's raster backend only, because the presenter Initializes a thread_local scratch backend over its VRAM snapshot on EVERY present (build 757 measured per-FRAME reuse because of that: reset=768 vs presents=772). Per draw, `resolveTexMip` hashes the generations over the resolved level's footprint (three variants: texture pages = an INDEX cache with the CLUT applied at sample time as fastP4 does; texture + CLUT page = a post-CLUT RGBA cache; texture blocks); each texel sample is attributed to (texture, signature) = one DECODE. Report: pairs / decodeTexels / samples / reuse per decode, a samples-vs-decode histogram by reuse bucket, generations per texture, and a per-texture table (psm, size, tbp0, cbp, page vs block generations, samples %, decode %, reuse per generation, share of generations at >=4x). Result on the recorded Helm's Deep fight (build 759, fight-only slice = last table minus the table at 12 M prims): 9.76 G samples vs 5.80 G decode texels = **1.68x**, not 3.03x; block == page for every texture (no false sharing: the re-uploads rewrite the textures' own blocks); bimodal and STABLE per TEX0 -- pairs at >=4x carry 61% of the samples for 5.8% of the decode (T4 64x64 @12519: 20.7% of all samples at 479x; T8 128x128 @13315: 18.7% at 116x; each ~100% of its generations high), while the 256x256 T4 stream textures sit at 0.1-1.6x with 0-10% high generations and hold ~50% of the decode. Design consequence: decode per draw on a per-TEX0 reuse predictor, not on first touch. | cont.320
| `86-gs-texcache` (new, cont.320b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | **`PS2X_GS_TEXCACHE` (default ON; `=0` = the swizzled tap): the DECODED-INDEX TEXTURE CACHE on a per-TEX0 reuse predictor** — the row-84 per-draw hoist one level down, the texel gather. Per raster thread (thread_local, like the CLUT cache), 16 slots keyed on the resolved level (tbp/tbw/psm/w/h), PSMT4/PSMT8 with a decoded palette, REPEAT/REPEAT or CLAMP/CLAMP, ≤ 64K texels / ≤ 16 pages; the slot holds the level's palette indices linear `[v*w+u]`, so the tap is `clut[lin[v*w+u]]` for `clut[PixelStorageTraits<>::Read(...)]` — the same index byte, bit-exact by construction. Validity = the exact per-page upload generation over the level's footprint + a per-draw stomp test; a level the current draw writes is served swizzled (the hardware's live read). The predictor (taps per generation / texels, EMA; decode when ≥ `PS2X_GS_TEXCACHE_THR`, default 4 = the row-85 census's ≥4x bucket; 0 = unconditional) is what the census demanded: unconditional decoding serves 99% of taps but decodes 24.2 G texels for 24.1 G taps and LOSES presents. Runtime changes for the validity: `UploadImageUnlocked` bumps `g_gs2PageGen` over the chunk's EXACT pages (was whole VRAM) and `WriteVramUnlocked` no longer bumps per pixel (512 atomics per pixel on the generic path — every PSMT8 upload; the callers bump once); `noteDrawWrites` runs the CLUT/texture stomp test for band-skipped draws (★ a latent hole since cont.318: a draw this thread skips still overwrites the page a cached palette came from, and the stomp test never ran); `PS2X_GS_TEXVERIFY` now forces the single-thread path at both fan-out gates (it ran multi-threaded with a shared fill cache before, and its dump never printed under the pool). Both samplers serve the cache (the four-wide `tapL` arm; the scalar general arm), so the oracle checks it: 4.10 G live checks, T4 2.82 G mismatch 0, decode-fault 0 for every format (the T8/CT24 mismatches are the oracle's own stale fill: fresh decode == production); `PS2X_GS_TEXCACHE_MUTATE=1` makes it fail on the cache's top key. Bench `3467e2e7823fcb8e` every config; 1 thread 6.86 → 6.30 G instructions per rep (−8.9%), 7 threads 207/210 → 196/193 ms (−6..7%, non-overlapping); live fights six-vs-five alternating: presents mean 2627 vs 2568, matched bins ≈ +2% (inside scene noise), 0 not-found; 60-69% of live taps linear. Instrument `[gs2:texcache]` with every thruput line. Reading: the gathers' instructions are gone, but the 7-worker raster's wall is only partly instructions — this is not the 3 → 2 vblank lever. | cont.320b
### Verify reproducibility (do this after ANY patch change)

```bash
LIVE=tools/<game>/PS2Recomp; SC=tmp/verify_clone
rm -rf $SC && mkdir -p $SC && git -C $LIVE archive 14b1e5c | tar -x -C $SC
cd $SC && git init -q . && for p in ../../patches/*.patch; do git apply "$p" || echo "FAIL $p"; done
# then diff every patched file against $LIVE -- must be byte-identical
```
Last verified 2026-09-01 (cont.229): **25/25 patches apply, 25/25 patch-covered files
byte-identical** (only 10 changed this cycle). (cont.228: 25/25, 09/10/26/27. cont.227: 25/25. cont.225: 25/25, 26 and 27 new then.) (cont.222: 23/23.) (cont.216: same.) (cont.215: 22/22 apply, 18/18 byte-identical.)
Previously **22/22 patches apply, 18/18 patch-covered files
byte-identical** (the 19th changed file in the live clone, `ps2xRuntime/src/runner/register_functions.cpp`,
is generated and wiped every build -- no patch covers it).
(2026-08-27 upstream migration: 11/11 apply, 11/11 byte-identical.)

## Topic details (kept patches)

### SQRT/RSQRT read ft (`01`)

`ps2xRecomp` emitted `SQRT.S`/`RSQRT.S` reading **fs**, but R5900 `sqrt.s fd, ft` reads **ft**
(rsqrt = `fs / sqrt(ft)`, with a divide-by-zero guard). ~130/~180 game sites wrong; zeroed the
camera basis → every vertex at (2048,2048) → black screen. | cont.28/29

### sceGsExecLoadImage BLOCK units (`05`)

`sceGsExecLoadImage`/`ExecStoreImage` stubs converted `vram_addr` with ×8 (`*2048/256`); the SCE
convention is already **64-word BLOCK units**. The 14-bit DBP field then truncated mod 16384, so
every upload through the sceGs path landed at `addr*8 mod 16384` — fonts/CLUTs uploaded to aliased
addresses; menu text invisible. | cont.37

### runner unity excludes overrides (`04`)

**Build-hygiene.** Reads `src/runner/game_overrides.manifest` (written by
`scripts/03_build_game.sh`) and sets `SKIP_UNITY_BUILD_INCLUSION` on exactly those files, so each
override module is its own TU (stable unity batches; no cross-module static collisions). No
manifest = plain upstream behavior. | cont.47

### headless CROSS driver (`13`)

**Test-infra, default-OFF.** `PS2X_PAD_CROSS_AT`/`_LEN` force a CROSS press→release edge counted
in `readState()` CALLS (not frames), no window-focus dependency; `PS2X_PAD2_LOG=1` host-sample
trace. | cont.49

### undefined psm decodes as CT32 (`10`)

The GS-refactor backend rebuilt the psm→function table with a `ReadNull`/`WriteNull` default for
undefined psm codes — the same defect class fixed pre-refactor (cont.69/cont.116e). **PCSX2
`GS/GSLocalMemory.cpp` defaults every undefined `m_psm[64]` slot to PSMCT32**; LOTR ROTK's movie
strips carry the game's LITERAL `psm=0x20` (hardcoded immediate @0x1fa3d4 fed raw into TEX0), so a
Null read makes every FMV texel 0 (invisible movie). Table sizing itself (64 slots) is correct
upstream now — only the fallback choice is patched. | re-derived 2026-08-27

### IOP DBCMAN pad2 HLE (`20`/`21` + `07` wiring + `04` build entry)

Controller input for pad2/libdbc games — **DBCMAN.IRX HLE (Sony DualShock2 manager, sid
`0x80001300`)**: `answerDbcManRpc()` handles `SetWorkAddr`/`CreateSocket`/`GetDepNumber`/
`ReceiveData`/`SRData` (DS2 digital+sticks, profile `FF FF 0F 00 00`), and `deliverPadData()`
emulates the IOP's per-vblank DMA of pad state into the EE double buffers, sourced from
`PSPadBackend`. LOTR drives DBCMAN over the RAW SIF transport (guest `0x11bfa8/0x11c178`), which
the game override intercepts, calling `answerDbcManRpc()` directly. `deliverPadData()` MUST run at
vblank frequency: it re-asserts the per-port work table each tick, which the game wipes during its
BSS load; too-rare delivery ⇒ controller reads "removed" ⇒ self-exit. Kill switch
`PS2X_PAD2_HLE=0`. | cont.40 / ee14958 migration / re-homed 14b1e5c

### DMA scratchpad + chain walk + TTE embeds (`12`)

**(a) SPR channels** (fromSPR ch8 `0x1000D000`, toSPR ch9 `0x1000D400`) were unimplemented: the
CHCR STR write fell through with no transfer and STR reads back clear, so the copy silently never
happened. Load-bearing for **all text**: LOTR builds each text GIF packet in the scratchpad and
copies it out in `13F030`, which forks on size (`qwc<=64` → memcpy, `qwc>=65` → fromSPR DMA) — the
long-standing "only short strings render" symptom (legal screen, language-select header). | cont.46

**(b) Chain-walk cap.** `kMaxChainTags = 4096` silently severed long display lists (the 4th FMV
column quad → right quarter of every FMV frame black). PCSX2 (`pcsx2/Vif1_Dma.cpp`) ends a chain
ONLY on `TAG_REFE`/`TAG_END`/`IRQ&&TIE` — no tag ceiling. Raised to `1<<20` as a pure runaway
guard (`PS2X_DMA_MAXCHAINTAGS`). | cont.132 cycle 76

**(c) `PS2X_DMA_TTEQW`** (default ON): embed a TTE tag as a FULL 16-byte qword `[0,0,vif0,vif1]`
instead of the bare 8-byte upper half (upstream still embeds 8 bytes at 14b1e5c). The EE DMAC
transfers the whole 128-bit tag slot (PCSX2 `Vif1_Dma.cpp` `masked_tag._u64[0] = 0`); a bare
8-byte embed shifts stream phase by half a qword, which qword-granular DIRECT framing does not
tolerate — the level texture-desync family. | cycle 86

**(d) `PS2X_DMA_REFVIF`** (default ON): ref tags (id 3/4) with TTE carry 2 VIF codes in the
DMAtag's upper 64 bits (for LOTR: the `DIRECT imm=qwc` that wraps the ref'd GIF packet); PCSX2
sends qword-0 to VIF when TTE is set for EVERY chain tag, upstream only for local-data ids
1/2/5/6/7. | cycle 84

### VIF1 DIRECT carry/span (`11`/`15`)

**`PS2X_VIF_DIRECTSPAN`**: cross-transfer DIRECT span state (PCSX2 `vif1.tag.size` persists across
transfers); reset hooks live in `12` (VIF1 FBRST/initialize). **`PS2X_VIF_DIRECTCARRY`**:
payload-aligned Path2 image continuation via `GsPendingImageFn` (`11`) — currently INERT: the
wiring patch (old `14`) died with the GS refactor; both call sites null-guard the fn. Re-wire via
a `pendingImageQwc()` accessor on the new `gs_frontend` if the image-carry defect reproduces. |
cycles 83/86

## Maintenance

- **Regenerate one patch** after editing the clone:
  `git -C tools/<game>/PS2Recomp diff -- <the one file> > patches/NN-name.patch`
- **Never let two patches touch the same file** — that is what broke the previous stack.
- `01_setup.sh` **fails loudly** if any patch does not apply: a partial stack is not a usable
  toolchain. If upstream absorbed a fix, delete that patch deliberately; if upstream drifted,
  rebase it and re-run the verification above.
- Patches apply with plain `git apply` from the clone root; paths are relative to it.
| `87-gs-subfix-sprite-class-and-key2-seam` (new, cont.321) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | **`kPipeSubFix` -- the ZTE=0 full-screen sprite PAIR four-wide (`PS2X_GS_SPRITE4` bit 8, default ON), and key 2 (untextured, gouraud, no fog) on the StdAlpha four-wide loop (`PS2X_GS_SEAMKEY2`, default ON).** cont.321 first answered NEXT item 1 by measurement (no code): the Helm's capture at 1/2/3/4/6/7/8 threads and pinnings, `cycles`/`task-clock` per thread, and the `[gsgpu:bandbal]` split -- the 7-worker wall IS instructions: the box has 4 physical cores (SMT pairs 0/4 1/5 2/6 3/7), per-thread IPC 2.87 -> 1.93 under SMT (core IPC 3.9), clock 3.09 GHz at 1 thread / 2.95 at 7, barrier idle 9% per participant, threads busy 88% of the wall; the '3.6x of 7' gap is the core count, not an idle bucket. The live key census (build 764, `PS2X_GS_KEYCENSUS`) then put the largest non-four-wide item at **spr 0, abe ABCD=0122 FIX=128, ZTST=ALWAYS (ZTE=0), zmask = 13.4% of raster ticks** (the pair cont.319b un-dropped: two 512x448 draws per frame on the per-pixel path, `writePixelDispatch` 7.3% + `DrawSprite` 2.7% of raster samples) and tri 2 StdAlpha untextured at 2.6%. `spritePipeClassFor` maps ABCD=0122 (A=Cs, B=Cd, C=FIX, D=0) to `kPipeSubFix`; `writePixelQuad` gets the arm `clampU8(((Cs - Cd) * fix) >> 7)` = `mulShift7_epi16(Cs - Cd, FIX)` with no destination term (the same 17-bit signed product as StdAlpha with FIX for As; the scalar generic arm's `pickRGB` chain with the selectors resolved, bit-identical); `DrawSprite`'s untextured branch dispatches it under bit 8. `drawTriangleFastDispatch` adds `case 2u` under the StdAlpha class to `drawTriangleRowsSimd4<false,false,false,true,false,kPipeStdAlpha>` (+ the Fast fallback) -- the loop already carried the kTme=false arms. PCSX2 reference: `GSDrawScanline.cpp` / `GSDrawScanlineCodeGenerator` blend `(A - B) * C >> 7 + D` with C = AFIX for ALPHA.C=2 and D = 0 for ALPHA.D=2; PCSX2 also wraps instead of clamping when COLCLAMP=0 -- our scalar path has never implemented COLCLAMP (both pixel writes clamp), so the quad arm mirrors OUR scalar (the hash oracle), and COLCLAMP=0 stays a known fidelity gap for the first draw of the pair (cclamp=0). Gates (build 765): Helm's `3467e2e7823fcb8e` and e59 `647a498ab2b94862` at 1 and 7 threads, every flag combination; 10-rep totals Helm's 62.33 -> 55.89 G instructions (-10.3%; SubFix alone -8.0%, key 2 -2.5%), e59 22.45 -> 19.66 G (-12.4%); 7 threads Helm's 184 -> 170.5 ms (two alternating pairs), e59 70 -> 65 ms. Live fresh-card fights, three alternating pairs, 150 s: presents 2750/2628/2636 vs 2560/2579/2482 (+5%, non-overlapping), coordinator raster 88-91 s vs 97.5-99 s (-9.6%), 2-vblank frames 36.3% vs 26.9%, 0 not-found; the coordinator's `qwaitMs` rose 31 -> 41 s (the EE is co-critical again). With-save boot reaches Pat01, 0 not-found. | cont.321
| `88-gs-addfix-popcount-dynstomp-rawserial` (new, cont.321b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | **Four changes, builds 766-772.** (1) `kPipeAddFix` + `kZGreater` (`PS2X_GS_SEAMADDFIX`, default ON): key 0 (untextured, flat, no fog) on the plan abe ABCD=0221 FIX=128 (Cs,0,FIX,Cd => Cd + Cs), ZTST=GREATER, zmask -- 3.9% of the live fight's raster ticks, 12 M tiny draws -- takes `drawTriangleRowsSimd4` with a new `kZ` template parameter (`writePixelQuad` gets the unsigned `z > stored` compare and the AddFix arm `Cd + (Cs*FIX>>7)`); no Fast instantiation (writePixelT's specialised arm assumes GEQUAL) and the four-wide loop's scalar tail retires a non-GEQUAL class through the generic arm; selected by `isAddFixPlan()`, NOT `pipeClassFor()` (the key census packs that value into two bits). Helm's -1.0% / e59 -2.4% instructions -- the tiny draws are per-draw cost. (2) `__builtin_popcount` in the sampler's tap counter was a libgcc CALL per quad (no -mpopcnt): 1.4% of raster samples; a 16-entry table (-2.7% instructions on Helm's). (3) ★ A STALE-CACHE HOLE in the dynamic claim loop (cont.318 `PS2X_GS_BAND_DYN`, the default since 746): a primitive that never intersects a thread's claimed groups was skipped SILENTLY, so a thread never learned that the run overwrote the pages one of its cached palettes / decoded texture levels came from (the striped loop noted them since cont.320). Surfaced when build 766 made the writers faster: e59 hashed non-deterministically at 7 threads (4 of 9 replays, 3 distinct wrong hashes), and with the note forced off Helm's itself produced a wrong hash (1 of 14). Fix: the coordinator publishes per-primitive frame/Z page intervals (`m_bandPg`) with the extents and the run's target page set (`m_bandTgtW`); a thread notes a skipped primitive only while a cached page lies in the run's target (`noteGate`, per group and after every fill -- `t_texFillSeq`, monotonic, because `t_texStats.fills` is flushed to zero), and a thread that claims NO group of a run walks it once for notes (the last residual: 8 of 63 replays). Cost: 0.0% instructions once gated (the ungated build 767 was +1.9%). `PS2X_GS_DYNNOTE=0` = the old behaviour (A/B only). (4) `PS2X_GS_RAWSPLIT` (default 2): a draw whose TEXTURE pages intersect the run's frame/Z pages -- render-to-texture inside a run; in this game small sprites sampling a PSMT8H texture that LIVES IN THE Z BUFFER's pages (tbp0=8192 = zbp 256: a mask written into Z's unused high byte and read back; ~80 per frame on the fight) -- is drawn WHOLE by the coordinator after the run so far has fanned out and completed, since its rows read each other's writes (splitting it into its own banded run made e59 LESS deterministic). Counters `rawTex=` / `rawSplits=` on the handoff line; `=1` counts only, `=0` off; `PS2X_GS_RAWSPLIT_LOG=1` describes the hazardous draws. Cost: +0.04% instructions on Helm's, +2% on e59. PCSX2 reference: GSRendererSW draws in submission order on one thread per draw (GSRasterizerList splits by scanline but each draw completes before the next starts through `GSJobQueue` ordering), so a draw sampling its own target sees every earlier draw -- the serial path restores that order. Gates (build 772): Helm's `3467e2e7823fcb8e` (1 and 7 threads, 12+6 reps), e59 `647a498ab2b94862` at 1 thread and **63/63 at 7 threads** (was 55/63 on 771, 5/9 on 766); Helm's 1 thread 60.90 -> 53.80 G instructions per 10 reps vs the old classes (-11.7% incl. row 87's), 7 threads ~173 ms; live fights (770, two alternating pairs) presents 2701/2736 vs 2610/2561, coordinator raster 91.0/90.9 s vs 97.1/96.0 s, 0 not-found; with-save boot -> Pat01. | cont.321b
| `89-gs-pipeline-consumer-affinity` (new, cont.322) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_gs_pipeline.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_gs_pipeline.h` | **`PS2X_PIPE_CPUS`** (default `gs`): the stage-2 consumer thread pins itself to the raster pool's CPU list (`ee` = the EE's list, `all` = every online CPU, or an explicit list). WHY: `ensureThread()` creates the consumer from the EE thread on the first push, so it INHERITED the EE's auto-plan mask (`EE=0`) -- since mode 2 became the default (build 752) the producer and its consumer had been time-slicing ONE logical CPU: on the recorded fight (build 772, /proc schedstat sampled every 10 s) the EE thread was runnable-but-waiting 32-38% of the wall and the pipeline 43-46%, the coordinator waited for items 20-45% in the light scenes, and cont.321's 'EE/pipeline co-critical' reading came from profile windows that landed on exactly those episodes. Proof before the build: `taskset -cp` on the live `GsPipeline` tid, A/B/A/B every 30 s in one run at a matched 9.6k prims/frame scene -- 2-vblank frames 43-76% -> 85-93%, presents 208 -> 260 per 10 s, run-delay 4-8%. PCSX2 reference: `VMManager::SetEmuThreadAffinities` -- with thread pinning on, the EE, the VU (MTVU) thread and the GS thread get DISTINCT processors (`s_processor_list[0..2]`) and the software renderer the rest; with pinning off every mask is cleared (`SetAffinity(0)`); the EE and the VU thread never share a logical CPU in either mode. Gates (build 774): two alternating pairs of 240 s fights, prim-matched (`fpsbins.py`): 20k prims/frame 18.8 vs 16.5 fps (n 14/21), 12k 21.4 vs 18.7, 8k 29.6 vs 24.1, 24k/32k unchanged (raster-bound, EE back-pressured 45-58%); sampler EE run-delay 4-8% / pipeline 1-4%; 0 not-found in all four; with-save boot -> Pat01; bench hash `3467e2e7823fcb8e`. | cont.322
| `90-gs-pipeline-payload-pool` (new, cont.322) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_gs_pipeline.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_gs_pipeline.h` | **`PS2X_PIPE_POOL`** (default 1) + `PS2X_PIPE_POOL_MB` (default 64): ring payload buffers are RECYCLED between commands (best-fit take by capacity, returned by the consumer after execute, bounded 64 MB / 32 entries, the larger buffers kept on eviction) instead of a fresh `std::vector` per command. WHY: a VIF1 command is a whole DMA transfer -- 5.5 MB on average on the Helm's Deep fight, ~110 MB/s -- so every push was a multi-megabyte malloc page-faulted on the EE thread during the copy (`/proc schedstat` minflt: 5,000-24,000 faults/s on the game thread = the payload rate at 4 KB/page) and every dequeue a free/trim on the pipeline thread. With the pool the game thread's faults are 0 after warm-up (pool hits 2083/misses 1862 on the first run: the heavy opening scene carries bigger transfers than the pool had learned). PCSX2 reference: `MTGS.cpp` -- `BufferedData RingBuffer` (`u128 m_Ring[RingBufferSize]`), `MTGS::PrepDataPacket` + `MemCopy_WrappedDest`: the copy into a FIXED ring stays, there is no per-packet allocation; same model here (the guest may overwrite the source, so the copy is kept). Sizing (honest): EE on-CPU 24.26 -> 23.57 ms/frame (-2.8%) over the heavy scene at matched ~23k prims/frame, pipeline unchanged (26.5 vs 26.9), prim-matched fps unchanged within noise (16k 18.3 vs 18.1, 20k 17.0 vs 18.1, 24k 17.6 vs 15.9, 28k 13.8 vs 14.5) -- the fight is raster-bound there and the EE (24 ms) and pipeline (27 ms) both already fit a 2-vblank frame. Gates (build 775): 0 not-found x4, with-save boot -> Pat01, bench hash `3467e2e7823fcb8e`. `=0` = the old per-command vector. | cont.322
| `91-gs-texcache-block-fill` (new, cont.322c) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **`PS2X_GS_TEXFILL`** (default 1): the decoded-index cache FILL walks VRAM one 256-byte block at a time through a precomputed in-block order table (block 0 of the runtime's own P4/P8 page table, so it is the same swizzle the per-texel read uses) instead of resolving every texel through `PixelStorageTraits::Read`; the block's base is one `Address()` call per block. `=0` = the per-texel fill. Plus **`PS2X_GS_TEXCACHE_SLOTS`** (default 16 = the former constant) and a bench-end census `[gs2:texlive]` of the draws the cache does not serve (reason / psm / tbp / size / wrap, with their swizzled tap counts). PCSX2 reference: GS/GSBlock.h `ReadBlock4P` / `ReadBlock8` (the SW texture cache unswizzles whole blocks into a linear buffer; its kernels are the shuffle form of this table walk). Gates: `PS2X_GS_TEXVERIFY=2` with `THR=0` over the bench: decode-fault 0 on every psm (the mismatches are the known STALE class); Helm's `3467e2e7823fcb8e` (1 and 7 threads), e59 `647a498ab2b94862` (1 and 7 threads) at `THR=0` and at the default. **Sizing, honestly (this closes the texture-path item):** bench Helm's 1 thread: default 53.77 -> 53.52 G (the fills that happen got cheaper, -0.5%); `THR=0` 54.12 G with the old fill -> 53.12 G with the block fill (decoding everything is now net -0.7%, was +0.6%); the census shows every P4/P8 tap already linear at `THR=0` (211 M lin / 0 swz), the `PixelStorageTraits` share still inside the loops is the FRAME/Z addressing of the pixel write, not texture taps, and the fill cost at 16 slots is ~1% of the bench (720 fills/rep: the working set exceeds even 256 slots, and more slots cost MORE -- the slot search is linear per draw: 56.1 G at 64, 61.5 G at 256). Live `THR=0` is a LOSS (per-frame uploads: 3.7 M fills, 58 G decode texels vs 45 G taps; prim-matched 20k 14.4 vs 17.2 fps) -- the predictor threshold 4 stays, as cont.320's 1.68x reuse predicted. | cont.322c
| `92-gs-fzb-row-col-tables` (new, cont.322d) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **`PS2X_GS_FZB`** (default 1): the four-wide pixel write addresses the frame and Z buffers as PCSX2 does -- `row[y] + col[x]` from two per-buffer tables (2048 entries each) built once per (fbp, zbp, fbw) configuration and kept per thread (4 configurations, LRU), instead of two `PixelStorageTraits::Address()` calls per quad (PageId divide/multiply + the three-level page table) plus a page-table row read per lane. WHY it is exact: the GS swizzle is separable -- every block table (C32/Z32/C16/Z16/S variants, P8, P4) and the 32/16-bit column tables are row+column sums (checked numerically), and PageId is `(y/py)*stride + x/px` -- so `Address(x,y) == Address(0,y) + Address(x,0) - Address(0,0)`; each table set is SELF-CHECKED against `Address()` on a 26x30 grid at build time and a failing configuration keeps the old path (`[gs2:fzb] … NOT separable`, never seen). PCSX2 reference: GSScanlineEnvironment `fzbr`/`fzbc`, filled in GSRendererSW::GetScanlineGlobalData from `context->offset.fzb4->row/col` (GSLocalMemory GSOffset) and applied in GSDrawScanline::DrawScanline as `fza_base = &fzbr[top]; fza_offset = &fzbc[left >> 2]`. Gates (build 779): Helm's `3467e2e7823fcb8e` and e59 `647a498ab2b94862` at 1 and 7 threads; Helm's 1 thread **54.38 -> 50.56 G instructions (-7.0%)**, 645 -> 619 ms; 7 threads **175.7 -> 165.4 ms (-5.8%)** over two alternating pairs; live (two alternating 240 s pairs, prim-matched) 16k 18.9 vs 17.1 fps, 20k 18.0 vs 16.2, 24k 16.8 vs 15.6, 32k 14.6 vs 13.1 (**+8..11%**), coordinator raster 174.6/175.9 s vs 178.4/178.5 s, 0 not-found x4, 0 fallbacks; with-save boot -> Pat01. `=0` = the Address() path. **Build 780:** the scalar `writePixelT` (the sprite per-pixel path, row tails, the generic pipe) goes through the same tables at its six Address() sites (`gs2FAddr` / `gs2ZAddr`, guarded by the plan's pointers): hashes unchanged on both captures at 1 and 7 threads, bench unchanged within noise (the scalar path is ~4% of it), one live pair + the pooled 779 pairs prim-matched 16k 18.3 vs 17.1, 20k 18.4 vs 16.3, 24k 18.0 vs 16.1 fps, 0 not-found, with-save boot -> Pat01. | cont.322d
| `93-virtual-time-deterministic-replay` (new, cont.322e) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/EeScheduler.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/Stubs/CD.cpp` | **`PS2X_VIRTUAL_TIME`** (default OFF): DETERMINISTIC REPLAY. The scheduler's deadlines carry a cycle (`m_eeCycle`, credited per guest backward edge) and a host time; normally both must have arrived, the host floor paces the vblank ladder in real time and the idle wait sleeps to the host deadline. In virtual time the host clock is never consulted: a deadline is due when its cycle has arrived, the idle wait returns at once and the catch-up jumps the cycle clock to the next deadline, EE timers are compared by cycles, COP0 Count is the cycle clock, and `sceCdReadClock` returns a fixed epoch (2004-01-01 UTC) plus the vsync tick at 50 Hz (this game reads the RTC from 0x1380B0 and 0x2629E0 -- with the host date it diverged at the level's first actor event). Result: every run replays the SAME frames -- two runs of the recorded fight are identical over 788 script/actor events and ~3000 frames (the only log differences are pipeline-thread lines interleaving on stderr); the pad script, indexed by pad reads = vblank callbacks, lands on the same game frames because every frame is exactly the same number of vblanks (2 here). NOT for play (the clock is not real time; the game runs as fast as the host allows). PCSX2 reference: pcsx2/Counters.cpp credits counters and vsync from EE cycles (cpuRegs.cycle); the frame limiter only throttles, so with it off PCSX2 is deterministic by construction -- this mode restores that cycle gating on an otherwise host-timed scheduler. Harness: game `tmp/vtab.sh <tag> <pairs> <A env> -- <B env>` (alternating pairs, wall seconds per fixed hero-frame window via `tmp/vtwin.py`; run-to-run noise on identical frames ≈ 2-5%, so still use pairs). Default path untouched (every change is behind the flag); default-mode gates on build 782: fight 0 not-found, with-save boot -> Pat01. | cont.322e
| `94-gs-quad-ctx-tapidx` (new, cont.324) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | The four-wide raster loops' per-quad RELOADS and scalar seams, from the cont.323 precise-instruction annotate of the hottest loop (`drawTriangleRowsSimd4<tme,!fst,lin,!iip,!fge,StdAlpha,GEQUAL>`, 37% of the Helm's bench; ~470 instructions per 4-pixel group). (1) `QuadWriteCtx` / `QuadTexCtx`: the per-draw plan values (fbaSet, zmask, ATST/AREF, the fail mask, fbmsk, the four frame/Z table pointers) and the sampler's m_draw fields (texWf/Hf, texLin/W, wrap masks, palette pointer) are copied ONCE per primitive into caller-locals and passed to the always_inline quad helpers. WHY: the pixel write stores through a `u8 *` (VRAM), a byte store may alias any object with a visible address (the escaped `rs.plan` reference, the thread-local m_draw), so the compiler re-read a dozen plan bytes + the plan pointer itself and eight TLS fields on EVERY quad; a non-escaping local cannot be aliased. The fail-mask / `PS2X_GS_QUADOFF` booleans fold into lane masks and a resolved ATST (`writeX ? all : pass` == `pass | failX`). (2) The frame/Z byte offsets `(addr*4) & (MEMORY_SIZE-4)` in lanes (pslld+pand) once instead of per lane at read AND write; the Z24 read's `& 0x00FFFFFF` on the four raw dwords at once; the frame store as four explicit `memcpy`s (the rolled 4-iteration loop reloaded the VRAM base every iteration -- the top instruction of the annotate at 2.1%); the Z24 store keeps its per-lane read-modify-write with `old` re-read after the frame stores exactly as `WriteAt` did (frame and Z may alias in VRAM). (3) **`PS2X_GS_TAPIDX`** (default 1; `=0` = the per-pixel form): the lin tap's `v*lw+u` for both rows and all four texels as two `pmulld` + four `paddd` per QUAD (32-bit lanes wrap as the scalar u32 did) instead of two `imul` + three zero-extends + four stack reloads per PIXEL, and the palette read as floats -- `ResolvedDraw::clutF[256][4]`, filled at CLUT decode with `bilinear4`'s own unpack (`cvtepu8_epi32 -> cvtepi32_ps`) -- one aligned load per texel into the same filter (`bilinear4Lerp`, the split-out body of `bilinear4`). `texTaps` accumulates in the ctx and is flushed to m_draw once per row function (the predictor reads it at the next resolve on the same thread). Same loads, same arithmetic, same order: **bit-exact** -- Helm's `3467e2e7823fcb8e` and e59 `647a498ab2b94862` at 1 and 7 threads (11 + 14 reps each), TAPIDX on and off. Helm's 1 thread **50.818 -> 45.293 G instructions (-10.9%)**, best 619 -> 582 ms; TAPIDX alone is 2.4 of the 10.9 points (46.42 G at `=0`). Live, deterministic replay (`vtab`-style, two alternating pairs 783 vs the 782 binary, identical 812/811-event sequences): wall over hero frames 1000-2500 **86.4 s [83.3..89.5] vs 92.5 s [92.2..92.8] = -6.6%, non-overlapping**; 0 not-found x4; with-save boot -> Pat01, not-found=0. PCSX2 `GSDrawScanline` keeps every per-draw selector in its JITted scanline's constants (`GSScanlineGlobalData`/`GSScanlineLocalData`) and reads nothing through an aliased pointer per pixel -- this is the same shape without the JIT. (progress.md cont.324) |
| `95-gs-stomp-union-quad-consts` (new, cont.324b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | Two more per-quad / per-draw seams from the cont.324 annotate of build 783. (1) **`PS2X_GS_STOMPUNION`** (default 1; `=0` = the plain walk): `resolveDraw`'s per-draw stomp test over the cached texture levels (16 slots x their pages x `gs2DrawCoversPage`, on EVERY primitive, on EVERY band thread since `m_draw` is thread-local -- 76% of resolveDraw, 2.5% of the Helm's bench at 1 thread and far more of the 7-thread wall) now runs behind cont.321b's page-interval union rejection, in one helper `texStompSlots` shared with `noteDrawWrites` (which already had it). Exact: the union is the min/max page over the live un-stomped slots, refreshed on every slot change; the draw covers two page intervals; no overlap = the walk would mark nothing. (2) Hygiene in the four-wide loops, no flag: the sampler prologue's broadcasts (`-0.0f`, `1e-8f`, `1.0f`, `0.5f`, `texWf/Hf`, the wrap masks / clamp limits, `lw`, `1`) as per-draw `QuadTexCtx` members -- the compiler re-materialised each from `.rodata` / a scalar (movss+shufps, movd+pshufd) on every quad; the cover mask -> lane mask as one table load (`s_laneCoverMask[16]`); ATST=ALWAYS skips the alpha-test jump table; the `cq/fq/zq/texq` zero-inits moved to the scalar tail (the four-wide shade stores all four lanes). ⚠ The single-precision `lround` (cont.234's form) was re-verified exact over every float in [0, 256) and then NOT taken: for a degenerate q the lerp can reach +-2^31 where `cvttps` of `v + 0.5f` saturates while `cvttpd` still converts, and the guard costs what it saves. Gates (build `stompctx-784`): bit-exact -- Helm's `3467e2e7823fcb8e` / e59 `647a498ab2b94862` at 1 and 7 threads, STOMPUNION on/off; Helm's 1 thread **45.293 -> 42.869 G (-5.3%; STOMPUNION 2.9 points, the hygiene 2.5)**, cumulative since 782 -15.6%; live deterministic replay vs the 783 binary, two alternating pairs, identical 824/819-event sequences: **85.6 s [83.4..87.7] vs 88.2 s [87.9..88.4] = -2.9%**, non-overlapping by 0.2 s (at the bar); Helm's 7 threads cool 150.6/150.1 vs 153.7/153.8 ms (-2.2%, non-overlapping); with-save boot -> Pat01, not-found=0. PCSX2 `GSTextureCacheSW::InvalidatePages` walks only the textures whose page bitmaps intersect the written pages (`GSOffset::PageLooper` + the per-page texture lists), never every texture per draw. (progress.md cont.324b) |
| `96-vif-v4-5-expand` (new, cont.324d) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_vif1_interpreter.cpp` | **`PS2X_VIF_V45`** (default 1; `=0` = the old decode): the VIF1 UNPACK V4-5 (vl=3, vn=3: RGBA5551 in one 16-bit word) now expands the 5-bit fields to 8-bit colour and the alpha bit to 0x80 as the hardware does -- PCSX2 `Vif_Unpack.cpp` `UNPACK_V4_5`: X=(d&0x1f)<<3, Y=(d&0x3e0)>>2, Z=(d&0x7c00)>>7, W=(d&0x8000)>>8, MODE ignored ("act as mode==0 always"). The old decode handed VU1 the RAW 5-bit values and a 0/1 alpha: every V4-5 colour 8x too dark, alpha never 0x80. Found by NEXT item 5's audit of the unpack rules against PCSX2 (the other three divergences -- V3's W from the next word, V2's v0v1v0v1, mode 3 setting ROW -- are masked to ROW/COL or never issued by this game per the `[vif:shape]` census: V3-32 always mask W=COL[0], V2-16 always mask Z=ROW W=COL, modes 2/3 = 0 uses). This game issues V4-5 unmasked, mode 0, ~327k unpacks (5.2 M vectors) per 150 s of the Helm's fight. Evidence (build `v45-786`): two deterministic replays (identical 748 events, 0 not-found) captured at 12 M prims -- the 20k batches are byte-identical (that window is not V4-5-fed) but 126 VRAM pages of the completed previous frame (fbp 128) differ; rendered side by side (`tmp/v45_before_after_frame12M.png`) the fire, its glow and the scene's firelight are far brighter with the fix, and the user watching the live window saw "many more effects in the Helm's Deep stage with Gandalf". With-save boot -> Pat01, not-found=0. The raster captures are post-VU data, so the bench hashes are untouched by construction. ⚠ Validate against real hardware (the fire/effects brightness) when convenient. (progress.md cont.324d) |
| `97-gs-fzpair-paired-frame-z` (new, cont.325) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | **`PS2X_GS_FZPAIR`** (default 1; `=0` = the per-lane gathers): the four-wide pixel write (`writePixelQuad`, both triangle and sprite loops) reads and writes a 4-aligned group's frame and Z as TWO QWORDS each -- `movq` at the group's first pixel, `movhps` 16 bytes on -- instead of four `pextrd`/`lea`/`pinsrd` gathers per buffer, four `pextrd`-to-memory stores and a 25-instruction scalar Z read-modify-write. PCSX2 reference: `GSDrawScanlineCodeGenerator.all.cpp` `ReadPixel` (`movq(dst, qword[base]); movhps(dst, qword[base + 8 * 2])`) and `WritePixel` (the same pair, `fast` path). WHY it is exact: the CT32/Z32 column table (`ColumnTable32`, shared by C32 and Z32) runs 0,1,4,5 / 8,9,12,13 / ... per row, so a group starting on a multiple of 4 in x occupies words +0,+1,+4,+5 of one block -- two contiguous pairs 16 bytes apart -- and the row term is common to the lanes; the table builder verifies the pattern on every 4-aligned column of both tables (`t.pair`, `[gs2:fzb] ... NOT pairable` otherwise, never seen). Per ROW (`QuadRowCtx` / `makeQuadRow`): the two row terms and the 4 MB wrap guard (`(row + prefix-max column over the row's span) * 4 <= MEMORY_SIZE - 24`, 64-bit, conservative), so the quad does two column loads, two adds and the four moves, with no per-group compare; a VRAM byte store may alias the u32 tables, which is why the row terms are caller-locals. ⚠ The column entries are SIGNED offsets from Address(0,0) -- the Z32 block table is not monotonic in x, so `zCol[x]` wraps "negative" for some columns and the u32 sum wraps back to the true address: the prefix maximum is taken as int32 (build 788/789 took it unsigned, read 4294966285, rejected every row and measured NULL -- the profile put 0 samples on the paired moves). `PS2X_GS_FZPAIR_LOG=1` prints each table build and the first 16 rows' guard numbers. The frame stores stay all four dwords (`blendv` against what was read) and the Z read stays AFTER the frame stores, as the lane form did. Gates (build 790, measured against the 784 binary = the same raster code as 786): Helm's `3467e2e7823fcb8e` and e59 `647a498ab2b94862` at 1 and 7 threads, flag on and off; Helm's 1 thread **42.869 -> 41.198 / 41.186 G instructions (-3.9%)**, 567 -> 546 ms; Helm's 7 threads A/B/A/B in one chain **147.7 / 148.1 vs 152.3 / 153.6 ms (-3.3%, non-overlapping)**; e59 7 threads 64.8 ms; live deterministic replay, two alternating pairs of BINARIES **84.3 s [82.7..85.9] vs 88.1 s [86.7..89.5] over hero frames 1000-2500 (-4.3%, non-overlapping)**, identical over 826 events, 0 not-found x4; with-save boot -> Pat01, chunks 63/63, not-found 0. Shipped as build 791 (the prefix-max loop's accumulators rewritten as plain locals after the measurement; hash and instruction count re-confirmed on 791). | cont.325 |
| `98-raster-avx2-option` (new, cont.326) | `ps2xRuntime/CMakeLists.txt`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | **`PS2X_RASTER_AVX2`** (CMake option, default **OFF** -- measured a loss): compiles the CPU rasterizer unit alone (`gs_cpu_backend.cpp`) with `-mavx2 -ffp-contract=off` (VEX three-operand encoding; no FMA, nothing fused, so the arithmetic is bit-identical -- Helm's `3467e2e7823fcb8e` / e59 `647a498ab2b94862` unchanged at 1 and 7 threads), plus a static-initializer CPU check in `ps2_runtime.cpp` that aborts with a message on a host without AVX2 (instead of a SIGILL). RESULT (build 794 vs the 791 binary, quiet box, A/B/B/A/A/B): Helm's 1 thread **41.18 -> 38.58 G instructions (-6.3%)**, fused-domain uops 36.8 -> 34.9 G (-4.6%), decoded-icache coverage 92% both, hot-loop bytes unchanged -- and **cycles 17.42..17.63 -> 17.80..17.90 G (+2.0%), best ms 543 -> 555 (+2.2%)**, 7 threads flat (148-150 vs 148-149 ms). The removed register copies were free (rename-stage move elimination); the loss is diffuse across every raster loop (per-instruction cycle annotates of both builds have the same top list) -- a back-end scheduling/folding effect, not the front end. The option is kept for an 8-lane (256-bit) rasterizer, which would need the encoding; the shipped build stays SSE4.1. PCSX2 note: its software renderer JITs both an SSE4.1 and an AVX2 scanline (`GSDrawScanlineCodeGenerator` `USING_XMM` / `USING_YMM`) -- the AVX2 one is 8 pixels wide, i.e. the width IS the point there, not the encoding. | cont.326 |
| `99-tricensus-4x2-lanes` (new, cont.327) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | `PS2X_GS_TRICENSUS` (read-only, default OFF) now also counts LANE UTILISATION: the lanes of the four-wide groups as walked (4x1, x aligned to 4) and of a 4x2 BLOCK (two consecutive rows of one triangle paired by y parity, the block spanning the union of the two walked spans; an unpaired row is a block with one empty row; a pending even row is flushed at the next triangle) -- the sizing of an 8-lane (256-bit) rasterizer on the real geometry. Helm's fight capture (build 797): tris=238,200, rows 13.0/tri (38.6% empty), mean walked row 13.89 px; **4x1 lanes 51.29 M (covered 74.1%), 4x2 lanes 53.70 M (covered 70.7%; 1.59 M blocks, 12.9% with one empty row) -> a 4x2 block does 1.05x the lane-slots of the 4x1 groups**, i.e. the lane-parallel part of the loop (skeleton, lerps, sampler prologue, write -- ~60% of it) would run in ~52% of its operations; the per-covered-pixel tap loop does not shrink with width. The census forces the scalar walk and one thread, as before; the plain bench is unchanged (41.196 G, hash exact). | cont.327 |
| `100-gs-renderer-seam` (new, cont.329) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **`PS2X_GS_RENDERER`** (default `cpu`; `gl` selects the new path): the selector and the SEAM for the GL renderer arc (engine `docs/gl-renderer.md`). Phase 1a is the switch ONLY -- with `=gl` the CPU pixel loop is skipped and nothing draws in its place yet; the point of landing it alone is to prove the switch reaches EVERY path into the pixel loop. There are three, and two are easy to miss: `RasterRunFanOut()` (the banded pool, the normal path), `Submit()`'s inline call (`PS2X_GS_THREAD=0`), and `workerLoop()`'s DrawRun arm when the band pool is empty (`PS2X_GS_RASTER_THREADS<=1`). The latter two both funnel through `DrawPrimitive()`, so guarding `RasterRunFanOut` + `DrawPrimitive` covers all three -- the same two sites `PS2X_GS_NORASTER` occupies, which cont.231 established the hard way (it guarded only `DrawPrimitive`, so with the band pool on it silently drew two of every three bands and read as "the raster is not the wall"). Implemented as `s_gsSkipCpuRaster = s_gsNoRaster \|\| s_gsRendererGl` so the ablation and the seam share one guard; the throughput line now reports `raster GL-seam`, and the selection is announced ONLY when non-default so an unset run's log stays byte-comparable with every earlier log. No hardware modelling changes in this commit -- it only skips work -- so there is no PCSX2 faithfulness question here; the DESIGN it opens is grounded in PCSX2 `GSRendererHW.cpp` (AFAIL strategy, `:8960-9030`), `GSDevice.cpp` (`m_blendMap`) and `GSTextureCache.h` (the target/source authority model), all cited in the engine design doc. Gates: bench `vramHash=3467e2e7823fcb8e` UNCHANGED with the selector unset (549.32 ms vs 546.42 on build 797, within noise). |
| `101-gs-renderer-target` (new, cont.329) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **GL renderer phase 1: a real render target, and the frame reaches the window through it.** `GsGpuPresentDevice::RenderFrameGl()` (new, synchronous `Job::Kind::GlFrame`) renders into an RGBA8 colour texture + 24-bit depth renderbuffer at display size x **`PS2X_GS_SCALE`** (default 1, clamped 1..8), **resolves DOWN to display size with a GPU blit**, and reads that back; `CopyFrameToHostRgba` routes to it when `PS2X_GS_RENDERER=gl`, falling through to the CPU decode if the device is unavailable. Resolving before the readback is the design point: readback cost is flat in the upscale factor, so the entire existing present path (640-stride repack, `UpdateTexture`, aspect-fit blit) is reused untouched and no GL context sharing with raylib is needed. The GL entry-point table gains 17 functions (textures, FBO attachments, renderbuffers, clear, blit, readback, pack alignment), tracked in their own `glrOk` so a missing one disables the renderer without touching the hw-raster spike. **PHASE 1 only CLEARS the target -- nothing is drawn into it yet** -- which is the point: it proves context + FBO + resolve + readback + present integration in one testable step before any GS state is translated. Two defects the VISUAL gate caught while the logs looked healthy (no errors, correct boot, `not-found=0`): (1) the presenter's display height ALTERNATES 512/511 every frame (field parity), so an exact-size test rebuilt two textures, a renderbuffer and two framebuffers twice per frame -- **2168 reallocations in one 150 s run**; fixed by grow-only high-water allocation with every viewport/blit/readback using the REQUESTED rect (**2168 -> 1**). (2) `outPixels` is an **interface**: every consumer indexes rows as `y * 640 * 4` into a 640x512 buffer with the image top-left, and returning a tightly-packed display-sized buffer made the presenter read 640-stride rows out of a smaller allocation -- the saved frame showed exactly the valid fraction (512*511)/(640*512) = **79.8%** correct with out-of-bounds garbage below. Gates (build `bld-eur-glfbo2-800`): selector unset ⇒ bench `vramHash=3467e2e7823fcb8e` UNCHANGED (549.37 ms); `=gl` ⇒ every presented frame is EXACTLY the clear colour `(10,13,36)`, 1 distinct colour, 16/16 saves, boots to Hel01, `not-found=0`, 50.00 Hz, 1 target allocation; `=gl PS2X_GS_SCALE=4` ⇒ target `ALLOC 2048x2048 (display 512x512 x4)`, resolve 512x512, same uniform frame, 1 allocation. |
| `102-gs-renderer-geometry` (new, cont.329) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **GL renderer phase 2: geometry and blending.** `gs2GlSubmitRun()` translates a run of `GSPrimitiveBatch` into a flat `GsGlVertex` array plus `GsGlGroup` state runs (one `glDrawArrays` each); `DrawBatchGl()` queues it; the device draws through a small vertex+fragment program. Triangles pass through, **sprites expand to two triangles flat-shaded from the SECOND vertex** (IIP does not apply to sprites), IIP=0 flat-shades from the last vertex. **Depth is inverted from GL's default**: the PS2 treats a BIGGER Z as nearer, so Z is cleared to **0** and compared `GEQUAL`/`GREATER` (`ZTE=0` disables the test AND the write, PCSX2's rule). **Scissor flips**: GS bounds are INCLUSIVE and measured from the TOP, `glScissor` takes width/height from the BOTTOM. `glColorMask(1,1,1,0)` (FBMSK is `A` on 100% of draws). **Blending** maps the PS2's `((A-B)*C)>>7+D` onto GL factors for the six combinations that occur (`0101`/`0201`/`0221`/`0121`/`2101`/`0122`); anything unmapped falls back to alpha-over **and is counted** so an unexpected mode is a number, not a silently wrong picture — measured **`blend-fallback=0` over 233 M vertices**, confirming the cont.329 census. ★ **The alpha ×255/128 rescale** is in the fragment shader: the PS2 divides blending by 128 so `0x80` = 1.0, while `GL_SRC_ALPHA` divides by 255 and would read it as 0.502 — every blended draw at HALF strength, which reads as "a bit dark" rather than as a bug (PCSX2 carries the same compensation, `BLEND_HW3`). Three defects fixed in-cycle, all found by LOOKING at saved frames while the logs read healthy: (a) the resolve armed a clear, and `CopyFrameToHostRgba` runs **more than once per present**, so the second call wiped the frame and presented empty background — the clear now belongs to the first DRAW after a resolve, with one clear at target creation (**4/16 empty frames → 0/16**); (b) the group key packed state into 64 bits with a `^`/`\|` precedence bug and no room for the blend selectors — two draws with different blending would have silently shared one draw call, so it is now a **field-by-field comparison**; (c) the single-threaded path (`Submit`'s inline call and `workerLoop`'s empty-band-pool arm) needed its own submit or `PS2X_GS_RASTER_THREADS<=1` would render nothing while the banded path looked fine. **Not yet:** textures, alpha test/AFAIL (both need texture alpha to mean anything), render-target tracking. Gates (build `bld-eur-glframe-803`): selector unset ⇒ bench `vramHash=3467e2e7823fcb8e` UNCHANGED; `=gl` ⇒ **100.8% of primitives translated** (correct: sprites make two triangles), `blend-fallback=0`, **0 GL errors**, 0/16 empty frames, boots to Hel01, `not-found=0`, 50.00 Hz. |
| `103-gs-renderer-textures` (new, cont.329) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | **GL renderer phase 3: textures. The renderer now draws a recognisable scene.** `glSubmitRun` became a MEMBER so it can use the rasterizer's own per-draw resolves (`resolveDraw`/`resolveTexMip`), decoded CLUT and `ReadVramUnlocked` -- one source of truth rather than a second decoder that can drift. Each draw's texture is decoded to RGBA8 (CLUT- and TEXA-resolved), shipped once per key in `GsGlBatch::texUploads`, and cached on the device by key. ★ **Texture coordinates are `noperspective` + an explicit per-pixel divide**: the GS interpolates s/t/q LINEARLY IN SCREEN SPACE and divides per pixel, which is NOT GL's perspective-correct path (that divides by clip w and gives a different curve across large triangles). FST draws are normalised to the same (s,t,q=1) form so both modes share one shader path; sprite corners take texture coordinates from the SAME two corners as the position. TFX MODULATE/DECAL/HIGHLIGHT/HIGHLIGHT2 + TCC in the shader, with **MODULATE as ×255/128, not ×2** (the GS shifts by 7). Wrap/filter set per bind (per-DRAW on the PS2, per-OBJECT in GL). **Result: the first frames with real content -- legible textured text, then the Helm's Deep fight with every character, weapon and piece of armour correctly textured.** ⚠ **Two defects and one open problem.** (a) FIXED: the cache key's page span used a fixed 32bpp for every format, so a 256x256 T4 texture (4 pages) hashed **32** pages of generations and any nearby upload changed the key -- `up=2279 / binds=2280`, **zero cache hits**, 101 hero frames instead of ~2600; the span is now computed from bits-per-texel (T4HL/T4HH/T8H span as 32bpp since they live in a 32bpp surface's unused bits). (b) **STILL OPEN after that fix**: reuse did not improve (`up=2285 / binds=2286`), so the volatility is the per-draw texture generation itself, not the span -- the whole-texture decode per generation change is the wrong shape, where the CPU cache decodes lazily behind a reuse predictor and gets ~100x (217k fills for 22M textured draws). **This is the phase-3 follow-up and it is a PERFORMANCE blocker, not a correctness one.** (c) Not started: render-to-texture invalidation -- `g_gs2PageGen` does not cover draw writes, so a buffer the game renders into and then samples serves a stale decode (phase 4). Known-wrong in the picture: the background terrain is missing (reads white) and shadow draws come out pink. Gates (build `bld-eur-gltex2-805`): selector unset ⇒ bench `vramHash=3467e2e7823fcb8e` UNCHANGED; `=gl` ⇒ 0 GL errors, `blend-fallback=0`, boots to Hel01, `not-found=0`, 50.00 Hz, and a **recognisable textured scene**. |
| `111-gl-renderer-pipeline-census` (new, cont.329g) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | **`PS2X_GS_GLR_CENSUS`** (read-only, default OFF) + sprite coverage and a PERIODIC dump for `PS2X_GS_TRICENSUS`: count what survives EVERY stage of the GL draw path in ONE run, because seven candidate causes for the missing scene had been tested one at a time and four permanent eliminations said the MODEL of the path had a hole rather than that the next hypothesis would be luckier. BACKEND half: runs, primitives in / zero-vertex / dropped / translated, vertices, state groups, texture uploads, flushes by cause (threshold vs target change), batches and vertices shipped, and **geometry left STRANDED in the coalescing accumulator at the instant the presenter resolves a frame** (nothing flushes on a frame boundary, so anything pending is drawn into the NEXT frame) -- plus the primitives' OWN screen area from the guest coordinates (sprite rect, triangle cross product), i.e. the fragments the geometry should produce. DEVICE half: batches in/drawn and dropped BY REASON (empty, bad reference extent, no target, no program), groups in and skipped BY REASON (empty, degenerate scissor), draw calls, vertices drawn, texture binds and **keys with no GL object behind them**, fragments that passed the depth test (`GL_SAMPLES_PASSED` through a 4-deep query ring, so the device thread never stalls on its own query), and PER RENDER TARGET: clears, resolves, **resolves that do not arm a clear**, and the mean/max **groups drawn between two clears** -- the accumulation hypothesis stated as a counter. CPU side: `PS2X_GS_TRICENSUS` now also counts SPRITE coverage (the scissor- and band-clipped rect, once per draw, no per-pixel cost) and dumps from `gs2PrintRasterThroughput` instead of only at bench end -- it had reported NOTHING in every live run, because a `timeout`-killed run never reaches the destructor. RESULTS (build 828, deterministic replay `PS2X_VIRTUAL_TIME=1`, Helm's Deep, matched guest-frame windows): **no stage drops anything** -- prims in = translated (123.7 M, 0 dropped), vertices emitted = shipped = drawn (375 M), batches in = drawn, groups in = drawn, 0 skipped, 0 missing textures, **0 stranded vertices at all 17408 resolves**; **~1 clear per guest frame** (groups between clears 70.5 vs 66.9 per frame) so **accumulation is FALSIFIED**; and, decisively, on the SAME guest frames the GL path generates **5.51 M fragments/frame (depth off) against the software rasterizer's 5.97 M covered (triangles 4.62 M + sprites 1.36 M)** -- within 8%, with the sprite figure matching the backend's independent geometry-area census to four digits. Depth rejects only 17.7%. ⇒ the GL renderer draws the same coverage as the CPU rasterizer, so the missing detail is in the COLOUR those fragments produce, not in geometry that never arrives. No hardware modelling changes here -- counting only, every counter behind the flag -- so there is no PCSX2 faithfulness question; the occlusion-query shape mirrors `GSDevice` capability queries in kind only. Gates: selector unset ⇒ bench `vramHash=3467e2e7823fcb8e` UNCHANGED (9.407 G insn, 591 ms); `=gl` ⇒ boots to Hel01, `not-found=0`, 50.00 Hz, `blend-fallback=0`. ⚠ Rows 104-110 (cont.329f) were committed without their doc rows; this file is behind by that much. | cont.329g
| `112-gl-renderer-target-split` (new, cont.329h) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_TGTSPLIT` (default ON; `=0` restores the old behaviour): the GL path never got the RENDER-TARGET SPLIT.** `RasterRunFanOut` splits a run on `(fbp, fbw, psm, zbp, zpsm)` before rasterizing -- but that split sits BELOW the `s_gsSkipCpuRaster` early return, so only the CPU rasterizer ever saw it, and `glSubmitRun` assigned the whole batch to `items[0]`'s target (its own comment, "runs are split on the target upstream", was false for this path). Measured on the deterministic replay: **4,323 runs per 200 s mixed two framebuffers and 7,977,252 primitives were drawn into the wrong one** -- including the frame's post-process chain, whose passes then landed on the VISIBLE buffer. The per-draw trace caught it exactly: after a normal scene (luma 18.9, 899 colours) a 6-vertex full-screen quad with `abe=1 ABCD=0122 FIX=128` (Cv = (Cs-Cd)*FIX, an INVERT) took the target to **luma 236.1**, a second quad filled it black, 864 additive sprites drew into that, and a final invert left the whole frame at **luma 255.0, one distinct colour**. With the split, those 27 M vertices per run go to scratch target `fbp=0x180` where the game put them. **Presented-frame luma at matched guest flips (CPU / GL before / GL after): 41.3 / 169.5 / 22.8 at flip 6500, 51.6 / 194.5 / 31.2 at 7500, 50.4 / 167.1 / 35.4 at 8000** -- the white wash that had read as "~40% of the scene is missing" is GONE and the picture is the scene again (evidence: game repo `docs/reference_screenshots/cont329h_target_split.png`). ⚠ STILL OPEN: the background is now DARK rather than white (distinct colours 14-19k against the software path's 27-36k) -- scratch target `0x180` takes 27 M verts, is never resolved, and what composites it back is the next question. Also in this commit: **`PS2X_GS_DRAWCMP`** (read-only) -- the per-draw-call comparison that found it. It arms at a cumulative PRIMITIVE COUNT (⚠ NOT a display flip: arming on a flip caught 35 of ~24,000 primitives of that frame, because the presenter advances the flip counter while the GS worker draws whatever is in its queue) and snapshots the draw target at every run boundary from the worker loop's Draw/DrawRun arm -- the one point BOTH renderers pass through in the same order, so snapshot k holds the same primitives in a CPU run and a GL run; `PS2X_GS_DRAWCMP_TRACE=<prims>` then traces DRAW CALL BY DRAW CALL on the device, printing each group's full state beside a readback of what it produced. Plus **`GsGlGroup::fbmsk`** (FRAME.FBMSK was not carried at all) and **`PS2X_GS_GLR_FBMSK`** (default OFF): the FBMSK census says this game uses only `ff000000` (163 M draws), `00000000` (14,539) and `ffffffff` (ONE draw), so honouring it changes one draw here -- the cont.329 census was right and FBMSK is NOT the cause; the flag exists so that stays measured rather than assumed. PCSX2 reference for the split: `GSRendererHW` flushes and re-targets on a FRAME change (`GSState::FlushPrim` is per draw-environment), which is the same invariant -- one draw call, one render target. Gates (build 834): selector unset ⇒ bench `vramHash=3467e2e7823fcb8e` UNCHANGED (9.430 G insn, 555.84 ms); `=gl` ⇒ boots to Hel01, `not-found=0`, 50.00 Hz, **0 GL errors**, `blend-fallback=0`, 9210 guest frames in 200 s (0.8% under the pre-split 9281, and still 2.4x the software path's 3809). | cont.329h
| `113-gl-target-source-pixel-format` (new, cont.329i) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_SRCTEXA` (default ON; `=0` reverts): a promoted render-target source is NOT necessarily RGBA — read it in the PIXEL FORMAT the guest named.** cont.329h left the scene rendering DARK; the question was what composites scratch target `0x180` back. The `PS2X_GS_GLR_TGTSRC` census (new here: every distinct draw target, and every texture source at or above the first scratch page, with promotion counts) found exactly two composites, and **both were already promoted**: `tbp=12288` (page `0x180`, page-aligned, **PSMCT24** 512x512, **DECAL**, ~4,000 draws) and `tbp=8192` (page `0x100`, **PSMT4HL** 256x256, MODULATE, ~200,000 draws). The per-draw film strip (one PPM per traced draw call, named by target — also new here) then showed the CT24 one taking the VISIBLE buffer to **luma 0.0, ONE distinct colour**. Its blend is `ABCD=2101` = `(0−Cd)·As + Cd` = **Cd·(1−As)**, so As=1 is a wipe — and **a 24-bit source HAS no alpha**: on the PS2 it comes from TEXA. We bound the target's GL colour texture and sampled its own alpha, which is **1.0 everywhere** (cleared at creation, and never written since `glColorMask` masks alpha on every draw). The fix reads it as the CPU sampler does — `a = (TEXA.AEM && rgb==0) ? 0 : TEXA.TA0` (gs_cpu_backend.cpp's `ReadTexelExpand`) — measured on this composite as **TA0=0x46, AEM=1**, i.e. a 45% darkening pass, not a wipe. **Result, aggregated over 7 matched gameplay flips of the deterministic replay: mean luma 30.9 → 46.0 against the CPU rasterizer's 49.3 (median 31.2 → 51.7 vs 51.6, i.e. 100% of the reference), distinct colours 13,258 → 22,656 vs 29,253 (45% → 77%).** The picture is now the reference scene: wall, rubble, ladder, fire, ground and characters all present and correctly lit (evidence: game repo `docs/reference_screenshots/cont329i_ct24_source_alpha.png`). ★★ **MEASURED AND RULED OUT: the T4HL composite (PCSX2's "channel shuffle" — a 4-bit index taken from the target's ALPHA byte through the CLUT) is a NULL here.** `PS2X_GS_GLR_SRCNEUTRAL=1` (default OFF, diagnostic) samples such a source as neutral white and moved mean luma by **+0.8** with distinct colours unchanged, and at one flip it was WORSE — so implementing channel shuffle would buy nothing in this game, even though it is 200,000 draws. Do not invest there. ⚠ STILL OPEN: distinct colours at 77% of the reference, and one HUD text element renders as a flat bar. PCSX2 reference: `GSTextureCache::Source` carries the source's own TEX0/TEXA and `GSRendererHW` expands 24-bit alpha from TEXA rather than from the target (`GSLocalMemory::ReadTexel24`), which is the same rule. Gates (build 838): selector unset ⇒ bench `vramHash=3467e2e7823fcb8e` UNCHANGED (9.407 G insn, 547.94 ms); `=gl` ⇒ boots to Hel01, `not-found=0`, 50.00 Hz, **0 GL errors**, `blend-fallback=0`, **9,128 guest frames in 200 s = 2.4x the software path's 3,809**. | cont.329i
| `114-gl-sampling-fidelity-and-the-variety-ablation` (new, cont.329j) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h` | ★★★ **Four sampling/shading fidelity gaps closed in the GL renderer, and the remaining colour-variety deficit LOCALISED BY ABLATION (not closed).** cont.329i left the GL frame at 77-84% of the software path's distinct colours with matching brightness. Four features were missing and are now implemented, each measured: (1) **`PS2X_GS_GLR_MIPS` (default ON)** -- the game's own MIP CHAIN. TEX1 census: **74.4% of textured draws carry MMIN=5 (trilinear) with MXL=3**, and the GL path uploaded level 0 only and never mipmapped, while the CPU rasterizer samples the level the GS selects. Levels 0..MXL are decoded through `resolveTexMip` (so each level's address comes from MIPTBP1/2 exactly as the CPU sampler reads it) and MIN_FILTER follows MMIN. ⚠ With GL's own LOD rule this OVER-BLURRED (adjacent-pixel roughness 2.10 against the reference's 6.63 on a grazing ground plane), so the level is computed in the shader from **the GS formula, `LOD = (log2(1/|Q|) << L) + K`, fixed at K when LCM=1** (PCSX2 GSRendererHW's manual_lod, K/16) and fetched with `textureLod`: roughness **4.37 vs the reference's 4.47**, where the no-mip path was 4.60. (2) **`PS2X_GS_GLR_FOG` (default ON)** -- PRIM.FGE/FOGCOL were not implemented at all; now `C = ((F*C) >> 8) + (((255-F)*FOGCOL) >> 8)` as writePixelT does it, with F as a vertex attribute. 30.1% of draws set FGE -- and the per-vertex F census says **100% of those vertices sit at F >= 224**, so it is correctly INERT in this scene rather than not running. (3) **`PS2X_GS_GLR_NOPERSPCOL` (default ON)** -- gouraud colour interpolated SCREEN-LINEARLY, as the GS does (GL's default divides by clip w). (4) **`PS2X_GS_GLR_PALTGT` (default OFF = the fix)** -- a PALETTED target-as-source read is no longer promoted: a CLUT lookup of a GL colour texture is meaningless, and the ordinary VRAM decode is at least the right shape. ★★ **ELIMINATED BY MEASUREMENT, so nobody builds them: dither** (absent from BOTH renderers), **COLCLAMP** (the CPU rasterizer does not implement blend WRAP either, though 7.3% of draws ask for it -- a shared divergence from hardware, not a GL defect), **FBA / PABE / REGION wrap modes / CT16 TEXA** (all zero here), and -- the expensive one -- **DEPTH-AS-TEXTURE + CHANNEL SHUFFLE**: page 0x100 is the Z BUFFER (`ZBUF{zbp=0x100}` on every target) and ~200,000 draws read it as PSMT4HL, a 4-bit index from the spare byte of PSMZ24 through the CLUT; the probe confirmed it samples a REAL 16-level map, but **ablating every Z-sampling draw from the REFERENCE changes the frame by 0.6% of colours with 97.1% of pixels within 8 LSB** -- a null, so PCSX2's channel-shuffle machinery is NOT needed for this game. ⚠ **STILL OPEN, now localised: the remaining deficit is target `0x180`'s contribution.** Ablating every draw into it on the REFERENCE (`PS2X_GS_SKIPTGT`) drops the software path from luma 49.3 / 29,860 distinct to **41.8 / 22,621**, bracketing GL's 47.9 / 25,380 -- so GL receives part of what 0x180 contributes and not all of it. Visually the difference reads as a HUE shift (a character's cream robe renders brown). New read-only instruments: `PS2X_GS_GLR_TGTSRC` (draw targets with fbw/psm/ZBUF/vertex extent + texture sources with promotion counts), `PS2X_GS_T4HLPROBE`, and the CPU-side ablations `PS2X_GS_SKIPZTEX` / `PS2X_GS_SKIPTGT=<page>`. Gates (build 850): selector unset ⇒ bench `vramHash=3467e2e7823fcb8e` UNCHANGED (9.409 G insn, 547.60 ms); `=gl` ⇒ Hel01, `not-found=0`, 50.00 Hz, **0 GL errors**, `blend-fallback=0`, **9,419 guest frames in 200 s = 2.47x the software path's 3,809** (up from 9,128 -- the mip chain costs decode work but reads less texture bandwidth). | cont.329j
| `115-gl-target-source-freshness-and-the-0x180-verdict` (new, cont.329k) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | ★★★ **The cont.329j question -- "what does target `0x180` contribute that GL is missing?" -- ANSWERED, and it is NOT the colour-variety gap.** The range ablation (`PS2X_GS_SKIPTEXPAGE`) shows **pages 0x180..0x1FF ARE this game's texture memory** (ablating reads of them collapses the reference to 2,257 distinct colours), and the game RENDERS into that region -- 9.4 M draws per run -- so the GL renderer, which never writes `m_vram`, serves stale bytes for anything rendered there. **`PS2X_GS_GLR_VRAMFEED`** (new, diagnostic, slow: runs the CPU rasterizer alongside GL purely to keep VRAM fresh) sizes exactly that: per-pixel agreement with the reference rises **45.9% -> 60.5% of pixels within 8 LSB**, while distinct colours move **25,380 -> 25,550 against the reference's 29,863** -- i.e. **a real Source-from-Target would buy pixel ACCURACY, not the missing variety.** ★★ And the variety metric's own floor is now known: on simple screens the two renderers agree at **100.0% within 8 LSB, mean |delta| 1.00** -- the fragment pipeline is fundamentally correct, and divergence is scene-dependent, not systemic. Shipped alongside (all strictly more correct, all measured INERT in this scene, all default ON): **`PS2X_GS_GLR_CLUTGEN`** -- the CLUT's page generation folded into the decoded-texture cache's generation, because the content hash covers the palette but is only consulted when the generation MOVES, and a CLUT lives on a different page (a palette re-upload could not invalidate; measured: uploads 122,755 vs 122,971, so this game does not animate palettes); the run split now keys on **(fbp, FBW)** rather than the address alone, since `refW = fbw * 64` is the extent the vertex transform divides by and it is taken from `items[0]`; and **`PS2X_GS_GLR_TGTFRESH`** -- a target-as-source read is promoted only while the GL target is still fresher than VRAM (page-generation fingerprint over the target's first pages). ★★ **ELIMINATED this cycle, each by measurement**: the CLUT-generation staleness, over-range PS2 blend factors (`As > 0x80` is **0.15%** of As-blended vertices), and frame misalignment (best integer shift varies per flip: (-1,+2), (0,-1), (+1,+1) -- it is content, not registration). ⚠ **NEW BUG FOUND AND LOCALISED, NOT FIXED: the pre-rendered background / FMV screens render TILED 4x.** Those screens are four 128-px column strips, each legitimately sampling the whole 128x512 texture at `tbp=12291` (STQ s,t = 0..1, verified per draw) -- correct in both renderers -- which the CPU then OVERWRITES with a 512x512 `tbp=12288` composite. GL promotes that composite to target `0x180`, whose GL copy is **empty on that screen** (dumped: black), so it contributes nothing and the strip backdrop stays visible. Neither the (fbp,fbw) split nor the freshness rule stops it: the promotion still fires 382/382, because **no upload or transfer bumps those pages** -- how the image reaches `0x180` is the open question. New read-only instruments for it: `PS2X_GS_GLR_PAGEMAP` (per-page written/read map + stale-read census with the worst offenders), `PS2X_GS_GLR_DUMPTGT` (dump every live GL target), `PS2X_GS_GLR_UVPROBE`/`_UVPROBEW`/`_UVPROBETW` (per-draw texture state and EMITTED texture coordinates), `PS2X_GS_T4HLPROBE`, the CPU-side ablations `PS2X_GS_SKIPZTEX` / `PS2X_GS_SKIPTGT=<page>` / `PS2X_GS_SKIPTEXPAGE(S)`, and the per-draw trace now prints the batch's reference extent and each group's full texture key + TBP. ⚠ `perf` is currently unavailable on this box (`perf_event_paranoid = 4`), so `hbench.sh` prints no counters; the bench was run directly for the hash. Gates (build 868): selector unset ⇒ bench **`vramHash=3467e2e7823fcb8e` exact** (best rep 547.20 ms); `=gl` ⇒ Hel01, `not-found=0`, 50.00 Hz, **0 GL errors**, **9,312 guest frames in 200 s = 2.45x the software path**, and the matched-flip gameplay metrics unchanged (luma 47.9, distinct 25,254). | cont.329k
| `116-gl-target-dirty-diagnosis` (new, cont.330) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_TEXTRACE=<tbp>` (read-only, default OFF) -- the probe that ANSWERED cont.329k's open question and RETRACTED its premise.** cont.329k left the FMV / pre-rendered background screens rendering TILED 4x, with the chain localised but the cause recorded as "no upload or transfer bumps those pages, so how the image reaches `0x180` on the CPU is the open question". TEXTRACE prints, for one watched TBP, the three quantities that actually decide a texture-cache and a target-promotion decision -- the summed page generation, a live content hash, and the promotion's every input -- plus the SITE of every non-draw VRAM mutation landing in those pages. One run settled it: the picture arrives by ORDINARY host->local uploads into pages 0x180+ (128px-wide CT32 at TBW=2, in row chunks) and they DO bump the page generation -- so the premise was wrong. The real defect is that `PS2X_GS_GLR_TGTFRESH` compares the target's fingerprint at the last DRAW INTO it against the sample, and the guest draws one full-screen sprite into `fbp=0x180` every frame AFTER the upload, which re-stamps the fingerprint: `stamped == live` on 1896 of 1899 promotions. The rule can only ever catch an upload landing between the last draw and the sample, and `m_glTargetKeys` is never erased, so an address rendered into once is promoted forever -- while a GL target is NEVER seeded from VRAM. PCSX2 handles exactly this with `GSTextureCache::InvalidateVideoMem` + per-Target dirty rects flushed into the target before use (`pcsx2/GS/Renderers/HW/GSTextureCache.cpp`); the fix is target-from-VRAM seeding, NOT disabling promotion (gameplay depends on it). |
| `117-gl-target-from-vram-seeding` (new, cont.330) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h` | **`PS2X_GS_GLR_TGTSEED` (default OFF -- INCOMPLETE, see the header comment): per-page target-from-VRAM seeding, mirroring PCSX2's `GSTextureCache::InvalidateVideoMem` + per-`Target` dirty rects (`pcsx2/GS/Renderers/HW/GSTextureCache.cpp`).** A GL target holds only what GL drew into it, so a guest UPLOAD into its pages is invisible to it (cont.330). Adds `g_gs2PageXferGen[512]` -- a SECOND per-page counter bumped only by host->local uploads, because `g_gs2PageGen` is whole-VRAM bumped by clears/pokes and would mark every page of every target dirty every frame; per target a per-page snapshot of it is the dirty set, gated on one global `g_gs2XferSeq` so the per-draw cost is a single atomic load. Dirty pages are decoded from VRAM in the TARGET's own psm/fbw (page shape from the same `gs2PageGeom` table `gs2RectPages` uses, so forward and inverse mappings cannot drift) and shipped as `GsGlTargetSeed` rects applied by the device after the clear and before any draw in the batch. ★ **BACKEND HALF VERIFIED, DEVICE HALF DOES NOT LAND.** `[gs2:glrseed/t]` reports target=0x180 h=512 fbw=8 psm=0x00, 114 dirty pages, 222,888/233,472 decoded texels non-zero -- real picture data -- yet `PS2X_GS_GLR_DUMPTGT` still dumps 0x180 pure black; the blit in `glrApplyTargetSeeds` is the suspect. ★ **And it is not sufficient anyway:** a one-binary A/B (`=1` vs `=0`, deterministic replay, matched guest flips) leaves the FMV screens equally tiled at flips 950/1050, so a second independent cause produces the visible tiling. ⚠ A local->local blit deliberately does NOT dirty a target: its source is VRAM, which under GL lacks what GL drew, so it would copy emptiness into the target. Gates: selector unset ⇒ `vramHash=3467e2e7823fcb8e` exact (4/4, best 547.65 ms); default-off GL run logs zero `glrseed` lines. |

They proved the OPPOSITE of the recorded suspicion: the blits DO execute and DO land in the right object -- 57,684 rects / 118 M pixels, `read-fbo-status=0x8CD5` (GL_FRAMEBUFFER_COMPLETE), `gl-err=0x0`, and the blit's `fbo=5` is the very FBO the dump reads back as `fbp180_fbo5`. Even a CONSTANT magenta source leaves the target at exactly 0. ⇒ the seed is applied and then **overwritten in the same batch by the guest's own full-screen sprite into `fbp=0x180`** (288 draws / 576 verts, 2 verts per draw, extent [0..512]^2 -- one per frame per buffer), which covers the entire target. Seeding therefore cannot fix this screen, and the open question moves to what that sprite samples and why it renders black. `tgt-missing=0` rules out a failed target-as-source promotion for it. ★ **Latent bug found on the way (NOT fixed, does not fire here):** `texKey = texAsTargetKey ? texAsTargetKey : 1u` remaps target key 0 to 1 while the device looks up `glrTargets.find(g.texKey)` and selects that map on `texIsTarget`, so a draw sampling the framebuffer at `fbp=0x0` as a target source binds NO texture and renders untextured. Needs a repro before changing, since `texEnable` also keys off `texKey != 0`. |
| `119-gl-tgtsrc-dst-and-promotion-refusal` (new, cont.330d) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **Three read-only diagnostics that CLOSED the cont.330/330b/330c thread as a dead end and moved the FMV question from the texture SOURCE to the composite's BLEND.** (1) **`PS2X_GS_GLR_TGTSRC_DST=<fbp>`** -- the `[gs2:tgtsrc]` source table also records every source drawn into that target, **unfiltered by page** (the existing table lists only sources at/above page 0x100); table grown 32 -> 96 rows with an overflow counter, because a silent overflow would read as "that source does not exist". (2) **per-target `untextured=N fbmsk{none/all/other}`** on the DRAW TARGETS row -- shows those fills carry neither FBMSK 0 nor 0xffffffff. (3) **`PS2X_GS_GLR_NOPROMOTE=<page>|all`** -- refuse the target-as-source promotion so the read falls back to the VRAM decode, with a `PROMOTION REFUSED: N reads fell back` counter (inside the `TGTSRC` census) so a null is distinguishable from a knob that never fired. ★★ **Four causes ELIMINATED by matched-flip A/B on ONE binary under `PS2X_VIRTUAL_TIME=1`**, all byte-identical to the control at every flip 900-1350: honouring **FBMSK** (`PS2X_GS_GLR_FBMSK=1`, proven live at `masked-draws=4908` vs 0 -- target `0x180` reads `mean 0.00` in all 45 dumps of BOTH arms), refusing the **promotion** (9,405 refusals), **`VRAMFEED`** alone, and **VRAMFEED + NOPROMOTE together**. ⇒ the `tbp=12288` composite contributes nothing *whatever its texture resolves to*, so the remaining suspect is its **alpha**: `Cs*As + Cd*(1-As)` with `As=0` is invisible for any `Cs` (GL's CT24 decode sets `alpha = TEXA.TA0`; the promoted path takes alpha from TEXA via `srcMode=1`, cont.329i). Also confirmed NOT a size mismatch: `TEX0.tw/th=7/9` = 128x512 = `m_draw.texW/H`, and both renderers use the same UV scale (`texWf = float(texW)` vs `o.s = v.s` over a `texW`-wide decode). ★★ Method: **LOOK AT THE PICTURE** -- three numeric instruments gave clean nulls while the answer ("same image, quarter width, tiled 4x" vs the CPU's full-width EA logo) was one PPM conversion away; and **two gates in series make each other read as a null** -- the promotion blocks VRAM, and VRAM is empty without the feed, so neither knob alone can move the frame. Gates: `functions.csv` untouched; registrations `✓ 403 vs HEAD`; all new code env-gated default OFF, so an unset run is build 877/878 behaviour. | cont.330d
| `120-gl-batch-texture-overwrite-split` (new, cont.330e) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★★ **THE FMV / PRE-RENDERED-SCREEN 4x TILING IS FIXED.** Cause: the device applies ALL of a batch's texture uploads BEFORE any draw in that batch, into ONE GL object per key ("contents replaced in place" -- an epoch in the key was measured at 5866 objects, a leak and 77% of the device thread, so it is not an option).
