| `121-gl-present-and-fragment-probes` (new, cont.330f) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★ **Read-only probes that characterised the GL "black gameplay era" and eliminated four causes.** **`PS2X_GS_GLR_PRESENTLOG=<n>`** (device): every n-th GL present, print which target key the DISPLAY selects, whether it already existed, how many draws ever landed in it, and the draw counts for every key drawn into -- because `ensureGlTarget` CREATES a missing key and returns it CLEARED, so presenting an address GL never drew into is a silently BLACK frame with no GL error. It **falsified** the "DISPLAY points at an undrawn key" hypothesis: the key alternates 0x0/0x80, both existed, both accumulate draws. ⚠ its per-key counters are PER DEVICE INSTANCE (the present numbering repeats and the totals sum to 6,484 of 35,626 groups) -- partial, not a census. **`PS2X_GS_GLR_ASPROBE=0xFFFFFFFF`** now matches ANY draw (textured or not), and the line gained `TEST{ate atst aref afail date zte ztst}`, `ZBUF{zbp psm zmsk}`, `FBMSK`, `tme` and the vertex Z range -- so one run answers "what is this frame made of" instead of ablating one test per 100 s run. | `122-gl-frame-draw-state-census` (new, cont.330g) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_FRAMEHIST=1` (default OFF, read-only): a census of EVERY draw in the probe flip window, keyed by the state that decides whether a fragment survives (`TEST{ate atst aref afail zte ztst}`, `abe`, the ABCD blend selectors, `FBMSK`, `tme`, target-source, `tfx`, `tcc`), with both draw counts AND scissor-clipped geometry AREA.** The area column is the point: a state can be 86.5% of DRAWS while covering 0.7% of the frame, and draw counts alone cannot tell that from "this state is the frame". Six hypotheses are now out on this frontier: DISPLAY key, depth test, stale VRAM, `aref` units, the alpha test (by area), and the frame clear. **OPEN:** why the GL target's prior content is missing at the start of a gameplay frame -- candidates are the double-buffer alternation (one GL target per fbp), the resolve/readback path consuming the target, and whether the scene geometry goes to a different target than the fade composites into. ★★★ Method rule this instrument encodes: **count AREA, not draws, and make an instrument report that it RAN** -- it prints its totals and a table-full warning, so its one empty run was diagnosable in seconds rather than becoming another wrong theory (six instrument defects in this frontier each produced silence that reads exactly like a result). | cont.330g
| `123-gl-authority-phase-a` (new, cont.331) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | **`PS2X_GS_GLR_AUTHORITY` (default OFF): phase A of the `gl-renderer.md` authority model** — a GL target takes its pending dirty VRAM rects when the target is **USED**, applied at the place the existing seed never ran: the **present resolve**. (The old seed emitted only inside the DRAW path via `m_glTargetSeedSeq[drawTargetKey]`, so a target the guest rarely draws into — e.g. the display buffer while a movie rewrites it — was dirtied every frame and seeded 4 times in a whole run.) Seed-only batches (no vertices, only `targetSeeds`) are now legal through `DrawBatchGl`; the device applies them and returns before the draw loop. ★★★ **Two REAL bugs found on the way, both fixed, both fatal to any phase of this model: the PRESENT path runs on a DIFFERENT `GSCpuBackend` instance from the renderer** (the snapshot backend — also why the device's present numbering repeats `#1400, #1600, #1400`), so per-instance state is invisible AND non-persistent there. `m_glTargetH` read empty ⇒ the emitter bailed on `h==0` for **1027 of 1536** calls while reporting a plain "no dirty rects"; `m_glTargetSeedGen` read empty ⇒ every call hit the emitter's "first observation records only a baseline" rule and emitted nothing (**1261 zero-rect calls that read exactly like a null**). Both are now global page-indexed tables (`g_glTargetHByPage`, `g_glSeedSeenByPage`/`g_glSeedSeenInit`), matching the shape of `g_gs2PageXferGen` — the one piece of the existing machinery that never had this failure. ⇒ **design constraint for phases B/C: ALL authority state must be global/page-indexed, never a member.** ⚠ **Phase A does NOT yet work**: after both fixes, `present-seed calls=1024 events=0 rects=0 | declined{seq-unchanged=458 no-dirty-rects=566}` — the emitter runs and finds nothing dirty in the display target's pages, and `gs2BumpXferPage` is genuinely called from the host→local upload path, so **the pages dirtied during the FMV are not the display-buffer pages cont.330h assumed.** ⚠⚠ That diagnosis is **RETRACT-PENDING**: `[gs2:textrace/mut]` stops printing after 200 events, so its 16x18 macroblock uploads are the run's FIRST 200 mutations (early boot), not the flip-1350+ era — the tile pattern is real, its era and pages are not established. Re-take that census at the FMV era, attributing each site to its page range, before building further. ⚠ Known limitation of the per-page watermark: it is keyed by PAGE, correct only while targets own disjoint page ranges (here `0x0`→0..111, `0x80`→128..239); the full model needs (target, page). | cont.331
| `124-gl-authority-phase-a-working` (new, cont.331e/f) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | ★★★★★ **THE FMV RENDERS UNDER THE GL RENDERER.** Phase A of the `gl-renderer.md` authority model, working: a GL target takes its pending dirty VRAM rects when the target is **USED** — including at the **present resolve**, which is where the old draw-path-only seed never fired for a target the guest rarely draws into. Flips 1350+ go from `0.0 / 1` to **92.5 / 45,751** against a CPU reference of 93.5 / 47,911, with the intro era **unchanged** at parity; the screenshot is the Return of the King title screen, correct and unbanded. **`PS2X_GS_GLR_AUTHORITY=1`, default OFF.** ★★★ **Four defects had to be cleared, and every one produced silence that read exactly like "the model does not help":** (1) `m_glTargetH` is a per-instance member and the PRESENT path runs on a different `GSCpuBackend` instance, so the emitter bailed `h==0` on **1027/1536** calls — fixed with the global page-indexed `g_glTargetHByPage`; (2) `m_glTargetSeedGen` likewise, so every call hit the emitter's "first observation records only a baseline" rule and emitted nothing (**1261 zero-rect calls**) — fixed with the global `g_glSeedSeenByPage`/`g_glSeedSeenInit`; (3) the resolve's frame cache returns the PREVIOUS frame whenever `glrDrawsSinceResolve == 0`, and a seed-only batch did not increment it, so the seed was applied and then never read back; (4) **`drawBatchGlOnThread` drops a batch at its TOP** (`if (b.verts.empty() || b.groups.empty())`) **before `glrApplyTargetSeeds` is reached**, so every seed batch was counted as "dropped empty" and discarded — caught only by comparing a backend counter against a device counter for the SAME quantity (`backend rects=264498` vs `[gsgpu:seed] lines=0`). ★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_SEEDVERIFY=1`** (new, read-only) reads the destination back **in the same device job** and prints SRC vs DST: `SRC nonzero=2048 mean=150.34 | DST-AFTER-BLIT nonzero=2048 mean=150.34` (and 259.98 / 655.29 / 630.14, all exact) — **the blit is correct, confirming cont.330c's retraction of it and refuting my own challenge to that retraction.** ★ Final fix: the blit read its source **top-down** while the GL target is bottom-up, so each 32-row page band was placed correctly but was internally upside-down — the FMV presented as horizontal banding with the title text doubled and mirrored. Source rows now inverted (`BlitFramebuffer` src `y1/y0` swapped). ⚠ **Measurement note: mean brightness and distinct-colour count are INVARIANT under a vertical flip** — both read `92.5 / 45,751` before and after, so only the screenshot could verify this fix. **Design constraints for phases B/C:** all authority state must be global/page-indexed, never a member; and every operation that mutates a target without drawing must count toward `glrDrawsSinceResolve`, or the resolve silently serves a stale frame. | cont.331e/f
| `125-gl-flipresolve-noop` (new, cont.331h/i) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **`PS2X_GS_GLR_FLIPRESOLVE=1` (default OFF) — a FALSIFIED hypothesis, kept as a recorded negative.** cont.165 established that this game issues 128 glyph draws per inter-flip window AFTER the flip, onto the buffer being displayed, which is why the CPU path pairs its VRAM snapshot with the PRE-FLIP display registers. The GL present appeared to resolve the LIVE display register instead, which would lose exactly the UI text and nothing else — the user-reported missing "press start button". The flag substitutes the pre-flip fbp captured in `OnDisplayFlip`. **Result: byte-identical.** `FLIPRESOLVE=1` vs `=0` on the same binary at flip 1400 is 100.00% of pixels within 8 LSB, means identical — `frame.fbp` was ALREADY the pre-flip fbp (that is what the flip snapshot pairs), so the substitution changes nothing and **the GL path was resolving the correct buffer all along**. ⚠ A `g_glFlipResolveUsed` counter was added and never printed; the frame comparison is what settled it. Also here: the `[gs2:asprobe]` line prints the full **`vtx-rgba`** instead of the alpha alone, which is what identified the alpha-tested draws over the title screen as `(0,0,0,126)` black-modulate DARKENING passes rather than the text (cont.331h). | cont.331h/i |
| `126-gl-row-page-censuses` (new, cont.331j) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★★ **The censuses that CAUSED the missing "press start button", plus two silent-null fixes.** All read-only, all default OFF. **`PS2X_GS_ROWCENSUS=<y0>:<y1>[:<x0>:<x1>]`** — every primitive whose SCREEN bounding box meets that rectangle, deduped by the full draw state (FRAME, TEX0, blend, TEST, vertex RGBA) with the flip range and the accumulated bbox, noted at the top of **`RasterRunFanOut` AND `DrawPrimitive`, ABOVE the `PS2X_GS_RENDERER` guard**, under a thread-local depth guard so the fan-out's own recursion and its `DrawPrimitive` arms cannot double-count. That placement is the point: every census in this arc so far lived inside `glSubmitRun` (GL only) or on the non-draw mutation sites, so `=cpu` and `=gl` could never be compared on the same population — cont.331e's own rule. **`PS2X_GS_DRAWPAGES=<p0>:<p1>`** — the same census keyed on the draw's DESTINATION VRAM pages instead, for a draw into a shifted `FRAME.FBP` that writes the bytes the display shows lower down while its own y stays small. **`PS2X_GS_PAGEWATCH=<p0>:<p1>`** (+ **`PS2X_GS_PAGEWATCH_Y=<y0>:<y1>`**) — every NON-draw VRAM write intersecting those pages and rows, uncapped, by site, at the single `glTexTraceMutation` funnel that uploads, pokes, blits and clears all pass through; the four call sites' `if (s_glTexTrace \|\| s_mutCensus)` gates now admit it too. **What they found:** the title string is 36,512 glyph sprites at `bbox=[133..386]x[438..470]`, `TEX0{tbp=9237 psm=0x24(PSMT4HL) cbp=12288}`, `TEST{ate=1 atst=GEQUAL aref=1}`, `vtx-rgba=(243,217,76,…)` — and `tbp=9237` is block 21 of page **0x120**, which `[gs2:pagemap]` reports as `w=6136 r=240110`, i.e. WRITTEN BY DRAWS and read as texture. The GL renderer never writes `m_vram`, a paletted source is never promoted (cont.329j) and `9237 & 31 = 21` is not page-aligned, so the decode reads stale VRAM, alpha comes out 0 and the alpha test discards the whole string. Proven by `PS2X_GS_GLR_VRAMFEED=1` on the same binary: text band 17.4 → 45-57, the words correct on screen. ★★★ **Two silent nulls fixed along the way.** (1) The `[gs2:glcensus] STRANDED at resolve` figure read `m_glPendCount`, a MEMBER, from the present path — which runs on `thread_local GSCpuBackend snapshotBackend`, a different object that never accumulates a vertex, so it had reported `events=0/16128 verts=0` since cont.329g. Read from a global (`g_glPendGlobal`), the real figure is **12,492 of 12,800 resolves carry pending geometry, mean 12.6, max 456** — nothing flushes the GL accumulator on a frame boundary. (2) The new flags' parser treated any value beginning with `'0'` as OFF, so `PS2X_GS_PAGEWATCH=0:511` disabled itself; only the exact string `"0"` disables now. ⚠ **And one of my own results was RETRACTED by the instrument:** `GSVertex.x/y` are GS coordinates, not screen pixels (the raster subtracts `XYOFFSET>>4`, here 1792), so the first cut compared `435..465` against `[1792..2304]` and reported a confident `0 hits in 176,249 primitives on BOTH renderers`. The census now converts to screen space exactly as `RasterSprite`/`RasterTriangle` do. | cont.331j |
| `127-gl-frame-boundary-flush` (new, cont.331k) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★★ **THE GUEST FRAME BOUNDARY IS A FLUSH POINT — `PS2X_GS_GLR_FLIPFLUSH`, default ON (`=0` restores the old behaviour for an A/B; it only runs under `PS2X_GS_RENDERER=gl`, so an unset run is unchanged).** The GL path coalesces draws in `m_glPendVerts` and ships a batch only on a **512-vertex threshold**, a target change, or a texture overwrite — **nothing flushed on a frame boundary**. The code already said so (cont.329g: *"nothing flushes on a frame boundary, so anything pending is drawn into the NEXT frame"*), but the census meant to size it read `0/16128` for three cycles because it read the MEMBER `m_glPendCount` from the present-side `thread_local snapshotBackend` (fixed in row 126). Read correctly, the menu/title era strands **898,200 of 1,552,008 vertices — 58%, mean 121 of the ~209 vertices a whole guest frame produces there**. A frame smaller than one batch therefore shipped **nothing**, `glrDrawsSinceResolve` stayed 0, the device's frame cache handed back the previous frame, and the era presented as **exactly black** (whole-frame mean 0.00 from flip 1400 on with the authority seed off). The CPU path has had the matching rule since **cont.165** — `OnDisplayFlip` drains, because this game issues its UI text at the very end of a frame and a snapshot without the drain captures the frame *without its text*. This is that rule for the GL accumulator: the flip marker `OnDisplayFlip` enqueues is already ordered after the pending `DrawRun`, and the worker is the only thread allowed to touch `m_glPendVerts`, so the flush goes there (and on the drained `PS2X_GS_FLIPASYNC=0` path, where the drain has already parked the worker). **Measured:** stranded at resolve `7,385/7,424 resolves, 898,200 verts (mean 121)` → **`2,419/12,800, 17,424 verts (mean 1.4)`**; 5,395 frame-boundary flushes carrying 932,010 vertices; and with the authority seed OFF the user-reported "press start button" goes from a text band of **0.00 to 45-47**, drawn by GL's own geometry on an otherwise black screen — the clean control, with nothing for VRAM to confound. ⚠ **Also isolated here, NOT fixed: the authority seed clobbers GL-drawn content.** With `AUTHORITY=1` the same frame is byte-identical to the pre-flush build, because `glSeedDisplayTargetForPresent` runs at the **present resolve** — after the frame's draws are shipped — and the device applies a batch's target seeds before its draws, so the later seed batch overwrites the frame. Its own `glFlushPending()` guard ("*Any draws still pending for this target must land BEFORE the seed*") has never done anything: it runs on the present-side instance, whose accumulator is always empty. The fix is the authority model's real rule — per page, whoever wrote last owns it: a per-page "a GL draw wrote here" generation beside `g_gs2PageXferGen` (a `Gs2PageMask` OR per draw via the existing `gs2RectPages`), with the seed applying a page's rect only when the VRAM write is NEWER. | cont.331k |
| `128-gl-page-ownership` (new, cont.331l) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **`PS2X_GS_GLR_PAGEOWN`, default OFF — the seed-clobber half of the authority model, built and MEASURED, and NOT a net win on its own.** cont.331k showed `glSeedDisplayTargetForPresent` runs at the present resolve, after the frame's draws are shipped, and the device applies a batch's target seeds before its draws — so the later seed batch overwrites everything the frame drew (title screen with the seed off: text band 46.9; with it on: 17.4, byte-identical to the pre-flush build). This implements the authority model's own rule on the clock the seed already uses: per page, record the value of `g_gs2PageXferGen[pg]` in force when a GL draw last wrote that page, and skip seeding a page whose stamp still equals the page's current generation (GL drew after the newest transfer there, so GL holds the fresher copy). Stamp initialised to a generation no page can hold, so "never drawn" is not "drawn at gen 0"; tracked only when `AUTHORITY` is on, so an unset run pays nothing; reported on the `[gs2:authority]` line as `pages-owned-by-GL-draws`. ★★★ **Measured: it wins the text and loses the background.** `PAGEOWN=1` takes the title screen's text band 17.4 → 44-47 and its whole-frame mean 92.5 → **1.2**; adding the draw-path seed (`TGTSEED=1`, so seeds land before the draws in the same batch) does not recover it. The cause names the next piece: this game **composites by drawing** — one full-screen textured sprite into the display buffer every other frame (`FRAME{fbp=0x0} tme=1 TEX0{tbp=12288 psm=CT24}`, `ABCD=2101`, 1,522 draws over 3,595 flips) — which stamps **all 128 pages** of that buffer as GL-owned, so the uploaded movie picture is never seeded again. Page granularity cannot tell "GL drew real content here" from "GL drew a sprite that sampled an empty target here", and that sprite's own source (texture memory at page **0x180**) is a target starved the same way: the guest draws into it, ownership blocks its seed, it stays empty, the sprite paints black. **The missing half is the SOURCE side** — `tbp=12288` is page-aligned CT24 and 0x180 is a known target key, so it is promoted to a target sample while VRAM holds the real picture; the `tgtFresh` rule (cont.329j) is meant to refuse promotion in exactly that case and is evidently not refusing. Gate check: build 907 with every flag at its default is **pixel-identical to 905** at three title-screen flips (100.00% within 8 LSB). | cont.331l |
| `129-gl-target-freshness-window` (new, cont.331m) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **`gs2TargetGenSum` watched only the target's FIRST 32 PAGES — `PS2X_GS_GLR_TGTFRESH_PAGES`, default 128 (`=32` restores the old window).** That sum is the fingerprint behind `tgtFresh`, the cont.329j rule that refuses to promote a GL target as a texture source once VRAM has been written since GL last drew into it. A 512x512 32-bit render target is **128** pages, so three quarters of it sat outside the window, and this game's movie uploads into texture memory land on pages **384..449** and **446..487** (`[gs2:textrace/mut]`) — largely outside the watched 384..415 — so they were invisible to the rule. Both sides of the comparison (the stamp at the draw and the check at the sample) read the same window, so widening it is consistent. ⚠ **Measured NEUTRAL, and kept on that basis, not as a fix:** build 908 at defaults is **pixel-identical to 905** at three title-screen flips (100.00% within 8 LSB), and it does not unlock the background under `PAGEOWN=1`. What the trace did establish, as ground truth for the next cycle: `PS2X_GS_GLR_TEXTRACE=12288` shows **3,120 of 3,123** samples of the composite sprite's texture are **PROMOTED** (`tgtKey=0x180 known=1 aligned=1 paletted=0 gen{stamped=483 live=483}`) — `tgtFresh` believes the GL target is the freshest copy while the picture is in VRAM and the target never received it — and the 3 that fall through to the VRAM decode hash non-zero (`2852d903b1ab9140`), so VRAM does hold the real image. The next step is therefore **seeding a target when it is used as a SOURCE** (phase A's "seed on use", extended from draw-destination and present to texture-source), or refusing promotion when the target has never received the pages the draw reads. | cont.331m |
| `130-gl-source-side-seed` (new, cont.331n) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **`PS2X_GS_GLR_SRCSEED` (default OFF): seed a target when it is used as a TEXTURE SOURCE** — the third way a target is used, and the one phase A never covered (it covers the draw destination and the present resolve). The seed goes out as its **own batch for the SOURCE key**, submitted ahead of the batch that samples it, with the pending batch flushed first so nothing is reordered past it; instrumented on the `[gs2:authority]` line as `source-seed{calls= events= rects= seq-unchanged= no-rects= no-height=}` so a null is distinguishable from silence. **Also: the page-ownership filter (row 128) is no longer shared with it.** Ownership is a rule about the *display* target at present time — "do not overwrite what this frame just drew"; a source seed asks the opposite — "does VRAM hold bytes this target has never received?". Running both through one page filter made the source seed report `no-rects=3057` of 3,057 calls, because the guest's own full-screen draws into the texture buffer claim every page. Separated with a `thread_local` flag, not a member and not a plain static, because the seed is reached from the worker thread (source) **and** the present thread (display, on the `snapshotBackend` instance). ⚠ **MEASURED, and it does NOT restore the picture:** with `SRCSEED=1` the seed fires 3,056 times for 348,384 rects and the frame is **unchanged** (`whole 92.5`, text band 17.4); with `SRCSEED=1 PAGEOWN=1`, 3,074 / 350,436 rects and the frame is black. ⇒ **feeding the promoted source target changes nothing, so the composite sprite's texture is not where the title background comes from** — it comes from the display buffer's own VRAM (consistent with cont.330h's macroblock uploads into the display buffer). The whole conflict therefore lives on `fbp=0x0`, where in one frame the movie uploads write VRAM and then the guest draws its glyphs over the same pages: page granularity cannot merge those. **The remaining piece is ORDER, not granularity — emit the display seed AT THE POINT THE UPLOAD LANDS, as its own batch on the worker thread, so it precedes that frame's glyph draws in the device stream instead of following them at the present resolve.** That is phase A moved to transfer time and needs no ownership rule at all. Gate check: build 910 at defaults is pixel-identical to 905 at three title flips. | cont.331n |
| `131-gl-seed-gate-and-the-darken-pass` (new, cont.331o) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ⚠ **A DEFAULT I TRIED AND REVERTED, and the measurement that RETRACTS cont.331n's "the remaining piece is ORDER".** The draw-path seed `PS2X_GS_GLR_TGTSEED` was defaulted ON, with a fix so its one-shot is consumed only when the target's own pages really were dirty (it had been stamping `seenSeq = xseq` up front, which burns the one-shot on the first draw after ANY transfer anywhere — and this game's frame order is *darken pass → movie upload → UI glyphs → flip*, so it was spent on the darken pass, before this target's pages were dirty). **The first half worked and the second did not.** The present-resolve seed was starved exactly as intended (`present-seed calls=7424 events=7 rects=56 | declined{seq-unchanged=3146 no-dirty-rects=4271}`) and the UI text came back (band 17.4 → 45.8) — **and the whole frame went black** (whole-frame mean 92.5 → 1.0). `TGTSEED` is back to default OFF; the gate fix is kept (it measured a null: 666,250 vs 666,264 pages over 6,656 events) and build 912 is again pixel-identical to 905. ★★★★ **What the measurements say.** `PS2X_GS_GLR_DUMPTGT` reads the display targets back mid-run at `max=2` while the GL clear colour `(0.04,0.05,0.14)` is `max=36` — two is thirty-six halved **four times** — and frames that did get a seed read back at `max=238`. `PS2X_GS_GLR_ASPROBE=12288` gives the pass exactly: `ALPHA{A=2 B=1 C=0 D=1 FIX=0}` = `Cd*(1-As)`, `tcc=1 tfx=1` (DECAL), source **CT24** so its alpha is `TEXA{ta0=64 ta1=64 aem=1}` ⇒ **As = 64/128 = exactly 0.5**, `FBMSK=00000000`. And the draw-path seed is not the weak link: 6,656 events / 666,250 page-rects, `nonzero-texels=259468/262144`, device `read-fbo-status=0x8CD5 gl-err=0x0`. ⇒ **the present-resolve seed works because it is the LAST writer, not because it is well placed**; any seed landing earlier is correct and is then darkened away. **Ordering is not the limiter — the darken pass over-darkens in GL**, where on the CPU the same pass leaves the title screen at whole-frame 92.7. Also eliminated: `NOPROMOTE=384` (no change — not a promotion problem) and `TEXAEM=1` (helps slightly, whole 1.0 → 1.1, does not fix it; a real fidelity gap, default OFF, and the guest sets `aem=1` on this pass). Next: the seam census proves both renderers are submitted the same primitives, so the divergence is below the seam — count device-issued groups for this state per frame against the seam's primitive count (cont.230's XGKICK bug was a draw applied twice), then compare the blend arithmetic with `PS2X_GS_DRAWCMP` + `_TRACE`. | cont.331o |
| `132-gl-drop-tbp-ablation` (new, cont.331p) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **`PS2X_GS_GLR_DROPTBP=<tbp>` (+ `PS2X_GS_GLR_DROPTBP_KILL=1`), default OFF: count — and optionally SKIP — every GL draw sampling that texture base.** Built to settle cont.331o's two candidates for the title screen's darken pass (`tbp=12288`, `Cd*(1-As)` with `As = TEXA/128 = 0.5`). **Answer 1 — no duplication below the seam:** `seen=3072 killed=3071 (flip=3626)` = **0.85 draws per frame**, matching the seam census exactly (1,522 + 1,522 over 3,595 flips), so the GL path issues this pass no more often than the CPU; the cont.230-style "applied twice" hypothesis is dead. **Answer 2 — ⚠ ablating it does NOT restore the background, so cont.331o's "the darken pass over-darkens in GL" is CORRECTED.** With the pass gone the text band recovers (36.1/25.0/10.8 → 46.6/46.9/47.3, i.e. the pass *was* eating the glyphs' contrast) while the whole-frame mean stays at **1.3** against the CPU's 92.9, and `DUMPTGT` still reads the display targets back at **`max=2`** — the same clear-colour-halved-four-times signature. Something else drives the display targets toward zero under `TGTSEED=1` and it is still unidentified. Where that leaves the arc: the present-resolve seed (`AUTHORITY=1` with `TGTSEED` OFF, the shipped default) is **the only** configuration producing a correct background, and the only known reason is that it writes the target immediately before the resolve reads it; every attempt to supply the same bytes earlier lands correctly (device-verified blits, `nonzero-texels=259468/262144`) and is gone by resolve time — for a reason now demonstrably **neither** overwriting draws (ablated) **nor** duplication (counted) **nor** promotion (eliminated). Next: stop inferring from aggregates and trace ONE target key through one frame in device order — every `clear` / `seed rects` / `group draw` / `resolve` for `fbp=0x0`, with the target's read-back mean after each; five hypotheses have now been formed and killed from aggregates around that sequence without anyone observing it. | cont.331p |
| `133-gl-device-order-trace` (new, cont.331q) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★★ **THE INSTRUMENT THAT CLOSED THE cont.331l-p ARC: trace ONE target in DEVICE ORDER.** Five causes had been formed and killed from AGGREGATES around a sequence nobody had watched. **`PS2X_GS_GLR_ORDER=<fbp\|disp>`** (default OFF) prints every `ALLOC+CLEAR` / `CLEAR` / `SEEDS applied` / group draw / `RESOLVE` that touches one target key, **in the order the device thread executes them**, each followed by that target's own `glReadPixels` mean/max/nonzero; the per-draw line reuses the existing `b.trace` state string, so a draw that moves the mean is named exactly (blend, source, alpha test, `FBMSK`). Every one of those steps runs on the single device thread (`Job::Kind::GlDraw` -> `drawBatchGlOnThread`, `Job::Kind::GlFrame` -> `glFrameOnThread`), so a counter incremented there IS device order -- no inference. `=disp` latches whichever key the first armed resolve selects, so the trace needs no PRESENTLOG pass to aim. Steps on OTHER targets print one line with no read-back, so the interleaving is on the record for free. **`PS2X_GS_GLR_ORDER_AT=<n>`** arms at global resolve n, **`_N=<n>`** (default 6) closes the window after n GLOBAL resolves -- ⚠ NOT resolves of the traced key: the display alternates between two buffers and a key it stops selecting would never close a key-counted window, flooding the log for the rest of the run. **`_MAX`** (default 6000 lines) is the second belt. It found the cause in its FIRST frame. |
| `134-gl-honour-fbmsk` (cont.331q) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_FBMSK` DEFAULT ON (was OFF "while it is being validated") -- ignoring FRAME.FBMSK was not a fidelity detail, it was DESTROYING THE FRAME, and it is the single cause of the whole cont.331l-p arc ("every seed placed before the resolve lands and is gone by resolve time").** This game opens each title-era frame with a full-screen 6-vertex quad carrying `FBMSK=ffffffff zmsk=0 abe=0 tex=n` -- a **depth-only pass** that writes Z and, by the mask, no colour at all. With FBMSK ignored the GL path did `glColorMask(1,1,1,0)` unconditionally and drew that quad's raw colour over everything, taking the target from the clear (mean **19.67**) to **exactly 0.0000, max 0, nonzero 0/262144**. Every seed placed before it therefore died; the present-resolve seed "worked" only because it lands AFTER the wipe; and cont.331p's darken-pass ablation did nothing because the darken pass runs one draw LATER, on an already-zeroed target. Honouring the mask: the same draw leaves the target at **92.4734 unchanged**, and the UI glyphs then composite ON TOP of the background -> resolve **93.4685** against the CPU reference's **93.5**. Flip-matched against `tmp/ti_cpu` over the whole title era, GL+seeds now tracks the reference (92.7-93.6 vs 92.5-93.2) -- **parity, a first for this arc**. Census over the run: **2,345 masked draws, `partial=0`** -- every mask in this game is whole-byte, so the per-channel `glColorMask` mapping is EXACT here, not an approximation. ⚠ **RETRACTS the cont.329h census claim that "FBMSK is 'A' (write RGB, keep alpha) on 100% of this game's draws".** ps2tek FRAME.FBMSK: *"prevent the specified bits in the framebuffer from being updated: `final_color = (final_color & ~mask) \| (frame_color & mask)`"* -- a set bit is PROTECTED, so an all-ones mask writes nothing; PCSX2 `GSRendererHW::IsRTWritten()` computes the same quantity as `written_bits = ~FRAME.FBMSK & GSLocalMemory::m_psm[FRAME.PSM].fmsk`. Also sets `glColorMask(1,1,1,1)` around the seed blit: core GL says a blit is affected only by pixel ownership / scissor / sRGB (and it measures identically here), but a per-draw mask is now live state on this thread and a driver that did apply it would silently drop seed channels. `=0` restores the old behaviour; it only reads under `PS2X_GS_RENDERER=gl`, so a CPU-renderer run is unaffected either way. |
| `135-gl-order-trace-flip-arm` (cont.331q) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★ **Aim the device-order trace at a GUEST FLIP.** `gs2CurrentFlip()` exposes the guest display-flip counter (a file-static in the backend) to the GL device thread, every `[gsgpu:order]` line now carries `flip=`, and **`PS2X_GS_GLR_ORDER_FLIP=<n>`** arms the trace at that flip instead of at a device resolve. Every era in this arc is named by a flip, because the present captures are flip-indexed (presentation is host-paced, so only the flip is a guest-driven instant) — while the device's `glrFrames` is a resolve count whose ratio to flips is **not fixed**: it moves with frame-cache hits and with how much the trace itself slows the device thread. Aiming at "the regression at flip 900" through `glrFrames` was guesswork; this makes it exact (`armed at guest flip 900 (>= 900), device frame 1878`). The window still closes on a fixed count of GLOBAL resolves, latched at the arm point. |
| `136-gl-seed-defaults-on` (cont.331q) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_TGTSEED` and `PS2X_GS_GLR_AUTHORITY` DEFAULT ON — the GL renderer now renders the title screen correctly at pure defaults.** A seed path is the ONLY thing that puts the guest's uploaded background into a GL frame (a GL target holds only what GL drew; the backdrop and the FMV arrive as VRAM uploads), so without one the whole frame measures **~19.7 = the clear colour plus glyphs, no background at all**, against the CPU reference's ~93. Neither flag could be measured honestly before row 134: the ignored `FBMSK` zeroed the target every frame, so every seed died wherever it was placed. ⚠ **This RETRACTS the cont.331o note that stood on `s_glTgtSeed`** ("MEASURED A REGRESSION … darkened away by the frame's own `Cd*(1-As)` pass") — the regression was the FBMSK wipe, one draw BEFORE the darken pass. Validated at pure defaults, flip-matched against `tmp/ti_cpu` across the whole title era: **92.49-93.57 vs the reference's 92.49-93.23**, and the presented frame is visually indistinguishable (backdrop, logo and "press start button" all composited). Over **270 densely captured flips** (`PS2X_GS_PRESENT_SAVE_BYFLIP=10`) seeds ON beat OFF by a mean of **+37.6** whole-frame, with only **2 of 270** samples favouring OFF — both single-sample ERA-BOUNDARY transients (flip 950 is the EA intro ending one 10-flip sample early; 940 ties and 960 is black in both). ⚠ **Method note that this row exists to record:** a single-flip read of that boundary looked like a real regression (flip 900, 34.08 vs 50.15) and was **not** — the EA intro is a host-paced FMV, so a guest flip index does not pin the movie frame, and a re-measure put seeds ON *ahead* at the same flip (60.24 vs 53.23). Sample an era, never a flip. ⚠ **Known, unfixed:** with both on, the draw-path seed and the present-resolve seed consume the same *global* per-page watermark `g_glSeedSeenByPage`, so whichever runs first burns it and the present seed fires only ~5-8 times a run. Harmless in the measured configuration; PCSX2 keeps dirty rects per target (`GSTextureCache::Target::m_dirty`, consumed by `Target::Update()`), and making the watermark per (target, page) is the next cleanup. |
| `137-gs-renderer-agnostic-clock` (new, cont.331s) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★ **THE TWO ARMS OF THIS ARC'S CENTRAL A/B WERE NOT INSTRUMENTED THE SAME WAY: a `PS2X_GS_RENDERER=gl` run carried NO WALL CLOCK AT ALL.** `[gsgpu:thruput]` — the line every speed tool reads (`tools/harness/vtwin.py` = wall seconds per fixed guest-frame window, `fpswin.py` = fps per load window) — is emitted from `g_perfCpuPrims`, the CPU rasterizer's primitive counter, and the GL seam skips the pixel loop that increments it. So a GL run prints **zero** thruput lines (verified: build 918's `tmp/final918.txt`, 0 occurrences vs the CPU reference's), `vtwin.py` returns nothing for that arm, and `vtab.sh` — the matched-scene A/B harness — could not have measured "is the GL renderer faster" even once. **`PS2X_GS_CLOCK_FLIPS` (default 32, `0` = off, and only when `PS2X_GS_THRUPUT` is on)** emits `[gsgpu:clock] t=<s> flips=<n> presents=<n> renderer=<cpu\|gl>` from `OnDisplayFlip()` — the one cadence **both** renderers turn (the same counter cont.331j's ROWCENSUS was moved onto, for the same reason) — so both arms carry an identical clock, and `vtwin.py`/`fpswin.py` accept it as a wall-clock source. It also makes a flip-indexed PPM capture convertible to the guest vsync (flip → wall → `[hero:dt]` vsync), which matters because the pre-hero era costs a different number of flips per arm: "flip N in both runs" was **~108 guest frames apart** between a cpu and a gl run, which is a different pose in a fight. Pure instrumentation — no hardware modelling, no PCSX2 faithfulness question; idle cost when `PS2X_GS_THRUPUT` is unset is one already-loaded bool test per flip. Gate (build `bld-eur-glclock-919`): the line appears in both a `=cpu` and a `=gl` run at a 32-flip cadence, and the first measurement it enabled is the one this arc was opened for — **frame-matched, deterministic replay, same binary: GL = 1.94x the CPU rasterizer** (see the game repo's `docs/progress.md` cont.331s). |
| `138-gl-decode-census-and-honest-counters` (new, cont.332) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **The GL texture path is the whole remaining frame-time tail (cont.332: 10.3% of live frames miss the game's 2-vblank quantum, 90% of that excess is in frames with >=20 texture decodes, and an upload-free GL frame costs 23.2 ms = cont.328's raster-free EE floor), so this measures WHY it re-decodes -- and fixes two instruments that were lying about it.** **`PS2X_GS_GLR_DECVERIFY`** (default OFF; it hashes ~90 KB per decode, diagnostic only) hashes the DECODED RGBA of each decode and compares it with what that cache key produced last time, printing `decverify{first= same= diff=}`: `same` is a re-decode whose texels did not move, i.e. one the page-level invalidation got wrong. `GlTexEntry::outHash` carries it. **The measurement (build `bld-eur-decverify-920`, Hel01 replay): `first=171 same=9,204 diff=31,073` -- only 22.9% of re-decodes are false**, so the storm is mostly REAL content change and a finer invalidation cannot buy back more than ~1/4 of a 5.3%-of-wall tail. **Instrument fix 1:** `g_glTexNew`/`g_glTexRegen` were incremented OUTSIDE the `if (needDecode)` branch whose two outcomes they name, so `[gs2:glrtex] decodes=` was the count of TEXTURED DRAWS -- a run printed `decodes=103,571,456 new=202`, which reads as a cache that never hits when it was serving **99.95%** of draws. They now count decodes; draws are printed as `textured-draws=`. **Instrument fix 2:** `[gs2:glrtexrej]` was an UNGATED `fprintf(stderr)` in the worker's hot path, one write per decode (58k in a 155 s run). Now gated on `PS2X_GS_THRUPUT`, as cont.331s gated `[gsgpu:clock]` -- every harness run still gets the per-decode line that `tools/harness/tailcen.py` counts per frame, a plain play run pays nothing. No hardware modelling is touched (counters, one hash, one print gate), so there is no PCSX2 faithfulness question; the invalidation model the measurement is aimed at is PCSX2's `GSTextureCache::Source::m_hash` + `Target::m_dirty` (hash per source, dirty rects per target) rather than our whole-8-KB-page hash -- build `bld-eur-decverify-920` |
| `139-gl-hires-present` (new, cont.332c) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | ★★★★★ **PRESENT AT THE INTERNAL RESOLUTION -- gl-renderer.md phase 5's actual payoff.** The GL scene is rendered at `display x PS2X_GS_SCALE` and then blitted DOWN to display size (phase 1's choice, which keeps the readback flat in the scale), so `PS2X_GS_SCALE=4` bought supersampled EDGES inside a 512x511 image and never a sharper one. **`PS2X_GS_PRESENT_HIRES`** (default OFF) reads the scene target at its own size into a second latch (`GsGpuPresentDevice::TakeHiresPresentFrame`, reached from the presenter through the new `ps2xGsTakeHiresPresentFrame`), which the raylib presenter uploads to a lazily-created texture of matching size and draws into the same aspect-fit rect. The 640-stride path is untouched, so the present cache, field presentation and `PS2X_GS_PRESENT_SAVE` keep their buffer. **`PS2X_WINDOW_SCALE`** (default 1) opens the window at N x 640x448 -- without it nothing can show a higher-resolution present, since the window is exactly one guest pixel per host pixel. **`PS2X_WINDOW_FILTER`** (default OFF) puts the window blit on `TEXTURE_FILTER_BILINEAR` instead of raylib's POINT. **`PS2X_GS_PRESENT_HIRES_SAVE=<dir>`** (+`_EVERY`, default 256) dumps BOTH images of the SAME present (the hi-res read and the display-sized resolve) -- the only honest way to ask 'is it sharper', after a cross-run screenshot A/B proved invalid (two live arms land on different beats: best cross-arm RMSE 0.072). **Measured (build `bld-eur-hirespair-922`, Hel01, `PS2X_GS_SCALE=2` -> 1024x1022):** same-present pair RMSE 0.0174 and the ladder/armour crops are visibly sharper (the display path is 2x2-blocky at the same zoom); device readback+flip **1.61 ms/frame**; deterministic replay **23.6 -> 27.8 ms per game frame (0.83x)**, every window agreeing 0.78-0.84; and **LIVE it is free -- 23.68 -> 23.56 fps** (2-vblank cadence 89.7% -> 87.7%), because cont.332 measured ~14 ms of headroom inside the game's 40 ms quantum. That is the trade the arc was opened for: headroom converted into resolution. ⚠ Still uncorrected (phase 6): the presented pixel aspect. The guest display is 512x511 and the window fits it ~square, where a PAL PS2 output belongs in 4:3 -- read `DISPLAY`'s MAGH/MAGV and SMODE2 rather than hardcoding it (PCSX2 `GSState::GetDisplayRect`) -- build `bld-eur-hirespair-922` |
| `140-present-pixel-aspect` (new, cont.332d) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | ★★★★★ **THE PRESENTED PIXEL ASPECT WAS WRONG, AND IT WAS A BIGGER VISUAL ERROR THAN THE RESOLUTION.** The window fitted the frame by its own width:height, so this game's 512x511 display was drawn ~square and every character was ~33% too narrow. The CRTC magnifies the source across a FIXED raster, so a 512-wide display belongs in the same 4:3 rect a 640-wide one does. **`ps2xGsPresentAspect()`** computes it in `PresentFromLocalMemory` from the DISPLAY registers and **`PS2X_WINDOW_ASPECT`** (default **ON**, `=0` restores the old pixel-square fit) applies it in the raylib draw rect. ⚠ **MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED, AND THE FIRST FORMULA WAS WRONG:** this game's DISPLAY reads `raw=0x001ff1ff0004828c` = DX=652 DY=72 **MAGH=0 MAGV=0 DW+1=512 DH+1=512**, so a raster fraction computed from DW alone says the display covers 0.2 of a 2560-VCK line and yields an aspect of **0.2667**. Hardware/libgraph would write `DW = w*(MAGH+1)-1` (MAGH=4 here, DW+1=2560) -- and our own GS HLE is a suspect: `Kernel/Stubs/GS.cpp` builds DISPLAY at three sites with `makeDisplay(dx, dy, 0, 0, w-1, h-1)`, i.e. MAGH=0 and DW=w-1, which is exactly the shape observed. So the aspect uses the register fraction **only when the CRTC is actually magnifying** (`MAGH>0 && DW+1 >= 2*srcW`) and otherwise falls back to the SD raster's own 4:3 -- which is what PCSX2 presents an SD mode at (`GSState.cpp` VideoModeOffsets/VideoModeDividers place the display INSIDE that shape; PAL = 640x256 per field over 2560x288, and its Aspect Ratio setting supplies 4:3). The always-on, change-triggered `[gs:disp]` line prints the raw register, the decoded fields, which branch was taken and the resulting aspect, so the next reader does not have to re-derive it. **Open, recorded not fixed:** make `makeDisplay` write the hardware convention (MAGH such that `DW+1 ~= 2560`) so the register-derived path becomes live -- build `bld-eur-aspect43-924` |
| `141-window-aspect-explicit` (new, cont.332f) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | ★★ **`PS2X_WINDOW_ASPECT` takes an explicit ratio**, so the presented frame can be fitted to something other than the shape the DISPLAY registers imply: `0` = off (pixel-square), unset/`1` = derive it (cont.332d, the default), `>1.1` = that aspect, e.g. **1.7778** for the widescreen hack. It is the presentation half of cont.332f: the game-side `LOTR_WIDESCREEN` hook widens the horizontal FOV (the PCSX2 ElHecht patch's 1.0 -> 0.75 at `0x14b13c`, applied to `[a0+0x10]` at the entry to `0x145960` instead of by patching the instruction), and this fits the result into a 16:9 rect. ⚠ Without the FOV half this flag is a STRETCH, not widescreen -- build `bld-eur-widescreen-928` |
| `142-flat-texture-detector` (new, cont.332g) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_FLATTEX` (default ON, `=0` disables): turn "a texture renders blank" into a COUNTED event.** A user-reported missing face/chest texture under `PS2X_GS_RENDERER=gl` (never under `=cpu`) could not be chased by looking at frames, so this counts decodes whose output is UNIFORM (strided sample, every 64th texel), how many distinct keys, and -- the question that matters -- how many of those keys are ever re-decoded into something non-flat (`GlTexEntry::flatLast`). **Found: `flat{decodes=2368 keys=21 recovered=0}`** -- 21 paletted (PSMT8/PSMT4) textures decode to ONE colour at **alpha 0** and NEVER recover, clustered at tbp 12291-12709 with CLUTs at cbp 12718-12737. That is a real defect nobody knew about. ⚠ **It is NOT the reported symptom**: a `PS2X_GS_ROWCENSUS` of the character region names its draws as `tbp=8192 psm=0x24 (T4HL)`, `tbp=12943 psm=0x14`, `tbp=12288 psm=0x01` -- none of them in the 21. The detector is default ON because it is a live-correctness census costing a strided sample per decode; logging is capped at 32 lines. ⚠ If a glitchy run reports ZERO flat decodes, the textures are innocent and the fault is on the DRAW side (collapsed UVs sampling one texel look identical on screen) -- build `bld-eur-flattex-929` |
| `143-gl-mip-selection-default` (new, cont.332h) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★ **ONE ENV VAR, TWO OPPOSITE DEFAULTS.** `PS2X_GS_GLR_MIPS` is read in two places: the backend half (`s_glMips`, decode + upload the whole TEX1.MXL chain) defaulted **ON**, and the device half (`glrMips`, select the mipmap MIN filter) defaulted **OFF** — so at pure defaults the runtime paid to decode and upload four levels per texture and then sampled level 0 for every draw, while the CPU reference samples the level the GS selects on 74.8% of this game's textured draws. It also made an ablation of the flag **half-void**: `=0` only disabled the half that was already inert, so mipmapping could not be A/B'd at all. The device half now defaults ON and both comments say so. ⚠ **The fidelity effect is WITHIN NOISE — this is a coherence + wasted-work fix, not a measured win.** A first reading (one run per config, +0.052 on `a`) was RETRACTED by repeats: `tools/harness/tonefit.py` (`GL = a*CPU + b`, same 7 gameplay flips) gives **old default a = 0.623, 0.613** and **mips selected a = 0.649, 0.620, 0.613** — overlapping. ⚠ The reason matters: under `PS2X_VIRTUAL_TIME=1` the CPU arm is **byte-identical** run to run (a = 1.000, b = −0.0 against another CPU run) but the **GL arm is not**, with a spread of ~0.04 on `a`; every GL measurement needs ≥3 runs. PCSX2 reference: `GSRendererHW` drives mipmapping from TEX1 (MXL/MMIN) the same way and enables it by default on the HW renderer. |
| `144-pixwatch` (new, cont.332h) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_PIXWATCH=<x>:<y>` (default OFF, read-only) — WHO PAINTS THIS PIXEL.** Every other route to "which draw produced these pixels" failed on the GL character defect: the row census reports bbox INTERSECTION (killing all 24.9 M draws that sampled its top texture moved the pixels by 0.02), `PS2X_GS_DRAWCMP`'s snapshots do not align between the two renderers (they split runs differently, so snapshot k is not the same primitives), and `PS2X_GS_GLR_ORDER` cannot be aimed at a gameplay flip. This logs every write that survives every test at one screen pixel, with the full TEX0, blend selectors, TEST bits, incoming source colour and destination — generalising the hardcoded (128,428) font probe that was already there. Capped by `_N` (default 64) and gated by `PS2X_GS_PROBEFLIP`. Armed, it routes through the new `g_gs2PerPixelDiag` flag, which is what every fast-path gate now tests (the four-wide and sprite loops bypass `writePixelT` entirely, so they must be off — exactly as `PS2X_GS_FONTTRACE` already did). First result: the cloak pixel the GL renderer gets wrong is built by **~13 distinct layered alpha-blended draws** (`prim=4 tme=1 psm=0x14` T4 with CLUTs at cbp 14215/14217/14218/14364, `tfx=0 tcc=1`, `ABCD=0101`), not by one material — so the defect is in how a STACK composites, not in a single texture. |
| `145-gl-pixel-watch` (new, cont.332i) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLPIXWATCH=<x>:<y>` (default OFF, read-only) — the GL-side counterpart of row 144's `PS2X_GS_PIXWATCH`.** Row 144 answered "which draws paint this pixel" on the CPU rasterizer, but it lives in that rasterizer's pixel-write path, which a `PS2X_GS_RENDERER=gl` run never enters — so the same question could not be asked of the arm under investigation, and the whole cont.332g/h arc had been comparing aggregates instead. This reads the watched pixel back with a 1-pixel `glReadPixels` once per batch (after the clear and the VRAM seeds) and again after every group, and prints a line only when the pixel **moved**, named by the same per-draw state string the device-order trace (row 133) builds. Aimed by the SAME `PS2X_GS_PROBEFLIP`/`_PROBEFLIPW` window as the CPU watch, so one flip number arms both arms; capped by `PS2X_GS_GLPIXWATCH_N` (default 256). Coordinates are the GS/display ones the CPU watch prints (y from the TOP); the row flips exactly as the scissor's does, and a batch whose target does not contain the pixel is reported as "outside", never as "unchanged". ⚠ **The `flip=` on a line is the flip counter as the DEVICE THREAD reads it**, and the device runs asynchronously behind the EE — two arms can be a whole buffer apart, so watch lines must NOT be aligned across arms by flip. Use it for the per-draw sequence; use flip-indexed present captures for anything frame-matched. First result: it found row 146 in one run.
| `146-gl-afail-pass-then-fail` (new, cont.332i) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★★★ **THE `AFAIL=FB_ONLY` TWO-PASS SPLIT IS ONLY VALID WHEN `independent_z` HOLDS, AND WE APPLIED IT ALWAYS — that is the user-reported flat/dark character under the GL renderer.** `PS2X_GS_GLR_AFAIL`, default **1** (PCSX2's selector; `=0` restores the pre-933 behaviour for an A/B, `=2` forces pass/fail, `=3` forces a plain discard). AFAIL=FB_ONLY means a fragment failing the alpha test still writes COLOUR but not Z. We emulated it as PCSX2's `SIMPLE_FB_ONLY`: pass 1 draws every fragment with **depth writes OFF**, pass 2 re-draws the passers depth-only. ⚠ `GSRendererHW.cpp::EmulateATST` picks that split only when `independent_z = (ZTST==GEQUAL && m_vt.m_eq.z) \|\| ZTST==ALWAYS \|\| !zwe \|\| m_prim_overlap==PRIM_OVERLAP_NO`, i.e. only when the Z result cannot depend on overlapping Z writes INSIDE the draw; otherwise it falls back to `PASS_THEN_FAIL`. Our own code comment had flagged the risk ("Z varies and primitives overlap") — what was missing was that the draw it lands on is **the one that paints the character**: one group, 1,524 vertices, 128x128 PSMT8, `ate=1 atst=GEQUAL aref=128 afail=FB_ONLY ztst=GEQUAL zmsk=0`. With pass 1 at `DepthMask(0)` the model's own 508 triangles never occluded each other and the pixel kept whichever was submitted LAST. The fix is PCSX2's own fallback: pass 1 = normal alpha test, failures discarded, colour **and Z** written; pass 2 = the test INVERTED (new shader `uAfailPass == 3`), only failures survive, colour written, `zwe=false` (`EmulateAlphaTestSecondPass`, AFAIL_FB_ONLY case). **Measured** (build 933, same binary, flag off vs on, 3 runs each, deterministic replay, 8 gameplay flips vs the CPU reference): watched pixel's character layer (24,25,24)→**(33,28,20)/(45,39,30)/(53,47,37)** off versus →**(115,104,81)/(125,116,94)/(104,94,79)** on, against a CPU reference of (134,118,93); whole-frame tone fit `a` **[0.622,0.649] → [0.699,0.779]** and the character column's arm/reference ratio **[0.706,0.731] → [0.913,0.926]**, both NON-OVERLAPPING; the menu/title era is unchanged at `a=1.002 b=0.6` on both arms. The always-on `[gsgpu:glrafail]` counter names which split each run took (26,626 pass-then-fail groups with the fix, 60,014 colour-then-Z without). ⚠ It rides the resolve, so it is gated to every 512th frame and its FIRST line necessarily reads `{0,0}` — read the LAST line.
| `147-gl-afail-one-pass` (new, cont.332j) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ⚠ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_AFAIL=4` — BUILT, MEASURED and REJECTED, kept as a recorded negative; and the measurement that shows NO two-pass AFAIL split can be correct.** Row 146's `PASS_THEN_FAIL` fixed the gameplay character but REGRESSED the character-select screen: there the cloak texels FAIL the alpha test, `AFAIL=FB_ONLY` means hardware still writes their colour, and pass 1 (passers only) writes a Z that then depth-rejects them in pass 2. A 4-config discriminator on the unchanged binary settles it at one pixel (CPU truth (23,14,12)): `AFAIL=0` **(33,20,16) correct**, default (146,108,86) wrong, `=3` wrong, `NODEPTH=1` correct. Mode 4 draws the group ONCE with no discard and colour+depth, to preserve submission ORDER — which is what both splits destroy. **Null: byte-identical to mode 1 on that pixel**, and that is the finding: *any* mode writing depth during the colour pass loses the cloak, so on that draw the hardware writes no depth at all. Measured on both screens (2 runs/mode, 8 gameplay flips vs the CPU reference): mode 0 = character select ✓ / gameplay column 0.723,0.720; modes 1 and 4 = character select ✗ / column 0.917,0.916 and 0.926,0.918. Each is right exactly where the other is wrong, because GL cannot mask the depth write PER FRAGMENT. PCSX2 agrees (`PASS_THEN_FAIL` is its fallback, "may be inaccurate"; the accurate path is FEEDBACK). Default unchanged at mode 1 by the user's call; the accurate design (ordered fragment-shader interlock for the depth test + a conditional depth write, colour left on the hardware blender — NOT cont.233's full software ROP) is recorded in the game repo's progress.md cont.332j. `[gsgpu:glrafail]` now also reports `one-pass=`. |
| `148-gl-afail-keepz` (new, cont.332j) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ⚠ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_AFAIL=5` — BUILT, MEASURED, REFUTED; plus a real GLSL bug it exposed, and the depth attachment is now a TEXTURE.** The idea: express `AFAIL=FB_ONLY` as the hardware states it — depth written ONLY by fragments that pass the alpha test — in ONE pass, so submission order survives (both two-pass splits destroy it, rows 146/147). A failing fragment writes back the depth already at its pixel, read from a copy of the target's depth taken before the group (`snapshotDepth`), so the store is a no-op. This required the target's depth to become a `GL_DEPTH_COMPONENT24` **texture** instead of a renderbuffer (a renderbuffer can be neither sampled nor blitted into something sampleable) — kept, it is harmless and needed by any future attempt. **REFUTED: byte-identical to mode 1 on both screens** (the user's Gandalf character select, 66 matched flips: mad 3.90 and the same face/robe pixels; gameplay: the same md5 at flip 7000). ★ **The reason is structural: GL performs the depth TEST with `gl_FragDepth` when the shader writes it**, so a failing fragment that writes back the old depth also TESTS with the old depth and is rejected against whatever a passer wrote earlier in the same draw — mode 1's exact failure. Testing with one value while writing another is not expressible in fixed-function GL, which is why PCSX2's accurate path is a SOFTWARE Z test (FEEDBACK). ⚠⚠ **And the first cut of this shipped a real bug worth remembering: `gl_FragDepth` was assigned only inside the `uAfailKeepZ` branch. GLSL leaves it UNDEFINED on every path that does not write it once a shader assigns it anywhere**, so build 936 gave garbage depth to EVERY draw — gameplay tone fit `a` 0.75 → 0.40 and column 0.92 → 0.63 on BOTH modes. It was caught only by the gameplay metric; a per-pixel check on one menu screen had 'cleared' it. Fixed in 937 by assigning it unconditionally. |
| `149-gl-afail-software-z` (new, cont.332l) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ⚠ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_AFAIL=6` — the ACCURATE `AFAIL=FB_ONLY` path (PCSX2's FEEDBACK shape), BUILT and MEASURED as a NULL on every draw tested; plus `=7`, its interlock-only ablation, and `PS2X_GS_GLR_DEBUGOUT`.** cont.332j concluded that no two-pass split can be right, because the hardware decides PER FRAGMENT and IN SUBMISSION ORDER whether Z is written. This does it exactly: the target's depth is copied into an **R32F image** (scissored to the group's own rect, a full-screen triangle through `glrDepthSeedProgram`), the fragment shader reads and updates it inside `layout(pixel_interlock_ordered)` (`GL_ARB_fragment_shader_interlock`, present on Mesa 21.2.6 / Intel Xe), GL's own test is `ALWAYS`, and `gl_FragDepth` carries the shader's decision back so the real depth buffer stays authoritative for later draws. Colour stays on the hardware blender — depth-only, not cont.233's software ROP. ⚠⚠ **The interlock calls may not appear in ANY control flow** (Mesa enforces the ARB spec literally; build 938's first cut put them under `if (uAfailSw)` and the program failed to compile, which SILENTLY DISABLED the whole GL renderer while the run still logged `renderer=gl` at 50 fps — the tell was `[gsgpu:glrafail]` and the pixel watch printing nothing). They now sit at `main()`'s top level with the work conditional, so the interlock is a property of the program — hence `=7`, the same program with the software Z never armed, which prices the interlock separately from the Z. **Result: mode 6 is byte-identical to mode 1 at the Isengard face/robe/cloak and at the save-boot cloak.** That is not a defect: `PASS_THEN_FAIL` is EXACT for a draw whose fragments all pass or all fail the alpha test, and only diverges on a MIXED draw; both character draws are homogeneous. Kept default OFF as the correct machinery for when a mixed draw does appear. `PS2X_GS_GLR_DEBUGOUT` (1 = the sampled texel through the same `textureLod` the shaded path uses, 2/4 = the texture coordinates with range flags in blue, 3 = the alpha the test sees, 5/6 = two FIXED texels of whatever object is bound) makes `PS2X_GS_GLPIXWATCH` report what the shader SAMPLED instead of the composite — that is what found row 150. |
| `150-gl-texkey-injective` (new, cont.332l) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★★★ **THE GL TEXTURE CACHE KEY COLLIDED, AND THAT IS THE USER-REPORTED BROKEN CHARACTER.** The key was `(tbp << 40) ^ (tbw << 32) ^ (psm << 26) ^ (tw << 16) ^ th ^ (cbp << 44)` — and `cbp << 44` OVERLAPS `tbp << 40`, so `(tbp, cbp)` and `(tbp ^ (d << 4), cbp ^ d)` land on the same entry, one GL object, last upload wins. `PS2X_GS_GLR_KEYCENSUS=1` (new, read-only, default OFF) found **14 colliding keys in one menu run**, including **four** distinct 128x128 PSMT8 character textures on the key our draw used (`tbp` 12291/12355/12419/12483 with `cbp` 12579/12583/12587/12591). The draw's own fixed-texel probe did not match its own decode — texel (40,41) read (170,164,156) where its decode holds (62,36,24) — which is why the character rendered pale and hue-drained, and why no AFAIL mode could fix it. Now packed into disjoint ranges: `tbp 14 | cbp 14 | tbw 6 | psm 6 | tw 11 | th 11`. PCSX2 never had this hazard: `GSTextureCache::HashCacheKey` (GS/Renderers/HW/GSTextureCache.h) stores the whole `GIFRegTEX0`+`GIFRegTEXA` and compares with `memcmp`, so two different TEX0s can never share an entry. **Measured, one binary, `PS2X_GS_GLR_TEXKEY=0` restoring the old key:** Isengard character select whole-frame mad **3.92 → 3.13**, character rect ratio **0.914 → 1.016** / mad **14.73 → 2.00**, and the three watched pixels go from (183,182,182)/(33,27,20)/(18,18,17) to **(111,85,68)/(159,151,141)/(97,90,84)** against a CPU truth of (110,84,67)/(159,150,140)/(95,89,84). Gameplay is unchanged (tone fit a=0.700 vs the recorded 0.699/0.781; column 0.916 vs 0.917/0.916) and FASTER, because colliding entries invalidated each other forever: on the GAME-frame metric (`tailcen.py`, two pairs, non-overlapping) game fps **22.33/22.77 → 23.87/23.82**, ms/frame **44.8/43.9 → 41.9/42.0**, tail frames **29.5%/26.4% → 17.1%/16.1%**, tail excess **11.5%/10.7% → 6.9%/6.3%** of wall, uploads/frame **34.9 → 2.7**. ★ That closes cont.332's open tail: its "90% of the excess is in frames with >= 20 uploads" is now **1%**, so the upload cliff it characterised WAS this collision, not an inherent cost of the GL texture path. The `(key >> 40)` decode in the device's pixel-watch print moved to `key & 0x3FFF` with it. |
| `151-pixwatch-depth-clamp` (new, cont.332m) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★ **The pixel watches now report DEPTH and the WRAP RULE, which is what killed four hypotheses about the user-reported character speckle.** `PS2X_GS_PIXWATCH` adds `z=`/`storedZ=` per write and `CLAMP{wms wmt minu maxu minv maxv}`; `PS2X_GS_GLR_DEBUGOUT=7` returns the GL fragment's depth as 24 bits across rgb. First result: at a CPU-only dot the CPU's two fragments are `z=494085` and `z=530662` — 36,577 apart, NOT a near-tie — and GL's surviving fragment reads back exactly `530662`, so both renderers keep the SAME fragment with bit-identical depth and differ only in its SHADING. The CLAMP readout then killed the wrap hypothesis too (`wms=0 wmt=0`). ⚠ Instrument limit, recorded because it cost real time: `GLPIXWATCH` prints the last fragment that CHANGED the pixel, so different `DEBUGOUT` values can report DIFFERENT fragments for the same pixel — texel/ST/depth from three runs are not guaranteed to describe one fragment. |
| `152-wrap-census` (new, cont.332m) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★ **`PS2X_GS_WRAPCENSUS=1` (default OFF, read-only): how many draws use each PS2 CLAMP mode pair.** The GL path collapses the GS's four modes (REPEAT / CLAMP / REGION_CLAMP / REGION_REPEAT) to GL's two in `gs_cpu_backend.cpp:7629`, and REGION_REPEAT (`u = (u & MINU) | MAXU`) is not expressible as a GL wrap mode at all — PCSX2 implements it in the shader. That made the collapse a prime suspect for a texel-level difference. **Measured: this game uses only REPEAT/REPEAT (97%) and CLAMP/CLAMP (3%)**, so the collapse is EXACT here and the suspect is eliminated — a negative result worth keeping, because it also means the GL renderer needs no region-wrap work for this title. |
| `153-gl-default-quadtex-off` (new, cont.332n) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★★ **THE GL RENDERER IS NOW THE DEFAULT, AND `PS2X_GS_QUADTEX` IS DEFAULT OFF BECAUSE IT IS MEASURED WRONG.** (1) `PS2X_GS_RENDERER` defaults to `gl`; `=cpu` restores the CPU rasterizer, which stays the fallback AND the differential oracle for the native-lift arc and is never retired. The GL arm is faster (game fps 23.5-23.9 vs 16.1 on the same replay; it holds the game's 2-vblank cadence on 90% of frames against 35%) and, since cont.332l's texture-key fix, more correct on characters. (2) The user reported isolated dark dots on the character under the CPU renderer. One-flag ablations on the character-select replay (26 matched frames, dots/frame in the character box): **CPU default 14.5 | TEXCACHE=0 14.5 | FASTTEX=0 14.5 | QUADTEX=0 4.4 | GL 4.4** — the four-wide texture-coordinate setup (cont.271) fetches texels that are not there: at one dot it yields ~(73,64,43) while the decoded texture's entire 9x8 neighbourhood around its OWN sample point spans 109..221. The scalar path and the GL renderer agree; this path does not. ⚠ cont.271 shipped it on a bit-exactness gate against ONE bench capture (Helm's Deep); this menu scene with a noisy paletted character texture is where it shows — a bit-exact hash over one scene is not a correctness proof. The recorded bench hash `aa9cd72b600021ce` moves with this flip: that hash was of the defect. `=1` restores the path for debugging its lane arithmetic (perspective divide / scale / -0.5 / floor / weights / wrap, ~line 11946); its -6.11% instruction win is forfeit until then. **Validated on BOTH memory-card states** (save-present and fresh card) plus the gameplay replay: all three report `renderer=gl`, reach PHASE_EXIT, zero asserts; character-select face/robe/cloak land within 1-2 LSB of the CPU reference and dots fall 14.5 -> 4.4/frame (the remainder is the texture's real smudge, which both renderers show). |
| `154-scalar-p8-tap` (new, cont.332o) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★★ **THE SCALAR SAMPLER READ PSMT8 THROUGH THE 4-BIT PSMT4 TAP — fixed; and row 153's `PS2X_GS_QUADTEX` default flip is REVERTED, it was a misdiagnosis.** `m_draw.texTapFast` is set for `texFastP4 || texFastP8` (cont.317 widened it) but the hoisted lambda was "the fastP4 arm verbatim" (cont.268) and always read `PixelStorageTraits<P4>` / `PageTableP4` with a 4-bit mask — so a PSMT8 draw reaching it took a 4-bit read of an 8-bit texture and indexed only the first 16 CLUT entries. LATENT in the default config (the cont.320 decoded-index cache usually serves PSMT8, and `tapFast` is false whenever it does) and invisible to the bench gate (that capture is overwhelmingly PSMT4). **`PS2X_GS_QUADTEX_VERIFY` (new, default OFF) found it**: the four-wide sampler's own oracle, comparing texel by texel on the same s/t/q and printing the first mismatches with their inputs (+ per-PSM totals, `PS2X_GS_QUADTEX_VERIFY_PSM` to filter). Before: PSMT8 **80.9% mismatch** (n=280 M), PSMT4 **0 of 2.8 G**, every failing line reading `tapFast=1 lin=0 fastP8=1`. After the `fastP8` branch: **PSMT8 0 of 299,192,921**, PSMT4 0 of 3.02 G. ⚠⚠ **Row 153's flip was wrong and is reverted**: it rested on a DOT COUNT (isolated dark pixels 14.5 → 4.4/frame with QUADTEX off), which is not a correctness measure. Pixel-wise against the GL renderer over the character box at the same flip: **QUADTEX=1 mad 2.19, QUADTEX=0 mad 39.09** — with it off the face reads (64,52,42) where GL and the decoded texture say (111,85,68); the character darkens, which removes dots by making everything worse. ⚠ STILL OPEN: `psm=0x20` (undefined, decoded as PSMCT32 by the GS and used by this game's full-screen sprites) is **100% mismatched, 262,144 texels** — the quad path has no PSMCT32 fallback where the scalar does; and the user-reported dots are UNEXPLAINED (at (233,251) the good CPU config gives (81,75,66) against GL's (145,140,132)). |
| `155-quadverify-guard` (new, cont.332p) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ⚠⚠ **`PS2X_GS_QUADTEX_VERIFY` VERIFIED A PATH THAT HAD NOT RUN — the whole `psm=0x20 is 100% mismatched (262,144 texels)` row of cont.332o is the oracle's own bug.** Two of the oracle's three call sites (the four-wide triangle loop's non-quadShade arm and the four-wide sprite loop) wrote `if (quadTex) sampleQuadBilinearP4(...);` UNBRACED, with the `if (s_gsQuadVerify) { ... }` block following it — at one site indented as though it were inside. So whenever `quadTex` was false the four-wide sampler never ran, `texq` still held the `_mm_store_si128(texq, zeroi)` zero-init from a few lines above, and the oracle compared that zero against a real scalar sample and counted every texel as a mismatch. It cannot be a real four-wide defect: `quadTex` requires `m_draw.texTapFast`, which `resolveTexMip` sets only for plain PSMT4/PSMT8 with a valid decoded CLUT (`gs_cpu_backend.cpp` ~7101-7115), so a draw with an UNDEFINED psm never reaches the four-wide path at all — and the example line the oracle printed says so itself (`quad=00000000 … psm=0x20 … tapFast=0`). Fixed by guarding the verify on `quadTex` at both sites (the third site is inside a braced `if (quadShade)` and was always correct). The PSMT4 (`0x14`) and PSMT8 (`0x13`) rows are unaffected — those draws really do take the four-wide path, so cont.332o's 80.9% → 0% PSMT8 result stands. |
| `156-audio-default-on` (new, cont.332q) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | ★★★ **`PS2X_AUDIO` IS DEFAULT ON — the runtime opens a host audio device unless told not to (`=0`).** It was default OFF from cont.245 on the premise "the game has no audible output yet (SPU2 is not emulated)", which stopped being true at cont.282: the game drives a real 44.1 kHz PCM stream sink (`ps2_audio.cpp` `streamOpen` / `streamPushAdpcm`, ADPCM state carried across chunk boundaries as PCSX2's `V_Voice::Prev1/Prev2` does in `SPU2/Mixer.cpp` `XA_decode_block`). Verified on build 954: a fresh-card fight replay opens **10** streams at pure defaults and a save-card menu/Pat01 replay opens **6**, with `underrun 0 frames` on the healthy beds. PCSX2 likewise brings its audio backend up by default (SPU2 + cubeb), so defaulting off made us the odd one out. ⚠ The ORIGINAL reason is real and unfixed, just rarer: raylib's `InitAudioDevice` runs on the window thread, and on a degraded desktop audio server (PipeWire sink in an error state) miniaudio's PulseAudio connect wait hangs ~30 s with the window unresponsive — `PS2X_AUDIO=0` is the escape hatch, now documented at the call site. Cost measured at **−0.2% wall** over 840 guest frames of deterministic replay (`PS2X_VIRTUAL_TIME=1`), i.e. indistinguishable from zero. |
| `157-audio-retrigger` (new, cont.332s) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_audio.cpp` | ★★★★★ **THE BACKEND SILENTLY DROPPED A RETRIGGER OF A SOUND THAT WAS STILL PLAYING — the user-reported "the language page plays the move sound on DOWN but not on UP".** `playDecodedSample` walked `activeSounds` and `return`ed if the same `sampleKey` was sounding, so a second key-on inside the first blip's length (17024 B @ 31992 Hz mono = **266 ms**) was discarded with no log. It is not direction, it is TIMING: the user's own run fired the trigger **7 times for 3 presses** (`[aud:onupd] EDGE` at flushes 71/86/134/149/162/176/190, all `tpl=0x0201`) and they heard about two — press, press-too-soon (dropped), wait, press (heard). The rule is our invention: SPU2 is 2 cores x 24 voices (PCSX2 `SPU2/defs.h` `V_Core::Voices[24]`), every key-on takes its own voice, and `SPU2/Mixer.cpp` simply mixes them — one waveform can sound many times at once. Now a retrigger starts another instance, bounded by `PS2X_AUDIO_SAMEKEY` (default 3) per key: at the cap the OLDEST instance of that key is stolen, as hardware steals voices, so a retrigger is always audible. `PS2X_AUDIO_RETRIGGER=0` restores the drop for an A/B. Also `kMaxConcurrentSounds` 4 -> `PS2X_AUDIO_VOICES` (default 16): the hard 4 evicted still-playing sounds in any busy moment, against the hardware's 48. Verified with a fast-tap probe: 12 triggers, **8 of them retriggers that were previously dropped**, cap-stealing engaged. **User-confirmed fixed on the language page (2026-09-17).** Validated on both cards at defaults: fresh -> Hel01 11/11 chunks 0 drops not-found=0, save -> Pat01. |
| `158-audio-sample-dump` (new, cont.332w) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_audio.cpp` | ★★ **`PS2X_AUDIO_DUMP=<dir>` (default OFF): write each DISTINCT sample the game plays to `<dir>/snd_<key>_<rate>Hz_<frames>.wav`.** "The sounds are weird" is not a diagnosable report and a log line cannot be listened to — this hands the person who knows what the game should sound like the actual waveforms. Deduped by sample key, capped at 256 files, and it `mkdir`s its directory (PRESENT_SAVE's lesson: a dump that does not create its own directory is a silent sink). First use answered two questions at once: Aragorn's 15 samples decode CLEANLY (22042 Hz, 0.23-0.83 s, no clipping, DC within ±180, ADPCM block-seam jump ratio ~1.0 so no decode clicks, RMS ~11-13 k of 32768 — not cont.287's 0.0019-gain silence), so the remaining defect is WHICH sound plays, not how it decodes; and `snd_00010400` (Legolas) and `snd_00010500` (Gimli) are **byte-identical 336-frame placeholders**, because both of those character banks are 192-byte stubs whose real audio must be streamed. |
| `159-audio-output-tap` (new, cont.334) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_audio.cpp` | ★★★ **`PS2X_AUDIO_TAP=<dir>` (default OFF): capture EXACTLY the frames handed to the audio device, per stream, as `<dir>/tap_<key>_<rate>Hz.wav`.** The user reported streamed dialogue stuttering — "the way is shut.. the way is shut. It was it was made by those those" — which no existing instrument could see: `PS2X_AUDIO_DUMP` (row 158) writes the SOURCE sample, and the pump log reports bytes fed, so both are blind to anything that happens between the ring and the speaker. The tap sits at the end of `fillSlot`, after the underrun zero-fill, so it records what is actually heard including the gaps. Producer-side cost is one `insert` on the audio thread against a vector reserved at `streamOpen` and capped at 120 s/stream; it flushes in `streamStop`, which our pump calls the moment a feed finishes, so a capture survives a Ctrl-C mid-scene. **Validated as an instrument before being trusted**: on a replay whose log shows one clean pass (`196608 B fed, 774 frames of underrun`), the tap of `0x0211` is 345,720 frames against the source's 344,064 samples, aligns at offset 0, and tracks a straight decode of the source bytes at ratio 0.9992 (p10 0.9901, p90 1.0028) with mean abs error 17.5 of 32768 — i.e. 0.05%, the difference between the reference decoder and `ps2_vag::decodeBlock`, with no repeated or dropped segment. That also establishes the negative result this was built for: **the stutter does not reproduce in the scripted replay**, so it depends on interactive/real-time conditions and needs a capture from the user's own run. |
| `160-stream-channels` (new, cont.334) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_audio.cpp`, `include/runtime/ps2_audio.h` | ★★★★★ **STREAMED DIALOGUE IS 2-CHANNEL INTERLEAVED ADPCM AND WE DECODED IT AS MONO — the user's "the way is shut.. the way is shut. It was it was made by those those".** A streamed instrument's tone record carries its channel count at `tone[+0x16]`, and AUDIOPF branches on exactly that (`StmPrimeVoices` @0x072f8: `lbu v0,0x16(v0)` then `slti at,v0,2`, setting a flag on the track when it is >= 2). The channels are interleaved **one 16-byte ADPCM block at a time** — block 0 is channel 0, block 1 channel 1 — so decoding the buffer linearly with a single predictor plays L-block, R-block, L-block…, chopping one utterance into alternating fragments. Measured offline on PAT01 `0x0202` tone 3 (589,824 B at `0x02200000`): linear = **23.41 s containing 7 fragmented utterances**; de-interleaved = **two channels of 11.70 s, each ONE continuous utterance (0.04-10.28 s)**. `streamOpen` now takes `channels` (clamped to 1-2 — the byte is data, not to be trusted), allocates the raylib stream with that count, and keeps a **separate `ps2_vag::AdpcmState` per channel**, exactly as PCSX2 gives every SPU2 voice its own `V_Voice::Prev1/Prev2` (`SPU2/Mixer.cpp` `XA_decode_block`). `streamPushAdpcm` consumes a whole **group** of `channels` blocks atomically and interleaves the decoded frames into the ring, which is what keeps the channels in phase; `streamFreeAdpcmBytes` and `streamQueuedFrames` account in groups/frames rather than samples, and the ring's fill test uses `kAdpcmBlockFrames * channels` slots. ⚠ This changes what a ring "frame" means: the ring holds INTERLEAVED samples, so `fillSlot` drains `frames * channels` of them and the underrun counter divides back out. **Validated at the device output** with row 159's tap on the same replay: `tap_0202` goes from **1 ch / 23.41 s / 7 fragments** to **2 ch / 11.75 s / 1 continuous utterance**, matching the offline prediction exactly; all six of PAT01's streams report `tone[0x16]=2`. ⚠ Cosmetic follow-up: the game-side `[aud:stm] STREAMING … (44097 Hz, 23.4 s)` duration is still computed as mono and now reads 2x the truth. |
| `161-vag-loop-end` (new, cont.338) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_audio_vag.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_AUDIO_VAGEND` (default ON; `=0` restores the old full decode): a one-shot ADPCM decode stops at the block carrying `XAFLAG_LOOP_END`, as the SPU2 does — the user's "clicky sound after the sound effects" (2026-09-18).** Every sample in this game's banks ends with an END block (flag 1), then the SDK terminator block `00 07 77 77 …` (shift 0, flag 7, nibbles 7 = +28672 for 28 samples), then up to five zero blocks before the next directory offset; `decode()` walked to the next offset, so every one-shot ended in a full-scale DC step. Census of `CHAR_ARA` (26 samples) and `FRO03` (25): all end in exactly that pattern; no sample carries flag 3 (LOOP_END+LOOP), so stopping is faithful for all of them. PCSX2 `SPU2/Mixer.cpp` `DecodeSamples`: `if (vc.LoopFlags & XAFLAG_LOOP_END) { ENDX |= 1<<v; vc.NextA = vc.LoopStartA; if (!(vc.LoopFlags & XAFLAG_LOOP)) vc.Stop(); }`. Streams (`streamPushAdpcm`) are untouched. |
| `162-pixwatch-window` (new, cont.341) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★ **`PS2X_GS_PIXWATCH` forces the scalar loops only inside the `PS2X_GS_PROBEFLIP` window.** Armed, the watch made the whole run take the per-pixel diagnostic path (`g_gs2PerPixelDiag` was a static const), so a Pat01 route that needs flip 6,600 reached flip 3,552 in 380 s and the watch never fired. `gs2PerPixelDiag()` now also requires `probeFlipOk()` when a probe flip is set; with none set the old whole-run behaviour is unchanged. Instrument only; no rendering change at defaults. |
| `163-pagewatch-window` (new, cont.342) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★ **`PS2X_GS_PAGEWATCH` honours the `PS2X_GS_PROBEFLIP` window.** Its 512-line print cap was spent on the boot era's uploads before the level had loaded, so a watch aimed at a gameplay texture reported nothing useful. With a probe flip set, only writes inside the window are counted and printed; unchanged otherwise. Instrument only. |
| `164-vu1-kick-window` (new, cont.343) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/vu/ps2_vu1_core.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/vu/microvu/pcsx2/x86/microVU.h`, `microVU_Log.inl` | ★★ **Aim the VU1 kick-scan dump at one screen point in one frame, and write PCSX2's own microprogram listing.** `PS2X_VU1_KICKSCAN_AT=<px>:<py>` (GS pixel coords) flags the kicks that emit a vertex within one pixel of that point; `PS2X_VU1_KICKSCAN_FLIP=<n>` [+ `_FLIPW`, default 2] restricts the scan to guest display flips [n, n+w) (`gs2CurrentFlip()`), and inside the window every flagged kick is printed (with `flip=`) and dumped as `kickscan_NN_{code,data,packet}.bin` (up to 64) instead of only the first. Without the window the first hit on the Pat01 floor vertex came from the level's first second, a different draw. `PS2X_VU1_PROGLOG=<dir>` (default OFF) compiles PCSX2's `mVUlogProg` in and routes `__mVUdumpProgram` (microVU_Log.inl, PCSX2's own listing, unchanged) to that directory; the fmt shim knows only `{}`, so the file name is built with `snprintf` (the `{:02d}` name had every program overwrite one file). Instrumentation only, all default OFF; no hardware behaviour modelled. Interpreter path for the kick scan (`PS2X_VU1_MICROVU=0`). |
| `165-colclamp-wrap` (new, cont.343b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_COLCLAMP` (default ON; `=0` restores saturation): COLCLAMP.CLAMP=0 makes the blend result WRAP modulo 256 instead of saturating.** PCSX2 `GSDrawScanline.cpp` (`if (sel.colclamp == 0) { rb &= 0x00ff; ga &= 0x00ff; }` before the pack). Our rasterizer only counted colclamp in a census (`IGNORED/APPROXIMATED STATE`) and every pixel path saturated.
