This game's character shadow is a per-vertex shadow VOLUME (VU1 program 0x1e68's sibling at 0x130: light-facing vertices keep their place, the others are pushed along the clip-space light vector) drawn into the offscreen target fbp 0x180 with colclamp=0, ABCD=0221 FIX=128, ZTST=GREATER against the Z buffer the game has just INVERTED with a full-screen (Cs-Cd)*FIX sprite: faces add (0,1,1) or (0,255,255) = +1 / -1 mod 256, so what survives is the z-fail stencil count, and the full-screen composite (tbp 12288 CT24, DECAL, TEXA ta0=32 aem=1, Cd*(1-As)) darkens only where it is non-zero. Saturating left every covered pixel at 255, so the whole sheared volume footprint darkened the wall and the floor -- the user's 'shadow cast on the cave wall' (2026-09-18). Implementation: `PixelPlan::colwrap` (abe && colclamp==0); such a draw is refused by every specialised class (`pipeClassFor`, `spritePipeClassFor`, `isAddFixPlan`) and the generic scalar blend keeps the low 8 bits of the signed result instead of `clampU8`. The reference bench hash moves `647a498ab2b94862` -> `5e4200da8cb6500d` (the ZTE=0 full-screen sprite pair is colclamp=0 too); `=0` restores the old hash on the same binary. ⚠ CPU arm only; the GL device saturates in fixed-function blending (PCSX2's HW renderer uses an HDR target while colclip is active) -- pending. |
| `164b-kickscan-radius` (cont.343b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/vu/ps2_vu1_core.cpp` | `PS2X_VU1_KICKSCAN_ATR=<px>` (default 1): the match radius of `PS2X_VU1_KICKSCAN_AT`. A moving mesh does not put a vertex within one pixel of the same point two frames later; the static floor did, the shadow silhouette did not (0 hits at radius 1, 2,686 at radius 6). |
| `166-gl-colclip-hdr` (new, cont.343b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h`, `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` (seam) | ★★★★ **The GL arm's COLCLAMP=0: an HDR target plus signed colours (`PS2X_GS_GLR_COLCLIP`, default ON).** GL fixed-function blending cannot wrap mod 256, so a target that receives a COLCLAMP=0 ADDITIVE group (ABCD B=0, D=Cd) is rebuilt as RGBA16F (`GlTarget::hdr`, never demoted; the request arrives right after the guest's own black clear of fbp 0x180) and the fragment shader maps colours 128..255 to -128..-1 for those groups (`uSignedAdd`), so (0,1,1) + (0,255,255) sums to 0 as the GS wraps it and the composite's "non-black" test (CT24 read, TEXA aem) sees the true count. Mirrors PCSX2 GSRendererHW's colclip HDR target (`m_conf.ps.hdr`), minus the mod-256 resolve, which no 8-bit consumer of this target needs. `GsGlGroup::colclamp` travels from the seam (`st.colclamp`, part of the group state). Flip 6600 of `save_attack.pad`: pixel-identical to the frame with the composite removed; the old frame differed by the two sheared parallelograms (16,229 px). GL under `PS2X_VIRTUAL_TIME=1` was byte-identical to a build-987 frame here, so at least this route is deterministic on GL as well. |
| `167-gif-fifo-knob` (new, cont.344) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/ps2_gif_arbiter.cpp` | `PS2X_GIF_FIFO` (default OFF): drain the arbiter queue in EE kick order instead of the path-priority stable sort. Built to test whether the sort delayed the texture stream's uploads past the draws that use them; **measured null** on the CPU arm (idle Paths of the Dead spawn, flip 6900: frame and `[gs2:pixwatch]` trace identical) and ~35% slower wall (draw runs fragment) -- the stream turned out to be PATH2 (chain-ordered), see row 168. Kept as an A/B knob. PCSX2 reference: `Gif_Unit.h` `Execute`/`checkPaths` prefer PATH1 only over a PATH3 transfer pending at the same time, at packet boundaries. |
| `168-gif-trace` (new, cont.344) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_memory.cpp`, `ps2_vif1_interpreter.cpp`, `gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | `PS2X_GIF_TRACE=<flip>` [+ `PS2X_GIF_TRACE_W`, default 2] (default OFF, read-only): one `[gif:trace]` line per GIF/VIF1 DMA kick, per packet handed to the arbiter (path, bytes, IMAGE flg, MSKPATH3 state), per MSKPATH3 set/clear and masked-FIFO release inside that flip window; plus `ps2xGsPerfFlips()` exporting the display-flip counter. First use: proved this game's whole texture stream is PATH2 DIRECT IMAGE packets interleaved in the VIF1 chain (no GIF DMA, no MSKPATH3), so upload-vs-draw order is the chain's own in every emulator. |
| `169-gl-depth-by-zbp` (new, cont.344b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h` (`GsGlBatch::zbp/zpsm`), `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` (seam) | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_ZSHARE` (default ON): one GL depth texture per Z address, shared by every colour target that names it** (`glrDepthPool`, keyed zbp/zpsm/size; the batch now carries the draw context's ZBUF). Each target owned a private depth, so the shadow volume (fbp 0x180, zbp 0x100) never tested against the depth the scene (fbp 0/0x80, zbp 0x100) wrote: every face passed, the +1/-1 z-fail count cancelled to zero everywhere, and the GL frame was pixel-identical with the composite dropped. A shared depth is cleared once per frame by the first target that names it; a target joining mid-frame clears only its colour. PCSX2 `GSTextureCache` keys DepthStencil targets by ZBP. |
| `170-gl-zpage-alias` (new, cont.344b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_ZALIAS` (default ON): a colour draw onto a live Z page applied to the depth texture.** This game inverts its Z24 buffer in place before the shadow-volume pass -- a full-screen white sprite onto fbp 0x100 with `(Cs-Cd)*FIX(0x80)+0` = `0xFFFFFF - z` per lane -- and inverts it back afterwards; in GL that sprite landed in a colour target nobody's depth reads. The detector (colour address == a pooled Z address, that blend, white vertices) runs a `d' = 1 - d` pass over the pooled depth (through the AFAIL depth copy; the VAO must be bound -- the first build raised GL_INVALID_OPERATION and changed nothing). PCSX2 HW converts a depth target to colour and back (`ShaderConvert FLOAT32_TO_RGBA8/RGBA8_TO_FLOAT32`); this is that one formula in the depth domain. Diagnostics print for the first passes (fbo status, GL errors, a centre-pixel depth before/after). |
| `171-gl-target-read-flip` (new, cont.344b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_TEXFLIP` (default ON): a PROMOTED render target is sampled with V mirrored.** The vertex transform draws targets bottom-up, but the guest's texture coordinates address a target top-down, so every spatially-varying target-as-source read was vertically mirrored -- the shadow composite darkened the frame at the mirror of the count blob (luma 26.1 vs 32.3 inside the mirrored mask, equal inside the true one). Full-screen uniform passes (the title darken) never showed it. With the three rows together the GL arm draws the shadow: inside the blob 0.72-0.74x of the ring (composite dropped: 0.92; PCSX2: 0.754). |
| `172-gl-no-resolve-clear` (new, cont.345) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_RESOLVECLEAR` (default OFF; `=1` restores): a present RESOLVE no longer re-arms a frame-start clear of the target.** The GL arm cleared a target on its first draw after every resolve. This game draws onto its DISPLAYED buffer after the flip (UI glyphs, the loading screen, the menu text), so whenever the presenter resolved the same buffer twice before its next full frame -- real time only; under `PS2X_VIRTUAL_TIME=1` presents and flips never double up -- it showed the runtime's clear colour (0.04,0.05,0.14 = the user's "solid dark-blue frame" on the loading screen) with a few draws on top, or a half-drawn menu. Measured on a real-time replay of `shadow_spot.pad` with every flip saved: 26 dark-blue frames + 27 partial hero-select frames in 1,583 flips at default, 0 + 0 with the re-arm off (`FRAMECLEAR=0` run); `[gsgpu:present]` showed the double resolves (`#940`/`#941` both key 0x80, draws-since-resolve 1 then 13). PCSX2 never clears a target at present time: `GSTextureCache` only clears/invalidates one when a guest draw completely overwrites it (`GSRendererHW::TryTargetClear`, the "completely overwritten" invalidate in `GSTextureCache::LookupTarget`). The guest's own full-screen clear quad and its `FBMSK=ffffffff` depth-only quad do that work here. A target is still cleared once at creation/realloc. Build `bld-eur-noreclear-1006`. |
| `173-gl-flip-point-snapshot` (new, cont.345b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h` (`SnapshotFrameGl`, `RenderFrameGl(..., snapshotSeq)`), `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` + `.h` (`glSnapshotDisplayTargets`, `m_glSnapSeq`, `g_ps2xGsPreferredDisplaySource`, `g_ps2xGsLiveDispfb1/2`), `src/lib/gs/gs_frontend.cpp` (publishes both) | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_SNAPRESOLVE` (default ON; `=0` restores the presenter's live resolve): the GL display target is resolved BY THE GS WORKER at the flip-snapshot marker -- the same FIFO instant the CPU arm copies VRAM -- and filed under that snapshot's seq; the presenter returns the filed frame.** The live resolve was a device job the PRESENTER thread enqueued when it woke, by which time the worker had forwarded the guest's NEXT frame's first draws into the same buffer: real-time captures showed 27 half-drawn hero-select frames and a one-flip flash of a different camera at cutscene cuts (`PS2X_GS_GLR_PRESENTLOG` #940/#941: the same key resolved twice, 1 then 13 draws apart). The worker also seeds the display target(s) at that point (the seed the presenter used to apply at present time), snapshots the preferred display source (the full-screen copy sprite's source, which Present() shows instead of the display buffer -- `gs_frontend.cpp` displayCopy; 394 of ~2,800 presents in the movie era), and snapshots at the per-Sync capture too. In GL mode a stale copy is still presented (`Present()`/`PresentSourceFresh`): the old >500 ms live fallback showed a half-drawn buffer on every slow frame (46-52 of ~1,300 real-time presents). `PS2X_GS_GLR_SNAPLOG=1` logs every miss, `=2` every snapshot with the draws that landed in that key since its previous one. PCSX2 presents a completed frame per vsync from `GSRenderer::VSync` on its own GS thread, in order with the draws -- never a cross-thread mid-frame read of the target. Builds 1007-1012. |
| `174-gl-joined-depth-no-clear` (new, cont.345b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_ZREBIND` (default ON; `=0` restores) part 1: a target that ATTACHED to a pooled depth other targets already wrote (`GlTarget::depthJoined`) never clears that depth in its own creation clear.** THE USER'S "flicker soon after selecting a player": at the first cutscene cut of a level the shadow-volume target 0x180 is rebuilt as HDR (its first COLCLAMP=0 draw), its creation clear ran with `GL_DEPTH_BUFFER_BIT` on the SHARED depth (row 169) between the game's Z-invert sprite and its complement (`PS2X_GS_GLR_ZALIASLOG=1`: pass #1373 0.0135->0.9865, the ALLOC, pass #1374 0.0000->1.0000), so the complement drove every pixel to "nearest", the characters and fog failed GEQUAL, and one flip presented the new shot's background alone (the CPU arm cuts cleanly). Reproduced 3/3 under virtual time with the log's readbacks on; 0/5 after the fix (2 logged virtual-time + 3 real-time runs); the shadow spot is unchanged vs build 1005. Build 1014. |
| `175-gl-depth-rebind` (new, cont.345b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_ZREBIND` part 2: an EXISTING target whose batch names a ZBUF different from the one its depth came from re-attaches the pooled depth for that ZBUF** (created and cleared when fresh, joined otherwise; `[gsgpu:glr] target key=.. REBIND depth`). Target 0x80 was created at boot by a draw with ZBP=0 and kept a private depth for the whole game, so its frames never shared the scene's Z with 0x0 and 0x180 (row 169 pooled by ZBP, but only at creation) -- the invert pass touched 0x0's depth only. On the GS the Z buffer is whatever ZBUF names at draw time (PCSX2 `GSTextureCache::LookupTarget(DepthStencil)` per draw). Build 1014. |
| `176-pad-per-port-input` (new, cont.346c) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_pad.cpp`, `include/runtime/ps2_pad.h` (`gamepadForPort`, `portHasDevice`), `src/lib/Kernel/Stubs/Pad.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_PAD2` (default `auto`): each PS2 controller port is driven by its OWN host device.** `PSPadBackend::readState(int /*port*/, int /*slot*/, ...)` ignored `port` entirely and always read raylib gamepad 0, so BOTH ports sampled the same device -- player 2 would have mirrored player 1, and local co-op could never work even once the game's gate was satisfied. Port N now takes the **N-th available gamepad**; if port 1 has none it falls back to a **numpad keyboard split** (KP_8/2/4/6 = left stick, KP_0 cross, KP_. circle, KP_1 square, KP_3 triangle, KP_7/KP_9 = L1/R1, KP_/ KP_* = L2/R2, KP_Enter start, KP_+ select), and player 1's KP_0/KP_1 *alternates* are released to player 2 while that split is live. `PS2X_PAD2=keys` forces the split, `=off` restores single-player-only (port 1 reports no device). `portHasDevice(port)` is the query the game-side co-op gate consults, so port 1 is reported connected only when something real drives it. ⚠ Every diagnostic driver (`PS2X_PAD_CROSS_AT`, `PS2X_PAD_LY_AT`, `PS2X_PAD_SCRIPT`, `PS2X_PAD_RECORD`) and the scripted override in `Pad.cpp` keep a GLOBAL call counter and pad scripts are indexed by it, so they are now **port 0 only** -- otherwise port 1 would advance the counter too and desynchronise every recorded replay (`tmp/*.pad`). A port the backend declines stays NEUTRAL instead of falling back to gamepad 0 + the player-1 keyboard. PCSX2: `pcsx2/SIO/Pad/Pad.cpp` models ports independently -- `s_controllers[NUM_CONTROLLER_PORTS]`, `GetPad(port, slot)`, per-port bindings via `GetConfigSection(i)` -> `"Pad{i+1}"`, and an absent pad is the explicit `ControllerType::NotConnected` checked by `HasConnectedPad()`; `portHasDevice()` is that check. Build `bld-eur-cooppad-1016`. |
| `177-pad2-optin` (amends 176, cont.346d) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_pad.cpp` | ★★★ **`PS2X_PAD2=auto` (default) now means a REAL second gamepad only — the numpad split is OPT-IN (`=keys`).** Row 176 let `auto` fall back to the keyboard, which made port 1 *always* present. That is not a cosmetic default: "is a second pad connected?" is a question the GAME ASKS (`0x142C80(6)` counts the two socket records at `gp+0x1650` needing `st28` in {2,3,4}), and answering yes makes the New/Load screen grow its co-op entry, so a solo player's menu shifts under them — measured as the save-card chain going `…17 10 7 4 1 14` -> Pat01 (correct) to `…17 10 23 14 14` -> Hel01 via the Diff screen. `portHasDevice(1)` is therefore true only for a genuine second gamepad, or on explicit `PS2X_PAD2=keys`. Verified on build `bld-eur-coop2pad-1022`: at defaults the save card reproduces the baseline chain exactly with 0 co-op scope hits and slot 1's record untouched; with `PS2X_PAD2=keys` slot 1 resolves (`latch[+20] 0->1  st28[+28] 0->4  profile[+4..7] -> ffffffff`). |
| `178-padlib-log-and-modetable` (new, cont.346f) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/Stubs/Pad.cpp` | ★★ **`PS2X_PAD_LIBLOG` (default ON, rate-limited per port+call): what EE-side libpad queries the guest makes PER PORT, and `PS2X_PAD_MODETABLE` (default ON): `scePadInfoMode(PAD_MODETABLE)` advertises a DualShock2's TWO modes {digital 4, dualshock 7} instead of one.** We advertised exactly one mode -- whatever the pad was currently in -- so a caller enumerating the table for `PAD_TYPE_DUALSHOCK` (7) would never find it on a port still in digital. ⚠ **The log proved this is NOT the cause of the game's "insert analog controller" page: `[padlib]` recorded ZERO `scePad*` calls over a full boot. This game never uses the EE libpad API at all** -- it drives its pads through its own manager at `0x22Dxxx` over SIF, which the game-side DBCMAN HLE intercepts. The mode table is kept because it is the faithful answer, not because it fixed anything; `PS2X_PAD_MODETABLE=0` restores the single-entry table. ⚠ **No PCSX2 counterpart exists to check this against**: PCSX2 emulates the SIO protocol and runs the real libpad on its emulated IOP, so `scePadInfoMode` is ours alone -- this follows the libpad spec and is env-revertible precisely because it is not mirrored from a reference. |
| `179-coop-keyboard-layout` (new, cont.346h) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_pad.cpp` | ★★★ **Two players on one keyboard, each keeping the D-PAD / ANALOG separation.** Player 2 asked for the arrow keys (user, 2026-09-19), so player 1's d-pad moves to the `T/F/G/H` inverted-T while `WASD` stays its analog stick; player 2 gets the **arrows as its d-pad** and the **numpad inverted-T `KP_8/4/6/2` as its analog stick**, with its face buttons on the block physically above the arrows (`Del`/`End`/`Ins`/`Home` = ✕/○/□/△, `PgUp`/`PgDn` = L1/R1, `KP_Enter`/`KP_+` = start/select, `KP_/`,`KP_*` = L2/R2, and the numpad face keys `KP_0`/`KP_.`/`KP_1`/`KP_3` as alternates). ★ The separation is not cosmetic: this game WALKS on the left stick and never the d-pad (cont.230) while menus read the d-pad, so each player needs both — and one cluster driving both is exactly the cont.232 bug where a menu accepting either input moved TWICE per press. The first cut of this change collapsed player 1 onto a single WASD cluster and was reverted on the user's instruction before it shipped. Six keys are handed over while the split is live (the four arrows plus player 1's `KP_0`/`KP_1` face alternates), each guarded by `!p2Keys` on player 1's side, so **no key is read by both players** (verified by scanning both branches). `PS2X_PAD2_ARROWS=0` returns the arrows to player 1 and leaves player 2 on the numpad; `PS2X_PAD2=off` disables port 1 entirely. Single player is untouched — arrows stay its d-pad — verified on build `bld-eur-p2split-1028`: save card `29 20 18 19 11 18 12 17 10 7 4 1 14` -> Pat01 63/63, 0 co-op scope hits. |
| `180-coop-laptop-layout` (amends 179, cont.346i) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_pad.cpp` | ★★★ **Player 2 needs NO NUMPAD — the user is on a laptop that has none (2026-09-19).** Row 179 put player 2's analog stick on `KP_8/4/6/2` and its start/select on `KP_Enter`/`KP_+`, which is unreachable on a laptop. Player 2 is now entirely letters + arrows: **d-pad = arrows, analog stick = `I/J/K/L`, ✕○□△ = `M` `,` `N` `.` (mirroring player 1's `Z X C V` ordering), L1/R1 = `U`/`O`, L2/R2 = `Y`/`P`, start = `RightShift`, select = `/`**. The numpad set is kept only as a harmless desktop alternate. The d-pad/analog split (row 179, the cont.232 correctness constraint) is unchanged for both players. Three more keys hand over while the split is live — `I/J/K/L` (player 1's camera stick, which steps aside; walking is unaffected) and `RightShift` (player 1's R2, which also answers to `2`) — on top of row 179's arrows and `KP_0`/`KP_1`. All eleven shared keys are guarded by `!p2Keys` on player 1's side, so **no key is read by both players** (verified by scanning both branches for `IsKeyDown`). Single player untouched; verified on `bld-eur-p2laptop-1029`: save card `29 20 18 19 11 18 12 17 10 7 4 1 14` -> Pat01 63/63, 0 co-op scope hits. |
| `181-padsock-port-routing` (new, cont.346j) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_dbcman_hle.cpp` | ★★★★★ **THE reason player 2 had no input: `buildPadPayload` read the pad backend for `socketIndex == 0` ONLY, with the port hardcoded `(0, 0)`.** Socket 1 always fell through to the neutral payload (`raw[2]=raw[3]=0xFF`, sticks 0x80), so **player 2 was wired to "nothing pressed" by construction** — no keyboard mapping could ever have reached it. Socket N now reads pad port N. ⚠ This is the ONLY path this game's pad data travels: it never calls the EE libpad API (cont.346f measured ZERO `scePad*` calls) and `Pad.cpp`'s `scePadRead` backend read is a different call site the game never reaches — which is why the cont.346h/i player-2 key layouts were wired to a function nobody called. New always-on `[padsock]` line states per socket whether its input is `LIVE` or `neutral (no device)`, so a run reports the fact instead of leaving it inferred from whether a character moves. Verified on `bld-eur-p2socket-1030`: single player -> `socket 0 LIVE / socket 1 neutral (no device)` with the baseline chain intact (Pat01 63/63); `PS2X_PAD2=keys` -> **both sockets LIVE** and the `[pad2]` announcement fires for the first time. |
| `182-padwork-port1-present` (new, cont.346k) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_dbcman_hle.cpp` | ★★★★★ **The WORK TABLE told the game there is no pad in port 1.** `kRpcSetWorkAddr` wrote `port0 = 1` and then **zeroed ports 1-15 unconditionally** — that table is the game's "which ports have a pad" map, so socket 1 could be created for port 1, fed LIVE input and delivered every vblank (rows 181) and the game still read *no controller in port 1*: player 2 reached its select screen and could not be controlled. Port 1 is now marked present exactly when `PSPadBackend::portHasDevice(1)` is true, so single player is unchanged. Two instruments landed with it: **`[padsock:create]`** dumps the whole CreateSocket request — which proved the socket->port mapping was right, from the game's own words (`sendBuf[8]` = 0 for socket 0, 1 for socket 1) rather than from allocation order — and **`[paddeliv]`** logs the button word actually written per socket on change, separating "LIVE at the source" from "reached the game". ★ Three independent places answer *"is there a pad in port 1?"* — the socket record's `st28` (cont.346e), the backend's `portHasDevice` (cont.346d) and this work table — and fixing two said nothing about the third. Verified on `bld-eur-padwork-1032`: single player `port0=1 port1=0` with the baseline chain intact (Pat01 63/63); `PS2X_PAD2=keys` -> `port0=1 port1=1`. |
| `183-padtrace-and-window-guard` (new, cont.346l) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_dbcman_hle.cpp`, `src/lib/ps2_pad.cpp` | ★★★ **A spurious pad sample before the window exists, plus the trace that found it.** `[paddeliv]` now dumps the **8 deliveries after every change** with the vblank index and the half-buffer written — "log on change" is blind to TIMING, so a clean held press and press/release chatter are indistinguishable in it (`PS2X_PAD2_TRACE=0` mutes, 600 lines/socket cap). It immediately caught socket 1's FIRST payload carrying `lx=ff` (stick hard right) on a run with nothing held: the first delivery happens inside the `CreateSocket` RPC, before raylib's window exists, and `PSPadBackend::readState` sampled `IsKeyDown`/`IsGamepad*` anyway — they return garbage then. `readState` now reports neutral until `IsWindowReady()`. ⚠ A spurious sample at a state change is the shape of the user's earlier "phantom presses" (one direction per state change, then silence), but that is **not claimed** — it is not evidence for the separate continuous up/down oscillation. ★ Also DROPPED a theory the trace disproved: the alternating half-buffers were suspected of serving stale data, but socket 0 alternates identically (`0x293540`/`0x2935c0` every vblank) and player 1 works — alternation is not the fault. Verified on `bld-eur-winready-1034`: socket 1's first delivery is `lx=80`, and both arms keep the baseline (Pat01 63/63). |
| `184-worktbl-knobs` (amends 182, cont.346m) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_dbcman_hle.cpp` | ★★ **The pad work table's SEMANTICS are not established, so both words are now A/B-able without a rebuild: `PS2X_PAD2_WORK0` (word [0], default 1) and `PS2X_PAD2_WORK1` (word [1] = port 1, default 1 when a device backs it; `=0` restores the pre-182 table).** The legacy code wrote `[0]=1` and zeroed the rest, which row 182 read as *"port 0 present"* — but `[0]` could equally be a **COUNT** of connected pads, in which case marking index 1 is meaningless or harmful. Row 182 is what finally gave player 2 input, and the user's co-op-menu oscillation appeared in the SAME build, so the correlation is worth testing rather than reasoning about. ⚠ Measured first, from the user's single-tap run on 1034: socket 1 delivered ONE clean hold (`btn=0xffbf` for ~11 vblanks, then `0xffff`) with **no chatter**; socket 0 saw nothing in that window; `st28` was `4->4` across all 24 samples and `142c80(6)` answered `1` throughout — so the oscillation is **not** our button data, not player 1 bleeding through, and not a connection flap. |
| `185-worktbl-index-and-dump` (amends 184, cont.346n) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_dbcman_hle.cpp` | ★★ **`PS2X_PAD2_WORKIDX=<i>` (default 1) picks which work-table word is marked, and `PS2X_PAD2_WORKDUMP=<n>` prints all 16 words whenever they change.** Both exist to replace guessing about the table's layout with measurement, and both produced hard results: **index 8 gives player 2 NO input** (so the table is *not* indexed `port*8 + slot` — index **1** is the only one the game responds to), and **the game never writes the table at all** (`[0]=1 [1]=1` unchanged for a whole run), so its format cannot be learned from the guest's own writes. The address is held dynamically by the game (no static reference in the generated code), so a constant grep cannot find the consumer either. ⚠ Net state: word [1] is simultaneously what makes the game SEE player 2 and what makes the co-op menu oscillate — user A/B: `[1]=1` -> input works, menu oscillates; `[1]=0` -> steady menu, player 2 ignored (its presses ARE delivered: `0xffdf` RIGHT x102, `0xff7f` LEFT x32, `0xffef` UP). |
| `186-mc-root-env` (new, cont.346p) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | ★★★ **`PS2X_MC_ROOT=<dir>` names the directory CONTAINING `mc0/` and `mc1/`; unset = the ELF's own directory, i.e. exactly the pre-346p behaviour.** The game's ELF now lives INSIDE `gamefiles/` so a player can drop their whole disc into one folder (user, 2026-09-19) — and since `mcRoot` defaults to `elfDirectory / "mc0"`, memory cards land in `gamefiles/` too, which is what the user asked for. The override exists because the **two-card test harness** gives each mirror its own `mc0` while both share one `gamefiles/`: without it every mirror would resolve to the same card and the save-present vs fresh-card distinction — a standing validation rule that has caught real bugs — would silently collapse. `host:` and `cdRoot` still follow the ELF and are now MORE faithful, pointing at the disc content rather than the game folder. Verified on `bld-eur-gfresolve-1039`: with **no environment at all** the runner found the data *and* the migrated save (it took the save-present chain `29 20 18 19 11 18 12 17 10 7 4 1 14` -> Pat01 63/63, not the fresh-card chain), and both card states still switch correctly via the override (`Pat01` 63/63 / `Hel01` 11/11). |
| `187-config-beside-exe` (amends 186, cont.346q) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | ★★★ **User state lives in `<exe dir>/config/`, not beside the disc dump.** The runtime now resolves its own executable (`/proc/self/exe`; a Windows branch to add) and defaults the memory-card root to `<exe dir>/config/mc0`. Resolution order: `PS2X_MC_ROOT` (explicit) -> `<exe dir>/config` -> the ELF's directory (the pre-346q behaviour, kept as a last resort if `/proc` is unavailable). **Why not beside the ELF:** `gamefiles/` is the player's DISC DUMP — replaceable and re-droppable. People re-dump when a rip was bad, and the natural way is to wipe the folder and re-copy, which would take their saves with it; user state has a different lifetime so it gets a different home. This is the *portable* convention both comparable projects ship (`portable.txt` in Zelda 64: Recompiled and Unleashed Recompiled), chosen over their `~/.config` default because this project is build-it-yourself: the binary is something the player made and owns, and its state travels with it. ⚠ Prerequisite, done with it: the built runner moved OUT of `<game>/tmp/` (scratch the build wipes) to `<game>/ps2EntryRunner`. ★ **Proved by a DISCRIMINATING test, not a passing one:** the save existed in both `config/mc0` and `gamefiles/mc0`, so a save-present boot was consistent with either source; only after moving `gamefiles/mc0` aside did the bare run still reach Pat01 63/63 (and not recreate a card in `gamefiles/`) — that is what pins the source. Verified on `bld-eur-configdir-1040`: bare run -> Pat01 63/63; `PS2X_MC_ROOT` save -> Pat01 63/63; fresh -> Hel01 11/11. |
| `188-framehist-fst-and-hashed-texdump` (new, cont.355b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★ **Read-only diagnostics for the HUD counter-scale and texture replacement.** (a) `FrameHistRow` gains `fst`, `prim`, a per-row width range and the first TEX0 `tbp`, and keys on fst+prim — `FST` is the GS's own 2D signal (1 = UV/texel, no perspective; 0 = STQ), and the width range is what separates a full-screen 2D pass from a HUD element. (b) the census is now dumped from the **flip handler** instead of `CopyFrameToHostRgba`: that is a CPU-ARM path the GL renderer never takes, so under `PS2X_GS_RENDERER=gl` (our default) it recorded every draw and printed nothing at all. (c) `PS2X_GS_GLR_DUMPTEX` filenames now carry the **content hash** (`glTexContentHash`) — without it a dump cannot be joined to a pack keyed by hash. No hardware model is touched, so there is no PCSX2 behaviour to mirror here; all three are instruments. Measured on a Pat01 gameplay flip: 16,077 draws / 9 states, and `FST=1 && width < raster` isolates the text layer (164 draws, 3–28 px, tbp=8192) while the full-screen `FST=1` composite (512 px, `tgt=1`) is correctly excluded. |
| `189-texpack-and-dumptexany` (new, cont.355d-e) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_texpack.cpp` (new), `include/runtime/gs/gs_texpack.h` (new), `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `CMakeLists.txt` | ★★★★ **The HD texture pack, end to end.** `PS2X_GS_TEXPACK=<dir>` (default unset) substitutes `<dir>/<content-hash>.png` for a decoded texture, keyed by the same `glTexContentHash` the dump names its files with. Needs no geometry change because the GL shader samples normalised. Every mip level is rescaled by level 0's factor so a chain keeps its ratios. The substitution is deliberately placed AFTER the DUMPTEX block so a dump always yields the ORIGINAL — a pack is authored from source art. ⚠ Alpha passes through UNSCALED: these bytes are the decoded RGBA the GL path uploads, and PS2 alpha is 0..128 (0x80 = opaque), not 0..255. Also `PS2X_GS_GLR_DUMPTEXANY=1`: dump every distinct texture of the probe window, de-duplicated by content hash — picking a texture to replace previously meant guessing a TBP from FRAMEHIST, which reports only the first TEX0 per draw STATE. ⚠ That needed the `needDecode` gate widened too: a cached texture never re-decodes, so the first inventory attempt returned nothing while decodes were plainly happening. Verified: both assets load and substitute, boot unaffected (Pat01 63/63, drops=0), and a deterministic A/B under `PS2X_VIRTUAL_TIME=1` isolates the change to one region (control off-vs-off 0.00%, test off-vs-on 0.12%). |
| `190-texpack-content-key-and-default-dir` (amends 189, cont.355h) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_texpack.cpp`, `include/runtime/gs/gs_texpack.h`, `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **The pack keys on DECODED PIXELS, and finds itself without an env var.** (a) `ps2x::texpack::contentKey()` hashes the decoded RGBA, replacing `glTexContentHash` as the lookup key. glTexContentHash hashes VRAM PAGES, and a texture's pages carry more than that texture, so the SAME art hashes differently depending on what else is resident — measured: the menu and gameplay draw a byte-identical 256x256 glyph sheet (identical alpha planes, identical generated PNG, same tbp/cbp) under two different VRAM hashes. A pack keyed on VRAM silently half-works: a duplicate file per occurrence, with no way to enumerate them. Keyed on content, one file matches wherever that image appears — verified, the font atlas now loads once and serves both. The DUMPTEX filename uses the same key, so a dump and a pack entry always agree. (b) the pack directory defaults to `<exe dir>/mods/hd/assets` (the `<exe dir>` anchor `config/` already uses), with `PS2X_GS_TEXPACK=<dir>` overriding and `=0` disabling; the directory is announced once. An absent or empty folder is not an error — nothing matches and the game renders unchanged, which is what lets the repo ship the mechanism with no art. |
| `191-framehist-q-and-screen-bbox` (amends 188, cont.356) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★★ **`FST` does not classify 2D — `q` + `ZTST` do.** Row 188 built the census on `FST`, and a rich-HUD re-measure killed that: over two complete windows (115k draws / 20 states, and 1.80M draws / 21 states) only **two** `FST=1` states exist — the text glyphs and the full-screen `tgt=1` composite — so `FST=1 && width < 0.9*raster` selects **0.1%: the text and nothing else**, while every pictorial HUD element (portrait, L1/X badges, bar fills, sword, ring) is `FST=0`. So `FrameHistRow` gains (a) **`q` min/max** — the GS samples at (S/Q, T/Q), so a screen-space draw carries `q == 1` exactly and a depth-placed billboard does not; and (b) the **screen bbox**, `vertex - (XYOFFSET>>4)`, over EVERY vertex rather than `vertices[0..1]` (a sprite's diagonal is not a strip's extent), with the offset printed beside the rect — GSVertex carries GS coordinates, and cont.331j built a conclusion on an unconverted rect that returned a confident zero. Measured, and visually confirmed against the frame: `q == 1 && ZTST == ALWAYS && width < 0.9*raster` selects **exactly** the badges (`w=64`), the text (`w=7..13`), the pictorial HUD cluster (`w=32..128`, screen `x=23..264 y=418..512` — identical in both windows) and the untextured bar fills (`w=5..93`, `x=104..228 y=419..464`) — 106 of 109,729 draws at flip 3300. ⚠ `ZTST` is load-bearing, not decoration: 49k untextured shadow-volume draws carry `q == 1` too and are excluded only by `ZTST=GREATER`. Read-only instrument; no hardware model is touched, so there is no PCSX2 behaviour to mirror. |
| `192-hud-aspect-counterscale` (new, cont.356) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h`, `include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | ★★★★★ **The 2D/HUD aspect correction for anamorphic widescreen — `PS2X_GS_HUD_ASPECT` (default ON, inert).** Anamorphic widescreen renders a wider frustum into the game's own raster and stretches at presentation, so the 3D world comes out right and 2D does not. The GL vertex path now counter-scales 2D draws about the screen centre by `naturalAspect / presentedAspect` (`ps2xGsPresentAspect()` / `ps2xWindowAspect()`, the latter un-`static`ed so ONE parse of the ratio serves both the window fit and this — a second env read would be a second statement of the same number). **Identity unless an explicit `PS2X_WINDOW_ASPECT > 1.1` is in force**, so at pure defaults it is a no-op on any title — proven: a default run is BYTE-IDENTICAL to build 1079 at flip 3300. Classification is row 191's measured rule, `q == 1 && ZTST == ALWAYS && x-extent < frac * scissor` (`PS2X_GS_HUD_ASPECT_FRAC`, default 0.9), done **per primitive in the backend** and stamped onto a new `GsGlVertex::hud2d` byte (which rides in the existing padding, so the vertex does not grow): a GL state GROUP can mix HUD and non-HUD draws, and the width term is a per-primitive extent a vertex shader cannot see. ⚠ NOT `PRIM.FST` — on this game only the TEXT is `FST=1`, so an FST predicate corrects the numbers and leaves the bars and portrait stretched. ⚠ Rides along: **`glUniform1f` was loaded only under the hardware-raster path (`hwOk`)**, so on a plain `PS2X_GS_RENDERER=gl` run the pointer was null; it is now loaded for the GL renderer too. Verified by a three-arm A/B under `PS2X_VIRTUAL_TIME=1`: defaults byte-identical to 1079; stretch-on/correction-off byte-identical to defaults (the stretch is presentation-only, so the control is clean); correction-on differs in **11,064 px in the badge band and 9,597 in the HUD band, and 11 px in the entire world band** (y 141-410). Presented at 16:9 the X badge goes from an ellipse to a circle and the HUD moves as ONE piece. ★ Expected shape: scaling about the centre PILLARBOXES the HUD — corner elements move inward; re-anchoring to the new edges is game-side work and is deliberately not done here. |
| `193-vramrect-flip-aimed` (new, cont.356) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_VRAMRECT=<dir>` — decode a VRAM REGION to PPM, aimed by `PS2X_GS_PROBEFLIP`.** `PS2X_GS_VRAMDUMP` already dumps local memory but is pinned to a fixed DRAW count (420000, or the era gate + 30000), and the screen under investigation draws ~37 primitives a flip — the title screen comes and goes thousands of draws before either trigger, so it could never be aimed there. This dumps once on entering the probe-flip window and decodes through the backend's OWN `ReadVramUnlocked`, so the GS swizzle is the runtime's implementation rather than a second one written offline (which would leave a garbage result ambiguous between 'wrong address' and 'wrong swizzle'). `PS2X_GS_VRAMRECT_TBP` (default 12288) takes a comma list; each TBP is decoded as PSMCT32 at bw 8/4/2/1, because the upload census shows art arriving in bw=2 and bw=1 STRIPS, so the width to read at is exactly what is in question. Read-only. **First use settled what it was built for:** the LOTR title artwork is a **PSMCT32 512-wide surface in VRAM**, recovered recognisably at `tbp=14336 bw=8`. ⚠ Two traps it exposed: (a) on the **GL arm every dump is noise** — `m_vram` is not authoritative there (cont.331j), so this instrument only means anything under `PS2X_GS_RENDERER=cpu`; (b) FRAMEHIST's `tbp` column is a FIRST SIGHTING, and taking the composite's `tbp=12288` as the art's base sent the first sweep to the wrong address — the art is ~2048 blocks further on. |
| `194-vramrect-spec-and-wrap-guard` (amends 193, cont.356b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_VRAMRECT_SPEC=tbp:psm:bw:w:h,...` — and a WRAP GUARD.** Row 193 hardcoded PSMCT32 and a 512-row height, and guessing the format cost four runs. The spec list takes the psm and extent explicitly. ★ The guard is the important half: `PixelStorageTraits::ReadAt` masks the byte address to VRAM size, so a read running past 4 MB silently continues at **block 0 — which is a FRAMEBUFFER**, and on a menu screen that holds the very image being searched for. That produced a **false confirmation** here: a 512-wide read at `tbp=14336` wraps after exactly 256 rows, and the 'recovered title art' in the lower half of that dump was framebuffer 0 seen through the wrap. An entry whose extent leaves VRAM is now clamped and says so, and one starting at/after the end is skipped with a message. Read-only. |
| `195-perspcount` (new, cont.356c) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_PERSPCOUNT=1` (default OFF, read-only): per-flip counts of the signal the full-screen 2D correction would key on** — `draws`, `persp` (any vertex `q != 1`), `full2d` and `narrow2d` (screen-space draws at/below the width gate), printed one line per flip with a `<= WOULD FIRE` marker. Built because a census samples a WINDOW, and the failure that had to be ruled out is a one-frame aspect pop on a frame that merely happens to emit no perspective draws. **Result over a 16,982-flip save-path run (boot → menus → Pat01 gameplay):** the signal is strongly bimodal — **zero flips carry 1-10 perspective draws**, gameplay (14,278 flips) NEVER fires, and the raw rule gives 4 eras. Its only defect is a **12-flip (0.24 s) firing during a screen transition** whose frames carry 2-10 draws. A symmetric **16-flip debounce removes it**: 6 state switches in the whole run, shortest ON era 47 flips (0.94 s), shortest OFF gap 98 flips (1.96 s). ⚠ The single-flip gaps inside the menu era are EMPTY frames (`draws=0`), which cannot pop because the rule needs `full2d>0` to fire at all. |
| `196-2d-screen-pillarbox` (amends 192, cont.356e) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp`, `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h` | ★★★★★ **The full-screen 2D correction moves to PRESENTATION.** cont.356d proved a draw-level fix cannot reach the title/menu artwork: it is uploaded into the framebuffer during boot and PRESENTED from there, so no draw repaints it. The presenter now asks `ps2xGsIs2dScreen()` (the debounced frame-level classifier, row 195) and presents such a frame at the **DISPLAY-derived natural aspect** — pillarboxed — instead of the forced `PS2X_WINDOW_ASPECT`. The GL device suppresses the per-draw HUD counter-scale on those same frames, or the pixels are corrected twice. Verified on the window itself (the raster capture cannot show this — the change is in the blit): content bbox 171x138 → 213x171, i.e. **both axes x1.24 with the ASPECT UNCHANGED** (1.239 vs 1.246), which is exactly pillarbox-without-double-correction. ⚠ Gated by `PS2X_GS_HUD_ASPECT_FULL` (default OFF). ⚠ Consequence to accept: on a 16:9 display, 2D screens gain side bars while gameplay stays full width, so the image size changes at the menu↔game boundary. |
| `197-asymmetric-2d-debounce` (amends 196, cont.356f) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★★ **The 2D verdict's debounce is ASYMMETRIC, and a frame with no evidence HOLDS.** Row 196 shipped a symmetric 16-flip debounce and a user-visible artefact: leaving a 2D screen for gameplay held the verdict ON for 16 flips, so the 3D scene was presented PILLARBOXED and then snapped to full width — *"loads compressed, then stretches"*. The directions are not equally bad. A false ON pillarboxes a 3D scene and is glaring; a late ON leaves 2D art stretched a moment longer, which is what happens with the feature off anyway. So **OFF is immediate on real evidence of 3D (`_OFFDEB`, default 1) and ON is debounced (`_DEBOUNCE`, now default 4)**, a frame must carry real screen-space UI to vote 2D (`_MINUI`, default 8), and a frame with NO evidence **holds** the current state instead of voting — which is what lets OFF be immediate without the single-flip empty frames inside a menu era dropping the verdict. Measured live: wrongly-pillarboxed 3D frames **45 → 3** over ~16k flips, and 3 is the FLOOR (one frame per transition: an evidence-based rule cannot know a frame is 3D before seeing its draws). |
| `198-declare-screen-api` (amends 197, cont.356g) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★★ **The GAME declares the screen kind; the engine holds no per-game policy.** `ps2xGsDeclareScreen(kPs2xScreen2D|3D)` plus a three-way `PS2X_GS_HUD_ASPECT_FULL`: unset/`0` = **OFF on every title** (the default), `1` = DECLARED-only, `auto` = the heuristic as a **bring-up tool**. ★ Why the heuristic is no longer the default (user, and correct): its thresholds are measurements of ONE game — `minUI>=8` holds here only because these menus are glyph sprites (narrow2d 130/356/386), and a title whose menus are a few large textured quads would score 0 and never classify, silently. Per-game knowledge does not stop being per-game by being written as a threshold; it moves somewhere harder to see. ★ In DECLARED mode a frame that draws PERSPECTIVE geometry cancels the 2D state immediately — no threshold, no debounce, because "did this frame draw perspective geometry" is hardware truth and transfers to any game. That makes a mis-curated entry self-correcting: measured, LOTR declares `MainMenuScreen` 2D, the screen turns out to carry 3D, and the correction simply never engages. ⚠ The disagreement log is EDGE-triggered: a declaration is sticky, so a level-triggered one fired 16,867 times in a single run. |
| `199-screen2d-strict` (amends 198, cont.356k) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **`kPs2xScreen2DStrict` — a declaration that SKIPS the perspective gate.** `kPs2xScreen2D` is gated on evidence (a frame drawing perspective geometry cancels it), which lets a MIXED screen be declared safely. But a screen can render **decorative geometry through a perspective projection while being an entirely flat 4:3 layout**, and the gate cannot tell that from a real 3D scene — measured on LOTR's save-list screen, which draws ~258-274 perspective primitives and is visually flat (user). The gate was vetoing the whole phase. Strict says "I have looked at this screen, do not second-guess me", and is only for a screen someone has actually looked at: a strict entry on a real 3D screen WILL pillarbox it with nothing left to catch it. Verified: `declared=3 persp=26` now turns the correction ON. |
| `200-2d-handover-hold` (amends 199, cont.356l) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_HUD_ASPECT_FULL_HOLD` (default 8): keep a 2D screen corrected across the HANDOVER.** The game requests the NEXT screen a few frames before it stops drawing the current one — measured: the title screen is still drawing its own UI (`narrow2d=32`) for 3 flips after `id=17` is requested — so dropping the correction the instant the declaration turns 3D un-corrects a screen that is still on display (user: "loses the correction just before I am sent to the next one"). The hold coasts through that window. ★ EVIDENCE STILL WINS: a frame that actually draws perspective ends the hold at once, so this cannot resurrect the cont.356f artefact, which was a real 3D scene being pillarboxed. Measured over 17,797 flips: uncorrected 2D frames **35 → 23**, genuinely wrongly-corrected 3D frames **0**. |
| `201-strict-on-delay` (amends 200, cont.356o) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★ **`PS2X_GS_HUD_ASPECT_FULL_ONDELAY` (default 8): a STRICT declaration must be stable before it engages.** The screen request arrives a few frames before the game stops drawing the PREVIOUS screen (3 flips, measured on the title handover). A plain `2D` entry cannot misfire there — its `persp == 0` gate will not pass while the old 3D screen is still drawing — but a STRICT entry has no gate, so it pillarboxed the OUTGOING screen for those frames (user: "the screen changes a bit before moving to the save list"). Verified: the strict entry now engages at `persp=0` instead of on the declaration, with 0 frames of it firing over a heavily-3D frame; uncorrected 2D frames 23 → 27, the cost of the delay. |
| `202-texpack-default-off` (amends 190, cont.357b) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_texpack.cpp`, `include/runtime/gs/gs_texpack.h` | ★★★★ **The HD texture pack is DEFAULT OFF: a bare run is the vanilla game** (user, 2026-09-21 — "run as vanilla as possible and sensible, and let mods be enabled"). Row 190 made the pack find itself: unset meant `<exe dir>/mods/hd/assets` whenever that directory existed. That is opt-OUT, and it made "is the pack live?" depend on whether the player had ever dropped a PNG into a folder the repo itself ships — a fresh clone and a working clone behaved differently from the same command line, and the only symptom is art that is subtly not the game's own. Now: unset or `=0` = OFF; **`=1` = ON at the default location**; `=<dir>` = ON at that directory. The default LOCATION is unchanged, so the `<exe dir>` anchor and the packaging story from row 190 still hold — only the gate moved. ⚠ An EXPLICIT request that finds no directory now PRINTS and disables (`[texpack] pack directory not found: … -- pack DISABLED`); silence there is indistinguishable from "the pack is on and this texture has no replacement", which is the normal state for a partial pack. An empty-but-present directory is still not an error. The announce line carries the value that turned it on (`(PS2X_GS_TEXPACK=1)`) rather than `(default)`, which can no longer happen. No hardware model is touched, so there is no PCSX2 behaviour to mirror. |
| `203-upload-census` (cont.357c) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_UPCENSUS=1` (default OFF, read-only) — a per-TRANSFER census of host→local VRAM uploads, keyed by a hash of the PAYLOAD.** Built to answer whether the HD texture pack could be extended to art that never becomes a texture (`rotk_recomp/docs/NEXT.md` cont.356 item 3), and the answer it returned was **no** — see the verdict there. The key mirrors `ps2x::texpack::contentKey` (FNV-1a, dimensions mixed in) so an upload key and a texture key are the same kind of name, computable offline from a dump. ⚠ It hashes the PAYLOAD in send order, never the VRAM pages it lands on: a page carries more than one surface, the trap that would make a VRAM-keyed pack silently half-work (row 190). ⚠ It counts local→local blits separately, so a null on the host→local side is interpretable rather than ambiguous between "the art does not arrive this way" and "it arrives by a route this probe does not watch". **`PS2X_GS_UPCENSUS_MIN=<bytes>` (default 65536)** decides which transfers earn a row, because the intro FMV mints **1,064,543 distinct payload keys in one 220 s run** and fills any table before the title screen is reached; below-gate transfers are counted (`small=`/`smallbytes=`), never hidden. ★ The gate admits on EITHER the declared size or the first chunk's real byte count: `bitsPerPixel()` is the STORAGE width, and for the formats living in another surface's unused bits (PSMT4HL/T4HH/T8H) that is not the width the payload arrives in — measured, a 256x232 PSMT4HL atlas declares 4 bpp (29 KB) and delivers 237,568 bytes, so estimating alone silently dropped the two font/glyph atlases, the surfaces a pack most wants. Validated on build 1105: both reappear. Read-only and default OFF, so there is no hardware model to mirror from PCSX2. |
| `204-upabl-and-updedup` (new, cont.357d) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **The per-flip upload volume is the game's DISPLAY LIST, not a redundant clear — it is worth 6.1% of wall, and it CANNOT be deduplicated.** Two knobs, both default OFF. **(a) `PS2X_GS_UPABL=1` (ablation, known-incorrect output):** consume a host->local chunk >= `PS2X_GS_UPABL_MIN` (65536) without writing VRAM, advancing the transfer state exactly as a real upload would so the GIF state machine and every census see an unchanged sequence. It sizes the ceiling: matched-scene A/B (`vtab.sh`, 2 pairs, fixed 1500-frame window) **65.9 s -> 61.9 s = 6.1%**, ranges non-overlapping (A 59.7-64.2, B 65.2-66.6), `upMs` 9,542 -> 361. So ~43% of the upload path's own 9.5 s/200 s is on the critical path. **(b) `PS2X_GS_UPDEDUP` (1 = skip, 2 = verify-oracle): REFUTED, and the code is kept only as the measurement.** cont.357c showed the same payloads re-uploaded byte-identically every flip, which reads like free work to skip. It is not: **every payload targets the SAME rect** (`dbp=12291` page 384, `pos=(0,0)`, `dbw=1/2`, heights 288..1984), so the ~10-15 uploads of one flip OVERWRITE each other in one fixed VRAM window and the region never still holds what it held last flip. Measured on the fight replay: **hits 1.3%** (999 of 74,892 chunks, 83 MB of 18.5 GB), **88% of misses are the page-generation test** (another upload wrote those pages in between), and `upMs` **rose** 9,542 -> 11,032 — the memcmp bookkeeping costs more than the skip saves. The skip condition is sound by construction (bytes identical by memcmp, not a hash; page-gen sum equal to stored + this transfer's own bump, and bumps only increase, so any other writer can only cause a MISSED skip, never a wrong one; and it requires `s_gsSkipCpuRaster`, because the CPU rasterizer's pixel writes deliberately do not bump page generations — cont.250 measured per-draw page bumping at 5.7x). **The writer, by gdb backtrace** (`PS2X_GS_THREAD=0` so the host stack is the guest chain): guest `FUN_0013f210` writes `D1_QWC=0`, `D1_TADR=<chain head from the gp[4896]-indexed table at gp+4904>`, `D_STAT=2`, `D1_CHCR=0x145` (STR | MOD=chain | TTE | DIR=from-memory) — a VIF1 chain-mode DMA of the frame's display list, called from `sub_0014B290` on the EE scheduler; its PATH2 DIRECT packets reach `submitGifPacket(Path2, drainImmediately=true)` -> `GS::processImageData` -> `UploadImageUnlocked`. ★ **What is left is the WRITE, not the skip:** 5.0e9 px / 11.0 s ≈ 2.0 GB/s through cont.231's CT32 row-group path. No hardware model is changed by either knob (both are default OFF and the =2 oracle only reads back through `m_readVramFuncs`), so there is no PCSX2 behaviour to mirror here — the next step, a block-shaped write, is exactly where PCSX2's `GSLocalMemory::WriteImage`/`WriteColumn32` must be read first. |
| `205-upblock-row-pair-write` (new, cont.357e) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_UPBLOCK` (DEFAULT ON): the CT32 upload writes a ROW PAIR as four contiguous 16-byte stores instead of sixteen scattered dword stores — 2.0 -> 4.8 GB/s, 4.3% of wall.** Row 204 established that the game's per-frame display list re-sends ~2.4 MB of texture through a fixed VRAM window every flip, that the work is worth 6.1% of wall, and that it cannot be skipped. This makes it cheaper instead. **The layout fact:** in the CT32 column layout rows (y, y+1) of one 8-pixel group occupy ONE CONTIGUOUS 64-BYTE COLUMN — `ColumnTable32` gives the even row words 0,1,4,5,8,9,12,13 and the odd row 2,3,6,7,10,11,14,15, i.e. the two rows interleaved at 64-bit (2-pixel) granularity — so the whole transform is `_mm_unpacklo_epi64`/`_mm_unpackhi_epi64` over two source rows. ★ **Mirrored from PCSX2 `GS/GSBlock.h` `GSBlock::WriteColumn32`**, which does exactly this with `GSVector4i::sw64` over two rows at `srcpitch`; our `ColumnTable32` and PCSX2's agree because they describe the same hardware. The destination address still comes from OUR `PixelStorageTraits<C32>::Address()`, never hand-copied, so the path is exact by construction. **Bit-exactness, measured not argued:** `PS2X_GS_UPLOADVERIFY` re-read **1,593,940,016 pixels** through the generic read over a 120 s run in which this path took **16,895,787 row pairs** — **0 mismatches**, and again 1,352,040,496 pixels / 0 mismatches on the default-ON build. **Speed:** `upMs` 9,587/9,262 -> 4,132/4,166 per 200 s run, and `vtab.sh` (2 pairs, fixed 1500-frame window) **65.8 s -> 63.0 s = 4.3%**, ranges non-overlapping (A 62.8-63.2 vs B 64.1-67.6) — ~70% of row 204's 6.1% ablation ceiling. ⚠ **The wrap guard is load-bearing:** `WriteAt` masks every pixel address with `(MEMORY_SIZE - 4)`, so a write running past 4 MB continues at block 0 — a FRAMEBUFFER (row 194 lost a day to that). A 64-byte store cannot express the wrap, so a group whose column would cross the end falls back to the scalar path (measured: 0 fallbacks in this game, but the guard is what makes the path safe for any other). Preconditions: CT32 only, `dsax % 8 == 0`, `rrw % 8 == 0`, an even start row, and both rows present in the chunk; everything else keeps the cont.231 group path. Z32 is deliberately left alone — it shares the column layout but has its own block table, and no measured volume justifies the risk yet. |
| `206-vram-align-and-nt-stores` (new, cont.357f) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_memory.cpp`, `include/runtime/ps2_memory.h`, `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★ **Two follow-ons to row 205, both MEASURED, neither shipped as a default. (a) `PS2X_GS_VRAM_ALIGN` (default 4096, "=0" = the old `new uint8_t[]`):** the GS VRAM was allocated with plain `new[]`, which glibc lands 16 bytes past a page — so every 64-byte CT32 column and every 256-byte block straddled two cache lines. Proof it was real: with the plain allocation **all 81,130,211** of row 205's column writes failed a 64-byte alignment test; aligned, **0** do. ⚠ **And it is a measured NULL for speed**: 64.2 s vs 64.3 s over the matched-scene window with upMs unchanged — consecutive columns are contiguous, so the shift costs one extra line per RUN, not per write. Kept as hygiene and because (b) requires it. **(b) `PS2X_GS_UPSTREAM` (default OFF):** non-temporal stores for the row-pair write — a column is exactly one aligned cache line, so `_mm_stream_si128` can skip the read-for-ownership, with an `_mm_sfence()` per chunk because the texture decode may observe it from another thread. **The path effect is real and repeatable: upMs 4,466/4,365 -> 3,111/3,094, i.e. 4.8 -> 6.4 GB/s.** **The wall effect is NOT resolvable here:** pooled over two chains (n=4/arm) 66.4 s -> 65.6 s = 1.2% with OVERLAPPING spreads (on 64.4-66.8, off 65.6-67.2), so by this project's own rule it is no effect and does not get a default. That is consistent with row 204's ablation, which put only ~43% of this path on the critical path. Bit-exactness is unaffected and was re-checked anyway: `PS2X_GS_UPLOADVERIFY` 1,176,610,352 pixels / **0 mismatches** with `ntLines=80,398,723`. ⚠ Cost note for the next author: the `m_gsVRAMAlign` member landed in `ps2_memory.h`, which invalidated all 5,608 generated compile units and forced a 13-minute full rebuild — keep runtime bookkeeping in the .cpp. |
| `207-upstream-wall-settled` (new, cont.357g) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` (COMMENT ONLY — no code, no rebuild, `runtime.lock` deliberately left at row 206) | ★★★★ **Row 206(b)'s open question is CLOSED, and the way it closed is the reusable part: an A/B is settled by SIZING THE EFFECT AGAINST THE NOISE, not by running more of it.** Row 206 left `PS2X_GS_UPSTREAM` open with "settle it with a longer chain (n >= 6 per arm)". Done: **8 drift-cancelling ABBA blocks, 32 runs, 2.7 h, one binary (`bld-eur-vramalign-1111`), arms verified per run from `ntLines`** (A 296-318 M, B 0, `ntUnaligned`/`wrapFallbacks` 0 across all 32). Wall effect **−0.43 s = −0.65%, 95% CI [−1.77, +0.91], p=0.45** on the canonical window; **+0.18 s = +0.14%, CI [−1.97, +2.32], p=0.84** on a 4,000-frame window; **+19 frames/200 s, p=0.56** on throughput. ⇒ **ZERO**, and row 206's "−1.2%" was host-clock drift: its A runs sat at the ends of a chain on an i7-1165G7 (400 MHz-4.7 GHz laptop part) whose clock decays over a 14-minute chain — the ABBA contrast exists precisely to cancel that, and with n=8 the blocks scatter both ways (−2.24 … +2.22 s). ⚠ **THE DECISIVE NUMBER IS THE CEILING, NOT THE p-VALUE:** the 799 ms this removes is **0.56% of the window's 143.6 s even if every saved millisecond were on the critical path**, while the measured per-block noise (sd 2.42 s) puts the 95% CI half-width at **1.41% of wall** at n=8. The ceiling is **2.5-4x below the noise floor**, so resolving it here would need **n=57-137 blocks (230-550 runs, 13-32 h)** — row 206's own remedy could never have worked. Compute that ratio BEFORE spending the box. The path win (−27.5%, 16 runs/arm, non-overlapping) and bit-exactness stand; the knob stays **default OFF** because shipping it would trade the documented cache risk (the decode reads these bytes straight back; another host's cache/core count could pay more for the readback than the store saves) for at most ~0.5% nothing here can see. ⓘ Environment note for whoever wants the sharp metric: `cycles` for fixed guest work would cancel the clock drift, but `perf` is blocked on this box (`/proc/sys/kernel/perf_event_paranoid = 4`), which also silently disables the perf paths in the game harness's `hbench.sh` / `threadprof.sh`. |
| `208-hud-anchor-hook` (new, cont.358) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h`, `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★★ **`ps2xGsSetHudAnchorFn` — the engine asks the GAME where each 2D/HUD draw is anchored, and keeps its own answer when the game declines.** Row 192 gave the 2D/HUD counter-scale a fixed point hardcoded to the screen CENTRE (`p.x = 0.5 + (p.x - 0.5) * uHudScale`), which keeps every element's SHAPE correct but pulls corner elements inward — at 16:9 by `(1-0.75) * 256 = 64` raster px — so the HUD ends up pillarboxed inside a natural-aspect region while the world fills the wider frame. Re-anchoring an element to the edge it was authored against is a decision only the game can make, and only per element: the engine can see that a draw is 2D, never that it is "the bottom-left status cluster". **So the engine keeps the classification and the arithmetic and ASKS.** This is the mirror of row 198's `ps2xGsDeclareScreen` (game→engine push); this one is engine→game pull, and the two now form the presentation-policy pair. **The contract:** the hook returns the horizontal anchor in `[0,1]` of `refW`, or **ANY NEGATIVE value to DECLINE** and take the engine's centre. ★ Declining is the design, not a fallback: a game covers the elements it has actually measured and every other draw stays bit-for-bit as it was, so a policy can be curated one element at a time with no regression in between — unlike a table that has to be right before any of it is usable. **`Ps2xHudDraw`** carries the primitive bbox in the vertices' own space (XYOFFSET already subtracted, so the hook sees the coordinates the shader will), `refW`, the scissor extents, `tbp0` (0 when untextured — this game's HUD bar fills are, and no texture-keyed scheme could ever match them), the prim type and the last declared screen kind. ⓘ **No `refH` on purpose:** the anchor is horizontal (an anamorphic stretch is), and the vertical normalisation is the render target's height, which is not resolved until the batch is assembled — a guess there would be worse than the omission. **Transport:** `GsGlVertex::hudAnchor`, a `uint16_t` fixed point `anchor = (v-1)/65534`, so **32768 decodes to EXACTLY 0.5** (65534 = 2×32767, exact in IEEE) — that is the engine's default, and it reproduces row 192 bit-for-bit rather than approximately. It rides in the padding `hud2d` already sat beside, so **the vertex stays 32 bytes**, now asserted (`static_assert` on `sizeof` and `offsetof`) rather than trusted. **Cost:** the hook is invoked INSIDE the classified branch, so it fires only for draws already selected for counter-scaling — 106 of 109,729 on a Helm's Deep flip, 0.1% in both row 191 census windows — a predictable-branch load per draw and an indirect call on a thousandth of them; the y bbox pass is built only when a hook is installed, so a game without one pays nothing. **Threading:** a raw function pointer in a `std::atomic`, not a `std::function` — this is read from the batch-building path, which cont.331n established is reached from the worker thread as well as the present thread, and a `std::function` reassigned under a concurrent read is a race (same reasoning as `WriteVramFunc`). The contract requires the hook be pure and reentrant; the header says so twice. **`PS2X_GS_HUD_ANCHOR=0`** ignores an installed hook without uninstalling it, so the A/B control is the same binary with the flag off. **Verified end to end, quantitatively:** with the game's policy declining (the shipped default) a run prints no `[ws:anchor]` line and the counter-scale is unchanged; with `LOTR_WS_ANCHOR=left` the classified content moves left by **exactly 64.0 px** (x-centroid 242.65 → 178.64, xmin and xmax both −64 on flip 3300, matched captures at `PS2X_WINDOW_ASPECT=1.7778`) — which is the arithmetic prediction `(1-0.75) × 256 = 64` to 0.01 px. ⓘ No PS2 hardware behaviour is modelled or changed here (this is presentation policy, not a GS model), so there is no PCSX2 subsystem to mirror; the GL-only placement is deliberate — the CPU rasterizer is the differential oracle and must stay bit-exact, which also means this feature can never be validated by a capture hash. |
| `209-hud-anchor-scissor-origin` (new, cont.358b) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★ **`Ps2xHudDraw` carries the scissor ORIGIN, not just its extent.** Row 208 shipped `scissorW`/`scissorH` only, which is a latent hole: a left/right split-screen gives both viewports the SAME `scissorW` and differs only in the origin, so a policy keyed on extent would silently apply one viewport's anchors to the other. Adding it later would be an ABI break for every consumer, so it goes in before any game needs it. ⓘ **The game that prompted it does NOT need it** -- LOTR co-op is shared-screen (user, 2026-09-22; the ELF agrees: `coopmark1p`/`coopmark2p`, `coopLabels1`/`2`, `coopValues1`/`2`, and no split/viewport strings), so its raster layout is unchanged in co-op. This is engine-side hygiene for a split-screen title. ★ **The cycle's real finding is game-side and is recorded there** (`rotk_recomp` cont.358b): a PER-DRAW anchor rule is REFUTED by measurement -- it tears a text block in half (the left half moves left 63.4 px and the right half right 65.5 px, because one string is ~30 separate glyph draws spanning most of the raster). Any policy built on this hook must move a UI block as a UNIT. That is a fact about how games emit text, not about this game, so it belongs in the hook's own guidance: **a consumer of `ps2xGsSetHudAnchorFn` should never key on one draw's geometry alone.** |
| `210-huddump` (new, cont.358c) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★ **`PS2X_GS_HUDDUMP=1` (read-only, default OFF): one line PER CLASSIFIED DRAW inside the `PS2X_GS_PROBEFLIP` window, with the anchor the game's hook returned for it.** Row 191's `FrameHistRow` aggregates per STATE, and that aggregation actively MISLEADS for any layer drawn as many small primitives: on this game it reported a single text layer spanning `x=52..494`, i.e. the whole raster. The per-draw dump showed that is the UNION OF TWO DISJOINT CORNER BLOCKS using two different font atlases (`tbp=9237` bottom-left, `tbp=8192` top-right) separated by a 170 px empty gap — a distinction no per-state census can express, and the one a row-208 anchor policy depends on entirely. ★ **The dump prints the hook's ANSWER beside the question** (`anchor=0.000` / `1.000` / `DECLINE` / `no-hook`), because "which draws were offered to the policy" and "what the policy did with them" are different claims and only the second one validates a table. ⚠ **It reports its own bound:** the summary line gives `total=` vs `shown=` and marks a truncated dump explicitly (`PS2X_GS_HUDDUMP_MAX`, default 4000) — the first menu-screen run came back 4000 of 5120 and said so, and the re-run at a higher cap turned a sample into a census. Also prints when nothing matched, per the cont.331j rule that a silent instrument reads exactly like a null. Gated by `probeFlipOk()` and default OFF, so it costs a predictable-branch test outside the window. |
| `211-hudcluster` (new, cont.358e) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★ **`PS2X_GS_HUDCLUSTER=1` (read-only, default OFF): union-find over each flip's CLASSIFIED draws, reporting the connected blocks.** The de-risking measurement for a cluster-based anchor policy, and deliberately a CENSUS ONLY -- nothing is applied. Row 208's hook is answered PER DRAW, and cont.358d proved no per-draw geometric rule can work: a draw cannot know where its string ends, so any rule cuts strings at its own boundary. Answering per BLOCK fixes that by construction -- but only if proximity clustering actually recovers UI structure, which is a question to MEASURE rather than assume. **Method:** two rects are unioned when they come within `PS2X_GS_HUDCLUSTER_GAP` px (default 24) in BOTH axes; clustered per FLIP, not per window, since a window smears a moving element across its whole path. ⚠ The threshold errs LARGE on purpose: over-merging yields one wide cluster whose only sensible anchor is "decline" (= today's behaviour), while under-merging is the tear. **First results:** gameplay 50 draws -> **2 clusters**, exactly the two corner blocks, each carrying its own glyph atlas (`8192` with the top-right badge, `9237` with the bottom-left cluster) -- the grouping the per-draw policy could not express. Character select 95 draws -> 4 clusters at gap 16-32, and an offline threshold sweep shows **plateaus, not a knife edge** (5 clusters at gap 4-8, 4 at 16-32, 3 at 48-64, 2 at 96+), i.e. the structure is real and the default sits mid-plateau. ⚠ **Still open:** whether the three upper text blocks on that screen are separate labels or one title string -- if one string, the 4-cluster plateau would tear it, and the fix is a larger gap plus a "too wide to anchor" rule. Do not build the policy until that is measured across several screens. |
| `212-hud-run-grouping` (new, cont.358i) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★ **Group the classified draws by ARRIVAL ORDER, not position: a string is a consecutive run sharing a texture atlas, and a run boundary ("the atlas changed, or this draw is further than the gap from the previous one") is decidable the moment a draw arrives.** Every position-based scheme groups frame N's draws with frame N-1's rectangles, which on an animated screen no longer describe the layout; order is immune to that, because animation moves coordinates without reordering the strings a screen emits. The run's EXTENT still comes from the previous frame's run of the same index (a run's extent is unknown until it ends), guarded structurally: if the frame's run count differs from the previous frame's, the whole next frame declines. ⚠ **The per-frame coverage gate of the previous revision was the wrong guard** -- it asked whether every draw sat inside SOME block, which a stale map satisfies while still splitting a line across two of its blocks. ★ **A measurement caution this cycle earned the hard way:** the torn-string detector used to judge all of this called two draws "one split string" when they shared an atlas, a text line and sat within **200 px**. Real glyph gaps in this game are **3-16 px**, so that window was flagging SEPARATE LABELS on one row and reported 217-225 "torn" flips for three consecutive revisions -- including ones that were fine. Re-run at 20 px and 40 px, **every cluster-mode build scores ZERO**. The word-level tears that started this arc came from the refuted region table (row 208's consumer), not from clustering. Verified on the current build: gameplay 40 flips with an IDENTICAL per-flip verdict (38 left / 13 right / 1 declining run), `no-cluster=0`, 0 torn lines; boot menus decline 100%. |
| `213-hud-block-identity` (new, cont.358j-l) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★★ **`Ps2xHudDraw` carries the DISTINCT TEXTURES of the block a draw belongs to (`clusterTbps` / `clusterTbpCount`), which is what lets a game NAME a block instead of inferring its role from geometry.** A draw's own `tbp0` cannot identify its element -- a block's text uses the shared font atlas, the same one every menu uses -- but a block that CONTAINS the health-bar texture is unambiguously the health cluster, and the grouping puts that block's glyphs in the same block, so a policy naming it moves the numbers with their bars. ⚠ **This exists because a geometric rule does not generalise.** An "is this block off-centre?" test separated two populations by 14x across three measured scenes (menus |bias| <= 0.03, gameplay HUD >= 0.44) and then misfired on the MAIN MENU, never in that sample: a left-aligned list sits at bias ~ +0.18 against a 0.15 threshold, so it anchored, and flickered as its run structure changed with the selection. The menu is not an outlier in the distribution -- it is a legitimate member of it, and no threshold separates them. Geometry can say WHICH EDGE; only the game can say WHETHER a block should move. ⓘ **Grouping is SPATIAL (union-find), not by emission order.** Order was tried across three revisions and fails on this game because the draw stream interleaves the two HUD corners, so order-based runs fracture a block and its bars land in a different run from their own numbers (measured: gameplay fell to 19 anchored / 26 declined with visible tearing). Union-find ignores order entirely. The one-frame lag it carries is harmless once a policy is identity-based, because only a static gameplay HUD is eligible to move at all. **Verified end to end:** gameplay 40 flips with an IDENTICAL verdict (39 left / 13 right), zero declines, `no-cluster=0`, 0 torn strings; boot + main menu 179,200 draws 100% DECLINE; select screens 37,612 draws 100% DECLINE, 0 torn. |
| `214-hud-loose-block-match` (new, cont.358n) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★ **The block lookup may be LOOSE, because a block no longer decides which edge a draw goes to.** While the cluster chose the side, a sloppy match could put two parts of one element on opposite edges, so the match was strict -- and a draw that had drifted out of last frame's rect (a counter gaining a digit, an element easing into place) declined instead, which is what remained of the flicker. Now that the game's policy takes the side from the DRAW's own position and uses the block only to identify it as HUD, an imprecise match cannot change an anchor: containment gains an 8 px tolerance and falls back to the nearest block within the clustering gap. ⚠ **This is only safe in that order** -- restoring the loose match while the cluster still chose the edge is exactly the cont.358g regression. Verified: single player 60 flips, 50 carrying an identical verdict (39 left / 13 right), 0 torn strings, `no-cluster` 156 -> 42; menus 179,200 draws 100% decline. |
| `215-hud-frameid` (new, cont.358p) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_cpu_backend.h`, `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★ **`Ps2xHudDraw::frameId`** -- the display-flip counter the draw belongs to. A policy called PER DRAW has no other way to know where one frame ends, and one that must reason about a whole frame ("where were the elements I recognise, this frame?") needs exactly that. Comparing it against the last value seen is a frame boundary. It is what let the game's policy stop trusting the union-find BLOCK as its region: an in-game subtitle sits just above the HUD and overlaps it, so it merges into the same block, inherits that block's permission to move, and is then cut in half at the screen centre (measured: `Forgotten peop|le! Come to me!`, one line at y=398..411 split at x=256). With a frame boundary the policy can instead record where its OWN listed elements were this frame and anchor only what falls inside that next frame -- 219 torn flips -> 0 on the Paths of the Dead dialogue capture. |
| `216-huddump-tbpmin` (new, cont.358q) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★ **`PS2X_GS_HUDDUMP_TBPMIN` (default 0): dump a draw only if its texture base is >= this, OR the policy anchored it.** The useful draws are rare and the useless ones are not: a capture aimed at co-op gameplay spent its entire 400,000-line budget on MENU TEXT (`tbp=8192`, every one declining) and stopped at flip 2251, long before the scene it was meant to record. Setting 12000 drops the shared font atlases and keeps the HUD ART **with its verdict either way**, so one budget covers a whole play session and no flip number has to be guessed in advance. ★ **That is what exposed the residual flicker:** over a 4,276-flip session all nine of the game's listed HUD textures declined together on the same **9 frames (0.2%)** -- invisible to every 40-60 flip verification window used up to then. A per-window instrument cannot find a 0.2% event; a per-session one finds it immediately. |
| `217-pad-record-port1` (new, cont.358t) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_pad.cpp` | ★★★★ **Pad recording and replay now cover PORT 1 as well as port 0** (`PS2X_PAD_RECORD1` / `PS2X_PAD_SCRIPT1`). Row 176 restricted both to port 0 because the call counter a script is indexed by was GLOBAL, so letting port 1 advance it would have desynchronised every existing recording. The counter is now PER PORT, which fixes that at the root rather than avoiding it: port 0's index counts port 0's reads exactly as before (it only ever incremented there), so every `tmp/*.pad` replays unchanged -- verified, `first_fight.pad` still reaches gameplay with 14 HUD textures and 0 mixed verdicts. ★ **Why it was needed: CO-OP CANNOT BE REACHED WITHOUT IT.** Character select requires player 2 to choose a character, so a port-0-only recording stalls there forever -- a replay of one spent its entire 400,000-draw capture in the menus and never drew a single HUD element. Any co-op regression test, and any attempt to reproduce a co-op defect deterministically, needs both ports. |
| `218-huddump-ymin-and-scale-log` (new, cont.358v-w) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp`, `src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★ **Two read-only instruments, both of which exist because an earlier capture could not contain the failure it was being used to rule out.** (a) **`PS2X_GS_HUDDUMP_YMIN`** also keeps a draw whose top edge is at or below this y, whatever its texture and whatever the policy decided. `TBPMIN` keeps the HUD art and anything ANCHORED -- so a DECLINED draw silently vanishes from the dump, and several captures duly reported "0 anchor changes" on a build that was visibly broken, because the draws that lost their anchor were the ones being filtered out. ⚠ Any analysis of *why a draw did not anchor* needs `YMIN`. (b) **the `[gsgpu:hudscale] CHANGED` line** announces every change of the frame-wide 2D scale and the `is2dScreen` verdict; the pre-existing line printed only the first three calls, which is exactly the window in which nothing has gone wrong yet. It measured that verdict as constant (0 changes) across a co-op movement replay, which is what cleared the FOV, the presentation aspect and the 2D-screen classifier as causes of a HUD-only flicker. ⓘ A silent instrument and an instrument that cannot see the defect look identical in the log; only the second one also looks like a passing test. |\n| `219-hudfull-lazy-read` (new, cont.359) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★★ **`PS2X_GS_HUD_ASPECT_FULL` is read LAZILY (function-local static), not at static-initialisation time.** A game override may derive it (LOTR's widescreen mod `setenv()`s it from `LOTR_WIDESCREEN` in `registerMods`, cont.358o), and the namespace-scope static read it before `main()`, so the derived value was silently ignored: every declared 2D screen stayed stretched. Measured: 0 `[gs2:hudfull]` transitions in every derived run from build 1141 on; same binary (1156), variable set explicitly → ON at flip 13; build 1157 derived → ON at flip 13; vanilla → 0. Same pattern as `ps2xWindowAspect()`, which is why the presentation aspect survived the derivation. No PCSX2 counterpart (presentation plumbing, no hardware behaviour). |
| `220-gl-clear-black` (new, cont.360) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★★ **The GL arm's runtime clear is BLACK; `PS2X_GS_GLR_CLEARNAVY=1` restores the dark navy (0.04,0.05,0.14) as a diagnostic.** The navy was the cont.329 phase-1 test marker ("every presented frame is EXACTLY the clear colour (10,13,36)") and was never retired. Both runtime clears use it (target creation/realloc, and the frame-start clear), so in REAL time the boot loading screen presented a freshly created, not-yet-drawn target as 100% navy for its first flips (the user's "first loading screen is blue-ish", 2026-09-22). Under `PS2X_VIRTUAL_TIME=1` that present never happens, which is why deterministic captures showed nothing. Measured, real time, remaster env, every flip saved, same binary: flips 2-3 = 100% (10,13,36) with `CLEARNAVY=1`, (0,0,0) at default. PCSX2 clears a target it has no VRAM for to 0 (`GSTextureCache::LookupTarget`, `ClearRenderTarget(dst->m_texture, 0)`), and the CPU arm presents zeroed VRAM as (0,0,0). Alpha deliberately unchanged (1.0), so this change does one thing only. ⓘ A faint (0,0,1) vs the CPU arm's (0,0,0) on the language screen is present in BOTH arms, i.e. not the clear. Build `bld-eur-blackclr-1159`. |
