| `221-strict-2d-on-evidence` (new, cont.362) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★ **`PS2X_GS_HUD_ASPECT_FULL_ONEVIDENCE` (default ON; `=0` = the delay alone): a STRICT 2D declaration also engages on the first flip that draws NO perspective geometry**, whichever comes first with the `_ONDELAY` 8-flip delay (row 201). That flip is direct evidence the outgoing 3D screen has stopped -- what the delay waits for -- so it cannot pillarbox the outgoing screen the delay protects (the outgoing screen draws perspective). User report: 'selecting load game shows a split second of stretched background'. Measured on the id=10 -> 7 handover (deterministic, save card): backdrop persp=26 for flips 1775-1777, black 1778-1779, FADE-IN 1780-1781 (mean 12.6, stretched), pillarbox at 1782. Same binary: ON engages at **1778**, `=0` at 1782; every other `[gs2:hudfull]`/`[ws:movie]` transition identical on both cards. No PCSX2 counterpart (presentation policy). Build `bld-eur-strictev-1161`. |
| `222-raylib-sdl-desktop` (new, cont.363, branch `rumble`) | `ps2xRuntime/CMakeLists.txt` | ★★★ **`PS2X_RAYLIB_SDL` (default ON; `=OFF` = GLFW): raylib's desktop platform is built on SDL2, fetched (`release-2.30.12`) and linked statically.** Reason: rumble. raylib's GLFW backend has no vibration output (`SetGamepadVibration` logs "not available on target platform"; GLFW's joystick API is read-only), its SDL backend calls `SDL_GameControllerRumble` -- the path PCSX2 also uses (`pcsx2/Input/SDLInputSource.cpp`: SDL3 `SDL_RumbleGamepad(gamepad, large, small, ...)`, the renamed `SDL_GameControllerRumble`). The Vita and Android builds already run on SDL, so desktop was the outlier. Fetched, not system: raylib 5.5's SDL backend needs SDL >= 2.0.16 (`SDL_SetWindowAlwaysOnTop`, `SDL_OpenURL`); the distro here ships 2.0.10, which also lacks the `SDL2::SDL2` target raylib links -- and a player then needs no SDL package. `find_package(SDL2)` is satisfied by a generated stub config pointing at the fetched target. Verified (build `bld-eur-sdl-1162`): log reads `Platform backend: DESKTOP (SDL)`; save card -> Pat01 63/63, not-found 0; fresh card -> Hel01 11/11, hero moving, not-found 0. Input parity read from both backends' source: same button/axis map, triggers registered as buttons, keypad scancodes mapped. |
| `223-pad-vibration` (new, cont.363, branch `rumble`) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_pad.h`, `src/lib/ps2_pad.cpp`, `src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | ★★★ **`PSPadBackend::setVibration(port, small, large)` + `pumpVibration()`: the DualShock 2 actuators reach the host gamepad.** `setVibration` is callable from any thread (the EE-side pad HLE) and only records the bytes in atomics; `pumpVibration` runs at the top of every presenter-loop iteration on the MAIN thread, where raylib/SDL may be called, and issues `SetGamepadVibration(gp, large/255, small ? 1 : 0, 0.25 s)` -- renewed every 150 ms while nonzero (a PS2 motor holds its value; an SDL request lapses), one zero-intensity request on the transition to off, so a stalled main loop stops the motor within 250 ms instead of leaving it running. Mapping = PCSX2 (`pcsx2/SIO/Pad/PadDualshock2.cpp` Poll(): large = byte/255, small = (byte & 1) ? full : off; `pcsx2/Input/SDLInputSource.cpp`: large -> SDL low-frequency, small -> high-frequency, = raylib's (left, right)). A port with no gamepad (keyboard) gets nothing -- never faked. No caller in the runtime: the game hook decides (rotk `LOTR_RUMBLE`, default OFF). Verified headless (build `bld-eur-rumble-1164`, no pad attached): the game-side decode feeds it real fight values (large 34-99, small on/off); fresh card -> Hel01 11/11, not-found 0; save card -> Pat01 63/63 with the flag off (measured on 1163; 1164 changed only the flag-on decode). The motor itself is unverified until a human holds a pad. |
| `224-sdl-background-pad-events` (new, cont.363, branch `rumble`) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp`, `src/lib/ps2_pad.cpp` | ★★★ **SDL gamepad events are delivered while the window is unfocused** (`SDL_JOYSTICK_ALLOW_BACKGROUND_EVENTS=1`, set before `InitWindow` with no overwrite, so a user export wins), plus the always-on **`[pad:device]` census**: one line per gamepad CONNECT/DISCONNECT with its slot and name. Found by the user's first SDL run: the 8BitDo did nothing at the language screen. SDL's default drops joystick events without focus; GLFW polled the pad regardless, so this restores the pre-row-222 behaviour. A standalone raylib+SDL window proved the pad's buttons (A = 7, d-pad, triggers 10/12) arrive in slot 0 when focused. Verified by the user on `bld-eur-padfocus-1165`: the pad drives the menus; the log reads `[pad:device] gamepad slot 0 CONNECTED: Xbox One Wireless Controller`. |
| `225-keyboard-default-player2` (new, cont.363, branch `rumble`) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_pad.cpp` | ★★★ **The keyboard defaults to PLAYER 2 when a gamepad has player 1** (user, 2026-09-23: "the keyboard should default to player 2"), with the whole single-player layout -- nobody else shares it. `PS2X_PAD2` default `auto` = a real second gamepad, else (one gamepad) the keyboard; no gamepad = keyboard is player 1 and port 1 absent, unchanged. **`PS2X_PAD2=pad`** = the pre-225 `auto` (a real second gamepad only); `keys` / `off` unchanged. The layout block moved verbatim into `keyboardFullLayout()` so port 0 and the solo-P2 port share one definition. Found when the user's keyboard "did not work" with the 8BitDo connected: since upstream the keyboard was consulted only when NO gamepad drove port 0 (`else`, narrowed to `else if (port <= 0)` by row 176) -- not an SDL regression. ⚠ Consequence, accepted by the user: with one gamepad plugged in port 1 is CONNECTED, so the game's "second pad?" query (0x142C80(6), cont.346d) answers yes and the New/Load menu gains its co-op entry -- ⚠ so pad-script replays recorded on the solo menu need `PS2X_PAD2=pad` whenever a gamepad is attached to the box. Verified (`bld-eur-kbdp2-1166`, 8BitDo attached): `[padsock] socket 1 -> pad port 1, input=LIVE` + the `[pad2] player 2 = KEYBOARD` banner. |
| `226-settings-layer` (new, cont.363b) | `ps2xRuntime/include/ps2_settings.h`, `src/lib/ps2_settings.cpp`, `src/main.cpp`, `CMakeLists.txt` | ★★★★ **The player settings layer: `<exe dir>/config/settings.ini` (INI) resolved into the env flags, applied by one RE-EXEC.** A curated schema, registered at static init from any TU (the runtime owns its `PS2X_*` rows -- `display.renderer`/`render_scale`/`window_scale`, `audio.enabled`, `input.keyboard`, `enhancements.hd_textures`, preset `original`; the GAME registers its own beside the code that reads them). Precedence per option: explicit env > file > preset > the flag's built-in default; every resolution is logged with its source (`[settings] display.widescreen = true (file:2) -> LOTR_WIDESCREEN=1`). Bad values / unknown keys = WARNING, never silent. First run writes a self-documenting file with every option commented (= no change). ★ Applied by `execv` of the resolved exe path once (guard `PS2X_SETTINGS_APPLIED`): static-init flag readers run before `main()` (the cont.359 trap), so an in-process `setenv` is not enough. ⚠ exec'ing the `/proc/self/exe` LINK renamed the process `exe` (broke `pgrep -x`); fixed before commit. `PS2X_SETTINGS=<path>` / `=0` (disable; the replay harness passes it). Windows: `_putenv_s`, no re-exec (warns). PCSX2 keeps user settings in an INI (`PCSX2.ini`) the same way. Verified (`bld-eur-settings-1170`): template, validation, env-wins, preset + file override, process name, both card states with the layer disabled. |
| `227-launcher` (new, cont.363c, branch `launcher`) | `ps2xRuntime/include/ps2_launcher.h`, `ps2_settings_menu.h`, `src/lib/ps2_launcher.cpp`, `ps2_settings_menu.cpp`, `ps2_settings.{h,cpp}`, `src/main.cpp`, `CMakeLists.txt` | ★★★ **A pre-game LAUNCHER: the settings table as a controller-first menu, plus a disc check, before the game boots.** Pattern taken from the reference projects (Zelda 64: Recompiled's RmlUi launcher + in-game config menu; Unleashed Recompiled's installer wizard + in-game options): plain raylib for now. `SettingsMenu` draws/navigates ANY schema into a rectangle and owns no window or device, so the same widget can later be drawn over the game. The launcher runs in its own short-lived window; on Play it writes `settings.ini` (only the preset + explicitly set options; unknown hand-written lines are kept) and `applyAtStartup(..., forceReexec=true)` re-execs into the game, so the game never shares a window or an init with it. Disc check (game-agnostic): the ELF exists and the disc's own `SYSTEM.CNF` BOOT2 names it; a missing ELF blocks Play. Values show their source (set / preset / dim default / "locked" when an env flag pins it). Shown unless `PS2X_SETTINGS=0` (replay harness), the re-exec'd start, `PS2X_LAUNCHER=0` (engine `04_run_game.sh` passes it for LOGGED runs), or `[launcher] show = false`; `PS2X_LAUNCHER=1` forces. Settings API added for UIs (`schema/presets/readValues/writeValues/validate/environmentOverride`). | `228-launcher-title-screen-and-mods` (new, cont.363c, branch `launcher`) | `ps2xRuntime/include/ps2_launcher.h`, `ps2_mods.h`, `ps2_settings{,_menu}.h`, `src/lib/ps2_launcher.cpp`, `ps2_mods.cpp`, `ps2_settings{,_menu}.cpp`, `src/main.cpp`, `CMakeLists.txt` | ★★★ **The launcher reshaped like the reference projects' (Zelda 64: Recompiled `assets/launcher.rml`: the game's title + a short menu — Select ROM/Start game, Setup controls, Settings, Mods, Exit — version in a corner; Lato font): a TITLE SCREEN (the game's registered name, "Recompiled", disc status; Start game / Settings / Mods / Controls / Exit; build version) with Settings, Mods and Controls as sub-screens.** User asked for the reference look (2026-09-23) and for a Mods screen: browse mods, read each one's description, switch it on/off. **Mods:** every `<exe dir>/mods/<id>/` folder is listed; an optional `mod.ini` ([mod] name/description/author/version) DESCRIBES it and holds no settings (user: all settings in one place, `config/settings.ini`); the switch is the settings option `mods.<id>` found by folder name, so env > file > preset > default applies and every `[mods]` option leaves the Settings list. `registerAlias(old, new)` keeps renamed options working (`enhancements.hd_textures` -> `mods.hd`, the game's `display.widescreen` -> `mods.widescreen`). `registerGameInfo()` = the game's name + version (window title too). Lato (SIL OFL 1.1) downloaded at configure time and embedded, raylib-font fallback. | `229-glr-split-instrument` (new, cont.371) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp` | ★★ **`PS2X_GS_GLR_SPLIT=1` (default OFF, instrument only): where the GL device thread's wall goes.** Every 256 resolves `[gsgpu:split]`: device-thread idle vs busy by job kind (draw / snap / frame / other), a `glFinish` just before the resolve blit splitting the synchronous readback into GPU wait vs blit+read, the hi-res readback + row flip, `GL_TIME_ELAPSED` summed over draw jobs (upper bound on GPU busy), and producers parked on the full job queue. Result (2×/4× Helm's Deep fight): the readback COPY is < 1 ms (cont.365's 19.7 ms was GPU wait); the device thread is 62-72% idle at 2× and near GPU saturation only at 4×, where the 8192-job queue absorbs it (producers parked 0-2%). Costs ~2 ms mean update interval while on. |
| `230-gs-worker-scan-cache` (new, cont.371-372) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | ★★★★★ **The GS worker's per-draw page walks made exact O(chunks): `PS2X_GS_GLR_SCANCACHE` default ON (`=0` old path, `=2` oracle, `=3` mutation).** Always-on `[gsgpu:items]` counters `prodWaitMs`/`prodWaits` (GsPipeline parked in `enqueueWork`) and `runMs` (worker time in draw runs) showed the worker as THE gate in heavy scenes (95-99% busy, GsPipeline parked 34-55%, EE ring back-pressure 28-52%). ~55-60% of it was per-draw walks of page generations: the draw-path seed scan re-walking the whole target after any unrelated transfer (cont.331o's one-shot is only consumed on own-page dirt: 93 M scans/run) and `gs2TargetGenSum` (128 pages) per draw. Fix: running sums per 64-page chunk beside `g_gs2PageGen`/`g_gs2PageXferGen`, bumped in their only writers (all +1), read by `gs2WindowGenSum` (whole chunks + page edges = the same value); and a thread_local per-target seed-scan memo (page range + the range's transfer-generation sum read before the scan) -- equal sum ⇒ no bump ⇒ the scan would emit nothing and change nothing, so it is skipped (owner-honouring scans only). PCSX2 does this push-side: `GSTextureCache::InvalidateVideoMem` records dirty rects on the targets a transfer overlaps and `Target::Update` consumes them before use -- no per-draw rescan; the memo gives the pull-side scan the same cost shape without changing which pages are seeded. Oracle: 0 bad of 56.7 M skips; mutation 72.2 M bad (oracle can fail); chunked-sum mismatch 0.003% = the pre-existing torn read against `WriteVram`/reset bumps on another thread. Matched fight (`vtab`, 2×): 53.8 → 49.0 s (−8.9%, disjoint ranges); worker on-CPU 85-88% → 47-56%; EE back-pressure 5.4-16 s → 0.6-1.4 s per run. User play-test: "much smoother, Osgiliath little to no choppiness". |
| `231-user-dir-platform-config` (new, rotk platform-plan item 6.4) | `ps2xRuntime/include/ps2_user_dir.h`, `src/lib/ps2_user_dir.cpp`, `include/ps2_settings.h`, `src/lib/ps2_settings.cpp`, `src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp`, `src/lib/ps2_mods.cpp`, `src/lib/ps2_launcher.cpp`, `src/main.cpp`, `CMakeLists.txt` | ★★★ **User state (settings.ini + the memory cards) lives in the PLATFORM CONFIG FOLDER** (user decision, 2026-09-25), as in Zelda 64: Recompiled and Unleashed Recompiled -- and as PCSX2 itself does (pcsx2-qt: `~/.config/PCSX2` by default, a `portable.ini`/`portable.txt` beside the executable switches to portable mode). `ps2x::userdir::directory()`: `PS2X_USER_DIR` > `<exe dir>/config` when `portable.txt` sits beside the executable (the cont.346q layout) > `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` (absolute only) or `~/.config` / `%APPDATA%` / `~/Library/Application Support`, + `/<exe name>` so the engine stays game-agnostic and two games never share a folder. `settingsFile()` and the memory-card default root use it (`PS2X_MC_ROOT` still wins); mods stay in `<exe dir>/mods` (installed content). `migrateLegacy()` at the top of `main()` COPIES an existing `<exe dir>/config/{settings.ini,mc0,mc1}` into the new folder for whichever it lacks -- never deletes or overwrites, idempotent across the settings re-exec. Launcher text no longer names a path. Runtime only: no recompiler change, no regen. Verified on bld-eur-userdir-1193/1194: copy byte-identical, save-present boot → Pat01 63/63 from the new card folder, second start copies nothing, portable.txt reads `<exe dir>/config` and writes nothing to the platform folder. |
| `232-padsock-any-port` (new, rotk 4-player mod, 2026-09-25) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_dbcman_hle.cpp` | `buildPadPayload` reads the pad backend for EVERY socket (`socketIndex < kMaxSockets`, was `< 2`). The original game opens sockets 0/1 only, so it is unaffected; a mod that opens a third/fourth libpad2 socket (rotk `mods/fourplayer`, players 3/4) now gets pad port 2/3 = the 3rd/4th gamepad (`nthGamepad`), and a port with no device still reports "no pad" (`portHasDevice`), as before. The game's libpad2 socket table (`0x2FA008`, 820 B/socket) has room for 16. PCSX2 parity: more than two pads exist on hardware only behind a multitap (PCSX2 `SIO/Multitap`, ports 0-1 x slots 0-3); this HLE already abstracts sockets as "socket N = port N" (patch 181), so the change widens that abstraction, not the hardware model. |

With `PS2X_PAD_SCRIPT2`, rotk's player 3 socket reaches libpad2 state 1 and the hero walks on port 2 alone. PCSX2 parity: as row 232 -- more than two pads are multitap slots on hardware; this extends the HLE's socket-N = port-N abstraction. |

| `239-vu-memory-special-range` (new, rotk, 2026-10-01) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ps2_address.h` | ★ **Upstream bug, generic.** `Ps2IsPhysicalSpecialAddress` covered `[VU0 DATA, end of VU1 MICRO)` = `0x11004000..0x1100BFFF`, so VU0 micro (`0x11000000..`) and **VU1 data** (`0x1100C000..0x1100FFFF`) were treated as RAM: the recompiler emitted the constant-address `FAST_*` path and the runtime `READ*/WRITE*` macros skipped `Load/Store*`, so the access landed MASKED in main RAM. rotk: `sq $zero` x4 to `0x1100C840` (fn `0x147940`, a VU1 data clear) zeroed 64 bytes of the HUD texture set at `0x0100C840` -> a green dash under the health bar that real hardware does not draw. Now `[0x11000000, 0x11010000)`; `PS2Memory::mapVuMemory` already mapped all four. Needs `ps2_recomp` rebuilt + a full regen (4 constant stores re-emitted as `runtime->Store128`). Verified: the texture set is byte-identical to PCSX2's eeMemory, the CPU arm's frames differ from before ONLY in the dash's 96 pixels. PCSX2: `pcsx2/Memory.cpp` `memMapVUmicro` (VU0 micro 0x11000000, VU0 data 0x11004000, VU1 micro 0x11008000, VU1 data 0x1100c000, 0x4000 each). |

| `241-stop-cleanly` (new, rotk, 2026-10-02) | `ps2xRuntime/include/ps2_shutdown.h` (new), `src/main.cpp`, `src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | **The runner always ends.** rotk 2026-10-01 (4-player Min02 freeze): after a `[guest-branch:missing-target]` the runner kept going on corrupt guest state, ignored `timeout`'s SIGTERM and grew in memory until the machine locked up. Three causes, three fixes: (1) the default `ContinueToTarget` policy returned `true` for a call, so the generated caller resumed at the fallthrough (the call was silently skipped) -- the runner now sets `PS2X_MISSING_TARGET` (default `stop`): log, `[runtime:fatal]`, `requestStop()`, exit code **3**; `continue` / `skip` keep the old debug policies. The fatal exit is a flag the runner's `main()` sets, so the unit tests' `Stop` policy is unchanged. (2) SDL turns SIGTERM/SIGINT into a quit event and the present loop then JOINED the EE thread, which never reached a stop check -- `main()` now installs its own SIGTERM/SIGINT handler after `initialize()` (over SDL's): record the signal, a watcher thread calls `requestStop()`, exit code **128+N**; a repeat within 1 s is the same request (GNU `timeout` signals the child and its process group), a later one `_exit`s at once. (3) every stop arms a watchdog (`ps2_shutdown.h`): if the process is still alive `PS2X_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_MS` (default 5000) after the stop it flushes and `_Exit`s with the recorded code -- the end of the run loop arms it too, so a hung EE join is bounded for a window close as well. Kept out of `ps2_runtime.h` (every generated unit includes it). Verified (rotk `bld-eur-stopclean2-1330`): SIGTERM in play -> clean teardown, exit 143, watchdog not needed; a bad JAL target injected under gdb -> `[runtime:fatal]`, clean teardown, exit 3; SIGTERM with the EE thread parked in a `jmp .` loop under gdb -> watchdog forced exit 143 after 5 s. PCSX2: n/a (host process lifetime, no hardware modelled; PCSX2 likewise stops the VM on an unrecoverable CPU fault rather than running on). |

The `.gitignore` loses `/ipu_mpeg2.h` (the game deleted that module). Verified (rotk `bld-eur-noffmpeg-1333`): save-card replay -> boot FMVs, menus, level-intro FMV, Pat01; fresh-card replay -> legal, intro FMV, Hel01 fight; frames inspected. PCSX2: n/a (build configuration; PCSX2 decodes IPU in its own `IPU/` code, which is what the game uses here). |

Each works on the clone it lives in; the engine's old names are now wrappers. Behaviour unchanged (same flags, same `tmp/.build_status` marker, same skip-worktree guard); `ps2x-setup.sh` checks cmake >= 3.21 and takes the compilers from `command -v`. (2) With no argument the runner reads `SYSTEM.CNF` from `$PS2_GAMEFILES` or `<exe dir>/gamefiles/` and boots what its `BOOT2` line names -- how a PS2 picks a disc's executable -- instead of exiting with "Pass the guest ELF as argv[1]". | `246-guest-launch-args` (new, rotk, 2026-10-03) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/Syscalls/System.cpp` | **Launch arguments for the game: `PS2X_GUEST_ARGS`.** SetupThread (syscall 0x3C) ignored `$a3`, the crt0's args area, so every game saw argc 0. The real kernel copies the arguments ExecPS2/LoadExecPS2 stored into that area as `{ s32 argc; u32 argv[16]; char payload[256]; }` (ps2sdk crt0 `_args`), argv[0] = the boot path. Now: with `PS2X_GUEST_ARGS` set (space-separated, the arguments after argv[0]) SetupThread fills it, argv[0] = `PS2X_GUEST_ARGV0` or `cdrom0:\<ELF name>;1`; over 16 arguments / 256 bytes the rest is dropped with a log line. Unset (default) = the area is left as is, unchanged behaviour. PCSX2 delivers its launch arguments by rewriting EELOAD's argv (`pcsx2/R5900.cpp` `eeloadHook` / `eeloadHook2`) and lets the real kernel copy them: same layout, same argv[0]-first convention. Why: rotk USA's online co-op starts from `-online -host` / `-online -ip <a>` / `-opm <block>` (the lobby's LoadExecPS2 arguments). Verified (USA, gdb at `0x22D2E0`): `-online -host` -> online `[gp+0x26E0]=1`, flags `[gp+0x273A]=0x209` (defaults 0x208 + host); without the variable the boot is unchanged (Title at 26.1 s). |
| `247-vu1read-fence-knob` (new, rotk, 2026-10-03, MEASUREMENT ONLY) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_gs_pipeline.cpp` | **`PS2X_GS_FENCE_VU1READ=0` skips the stage-2 VU1-read fence** (site 1; default 1 = fence, unchanged). Unsafe by design -- an EE read of VU1 memory then races the pipeline consumer -- it exists to size what that fence costs. No hardware modelled, no PCSX2 counterpart (PCSX2 runs VU1 on the EE's timeline). rotk EUR heavy fight at 60 fps, `vtab.sh` 2 pairs: every fence of the run is this site (one EE read of VU1 data `0x1100C840` per frame, a dead stats copy); without it the EE waits in back-pressure instead (53-57 s vs 68-74 s of fence wait) and the game gains only ~8% (30.5/28.9 -> 32.9/32.1 updates/s): the pipeline thread is the pole. |

The arguments (path + argv after argv[0], read from the syscall registers) go to a handler the GAME may register (`ps2x::loadexec::setHandler`, static init); default: an exec of the running ELF restarts the process (`execv` of the resolved /proc/self/exe with the recorded host argv) with `PS2X_GUEST_ARGS` = the new arguments (row 246 delivers them) and the launcher skipped; any other path is logged and the runner stops cleanly. PCSX2 runs the real kernel's LoadExecPS2 (R5900OpcodeImpl.cpp only watches syscall 7 for its debugger), so the closest static-recomp equivalent of "reset the EE and load the ELF" is a restart. Verified (rotk USA): the main menu's Online entry -> `[loadexec] 'cdrom0:\ONLINEFE\ONLINE.ELF;1' argc=2 [-opm] [...]` -> the game's handler restarts into its launcher page. |

Text rows edit in place (typing, Backspace, Ctrl+V; while editing, letters are not commands). Each page gets a main-menu entry; `PS2X_LAUNCHER_PAGE=<id>` opens the launcher on it. | `250-fio-access-mode` (new, rotk, 2026-10-03) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/Syscalls/FileIO.cpp` | **fioOpen: the access mode is a 2-bit value.** `read = flags & RDONLY \|\| flags & RDWR` / `write = flags & WRONLY \|\| flags & RDWR` with RDWR = 3 made every `O_RDONLY` (1) open a write -> `"r+b"`, which fails with EACCES on a read-only disc copy (rotk USA: the existence check of `ONLINEFE/ONLINE.ELF` failed, so the Online entry never reached its exec). Now `access = flags & RDWR` compared as a value, as PCSX2 does (`pcsx2/IopBios.cpp` open: `switch (flags & IOP_O_RDWR)`) and as our memory-card path already did (`Stubs/MemoryCard.cpp openMcHostFile`). |
| `251-syscall-override-fallback` (new, rotk, 2026-10-03) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/Kernel/Syscalls/System.cpp` | **A syscall override whose handler is not recompiled code falls back to the built-in syscall; GetEntryAddress hands out a kernel scratch block for an empty slot.** The SDK's kernel patch (rotk USA `0x121628`, at every boot) copies kernel code to 0x80075000 and points syscall 0x5B there, installs its own kCopy for 0x5A, and re-installs 0x54-0x59 (the event-flag calls) from GetEntryAddress. Those handlers are not code we can run: the override path answered KE_ERROR, so GetEntryAddress returned -1, the event-flag syscalls failed for the whole session, and LoadExecPS2's argument buffer (`GetEntryAddress(3)`, the SDK reuses that kernel spot as scratch) landed at 0xFFFFFFFF -> an exec with an empty path. Now: a non-code handler -> the built-in implementation (logged once per syscall); an empty table slot (0 / -1) -> `0x80012400` (0x400 bytes after the 128-entry table, unused by the runtime). PCSX2 runs the real BIOS, which executes the copied kernel code and whose RFU slots point at real kernel addresses. |
| `252-vif-unpack-templated` (new, rotk, 2026-10-03) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_vif1_interpreter.cpp` | **The VIF1 UNPACK write loop, templated per shape; `PS2X_VIF_FASTUNPACK=2` (new default; `=1` = the row-73 SSE loop, `=0` = the generic loop).** The row-73 loop was 29.9 s of the GsPipeline thread's 190 s on the EUR 60-fps fight (`PS2X_VIF_PROF`; 75 M unpacks x 36.7 vectors, ~11 ns/vector): every vector paid a divide + modulo by `wl` (93% of vectors have cl = wl = 1), a runtime switch on the component count and four blends. Now: one instantiation per (VL, VN, sign, mode 0/1), write cycles walked as nested loops (no divide; `wl == 1` hoists the masks), and PCSX2's mask form precomputed per write cycle, `out = (data & D) \| (old & K) \| C` (`x86/Vif_UnpackSSE.cpp` `VifUnpackSSE_Simple::doMaskWrite` + `nVifMask`). Rules unchanged from row 73 (carried lanes, S broadcast, mode-1 ROW add before the select, cycle cap 3); same fallbacks (cl < wl, mode 2/3, V4-5). **Verified:** `PS2X_VIF_FASTVERIFY=1` 56 M unpacks, 0 mismatches vs the generic loop (boot -> Hel01 fight); write loop 29.9 s -> 17.1 s; `vtab.sh` 3 pairs, same binary `=1` vs default: frames 1000-2500 46.9 s -> 45.3 s (-3.4%, no overlap), +5% hero frames in 200 s. |

PCSX2 reference: the MTVU -> MTGS hand-off (`Gif_Unit.h` `FinishGSPacketMTVU` / `gsPackQueue`). Also **`PS2X_GS_FLIPHASH=1`** (diagnostic, default OFF): a whole-VRAM hash at every flip snapshot (the FIFO point, independent of presentation pacing). **Verified (rotk EUR, CPU renderer, virtual time):** split off vs off 0 diffs / 2837 flips (determinism), off vs on 0 diffs / 2778 and 2768 flips (boot -> Hel01 fight). **Speed (GL, fps 60, first_fight):** EE fence wait 49 s -> 12 s per 240 s; `vtab.sh` 3 pairs 44.4 s -> 43.2 s (overlapping: A 41.8-46.0, B 43.1-43.3), real time 26.0 -> 26.7 updates/s with row 252. The pole moved to the EE thread (74% on-CPU; a third of it memmove: the VIF1 chain buffer built by `_M_range_insert`, then copied again by the ring's `takePayload`). Default OFF until the EE stops being the pole. |

Now `ps2gs::enqueueVif1Owned` takes the buffer as is (capture tap unchanged, producer side), and VIF1 chains (pipeline threaded only) start from `ps2gs::takeBuffer()`, the largest buffer of the ring's payload pool, which the consumer recycles after execution. PCSX2 analogue: MTVU/MTGS copy each packet once into a fixed ring. **Verified (rotk EUR):** flip hash 0 diffs / 2885 flips vs the pre-change baseline (CPU renderer, virtual time); pool misses 5.5-7.3k -> 2.5-4.7k per run; EE 25.7 -> 24.4 ms of CPU per update (real time, single runs); `vtab.sh` 3 pairs 45.2 -> 44.0 s (overlapping). |

PCSX2 parity: `vu0ExecMicro` (VU0micro.cpp) does no scheduler reset on VCALLMS. **Verified (rotk EUR):** flip hash 0 diffs / 2885 flips (CPU renderer, virtual time); per-thread profiles off/on/on/off on the 60-fps fight: EE 23.7 / 20.8 / 21.9 / 23.5 ms of CPU per update, 29.6 / 31.1 / 30.4 / 29.6 updates/s (`resetScheduler` 5-6.7% of the EE -> absent). |

Now a cached source `Sound` per sample key (replaced when the PCM's hash / length / rate changes: keys are RAM addresses a bank reload can reuse), `LoadSoundAlias` per note (shared data, own pitch / volume / pan), `UnloadSoundAlias` on every unload path; a source lives while any alias plays (`shared_ptr`) and `stopAll` clears the cache while the device is open. Same converter on the same input: the converted data is identical. PCSX2 analogue: SPU2 voices read the sample data in place (`SPU2/Mixer.cpp`), no per-key-on copy. **Verified (rotk EUR, 60-fps fight, profiles off/on/on/off):** EE 22.0 / 20.7 / 21.1 / 23.3 ms of CPU per update, resampler 5.5% of the EE -> absent; 0 audio warnings, equal retrigger counts (20-22). |
| `257-glr-target-read-map` (new, rotk, 2026-10-04) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/gs/gs_gpu_device.h`, `src/lib/gs/gs_gpu_device.cpp`, `src/lib/gs/gs_cpu_backend.cpp` | **GL renderer: a read of a PROMOTED render target is addressed exactly, `PS2X_GS_GLR_TGTMAP` (default ON; `=0` = the cont.344 mirror `v = 1 - t`).** The guest's s,t are normalised by its texture size (2^TW x 2^TH), but a promoted target is a GL texture sized by the target's extent x scale (grow-only), guest row y stored at GL row (refH - y) * scale. Correct: u = s*texW*scale/allocW, v = (refH - t*texH)*scale/allocH -- the old `1 - t` only when texH == refH and allocH == refH*scale: true for a 512-line PAL frame, false for a 448-line NTSC one, so every target read on the USA disc was stretched by 512/448 and mirrored about the wrong centre. `GsGlGroup` carries the read's texW/texH (state groups split on it for target reads), `GlTarget` its logical refW/refH; uniform `uTgtMap`. Derived from this renderer's own target layout (DrawBatchGl's row rule), not ported from PCSX2 -- PCSX2 not consulted for this row; the CPU renderer and PCSX2's output are the oracles. **Verified:** rotk USA Min02 replay (`usa_min02_float.pad`) -- the hero's shadow composite moves from below his feet to under them, matching the CPU renderer and PCSX2 (user screenshot); EUR Min02 replay mapping on vs off: 17 of 20 flips pixel-identical, the other 3 differ only in continuously animated effects (capture phase). |

The copies read 40-byte vertices with 16-byte loads right after writeRegisterPacked wrote them field by field -- failed store-to-load forwarding (perf annotate put 63% of GS::vertexKick on that copy; that figure is skid-prone, see below). The base is file-scope (`s_vtxBase`), not a GS member: gs_frontend.h reaches the generated code. **Verified (rotk EUR):** flip hash ring off vs on 0 diffs / 2973 flips (CPU renderer, virtual time); per-thread profiles off/on/on/off on the 60-fps fight: updates/s 30.97 -> 31.78 and 29.78 -> 30.65 (+~2.7% in both pairs), GsPipeline 27.6 -> 26.6 and 29.1 -> 28.5 ms/update. Modest; vertexKick's share itself moved 9% -> 4-5% between sessions, so the annotate overstated the stall. |
| `259-gs-batch-aggregate` (new, rotk, 2026-10-04) | `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/gs/gs_frontend.cpp` | **`GS::buildDrawBatch` builds the per-primitive batch with a full aggregate initialiser, `PS2X_GS_BATCHFAST` (default ON; `=0` = value-initialise then assign, as before).** `GSPrimitiveBatch batch{}` zero-filled all 312 bytes with `rep stosq` before every field was assigned (perf annotate, GsPipeline, 60-fps fight: 42% of buildDrawBatch on that instruction); default-initialising is not enough (the NSDMIs `vertices{}` / `state{}` still value-initialise), so the fast path returns a prvalue with every member given -- the same values, vertices past vertexCount = `GSVertex{}` as before; only padding bytes differ and nothing reads them (no raw hash / memcmp of a batch). The ring slots wrap by compare instead of a modulo. Not a hardware-model change (PCSX2 `GSState::VertexKick` likewise fills its vertex/index buffers in place, no per-primitive zeroed struct). **Verified (rotk EUR):** flip hash 0 diffs / 6213 flips vs the `=0` arm (CPU renderer, virtual time); buildDrawBatch 5.2/5.8% -> 1.9/2.2/2.2% of GsPipeline over 3 runs (~1 ms/update by share); the whole-thread wall effect is inside run-to-run noise. **Tried and dropped in the same cycle:** a PACKED GIF loop with its cursor and state in locals and RGBAQ/ST/UV inlined -- processGIFPacket + writeRegisterPacked 13.3/13.6% -> 13.4/14.2%, no gain: the remaining cost is the packet-data load and the descriptor-switch mispredictions, not loop overhead. |

Real-time on-CPU ms/update (threadprof, 2 mirrored pairs) did NOT resolve it: 31.6/32.4 -> 31.5/32.2 (inside the host-clock noise). **Verified:** `PS2X_VU1_PROGVERIFY` over 3.2 M programs vf = vi = qp = pc = kick = 0, non-sticky flag mismatches at the baseline rate (392.9k vs 393.7k), mem = the pre-existing `startPC=0x140` class (22 vs 56 without the hack); only STICKY status bits diverge more (126k -> 480k) and no guest code reads them (the ELF's 27 CFC2 read only VU0 VI 6/8/11, FBRST and VPU-STAT -- never the status/MAC/clip registers 16-18). | `261-static-libstdcxx` (new, rotk, 2026-10-05) | `scripts/ps2x-build-game.sh` | **The runner links the C++ runtime statically, `PS2X_STATIC_LIBSTDCXX` (default ON; `=0` = dynamic, as before).** `-static-libstdc++ -static-libgcc` join `-pthread -fuse-ld=lld` in the runner's link flags. The runner is compiled with gcc-13 and needed its libstdc++ (GLIBCXX 3.4.32) at start-up, which most distributions do not ship -- so a game built in a container (or on a machine with gcc-13 from a PPA) would not start on a player's desktop. Its dynamic dependencies are now glibc + libEGL only; SDL2 and raylib were already linked in and load the rest (X11/Wayland, GL, audio, udev, dbus) at run time. Build-system only, no hardware modelled (no PCSX2 counterpart). Why: a game repo's Docker build environment (Ubuntu 20.04, glibc 2.31) must produce a binary that runs on the host. **Verified (rotk USA):** see the game repo's progress.md, "Docker build environment". |

Upstream 75d729c split RAM at 0x120000 (images bump-allocated below, a bump heap above, never reusing space): rotk's AUDIOPF (768 KB + 32 KB + thread stacks) exhausted the 896 KB heap while ~800 KB above the loaded images stayed unused, and its first `CreateThread` failed with KE_NO_MEMORY (-400) -> `start` returned NO_RESIDENT_END. With one pool all 8 rotk IRX load and AUDIOPF stays resident. `maxFreeMemory` = the largest free gap. PCSX2 runs the real BIOS sysmem (first-fit block list), which this mirrors. **(b) `PS2X_IOP_IMPORTLOG=1`** (diagnostic, default OFF): one `[iop:import] lib:ord a0-a3 ra -> v0 disposition` line per IOP import call. Default rotk config unaffected (no IRX reaches the IOP): Min02 replay 3,006 flips bit-identical. |
| `268-sif-command-channel` (new, rotk, 2026-10-06) | `ps2xIOP/src/emulator/services/iop_rpc.{h,cpp}`, `iop_emulator.{h,cpp}`, `ps2xIOP/include/ps2x/iop/iop_subsystem.h`, `iop_subsystem.cpp`, `ps2xRuntime/include/ps2_runtime.h`, `ps2xRuntime/src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp`, `Kernel/Syscalls/RPC.cpp`, `Kernel/Stubs/SIF.cpp` | **The SIF command channel between the EE and the emulated IOP, both ways** (upstream's IOP had sifcmd 4-11 as no-ops and the EE `sceSifSendCmd` dropped every command). IOP side, after ps2sdk `iop/system/sifcmd` (`exports.tab` ordinals, `sifcmd.c`): 32 software registers (6 `sceSifGetSreg`, 7 `sceSifSetSreg`); 8 `sceSifSetCmdBuffer` / 9 `sceSifSetSysCmdBuffer` keep the module's tables in IOP RAM (8-byte user, 12-byte system entries); 10/11 Add/RemoveCmdHandler write them (system slots in sifcmd's own table until a buffer is given). EE -> IOP: `sceSifSendCmd` (the `ps2_syscalls` one, row 72's t0/t1 arguments) copies the extra data into IOP RAM at once (head of the SIF1 chain; a destination outside IOP RAM is dropped, never wrapped) and queues the packet; the IOP's run loop hands it to the handler like `_sceSifCmdIntrHdlr` (index < table size, non-null handler, a copy of the packet + harg), and runs sifcmd's own `_set_sreg` / `_change_addr` / init for system cids with no guest handler. IOP -> EE: `SIF_CMD_SET_SREG` lands in the EE's sregs (ps2sdk `ee/kernel/src/sifcmd.c` `set_sreg`) instead of needing a registered handler. **Off by default** (`PS2Runtime::setIopSifCommandForwarding`, the game turns it on; rotk: `LOTR_IOP_LLE=1`), so a game on HLE services is unchanged. PCSX2 runs the real IOP sifcmd module from the BIOS, so the reference is ps2sdk. rotk: AUDIOPF passes `DmaThreadSync` and runs its command loop (EE flush dest = its real buffer 0x122340, was the fake 0x01000020); default config bit-identical to the pre-merge oracle (Hel01 fight, 3,191 flips). |
| `269-sif-command-handlers-now` (new, rotk, 2026-10-06/07) | `ps2xRuntime/include/runtime/ee_scheduler.h`, `src/lib/Kernel/EeScheduler.cpp`, `include/ps2_runtime.h`, `src/lib/ps2_runtime.cpp` | **Queued EE SIF command handlers are delivered at checkpoints INSIDE a host-pump scope** -- the SIF interrupt preempting the running code, as on hardware. A pumped chain (row 07: checkpoints suppressed) that waits on an IOP reply would otherwise wait forever: the run loop cannot deliver an invocation until the chain ends. `checkpointDueSlow`'s host-pump branch runs them via `PS2Runtime::runPendingSifCommandHandlers(rdram)` (to completion, ra=0 sentinel, on a 16 KB guest stack of their own, re-entrancy-guarded), with `EeScheduler::takePendingInvocation(kind)` and a pending-SifCommand counter so the check costs nothing otherwise. Only while `setIopSifCommandForwarding` is on (nothing else queues SifCommand invocations). rotk: AUDIOPF's cid-0 reply inside the level-load drain (`loader::hook_13aa10` -> 0x154880, `LOTR_IOP_LLE=1`); default config bit-identical (Hel01 fight, 3,244 flips); LLE frames identical to the first, hook-side version of this fix (4,093 flips). |
| `270-iop-sysclib-sprintf` (new, rotk, 2026-10-07) | `ps2xIOP/src/emulator/imports/iop_sysclib.cpp` | **IOP sysclib `sprintf` (19) / `vsprintf` (42) format for real** -- upstream copied the format string literally, so every formatted string an IRX built was wrong; rotk's AUDIOPF `FormatCDPath` (`sprintf(path, "\\%s;1", name)`) searched the disc for `\%s;1` and every file open failed. o32 argument fetch (sprintf: a2, a3, then the caller's stack from sp+16; vsprintf: the va_list's 4-byte slots; `ll` takes an 8-byte-aligned pair); conversions/flags as ps2sdk `iop/system/sysclib` (prnt): `d i u o x X c s p %`, `- + space # 0`, width/precision incl. `*`, `h`/`l`/`ll`. (PCSX2 runs the BIOS sysclib, so ps2sdk is the reference.) rotk `LOTR_IOP_LLE=1`: AUDIOPF's `sceCdSearchFile` now finds its files and serves every EE file read through `sceCdRead`; frames = the default run's within 2 flips (3,769 flips). |
